# Maple2 for the KMS 2525 client

This fork is upstream [Maple2](https://github.com/MS2Community/Maple2) with fixes for the Korean MapleStory 2 client, build 2525. The branch is `kms2525`. Upstream's README.md is unchanged, which keeps merges from upstream simple. Setup and running are the same as upstream.

## How changes are made and checked

The changes in this fork are written by AI agents. Before a change is published it passes automatic checks (commit author, message and leak scans), an AI review of its diff, a Release build of the servers and the full test suite with the KR and NA locales. No person reviews the code, and most changes have not been tried in a live game; where a change was tried live, its commit says so. Commits whose subject starts with `UNREVIEWED:` went through the same checks: the prefix was only a working marker, and newer commits leave it out.

## Data and ingest

The ingest (`Maple2.File.Ingest`) fills the metadata database from the folder `MS2_DATA_FOLDER`. This fork is built and tested with the data pack [MapleStory2-XML v1.3.2](https://github.com/MS2Community/MapleStory2-XML/releases/tag/v1.3.2) (2026-08-10). Take that tag, not `releases/latest`: the latest release is a nightly build without `Server.m2d` and with another `Xml.m2d`.

- `MS2_DATA_FOLDER` is a copy of a MapleStory 2 client's `Data` folder with the four files of v1.3.2 put into it: `Server.m2d`, `Server.m2h`, `Xml.m2d`, `Xml.m2h` (the two Xml files replace the client's own; use a copy so the client you play keeps its files).
- From the client's own `Data` folder the ingest reads `Resource/Exported.m2d`, `Resource/Library.m2d` and the packs under `Resource/Model/` (Map, Effect, Camera, Tool, Item, Npc, Path, Character, Textures), each with its `.m2h`.
- sha256 of the v1.3.2 files:
  - `Server.m2d` 37a46cc144d766bcad007f14e4995f76f6be023454df682af08ed47f516e6ba6
  - `Server.m2h` 244e6a8b2a50c30769cbd07ffa722932fa30d1eef4caf5245163c900c95c2d3c
  - `Xml.m2d` 7ce8b08c0d93e26fc600adbe1d38266d5027341eabd4536015e53307313f1bff
  - `Xml.m2h` 34c245de4cf34f427a4b0817b32c64d23a17ba6a3d708de33b678e03b5ed38ab
- `LOCALE` is the client's region (KR, NA, CN, JP, TH, TW; not set means KR). The ingest keeps the data rows whose feature is on for that region, and the servers read it too. Use the same value for the ingest and the servers, and the region of the client you play.
- `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` is optional: the path of the played client's own `Xml.m2d`, for a client of another version than the data pack. The ingest then takes the client-side rows (prices, furnishing rows, item merge ids) from it and leaves out maps, npcs, items and missions that client does not have. The bullets below that name this setting say what each does.
- `MS2_ORIGINAL_DROP_TABLE=1` is optional: the ingest reads the pack's original drop table instead of today's (see "Original (KMS) drop table").
- After pulling a commit that touches `Maple2.File.Ingest`, run the ingest again. The bullets below that say "needs a re-ingest" depend on it, and the servers stop at start when the metadata database is older than they are.

## How packet layouts are checked

Packet layouts are checked against the client source, not against another server. For each packet type and sub-type, a checker compares two things:

- Client to server: does the server read exactly the bytes the client's writer sends?
- Server to client: does the server write exactly the bytes the client's reader expects?

Each row gets one verdict:

- **pass:** the bytes match exactly.
- **warning:** the server writes more bytes than the client reads (trailing bytes).
- **fail:** the server reads past the end of what the client sends, the client would misread what the server sends, or the server does not handle a sub-type the client sends.
- **not checkable:** the client's reader or writer could not be followed automatically, or the server's code needs game state before it reads.

Every fix in this fork names the client function it follows (a file and line under the client source). The commit message quotes the checker's line before and after.

Results today:

| direction | rows | pass | warning | fail | not checkable |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| client to server | 680 | 239 | 5 | 67 | 369 |
| server to client | 1,193 | 592 | 4 | 0 | 597 |

The 67 client-to-server fails are all sub-types the server does not handle (listed below).

Server-to-client rows whose layout depends on client state are checked once per branch the client can take; a row passes when one branch reads the packet exactly. Of the server-to-client passes, 10 are weak (only a branch that ignores the packet, or a guessed condition, fits).

The four server-to-client warnings are kept on purpose; in each the client reads a prefix and ignores the rest:
- Trade sub-type 13 carries a trailing byte that the official server sends too.
- The skill state packet (sub-type 6) carries an extra int that shifts fields the client reads and then discards.
- The achievement favorite packet has no reader in the client (it handles sub-types 0 to 2 only).
- The skill macro load (sub-type 0) is read by a client handler that acts on sub-type 2 only.

## Fixes so far, by system

- **Opcodes:** client-to-server opcodes 0xAB to 0xBC are renumbered to the client's list.
- **Emote:** the list entry is 26 bytes; the favorite request (sub-type 4) is read as the client writes it.
- **Emote list flags and favourites:** the list entry is written as the client reads it (id, level, permanent flag, expiry, learn time, favourite flag). As KMS2 sends it, a character's starting emotes are permanent with both times 0, and a learned emote is not permanent, has expiry 0 and carries the time it was learned; the client lists permanent emotes first by id and the others by learn time. Before, every entry went out not permanent with learn time 0 and no favourite. The learn answer carries the learn time in its second time field (it was 0). The favourite request (sub-type 4) is now kept for a held emote, so the mark is still there after a relog; a request for an emote not held is dropped. Learn times and marks are in a new nullable column of character-unlock (migration AddEmoteStates); an emote learned before it has learn time 0. Left: no emote here has an expiry (KMS2 sends 0 for all of them and no item gives a timed emote), and using an emote not held is dropped without an answer, as the client offers only held ones.
  - A stored emote state entry without a state (a row edited by hand to `{"id":null}`) is left out at load and counts as no state; before, marking that emote as a favourite failed.
- **Trigger:** the sub-type numbers are the client's (skip cutscene, UI, load script, save script, discard script).
- **Next Trigger key (Trigger 0x04):** the client sends sub-type 4 with no body for key 300014, "Next Trigger" (keytable.cpp:778, bound to '=' by default for every player). The server dropped it. It is a developer key, so only a GameMaster account gets an effect; a press from anyone else is dropped with a debug line. Chosen here: at its next update on the field thread, each trigger of the GM's field acts as if the first condition of its current state that names a transition had passed, without testing it: that condition's actions run, then the state changes. A state with no such condition stays.
- **Trigger balloon text:** the cinematic balloon packet's first byte names the client string table that resolves a `$key$` text (`UICutSceneCinematic` 0x141909eb0, client/ms2/uicutscenecinematic.cpp): 0 is stringTrigger.xml, any other value stringNpcAi.xml. add_balloon_talk on an npc's spawn point sent 1 ("is an npc"), so every such trigger key showed "NO KEY : $...$" on the client; it now sends 0, like set_dialogue and add_balloon_talk on the player. NPC AI say nodes keep 1 (their `AI__` keys are in stringNpcAi.xml). Of the NA data's 481 add_balloon_talk keys, 401 are on an npc and none of them resolved before. After the fix, 84 balloon keys (in 02000532 to 02000535_bf and 52000187_qd) and 175 of the 7,425 trigger keys are still absent from the KR pack's kr/stringTrigger.xml. The KR pack's own copies of these triggers use the same keys, and its other languages have them, so the original client shows "NO KEY" there too.
- **Balloon on the player (spawn id 0):** add_balloon_talk and set_dialogue with spawn id 0 (104 and 147 uses in the trigger data) put the balloon over the first player in the field, and every player saw it there. The client keys a balloon by the packet's object id and runs no trigger itself, so the server picks the speaker. Chosen here: each player in the field gets the balloon over its own character, sent to its own session only; remove_balloon_talk with spawn id 0 (2 uses) also clears each player's own balloon. set_state now logs at debug level instead of error on every state change.
- **Trigger dungeon variables:** a variable never set reads 0; no client code or table gives a start value.
- **Trigger box rotation:** a trigger box was tested as an axis-aligned box and its placement rotation was dropped at ingest. In KMS2 the client tests a point in the box's own frame (`TriggerObject::Contains` @0x140de5f60, client/ms2/triggeractioncli.cpp): centred on the box, half its size each way, turned by its Z rotation (X and Y rotation are not read). The box now keeps its rotation and turns the same way. Of the 3,664 trigger boxes in all maps, 281 have a Z rotation that is not a multiple of 180 and 239 of them cover other ground than before (the rest are square boxes turned 90 or 270), in 133 maps; none has an X or Y rotation. A pack ingested before this loads every box as unturned until it is ingested again.
  - Chosen here: keep the server's 10-unit margin round the box and its prism height, and use the existing Rectangle polygon for a turned box (the axis-aligned box stays for 0 and 180).
- **Trigger score board:** score_board_score compares only in a field whose script called score_board_set_score; elsewhere it is the old test (the threshold compared with 0, so GreaterEqual passes). Chosen here (no client rule; inferred from the data): while a board is open, each npc that spawn_npc_range spawned adds its score when it dies, and a picked-up score orb (90000019, the item the bonus maps' spawn_item_range drops) adds its amount and gives no item. 80000014's bonus checks score_board_score >= 18000, and its data gives 8,300 (83 npcs x 100) + 1,500 (its 2001 npc) + 10 x 800 (the orbs) = 17,800, so the bonus needs the orbs as well as the kills. Each change sends the new points to the board (command 1). With no board open nothing is counted (02000385_bf, 02000393_bf and 99999908 score kills but open none). No packet score reaches the board, so 02020063/02020065 battle_1 (800 points) and 99999840/99999841 would otherwise never end. score_board_create now opens the client's board (0xE1, as the client reads it): type "ShadowGauge" as the boss gauge with the script's most points (02020063/02020065 battle_1), "ScoreBoardTopCenter" as the top-center score dialog (80000014/80000015 bonus); score_board_set_score sends the points to the open board (command 1) and score_board_remove closes it. The type "" (02020063/02020065 battle_2/3, 99999840/99999841) names no kind the client shows, so it still only logs. A player who enters while a board is open (from score_board_create or shadow_expedition_open_boss_gauge) gets it at its current points.
- **Trigger boss gauge points:** shadow_expedition_points reads its `point` attribute (it read `score`, which the data does not have) and passes once the open boss gauge holds that many points (the kill share below). The maps: 02000401_bf, 02000403_bf, 02000461_bf, 02000543_bf, 02020061_bf, 02020062_bf, 52010038_qd, 52020001_qd, 52020016_qd, 52100031_qd and 52100041_qd. With no gauge open it waits for one (52010038_qd tests 150 points before its gauge script opens the gauge). Chosen here: a field none of whose scripts opens a gauge passes at once, as before (every KR shadow map opens one: 02000403_bf's event_02, like its twin 52100041_qd).
- **Boss gauge kill share:** the boss gauge of shadow_expedition_open_boss_gauge fills with the monsters killed since it opened, out of the field's monsters at the open, scaled to its max_gauge_point (`ShadowGauge`); 0xE1 command 1 sends each new value. Chosen here (no client rule; inferred from the data): no table or npc field gives a monster points, and 02000401_bf notes that a kill before the open would keep the gauge from 100%. The count is the monsters alive at the open plus those spawned before the first kill (the scripts open the gauge and spawn the wave together); after that it is fixed, as 02000401_bf's spawn points refill every 10 s and a share of the live monsters would never fill. Every monster killed while it is open adds one, refilled ones too (a player's summon or a friendly npc does not, at the open or after it); a counted one removed without dying (destroy_monster) leaves the count; the points stop at the most and never drop. The open resets the score to 0; close_boss_gauge, score_board_create and score_board_remove stop the count.
- **Trigger dungeon room timer:** a dungeon room whose row has a timerType (clock or gauge) and a durationTick gets that room timer when its first player enters; players who enter later get the running timer and its lap marks. With isExpireTimeOut the room closes when the time runs out, as bonus maps do. Without it, the time running out only makes dungeon_timeout pass. The actions follow the shipped scripts' own comments on them. dungeon_set_end_time ends the time function, so dungeon_timeout no longer passes; the room still closes at its time with isExpireTimeOut, which the scripts note must be blank for set_end_time to work. dungeon_close_timer closes the timer window (client command 5). dungeon_stop_timer stops the time and closes the window (4). dungeon_reset_time runs the timer again for the given seconds (2). dungeon_set_lap_time and dungeon_move_lap_time_to_now set a gauge mark, in milliseconds from the start, and send all marks (3). Without a room timer they do nothing and dungeon_timeout stays false. Choice: the server starts the timer at the first player's entry; the client does not show when the official server started it.
- **Dungeon missions:** a dungeon room's missions (its partyMissions, then its userMissions, the client's order) are now sent when a player enters a room field. The server first sends RoomStageDungeon mode 0, which is where the client makes its mission list and registers the DungeonMission handler. Missions score by the client's DungeonMissionData::GetScore. maxScore is split into applyCount steps; a row without applyCount has one step. A penalty mission starts full and loses a step's score per count. DamageToNpc counts a player's damage to the npc named in value2 (any npc without it): one step per value1 / applyCount. LastHitNpc counts a step when the player's hit kills an npc in value1. dungeon_mission_complete fills a mission as before. A user mission counts for the acting player. Choice: a party mission counts in every player's record of the room, because the client keeps one list per player. DeathCount counts a step at each death of the player in the room (all 46 rows are penalty, named for not dying). DamageBySkill counts a step at each hit (not a miss) on the player by a skill in value1; a row with value2 needs that skill level (one row). GainBuff counts a step when a buff in value1 is put on the player; a refresh of a buff it already has does not count (choice). PlayTime gets its full score when the room is cleared within value2 seconds of the dungeon start: every row's name gives a minute limit that is value2 / 60, value1 is not read, and the start is the room's entry (choice: the names do not say whether the time starts at the boss). The PlayTime packet's two times stay 0, the client shows nothing from them. dungeon_first_user_mission_score compares the sum of the first entrant's user mission scores. In the fame challenge rooms (one player each) that is the score the client's reward window shows.
  - Mission types: the client registers 16 mission classes (dungeonmissioncli.cpp:59-74); the server has 8 (LastHitNpc, PlayTime, DamageBySkill, DeathCount, GainBuff, LimitUserCount, Trigger, DamageToNpc), and the ingest drops a row of any other type. No data uses the other eight (KillNpc, DamageCount, UseEp, UseSp, UseSkill, HealingPotion, UseInteractObject and the Event base): every row of `table/dungeonmission.xml` has one of the server's types in the NA data pack (802 rows), the KR client (302) and the KMS2_DEV client (295), so the ingest drops none and they are not built.
- **Dungeon stage end:** the client keeps the dungeon stage that RoomStageDungeon mode 0 makes (the mission list, the party window's give-up state, the quest guide leaving out field missions) until mode 1 (`ClearStage`); it does not drop it on a field change (`ClearDungeonState` runs only when the character object is made and at shutdown). The server never sent mode 1, so the stage of the last dungeon stayed for the rest of the session. KMS2 sends mode 1 as the player leaves a dungeon room field, 18 times in 21 stages; the server now sends it when a player moves from a room field to the lobby or out of the dungeon. A move between room fields keeps the stage (chosen here; the next field sends mode 0 again).
- **Dungeon give-up after the result:** DungeonMission mode 3 (`RoomStageDungeonCli::OnStage`) sets the stage byte, and the party window offers the give-up vote only while it is 0 and the give-up flag is set (`IsStageFlagSet`). KMS2 sends mode 3 with 1 first in every clear (17 of 17); the server did not send it, so the vote stayed on offer in a cleared room. It is now sent to the field when the room gets its result. Chosen here: also on a fail (no KMS2 fail was seen), and a player who enters a room field later gets the give-up flag the script set and, after the result, the stage byte, since a new stage starts with neither.
- **Dungeon give-up (DungeonReward 5):** the party window's give-up button, shown to the party leader while the stage byte is 0 and the room's script set the give-up flag, opens a confirm box (the challenge fails for all members) and sends DungeonReward with the mode byte 5 alone. The server had no case for it. It now answers a leader in a dungeon room without a result: before the script's `dungeon_enable_give_up` with RoomDungeon error 24 (`s_room_dungeon_cannot_giveup_yet`), after it with DungeonMission 5 to the field (`RoomStageDungeonCli::OnGiveUp`, the leader-gave-up notice) and the room's Fail result, which sends the fail result window as for a script fail. Anyone else, a lobby or a room that has its result gets nothing. Chosen here: the players stay in the room until they leave it through the result window, as after any fail (no KMS2 give-up was seen).
- **Trigger dungeon level:** dungeon_level compares the room's groupType (2 raid, 3 chaos raid; the scripts' states are named so), not its dungeonLevel, which is a character level. Outside a raid or chaos raid room it keeps the old answer (3), so a script that tests only 2 and 3 still goes on.
- **Trigger start_tutorial:** a no-op. 52000065_qd tutorial.xml calls it for jobs 1, 90, 100 and 110 next to opening portal 1; the client has no reader for it. The tutorial there is the client's own guide (table/guide_tutorial_100.xml): the script feeds it ServerTriggerEvent 260, and guide event 270 sends Tutorial 0x8A (CGuideActionClearTutorial) when the player leaves the map, which TutorialHandler serves. The skip dialog (UI 0x4ea5) sends nothing, and RequestSkipTutorial 0x59 has no sender in the client. Only job 1 starts on 52000065 (job.xml); 90, 100 and 110 start on their own maps.
- **Trigger dungeon max user count:** dungeon_max_user_count compares the client's DungeonRoom::GetMaxUserCount for the party size the room was made with (with isChangeMaxUser the size held between 2 and maxUserCount, else maxUserCount). The scripts test 1 to 4 only, so outside a dungeon room and in a 6 or 10 user room it keeps the old answer (1); dungeon_common/checkusercount has no other way on in a room.
- **Trigger transitions to a missing state:** 302 transitions in 193 of the 4,584 KR trigger scripts name a state their script does not have. The raw files of `Xml.m2d` give the same list once `<import>` files are merged, so neither the parse, the name fixes nor the feature filter loses a state: 170 of the targets are in no script of the map, 126 are states of a sibling script (98 of them the copied `*_customer` scripts of 02000387), 5 are states the source has commented out (02000066 `reward`, whose start state waits for box 103 and then moves to its commented-out spawn states; 02000179, 02000420, 02000498, 99999910), and 1 differs only in case (02000384 `quest_idle` / `quest_Idle`). The client's own trigger loader (`TriggerCli::GetState`, triggercli.cpp, a case-sensitive name map) makes an empty state for an unknown name, which does nothing and never moves on. The server does the same: the moving condition's actions run once and the state stops. The line it logs is now Debug, not Error. Chosen here: names are matched with their case, as the client's map does, and a state of a sibling script is not looked up.
- **Colosseum continue-play:** after the intro the Colosseum scripts run talk_npc on Nagi's spawn point; the server now starts that npc's talk with each player, as a click does (the client opens the talk window for a Respond it did not ask for, `UITalkManager::OnTalkPacket`). The choice's script function sets the DungeonPlayType user value (1 new game, 2 continue). The npc's script conditions `receivedRewardDungeonID` (a reward of the room was taken in its period, so the continue is offered) and `completeRewardDungeonID` (all were, so continue starts a new game) are read from the server pack's npcScriptCondition rows, which the parser does not read; their meaning is the server's reading of the names and the script flow (the client has no such table). Taken is the client's `DungeonRecord::GetBonusRewardCount` (the record's count while its cooldown period runs) against the room's rewardCount (`DungeonRecordCli::HasCountLeft`). set_user_value_from_dungeon_reward_count sets its key to that count of the room's one player, which for the Colosseum is its rounds cleared, so ClearRound starts the next round. A run that loses a round after the first ends with dungeon_clear uiType None; the server now clears the room there without the clear banner, so its rewards and the rounds it cleared are saved (before, only a run of all 13 rounds saved its rounds). A room takes one result: a later dungeon_clear or dungeon_fail changes nothing (no second result packet, clear count or dungeon_clear condition; logged at debug). The Colosseum is left out of that, because its script goes back to its start after each end and the next run in the same room ends with its own clear or fail. The conditions need a re-ingest; until then their rows check nothing, so continue is offered and always starts a new game.
- **Dungeon room cooldown from cooldownValue:** the client takes a room's cooldown type from its `cooldownValue` alone (`DungeonRoom::GetCooldownType`: 0 is the next day, any other value a day of the week) and ends the period at the next midnight of that day, numbered with Sunday 1, a week later when that day is today (`CalcCooldownEndTime`, `GetWeekdayStart`); the KR room table has no `cooldownType`. The ingest now sets the type the same way and keeps the value as the client numbers it, and a clear's reward period ends where the client's does. Before, the KR rooms had no type, their period ended at once and their reward count never held, so Nagi never offered the Colosseum continue after a run that cleared a round. Needs a re-ingest. A record whose period end was written by the older rule (a weekday counted from Sunday 0, at the clear's time of day) kept that end, which a clear only replaced once it had passed; now, at login and at a clear, a record still in its period whose end is not the rule's end gets the rule's end of its earliest possible clear (a period before the old end), its counts kept, so it ends with the week of that clear.
- **Dungeon clear result:** the result packet (`UIDungeonRewardDialog::OnRewardPacket`, `uidungeonrewarddialog.cpp:557`) carries 32 bonus bits; each of bits 1 to 9 adds a line to the bonus tooltip (1 clear count, 2 event bonus, 3 mission rank, 4 helper, 5 helper event, 6 mentor, 7 mentee, 8 mentee party gift, 9 weekly reward; bit 0 has an empty text). The server wrote them from a field nothing set, so no line was shown. It now writes the flags the clear collected, without bit 0: clear count (a clear with a reward count left), event bonus (a running DungeonBonusReward), helper (the helper reward was given) and weekly reward. KMS2 sends 0x2 for a counted clear and 0x202 when the clear also gave a weekly step reward; the server set the weekly bit on every counted clear, and now sets it with the weekly reward only. Other differences from KMS2 fixed with it: the bonus reward list holds only the amounts given (the server always wrote three zero amounts, and the dialog plays its bonus stage whenever that list is not empty); the main list names the clear's prestige exp (the `dungeonClear` amount of the prestige table, 75000 in KR), which is now also given with the clear, under the usual prestige rules; the clear's exp is sent with the dungeon message code 1085 instead of 0; a room without a rank table sends -1 and 0 as its scores. The clear is sent in the order of KMS2: the room records and the reward notice, the result, then what the result names (meso, exp, prestige exp, items, and last the weekly reward; chosen here: a helper's box items between the room's items and the weekly reward). The server gave the rewards first; the client's reader only stores the result, so both orders work. The weekly reward's exp carries its own message code 1146, and the meso packets carry the code of their exp (1085, 1146) in the int the client hands to its gain text (`CSceneStateGame::OnMeso`), where the server wrote 0. Every exp gain now sends the exp packet before the prestige packet it brings, as KMS2 does.
  - Chosen here: in a room with a rank table the two score fields are the best score before the clear and the run's score, so the dialog's new record effect (best below run, `:469`) plays on a first clear and on a better one; KMS2 shows one such result (best 2000, run 1000), which does not tell the best before from the best after. The mission rank, helper event, mentor and mentee bits are not set: the server has no such reward. The party byte stays 0, the only value seen.
- **Dungeon closing photo (DungeonRoom mode 10):** the client shows the clear or fail banner for 2 s, then the closing photo, then the reward window (`UIDungeonClosingPhotoDialog`); the result packet opens the reward window by itself. KMS2 sends mode 10 in none of its one-player clears (0 of 17), so a clear with one player no longer sends it and goes straight to the reward window. Chosen here: a clear with more than one player and a fail still send it; KMS2 gives no case of either. The players counted are the ones present in the room at the end (the ones the photo lists), not the party size: a party of four with three gone clears without the banner.
- **Dungeon fail result:** when a room changes to Fail, each of its players gets the reward packet with the fail flag and the room's seconds. The client's closing photo window opens the reward window when it closes (`UIDungeonClosingPhotoDialog`), and that window shows the fail title and the time from this packet (`UIDungeonRewardDialog::OnRewardPacket`); without it the window kept its placeholder text ("time", 0/0).
- **DungeonReward 2 and 3 are not sent:** the reward window (`UIDungeonRewardDialog::OnPacket`, uidungeonrewarddialog.cpp:386) reads mode 2 as the reward block alone, shown in its `_minigame` frame (`ShowMinigame`), and mode 3 as a win byte and the reward block, with the win pose or a random loss pose (`vfunc_25`'s case 4). The mini games' results go as mode 1 (two round ints, then the reward block) at end_mini_game and end_mini_game_round. No client code, request or table names the game or event that would get 2 or 3, so the server sends neither.
- **Dungeon season reward mail:** at the end of each season week the player is mailed the season's reward. The season total is the sum of the week's `CurrentRecord`s above 0 over the player's season rank rooms, as the client's `DungeonRecordManagerCli::GetTotalScore` sums them (dungeonrecordcli.cpp:580; the client has no caller of it and no cap); the server caps it at the season's `maxSeasonScore` (chosen from the name). The reward is the season's first reward whose `requireScore` is at most the total (`DungeonSeason::GetReward`, rewards highest first), an item with the row's `grade` and `count` (`DungeonSeasonItemGift` is an ItemEntity row; now ingested, so this needs a re-ingest), in a system mail with the season's `systemMailID`. The mail goes at the next login, weekly reset or season rank clear after the week's expiry (a week that ends while the player is offline is mailed at the next login; the clear mails the ended week before its score replaces the week's, which lost that week's mail before); the week's scores are then set to 0, which also marks the week as mailed, so no migration is needed. Weeks are told apart by their stored expiry: records written before the week end moved from midnight to the season's start time keep their midnight expiry, so in the week running at that change the rooms cleared again after it move to the new end and the week is mailed in two parts, each with its own total (no score is lost or counted twice). A total below every `requireScore` mails nothing. Not tried live.
- **Dungeon extra reward event:** the `DungeonExtraReward` game event is now ingested (needs a re-ingest) from its value1 rows as the client's `DungeonExtraRewardCountCtx::Load` reads them: `dungeonID`, `count`, `cooldownType` (`dayOfWeeks` or `nextDay` by name, any case, else none) and `cooldownValue`; rows with no room or no count are skipped and a later row of a room replaces the earlier one. The event packet writes the id, then per room the key, room id, event id and count, as `GameEventDungeonExtraReward` reads them. A room's record keeps its own event count and expiry (`ExtraClears`, the new `ExtraCooldownTime` of `AddDungeonExtraCooldown`); the record's long after the extra count is that expiry (`m_extraCount`), where the server used to write the first-clear time. The enter check passes while the event count is not used up before the daily and weekly checks, as `DungeonRecordCli::HasCountLeft` tests it first. Chosen here (the client does not settle it): a clear takes an event count only when the weekly count and bought counts are used up; a none-type count lasts until the event ends; with several events naming a room the lowest event id wins (the client keeps the first it gets). Not tried live.
- **Item merge:**
  - Request and response sub-types are renumbered to the client's.
  - The option preview (Select) is written in the client's layout.
  - The merge table row is found as in the client: by the item's equip subtype for a material of subtype 1, else part 0, with the level limits and the row's `partLimit` list. A slot without `part` is part 0, as the client reads it (the parser gives 1; the ingest stores it under 0, so it needs a re-ingest). The materials offered are every inventory item the table answers for, asked by its table id, not the table's keys looked up as item ids. The list is read at ingest from the client's `table/itemmergeoptionbase.xml` (the played client's pack has the lists; the data folder's has none), so it needs a re-ingest; rows without it are not judged by it.
  - The merge table id is the material's `param1`, as in the client, when the ingest has the played client's pack (`MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML`): the ingest reads it from that pack's `itemdata/*.xml`, and a material with a table id is asked by it first. Without that pack the table is asked by the material's item id. The hand copies of table 37000055 for 37000056..63 are made in both cases, only for an id with no rows of its own, so they serve only a material that got no `param1`. The data parser's item `Property` class has no `param1` field; with one, the data folder's own item xml could give it (the present data folder's item xml has no `param1` at all).
  - Known limit: the server's item rows come from the data folder's item xml, which is of another version than the played client's. There the merge materials have subtype 21 and 22 (the client's 1 and 2: 30 items of 21 are the client's 1, 35 of 22 the client's 2) and equips have subtype 0 or 16 (the client's is the id's kind, 12 to 83). So the equip subtype is taken from the item id (`id / 100000 - 100` for 112xxxxx to 183xxxxx, which holds for every equip in the played client's item data), a material of subtype 1 or 21 is asked by part and every other by part 0, as the client asks its subtype 2. 11 KR materials of type 19 (subtype 21/22, e.g. 37000013) are type 4 in the played client's item data, which has no merge row for them (`FindMergeData` asks type 19); the ingest now takes the client's type for them (see Bag tab below), so they are no merge material here either.
  - The smith's lines come as in KMS2, with no request from the client: the script's first event (8001) right after the staged item's material list (mode 1), the second (8002) after each result (mode 5), also after a choice. Before, the lines started at the crystal's selection and went on by a running count, past the script's last event on the fourth result. The trophy update (item_merge_success) now follows the result packet, as in KMS2 (bag updates, meso, ItemMerge, NpcTalk, Achieve).
  - Empower checks that the picked crystal is still held before it takes the materials. The crystal was looked up after the materials were used up, so a second request for a crystal that the first had used took the materials and gave nothing; it is now refused with the invalid material error and takes nothing.
- **Ride:**
  - RideSync reads one trailing int per record.
  - The ride start request carries a byte after the object id, and the response echoes it.
  - Boarding another player's mount (ride join) follows the client's rule: the joiner and the owner are alive and a seat is free; the owner riding alone takes no one. The client's friend, party or same-guild check applies only with the RideShareAuthority feature, which is off in KR and NA (feature.xml level 99), so anyone may board and the server checks none of these either.
  - A dead player's ride start is refused with the same notice as a field without rides. The mount swap (RequestRide 2) is taken only from the owner of a mount item ride and ends the old ride like a dismount: the passengers get off, a ride's stats and the key table go back. Before, the swap only forgot the ride, and a passenger who sent it kept its seat without a ride. Left: the swap and its answer are not in any KMS2 session, so only the client's reader backs it (it gets off and summons the new item, which sends a new start); the client's own refusals to get off a battle or object ride (notice 0x655) stay client-side, since the server ends those rides itself.
  - A refused mount swap (no ride, a passenger, a battle or object mount) is answered with the same s_action_cant_ride box as a refused ride start. Chosen here: ResponseRide has no refusal mode, and no sender of RequestRide 2 is named in the client source so far, so what the client waits for is not known.
- **Mentor:** the server no longer sends an initial packet that the client has no reader for, and its writer is removed.
- **Mentoring requests:** the client's mentoring window is closed with a season notice while no mentoring event (our ReturnUser event) runs (`uigamemenudialog.cpp:2053`), and KMS2 shows the feature idle: only the empty request list (Mentor 3) and two zero ids (Mentor 12) at login. The client's requests are Mentor 4 (register as mentee, `uimentoringmenteeregisterpage.cpp:164`), 8 (register as mentor, `uimentoringmentorpage.cpp:371`), 5 (a mentor invites a mentee by id or name, `uiinvitementoringdialog.cpp:424`), 6 and 7 (the mentee accepts or declines a request, `:453`, `:266`). The server read 4 only, took it for any role, kept the role nowhere the world could see and answered with a packet without the error code the client reads (Mentor 16: a byte code 1 to 9 and an int). Now: a registration stores the role on the character, tells the world (the party and guild member data carry it), sends Mentor 1 to the whole field (the client sets the role on the player with that object id) and then Mentor 4 for a mentee or Mentor 8 for a mentor; a second registration is dropped. An invite from a player who is no mentor is dropped; an unknown name gets error 2, the sender or another character of the sender's account error 3, a target who is no mentee error 6. While a mentoring event runs, an invite that passes these checks is stored as a request (one per mentor and mentee) and both players are told: the mentor with Mentor 5 (the mentee's id and name), the mentee with Mentor 14 (the mentor's name and the request), through the World server when the mentee is on another channel (new world rpc Mentoring). The mentee answers a request that names them as mentee with 6 or 7 (any other id gets error 2): an accept stores the pair, removes the mentee's other requests, sends Mentor 6 and sends the mentor their lists again; a decline removes the request and sends Mentor 7. At login and when the window asks for its list the player gets the requests waiting for them (Mentor 2), their pairs (Mentor 3) and which partners are online (Mentor 15: a count, then per partner the id and a bool; the server wrote one int). After that, a partner's login or logout sends Mentor 15 with that partner alone (the client sets the flag of each listed partner only). Refused: a mentee who has a mentor (error 7), the same pair again (8), a mentor with 3 mentees, the client's max (3), a mentor with `MentorMaxWaitingCount` requests waiting (KR 100, error 4), a mentee with `MenteeMaxReceivedCount` (KR 20, error 5; both rows have no locale, so NA uses them too). With no event nothing changes: login sends only the empty pair list, an invite is dropped after the checks and an answer gets error 2. Needs a migration (table `mentoring`).
  - Chosen here: a mentee registration with no running event gets error 1 (not a returning user); a mentor registration without one is dropped. The mentor's level limit is a client constant that is not in the server data, so it is not checked again. The two waiting limits are server constants the client never reads; a limit of 0 refuses every request. A mentee who accepts a request of a mentor who has filled up since gets error 3, the request is removed and the mentee's list is sent again. The limits are checked under one lock per Game server, with a unique index per mentor and mentee, so two servers taking requests at the same moment can pass a limit by one. For the same reason a request can be stored for a mentee whose pair another server accepted at the same moment: accepting it gets error 7, removes it and sends the list again. Not done: the mentoring coins and points (Mentor 10 to 12, error 9), the candidate roles (1 and 4) and ending or expiring a pair. The client has no expiry for a pair or request (its only dates, Mentor 10 and 11, are the coin and point counters), so rows from an earlier ReturnUser season come back with the next one.
  - Chosen here: a player who is registered as a mentee and asks to register as a mentor, or the other way round, gets error 3 (invalid target): the client's error list (`CMentoringManager::OnError`, 1 to 9) has no text for holding the other role. Asking again for the role already held stays dropped.
- **Party:** cancel party search reads one byte; the dungeon match confirm (sub-type 31) has no body; the survival squad register is sub-type 55.
- **Party load on a new party:** KMS2 sends a player who makes a party by an invite, or who joins one, a single Party 9 with the join byte 1 (the inviter's has one member), never a load without the byte first. We sent two loads (the inviter two without the byte, the joiner one without and one with); now one with the byte, also for a party made or joined through the party search, the dungeon recruit and the dungeon match. The load at login keeps the byte 0. Left as it is: the member's byte after the level data (ours the mentor role, 0 for most) is 4 for every member KMS2 sends at any level; the client only stores it (userproxy.cpp:1409) and nothing reads it. A guild member's raid rows (KMS2: five rows, raids 26001001 to 26005001 with 1000 each, on some members) are the member's value per guild raid on the guild dialog's raid page (see Guild raid records, under Guild).
- **Party member data by region:** the member data (Party 2, 5, 9 and 12) has a platform part after the 12 mastery levels, which the client reads through its region's platform proxy (`CreatePlatformProxy`, userproxy.cpp:193): KR and JP a string and a long, NA a string and three longs, CN an int, a long, a byte and an int. We wrote the KR part under every `LOCALE`, so an NA or CN client read the fields after it shifted. `PlatformBlock` now writes the `LOCALE`'s part (the KR one for TH and TW, which have no proxy of their own). The rest of the member data, the counters, the Party 9 tail and the recruit listing match the client's reads in order and width. The same user block opens FieldAddUser (after the object id) and each character list entry, and group chat members use the party's writer: the field and character list writers now use `PlatformBlock` too.
- **Party refusals:** kick (Party 4), leader change (Party 8) and leave (Party 3) now send the World server's refusal (not the leader, not found, ...) as the party error, which the client shows (`OnPartyResultMessage` handles every code the server has); before, the player saw nothing. A kick or leader change outside a party gets s_party_err_not_found instead of a lookup of party 0. An invite of one's own name is refused in any letter case (it was compared with the lowercased name only). A kick vote with too few members gets only s_party_err_vote_need_more_people; before, the vote went on after the error.
- **Party member down notice (Party 18):** KMS2 sends a party a member's character id and a byte 0 right after that member dies; the client (`CPartyManager::FUN_14162e3d0`, partymanager.cpp) prints the "member is down" line for another member, and its other line (dark tombstone) for a non-zero byte, and nothing for the player's own id. The writer wrote id 0 with byte 1 and was never sent. It now carries the member's id and is sent once to each member on the step from alive to dead, after the stats update; staying dead, hp updates and the revive send none.
  - Chosen here: the byte is 1 when the death state is the metal tombstone (KMS2 showed only 0), and the dead member's own session gets the line too, as the client drops it.
  - The notice comes with the world's next sync step (every two seconds; KMS2 sends it with the death), and the game server logs each one it sends.
- **Party notices (Party 21 and 37):** not sent: both carry any interface text and name no event. 21 (`OnPartyNoticeWithSound`, partymanager.cpp:1281) prints its text in chat type 2 and plays the sound key after it; 37 (`CPartyManager::FUN_14162ee70`, partymanager.cpp:1357) reads a member id, a name and the text, drops it for a blocked name or id and prints the rest in chat type 2. No client code says which party event sends them, and no party string the client leaves unused is a notice for one (s_party_auto_join_confirm is a question with no box behind it). The writers (`PartyPacket.NoticeWithSound`, `MemberNotice`) stay unsent.
- **Party member gear score (Party 15) is not sent:** the client's mode 15 (`CPartyManager::OnPartyMemberGearScore`, partymanager.cpp:1245) reads a character id and an int and stores it as the member's gear score (the user proxy's int after the outside position, userproxy.cpp:1271), which the dungeon windows take the party's lowest of (`GetMinMemberGearScore`, partymanager.cpp:876). The member block of Party 12 carries the same int (`PlayerInfo`'s GearScore), and the party's member sync sends 12 on a gear score change (PartyManager.SyncUpdate), so 15 adds nothing.
- **Queued player updates are compared when they are applied:** the world queues a game server's updates (death state, health, field) and applies them every two seconds, but it worked out what each one changes when it was queued, against the record of that moment. Two updates of one window (dead then alive, hp down then up, a field and back) were both compared with the record from before the window, so the second was dropped as no change and the record kept the first: a player revived inside the window stayed dead for the world, and their next death was no change (no Party 18, no tombstone). Each update is now compared and applied in one step when its turn comes. A failed sync step is logged and the next one runs (any error but a shutdown used to end the loop, and with it every queued update until a restart).
- **Hit damage reaches the world:** a hit on a player changed the hp in the field only; the world heard of hp from regeneration, hp costs and stat refreshes. A hit that changes the hp now sends the health with the queued updates, so a party member in another field sees it.
  - Chosen here: hits are sent at most once per 500 ms and player. The first change after a quiet 500 ms goes at once, later ones are held and the latest health goes with the field update when the 500 ms are over; a hit that takes the last health, a heal, a revive and a health cost go at once.
  - A held health is sent when the player leaves the field (each field has its own player object, so it would be lost otherwise).
- **Party dungeon info (Party 14) only on a change:** the member update compared the member's dungeon limit table before and after by reference, which always differ, so every member update (each hp change too) was followed by a Party 14. The tables are now compared by content (same dungeons, same limit for each), and Party 14 follows only an update that changes them.
- **Player update compares clubs and dungeon limits by content:** the update event compared the club list and the dungeon limit table of a request with the held ones by reference, which always differ, so every update that named clubs or limits was flagged as a club and dungeon change. The clubs are now compared as a list and the limits entry by entry (a request's limits are merged into the held table, so only its own entries count); an update that repeats them carries only what did change, and one that changes nothing is dropped. The listeners (buddy, club, group chat, guild, marriage, party) pick their packet by the exact change, so an hp or map change that repeated the clubs now sends the short hp or map line instead of the full member line. A member's copy of a player record now holds its own club list and limit table: it shared the table with the channel's record, which is merged in place before the listeners run, so the party's before/after compare saw no change and Party 14 was not sent for a real one.
- **Party buff:** the server constants' partyBuffID0 and partyBuffID1, 70000066 (exp +5/7/10% and meso +10/20/30% from slaying monsters, "playing near your party members") and 90000309 (prize fish), are given to a party member at level 1, 2 or 3 for one, two, or three or more other members of the party in the same field, checked each second, and taken off when none is left; not in a PvP field (the buff ends in a PvP zone).
  - Chosen here (the client does not settle it): "near" is the same field, as the client has no distance for it. The return-user buffs (returnUserPartyBuffID0/1) are left out: this server keeps no returning-user flag. The party's share of a kill's exp by level gap or distance is not built: the client has no rule for it (its only kill-exp message is the corpse-hit assist bonus), so each damage dealer still gets the full exp of its kill.
- **Party dungeon match (32, 33, 34):** a matchmaker in the World server. The rules are the user's choice (11-11), not read from the client:
  1. A match fills up to the room's maximum user count, dungeonroom.xml maxUserCount (the dungeon or raid cap), also for an isChangeMaxUser room. The client's DungeonRoom::GetMaxUserCount is not used for the match: for such a room it is the party size held between 2 and maxUserCount, the size of a room already made, so a search would count as full at 2.
  2. The party leader can stop the search early and go with the members found so far, at least the room's minimum. The client has no start-now control (no button, sub-type or confirm reply for it), so the closest client path is used: Party 34 (cancel) by the leader. When the search found someone and the party has at least the minimum of one of its rooms, the party enters that room; otherwise the search stops and the party stays as it is.
  3. A searcher joins an existing searching party for that room, the largest one with room for them, before a new party is started.
  4. The smaller goes into the larger, and on equal size the later into the earlier, whenever a group starts, as long as the merged size is at most a shared room's maxUserCount and the larger party's capacity. A party that starts a search merges into the largest searching party of the same kind, at least its size, that shares a room with it (the earliest on a tie); else it starts its own group, and that group then takes in the waiting smaller groups of its kind that share a room with it, the largest first, then the earliest, while they fit (their parties move into it as in a merge). The joining party's members move into the larger party (they leave theirs, which goes without a disband message) and the found count rises by their number. A merge or pull that fills the cap makes the room. A matched party cannot search again (error 43) and is not disbanded on a leave or kick while another member is online until its room is set; a party gone before then has its room destroyed. When a member move fails, the groups are put back as they were and no room is made; a party with a dungeon room set does not search (error 43) and is not merged.
  - From the client: Party 32 with no room ids searches the client's DungeonRoomTable::GetMatchRooms rooms (group types 1 and 2 by gear score, level limit and id, from the lowest member's level and gear score, at most DungeonMatchRecommendPickCount); with ids, only the picked rooms of the match kind whose level limit the lowest member meets. Party 33 searches its one room. The match kind is the client's map of the room's group type (normal and raid 1, worldBossDungeon 2, event 3, lapenta 4). The register reply (0x1e) and Party Load write the searching bits the client tests (bit kind; Load wrote a bool before); a stop writes 0. A match sends every member the match-found box (0x1f, which has only an OK button) and makes the room as a party dungeon, so the members enter it as from the party dungeon window.
  - Chosen here (neither the client nor the user settles it): no confirm wait and no decline, as the box has OK only; a member who does not press OK can still enter through the party dungeon. When several rooms are full, the lowest room id. Between groups of the same size, the one that started first. A joiner narrows the group to the rooms both search. Party 34 from a member who is not the leader gets error 40 (s_dungeonMatch_error_isNotChief); a Party 34 of another kind than the party's search, or with no search, changes nothing. A solo searcher who starts their own group gets a party of one (loaded before the register reply); a joiner or a merging party loads the party it moved into. Every member of the party counts for the group, its space, its full check and the leader's early start, online or not, so a party never passes the room's maxUserCount when someone comes back: a member who logs out keeps a slot, and the leader can kick them or start early. A leader who logs out (or is found offline before a search) hands the party over to an online member and the search goes on with the new leader; it stops only when no member is online. An invite, a leave or a kick resizes a searching group (it is matched when it becomes full, and stops when no room holds it); a group still searching then joins or pulls the others as a new search does. A searching party is not disbanded on a leave or kick while another member is online, so a party of two keeps its search for the member left; a disband ends the search with the stop (0x1e) first. When the search ends (stopped, cancelled, or its room cannot be made) a party of one is disbanded, with the disband message. When the room cannot be made the stop has already been sent. A search of a kind other than 1 with no picked room has no rooms (DungeonMatch error 1, see Party dungeon match errors). The reward flag of Party 32 is not used; the rooms' hidden flag and sortOrder (not ingested) are not checked. The register announce bool of 0x1e stays true. The party and queue state are under one lock in the World server; the channel calls a step makes (broadcasts, the room) are collected and made after the lock is released, and the room calls run on a task of their own with a deadline of 10 s (no answer = the room is not made). A leader change, the ready check, the kick vote and the recall are steps too, so their sends keep step order. A channel skips a member remove or disband for a party its player is no longer in. A party that moves away or is disbanded has its Party Finder listing removed. A join (an invite accepted, a Party Finder join) is refused with error 19 (the party is full; the client has no error of its own for it) while the party is matched and its room is being made, and while a dungeon room is set and the party holds that room's maxUserCount (also a room the leader made). A room the leader makes for the party is refused with error 43 while the party searches or is matched (Game tests the search before making it; when World refuses it, the room made is destroyed).
- **Party dungeon match errors (DungeonMatch 0x00EF mode 0):** a refused Party 32 or 33 got no reply. It now gets the client's error box (`OnDungeonMatchError`, `uidungeonhelper.cpp:333`: the error, an int and a string), from the room checks the channel makes before it asks World:
  - 1 notFoundDungeon: Party 32 with no picked room has no recommended room left once the refused ones are dropped, as the client's GetAvailableRooms drops them (the client's own text for an empty list, `UIDungeonSearchPage::CanStartSearch`, `uidungeonsearchdialog.cpp:431`); also a search of a kind other than 1 with no picked room.
  - 2 overMaxRegisterUser, with the count: the party has more members than a room's maxUserCount. For picked rooms the smallest such count; for recommended rooms, when none is left and one was refused only for size, the largest.
  - 3 lackRequire: the one room of Party 33, or the one picked room, is above the lowest member's level, or the player's enter state for it is 10 or more (the FieldEntrance limits, `CPartyManager::CanEnterDungeonRoom`, `partymanager.cpp:561`).
  - 4 containCouldNotEnter: a picked room is not in the table, of another match kind, or closed now (day, hours, open period); of several picked rooms any refused one gives 4 ("one of the selected dungeons cannot be entered").
  - 5 insideDungeonUser: a member's field is a dungeon room instance. The map alone does not tell (78 KR rooms use open field maps, 02000xxx, as their fields), so the member info World keeps now carries the dungeon id of the member's field (0 outside a dungeon instance) with the map and room id.
  - 6 isNotChief and 7 hasOfflineUser stay World's Party errors 40 and 41, which show the same texts (`partymanager.cpp:1409`).
  - Not sent: 8 and 9 (hasDungeonMatchCooldown, hasDungeonMatchCooldownParty) are for the [Comradery] buff (constant DungeonRandomMatchBuffID 90000340); nothing in the client or this server says when it is given, so the server never gives it and has no cooldown to refuse.
  - Random match bonus: a clear of a room the matchmaker made counts dungeon_random_bonus (code: the room id) for every member, however the room was made (a full group, or Party 34 going with the members found so far) and whether the members searched with Party 32, recommended or picked rooms, or Party 33. The client calls the matchmaker as a whole the random match: the room dialog shows its Party 33 party-search button when m_isUseRandomMatch (`uidungeonroomdialog.cpp:1200`), and the client's random-match test of a room (DungeonRoom::IsRandomMatchable, `dungeonroom.cpp:678`) takes the normal and raid group types. A room the party leader made does not count. World marks the rooms it makes (CreateDungeon random_match) and the room record keeps the mark. The bonus has no exp (Exp_RandomDungeonBonus is 0 in AdventureExpTable).
  - Chosen here: the order of the checks (5 first, then per room 4, 3, 2), and which count error 2 carries when several rooms are too small. Only the player's own enter state is tested; the other members' are not known on the channel.
- **Party kick vote:** how long a kick vote lasts is not settled by the client: it shows the time left from the end time the vote packet carries (`PartyVoteCli` reads it after the vote id) and has no kick-vote constant (its only vote constant, PartyVoteReadyTagExpireSeconds, is the ready-check tag). The vote keeps the ready-check length, Constants.PartyVoteReadyDurationSeconds, as before. The vote packet (ready check and kick) now carries that end time, the vote's start plus PartyVoteReadyDurationSeconds in unix seconds, where it wrote the time of sending, so the client's timer no longer starts at zero.
- **Party vote numbers and timers:** the client keeps the number a vote packet carries and answers the vote with it (`PartyVoteCli::SendParty` writes it before the yes or no); the server wrote 0. Each party now numbers its votes (ready checks and kick votes), sends the number with the vote and does not count an answer that carries another vote's number. A vote's timer ends only that vote: a ready check started again after a member answered "not ready" was cut short when the first check's time ran out. While a vote runs, a second ready check or kick vote is refused with s_party_err_already_vote, also for a kick vote, whose refusal used to reach no one. A kick vote that passed stayed open on World, so later answers kicked again and no new vote could start; it now ends, and a kick vote started by a member who is not the leader removes the target (it was refused as not coming from the leader).
- **Party member leaving during a vote:** the client takes a member who goes offline off its ready check (`PartyVoteReadyCli::vfunc_6` from `OnPartyMemberOffline`). World kept a member who left or was kicked among the ready check's voters, so a check where everyone else had answered never ended and ran out its time as "expired". Such a member is now taken off the check, with any answer they gave, and the check ends once everyone left has answered. A kick vote whose target leaves ends on World too (each channel already ended its copy when the target was removed); before, it stayed open and blocked the next vote until its time ran out.
  - Not settled by the client: the kick vote's byte after the target's name. The client keeps it as the reason and only formats it into s_party_vote_title_kick_user, a string-table text outside the client source, and its kick request (Party 45) sends no reason. The server still writes the votes needed there.
- **Dungeon recruiting (RoomDungeon 15 and 16, Party 39 and 43):** the party leader, or a player with no party, asks for helpers (15) or members (16) for a dungeon room. The World server sends the recruiting notice DungeonHelp 0x0109 (mode 0 for 15, mode 1 for 16; the layouts of UIMessengerBrowser) and keeps the recruit open for its chat link. The link's confirm box sends Party 39 (as a helper) or 43 (as a member) with the party id and the room id. From the client: the request carries only the room id; the client shows the buttons only for a room with a helperRequireClearCount, and drops a notice from a character it blocks, so the server does not filter blocks; the inside-the-cooldown answer is Party 40 with the milliseconds left. The user's rules (11-11): the notice goes to every online player whose level meets the room's level limit, outside a party that is full or in the matchmaker, and to the recruiting party's own members (the client starts its 10 s deadline only for its own party's notice). Party 39 and 43 both join as an ordinary member through the normal party join, held to the room's level, gear score (when the room has one) and maxUserCount; the joiner must have no party, the recruit must still be open for that party and room, and the party must not be searching or matched. A recruit closes when the party is full (its capacity or the room's maxUserCount), when its dungeon room is unset or set to another room, when it starts a match search, and 60 s after its notice; a closed or unknown recruit refuses 39 and 43. 15 and 16 get no reply when the notice goes out, and one party sends one notice per 10 s; inside that time only the requester gets Party 40 and nothing is sent to the party.
  - Chosen here (neither the client nor the user settles it): mode 0's last byte is the party's member count, all members online or not, as the matchmaker counts them. Mode 1's needMember is the room's maxUserCount minus all members (never below 0) and its needHelper is 0. Whether a joiner came as a helper or a member is not kept on the member. A recruit stays open 60 s. A player with no party who recruits gets a party of one, loaded before the notice; that party stays after the recruit closes, until the player leaves it. The 10 s wait is kept per party and per requesting character, and the larger time left is answered, so leaving or disbanding the party and recruiting again does not reset it; a requester's wait is dropped once it is over. The requester is held to the room's level and gear score like a joiner (errors 17 and 18, before any party is made). A party whose dungeon room is set to another room gets error 43 (s_party_err_dungeon_match_another), not RoomDungeon 27, whose text is about the search being switched off. A room with no helperRequireClearCount, or with the helper search off (disabledFindHelper), keeps the client's text for the search being switched off (RoomDungeon error 27). A request from a member who is not the leader gets error 4 (s_party_err_not_chief); from a party that is searching or matched, error 43; from a party that already holds the room's maxUserCount, error 19. A join refused for the joiner's own party gets error 5 (s_party_err_already); to a closed or unknown recruit, error 31 (s_party_err_not_found); to a party searching or matched, error 43; under the level or gear score, errors 17 and 18; over the room's maxUserCount, error 19. A new notice of the same party replaces its open recruit (and its kind and room). The client's own clear count gate (under 10 clears) is not checked by the server.
- **Room dungeon:** the dungeon result is sub-type 10; the stage packets write the int, byte and bool the client reads (create) and nothing after the sub-type (clear). A room is refused for a party larger than the dungeon's user maximum: a rule of this server, the client's dialog does not test it.
- **Interact object:** the start of an interaction keeps only objects in reach, and the end of an object that is not gathered is taken only in reach. Rules of this server: a gathered object's reach is the client's MasteryObjectInteractionDistance constant; any other object's reach is 1500 (the client tests the object row's own interactionDistance, which the server does not load); every reach test has 150 (one block) of slack for position lag; a reach constant of 0 tests nothing. Farming and breeding cubes are tested the same way with the constant.
- **Interact answers:** the end of an interaction is answered as KMS2 answers it. A mesh object (a chest, a lever, a quest object) gets InteractObject 13 (result 0) after its state update and no InteractObject 5; before, it got 5 and no 13. A UI object gets 5 and then 13 (13 was missing). A gathered object gets the mastery update, the drops, 5 and 13, then the experience; a farming or breeding cube gets the mastery update, the drops, the cube's update, the success or failure, then the experience. Before, the drops and the experience came before the mastery update, and the cube's update came first. The end of a telescope or a UI object changes no state: KMS2 sends 5 and 13 for it and never a state update (4), and the object can be used again at once; the fork sent a state update too. The result byte of 13 is 0 for a telescope as for the others (the client shows the new-telescope notice itself from the object's collection row), and the client's reader of 13 (`CSceneStateGame::OnInteractObject`: result, name, kind) agrees with the layout.
- **Craft mode:** RequestSetCraftMode is answered as KMS2 answers it (start: the guide, then SetCraftMode 1 with the cube the client sent; stop: SetCraftMode 0, then the guide's removal). The construction guide now carries the player's heading (x and y 0, z from 0 to 360) as in KMS2, where it was 0; the client turns the guide to it and takes the place from the owner (`CConstructionGuideObject`). The guide's last value stays 1: KMS2 sends 0 or 1 there and the client stores it and never reads it.
- **Instrument notes and fish album:** PlayInstrument 1 (another player's keyboard note: the instrument, the owner, the MIDI message as one int) and Fishing 7 (the login's fish album: the count, then id, catches, prize catches and largest size per fish) have the layout KMS2 sends and the client reads (`CInstrumentManager::OnPacket`, `CFishingManager`); the note is now read and written as an explicit int.
- **Instrument play end (PlayInstrument 2 and 4):** the client ends a score with 4 and an improvisation with 2. A 4 during an improvisation gave music mastery and play exp for the time improvised; it is now ignored. The end of an improvisation removes the field's instrument (it stayed in the field's list and was sent to every player who entered later). The time of a played score counts up to the score's length (a written score's own length, a ready-made score's `playTime`), and the play exp is never negative (a stop within the first half second gave a negative amount). An ensemble start sets each member's score (a player entering the field is sent it, and the stop gives that score's mastery; it gave 1), checks again that the member's score has uses left, skips a member who already plays and clears the member's ready mark, so one request starts one ensemble.
- **Score compose refusal (PlayInstrument 17):** after it sends a compose (8), the client's score writer locks until a composed score (8) or a 17 comes back (`UIWriteMusicDialog::OnMakeScoreResult`). A refused compose sent nothing and left the writer locked. It now answers 17: 10 (the writing ends) when the item is not a blank score with uses left or is already composed, 0x10 (the client's banned-word text) for a title on the server's ban list, which replaces the message box it got before, and 8 (the writer unlocks to be fixed) when the score fails the client's own checks.
  - Chosen here: the cap at the score length, and no exp before 500 ms; KMS2 shows the request but no answer to it.
- **Gathering mastery:** the mastery a gather or a farming cube gives follows the client's `CMasteryTable::Factor`: for mining, gathering, breeding and farming it is the recipe's rewardMastery times the masteryDifferentialFactor row for grade(current mastery) - grade(the recipe's requireMastery), rounded half up (100 %, 60 %, 20 %, then 0 with the KR table), and nothing when the difference is negative or has no row; the grade is the client's `Grade` over mastery.xml. Other mastery types get the reward whole. Before, the whole reward was given until the mastery level was 3 above the recipe's rewardMastery.
- **Mastery craft answer order:** a craft sends its result (Mastery 2) right after the crafted items, before the exp and the conditions, as KMS2 does; it was sent last.
- **Mastery grade reward and craft refusals (Mastery 1 and 2):** the id of a grade reward is the client's `CMasteryTable::MakeID`, type * 1000 + grade (`commonfiles/masterytable.cpp`). The server read the grade as id % 100, so 3102 was taken as mining grade 2 again and the same reward could be claimed under many ids; the grade is now id % 1000. A grade that is not in the table or has no reward item (grade 1 of every type, all music grades in the KR table), an id below 0 and a reward item that cannot be made are answered with the system error (5) instead of nothing; a craft of an unknown recipe likewise. Not built: a reward row with several items per job (the client filters the list by job; the KR table has one item per row).
- **Life-skill recipe quest and level (Mastery 2, gathering):** the client counts a recipe's `requireQuest` only while the quest is in progress (`CMasteryManager` calls `IsQuestActive`, `CQuest::IsActive` is state 1), for a craft and for a gather (`CInteractGatheringObject` checks the mastery, then the quest). The server took a quest in any state for a craft and did not check it for a gather, so the 12 tutorial recipes of the KR table (no cost, one item) could be repeated after the quest; both now answer error 3 unless a required quest is in progress. A craft also checks the recipe's `requireLevel`, as the craft dialog greys out the button below it (`UIMasteryDialog::HasRequiredLevel`, 0: none); the client's own check before it sends a craft has no level check, so checking the level first is chosen here (only a forced packet meets it). It answers `s_mastery_error_invalid_level` (7); the level is 50 for pet taming as `CMasteryReceipe::Load` sets it. The level is ingested (`MasteryRecipeTable.Entry.RequiredLevel`): needs a re-ingest.
- **Craft mastery abilities (Mastery 2):** a smithing, handicraft, alchemy or cooking craft gives the recipe's rewardMastery raised by that type's item ability (smithing, handicraft, alchemy and cooking mastery earned, special abilities 69, 70, 73 and 74), as the client's craft dialog works out the gain it shows (`UIMasteryDialog`, `uimasterydialog.cpp`): the ability's total / 1000 as a float rate, the reward times 1 + rate truncated (`ScaleByRate`, `commonfiles/gamerule.cpp`). The server gave the bare rewardMastery, less than the dialog showed for a player with such an ability. Not built: pet taming mastery from a capture (no table or client code gives its amount).
- **Gather item abilities:** a mining, ranching, foraging or farming gather drops each reward item's count raised by that type's item ability (special abilities 67, 71, 68 and 72), as the client's recipe list works out the count it shows (`UIMasteryDialog::CalcAdjustedItemCount`): the client script's `calcGatheringReceipeRewardItemCount` returns the recipe's count unchanged, and the count times 1 + the ability's rate, the rate capped at 1, is truncated (`FUN_1406198a0`, `commonfiles/itemremakescrolldata.cpp`). This holds for a field object, a farming or ranching cube and an automatic gather. The server dropped the bare count, less than the list showed for a player with such an ability (KR buffs give +100%). A count that comes to 0 drops nothing (chosen here). Not built: the life-skill exp abilities (75, 76); the client never reads them and no KR buff carries them.
- **Gathered object respawn:** a gathered object keeps its respawn per character, as the client does (`CInteractGatheringObject`: the object hides for the local player on the answer and returns resetTime later). The object's field state no longer changes on a gather and no update goes to the field, so one player's gather does not take the object from another. Every end of a gathered object gets an answer: an end before the character's own resetTime has passed is answered as a failed try with count 0 (chosen here). In KMS2 the object list sends 0 as a gathered object's respawn tick; this server sent 10.
- **Fishing exp:** every catch gives the common exp table's fishing award for the character's level (the fishing message, 1059), as in KMS2. Not built: the larger award and the second mastery notice KMS2 gives for the first catch of a fish; the rule is not settled, so a first catch gives the plain award and one notice.
- **Fishing catch order:** a catch is answered in the order KMS2 sends it: the fishing mastery and its notice, the catch (Fishing 8), the caught items and their notice (Fishing 5), the cast tile again (Fishing 4 with the append byte 1 and that one tile; `CFishingManager` keeps its other tiles), then the exp and the conditions. The catch came first, the mastery after the exp, and the tile was not sent. A miss is answered with the catch without an entry and the tile. A lost reel game (c2s 10) is answered with the tile alone and ends the cast; it got no answer and left the cast open. A result 0 on a cast without the reel game gets no answer and the result 1 the client sends after it (`CFishingManager::Update`) is answered as the miss, as in KMS2; the result 0 got the miss and the result 1 an error.
- **Fishing in a house:** Fishing 0 from inside a house is refused with error 8. The client makes this check itself before it sends (`CFishingManager`), so only a changed client asks.
- **Auto fishing off:** when the last AutoFish buff a fishing player holds ends, Fishing 10 with 0 turns the client's auto flag off at once; the client otherwise keeps the flag until the next catch answer (Fishing 8) and meanwhile goes on using bait items for their buff (`CFishingManager::Update`). No packet is sent when the buff starts: `CFishingManager::OnNotifyChangeBuff` recasts by itself when a buff of event type 1 is added, so a Fishing 10 with 1 would cast twice. Chosen here: Fishing 10 at the buff's end (no client code or data says when the server sends it). Tested: FishingRulesTests.AutoFishEndNotice.
- **Kill exp and meso rate:** the exp of a monster kill (common exp table or the npc's customExp) is raised by the player's exp rate (SpecialAttribute.Experience), and the mesos a death drop gives the player by the meso rate (SpecialAttribute.Meso), from buffs such as the party buff and from gear options; before, neither rate was read.
- **Quest exp from relativeExp:** a quest's complete reward gave only its fixed `exp`; `relativeExp` was ingested and never read. The client's reward line shows the larger of the fixed exp and the common exp curve of the reward's `relativeExp` type at the player's level (`FUN_140762500`, commonfiles/questmasktable.cpp:891; the curve is `table/commonexptable.xml`'s expTableID row of `table/expbasetable.xml` times its factor, rounded and at least 1, `Unk_ExpCurve::Scale`), then, for a quest with a rank, times Lua `calcQuestRewardExpFactorRateByRank` of the client's script: field quests 0.32, alliance quests by rank S to D 1.4/1.3/1.2/1.1/1.0, or 5/4/1.2/0.426/0.32 for alliances 1 to 5; other types are left as they are. The server now gives that amount (`QuestExpRules`, `ExperienceManager.TypeValue`). In KR data 6,077 quests have a `relativeExp`, 4,159 of them with no fixed exp, so they gave no exp at all: all 1,103 field missions (`mission`, at level 20 217 x 10 = 2,170), the navigator daily missions (`dailymission`, `dailymissionLevelUp`, `quest`), event and mentoring missions, and the high-level field and alliance quests (`questSkyFortress`, `quest`). Guild quests (`dailyGuildQuest`, `weeklyGuildQuest`) now give the curve (at level 20 4,644 x 8 = 37,152) where they gave their fixed 120. Epic quests are unchanged: KR's `epicQuest` factor is 0, so the curve is 1. The client's global quest exp rate is 1 (the server sends no global rates). The guild npc's bonus for guild quests is built (Guild quest npc bonus, under Guild). Not done: the player's quest exp rate stat (`seg_questreward`), which the reward line does not use either.
  - `rewardExpExtra` (KR: 1 on 615 quests, 2 on 188) has no effect: the client passes it only to Lua `calcQuestRewardExpFactorRateByRank` (commonfiles/questmasktable.cpp:910), whose script does not read it, so it changes no reward. It is not ingested, by choice.
  - Chosen here: the rates apply to the kill's death drops (global and individual boxes), not to hit or corpse drops; a rate below -100% gives nothing.
  - The exp rate on common kills: nothing in KMS2 speaks against it. An elite kill fits +5% rounded down with party buff lv1, as here. The lv23/24 common kills that gave the bare table value were made by a player alone in its own party, with no party buff (the buffed players in that field were another party; the player's own party list has one member and no party buff was ever added to it). Not seen: a common kill by a buffed player where 5% changes the number; the only such kills are lv5 (12 x 1.05 rounds down to 12).
  - Dead dealers: a dealer who is dead when the npc dies gets no kill exp (and no pet kill exp); it keeps its death drops. In KMS2 a party member who hit an elite, died and was still dead 1.5 s later when its partner killed it got no ExpUp, while the kill dropped one meso box for each of the two, and the dead one took its box after reviving; the same two got 130 exp each from the same elite with both alive. Chosen here: the field boss bonus exp and its notice (0x92) follow the kill exp; a dead dealer keeps its quest and condition credit (no sample: the one kill had no open quest). One sample; the dead dealer had done 1% of the damage, against 5% on the kill that gave exp, so a damage share rule is not ruled out.
- **Monster exp by npc class:** a kill gives the exp of the server monster exp table (monsterExpTable) for the npc's level, in the column its npc class picks: common for class 1, elite for 3, boss for 4, dungeon boss for 6 and dungeon elite for 7. Before, every mob gave the common column (the exp base table 2), so an lv12 elite gave 62 exp instead of 196 and an lv15 field boss 97 instead of 3326. The npc's customExp, when set, still wins, and the player's exp rate applies on top. This is an ingest change: the table is a new server table row, and a data pack ingested before it has no row for it.
  - Chosen here: the class-to-column mapping. The client has no use of the table. KMS2 kill exp fits it for classes 1, 3, 4 and 7 (a class 7 kill gives the dungeon elite column), and class 6 is the dungeon boss class (the boss-dungeon AIs, and the client counts boss damage for class 6 only). Any other class gives the common column, and a level the table lacks falls back to the exp base table 2.
- **Boss kill notices and the field boss bonus exp:** a kill's ExpUp carried no npc object id, and nothing sent AppendMessageKillBoss (0x90) or AppendMessageAssistBonus (0x92).
