In KMS2 every elite or boss kill (npc class 3, 4, 6 or 7) sends 0x90 with the last hitter's name and the npc id (UIMessengerBrowser `FUN_141a22ee0`, client/ms2/uimessenger.cpp: s_hunting_kill_boss), and the one field boss kill seen (class 4, lv18, kill exp 5026) also sent 0x92 with the npc id (`FUN_141a22fc0`: s_fieldboss_reward_assistbonus_notify) and, after the kill exp, ExpUp s_msg_take_assist_bonus_exp (60002) of 1507, both ExpUps with the boss's object id. The server now sends 0x92 to each dealer of a class 4 kill, then 0x90 with the last hitter's name, then the kill exp and a bonus of 30% of the kill exp (before the exp rate, rounded down) to each dealer.
- **World boss timer and kill notice:** the client keeps one spawn time per key of LegionBattle (`CSceneStateGame::OnLegionBattle` modes 0 and 1): a key it already has only gets its time replaced, and its lookups by npc id (world map, game dictionary) take the first key in order. The login list's key is the time event row id, while the update at each spawn used a running event number, so every spawn added a row and the lowest one, with an old time, was the one shown. The update now uses the row id. A timed world boss's death also sent s_hunting_kill_boss as a second notice next to the boss kill message above; that second notice is gone. No test of the notice on a client.
- **World boss lifetime:** the two lifetime notices (s_timeevent_boss_lifetimetext1 "about to disappear", lifetimetext2 "disappeared") were sent as plain chat messages, the first one a minute before the row's LifeTime and the second at it. KMS2 sends both with the notice flags 5 (message and alert); for a boss with a LifeTime of five minutes the first came 302 s after the spawn and the second 10 s later. Chosen here from that one KMS2 session: the warning goes out when the LifeTime has passed and the boss leaves 10 s after it. A boss that was hit within the last four minutes stays (the idle rule from upstream: warning after four idle minutes, gone after five, checked every 30 s; these three numbers are not from KMS2) and no longer gets the warning at the LifeTime, which it got although it stayed. The leave 10 s later used the same four-minute test again, so a boss last hit between 3:50 and 4:00 before the warning time got no warning and left all the same; it now leaves only if it was already idle at the warning time, and otherwise the idle rule takes over (tested on a field: idle or never hit, the notice and the boss leaves; hit 3:55 before the warning time, nothing sent and the boss stays). At login KMS2 sends nothing about a boss that is alive: the LegionBattle list (19 rows, each with its next spawn time, always ahead) is all, and WorldShareInfo "boss appeared" goes out only at the spawn, one row per channel to every player. The server sends its own channel's row only, and still sends the world map's boss list unasked at login, which KMS2 sends only as the answer to the map's request. The 19 rows' minutes of the hour and the two-hour row match the KMS2 spawn times; all 19 npcs have class 4, so each passes the kill notice filter. No test on a client.
  - Chosen here: the 30%, from that one kill; no table, constant or client code holds an assist rate (the constants' CorpseHit assist-bonus cooldowns and the drop tables' assistBonus flag are for corpse hits and drops, not coded: the flag is server data only, on 124 item rows of 12 field boss drop boxes and 19 global drop rows, the client never reads it, and the one field boss kill seen in KMS2 dropped no item, so nothing shows who gets a flagged row; it is not ingested). 0x90 goes to the whole field (the captures show other players' kills, not whether all were in the party); every class 4 dealer gets the bonus (only the killer's client was seen); the bonus spends no rest exp. The UiText 0x94 KMS2 sends in the same tick (actor, type 4, value 4) is not sent.
- **Exp gain reasons:** an ExpUp names why the exp came (the unsigned short after the gain). The server sent 0 for a monster kill, a quest, a dungeon clear, a mini game, gathering, crafting, an exp drop and the guild check-in, and the common map's 3001 for a hidden map. KMS2 names a reason on every gain: 60001 kill, 1010 quest, 1085 dungeon, 3007 mini game, 3103 gathering, 1062 exp drop, 3002 hidden map. The client (`FUN_1413606c0`, client/ms2/uiworldshareinfoeventlistener.cpp) shows the plain text for most of them, but opens the reward notice on 3007 like on a map's, and adds the PC room line on 1010 and 60001 (`FUN_140475e80`, commonfiles/gamerule.cpp). The server now sends those reasons, 3107 for crafting, 3301 for the guild check-in and 1053 / 3200 for the mission and pet taming exp types; a kill that pays the npc's customExp carries the kill reason and the npc's object id like any other kill. What a gain counts as for the adventure level is unchanged.
- **Pet capture exp and life-skill adventure exp:** a wild pet caught with a trap gave no exp, though the common exp table has a `petTaming` curve (factor 1, exp table 1) and the client prints the pet taming reason 3200 (`OnMastery`, `s_pettaming_mastery`). A capture now adds the curve at the tamer's own level, as the other life skills read it, named 3200. A gather, a craft and a capture also gave no adventure (prestige) exp: their reasons (3103 to 3110, 3200) counted as no exp type, so the server pack's `Exp_Gathering` (500), `Exp_Manufacturing` (250) and `Exp_PetTaming` (15000) rows of the adventure exp table were never read. A gather's mastery reason now counts as gathering, a craft's as manufacturing and a capture as pet taming, under the usual prestige rules (from `AdventureLevelStartLevel`). Not built: the capture rarity by trap level (the snare items' texts describe the odds of a normal, rare, exceptional and epic pet per trap level, but no rate table is in either pack; the rarity is still taming rank / 25 + 1), pet taming mastery from a capture (no amount is given anywhere), a trap failing (`TamingPetEscapeRate` is 0 in KR).
- **Adventure exp of kills, quests and mini games:** a kill, a quest and a mini game round gave no adventure (prestige) exp: their reasons (60001, 1010 and 1053, 3007) counted as no exp type, so the server pack's `Exp_KillMonster` (250), `Exp_EpicQuest` / `Exp_QuestETC` (15000) and `Exp_MiniGame` (75000) rows were never read, though they are what a player from `AdventureLevelStartLevel` (50) meets most. KMS2 sends a Prestige 1 after such an ExpUp at 50 and up: 250 for a common kill and 2500 for an elite (`AdventureExpFactorElite` 10); 15000 for every quest, epic, world, field, alliance and the 7200xxxx missions named 1053 (so `Exp_Quest` and `Exp_Mission`, 45000, are not the rows read); and for a mini game round 75000 times the round's exp rate (Crazy Runner 75000 at rate 1, Trap Master 15000 at 0.2, Sole Survivor 18750 at 0.25). The server now adds them: a kill its row times the elite or boss factor (`AdventureExpFactorBoss` 100 for class 4, an assumption; the dungeon classes 6 and 7 count as common), also for a kill that pays the npc's customExp; an epic quest `Exp_EpicQuest`, any other quest `Exp_QuestETC`; a round its row times its exp rate. Crazy Runner and the other massive user games now name 3007 as KMS2 does (they named 0). The day's 2% rule (`AdventureLevelFactor` after one level's worth, `AdventureLevelLvUpExp`) applied to a whole gain once the day's amount was passed; KMS2 splits a gain that crosses it (from 79250 with 86000 left at full rate, a 15000 quest adds 6750 + 8250 * 0.02 = 6915), and so does the server now. Not built: the mini game result dialog's prestige line (it still shows 0).
- **Prestige rewards:** KMS2 answers a claim (Prestige 4 for a rank reward, 6 for a mission) and then adds the item; the item went first. A rank reward with no room in the bag is refused with the inventory error and stays claimable (it was marked claimed after a failed add); when the add still fails the item is mailed.
  - A completed quest's exp is not always 1010. KMS2 names 1053 for an exploration mission (quest type 4, 42 gains) and for an epic quest of a standard level below 10 (levels 1 to 7 seen, 51 gains), and 1010 for epic quests from level 10 and world, field and alliance quests (255 gains); no quest is seen with both. The server follows this (`QuestExpRules.Reason`); 10 as the limit for the unseen levels 8 and 9 is chosen here.
  - A gather or a craft names its mastery type: 3100 plus the type, as the gathering drop kind does. KMS2 sends 3103 mining (11 gains), 3104 gathering (7), 3105 breeding (97), 3106 farming (74) and 3108 engraving (1), each after a mastery update of that type; 3107 is in the client's list. The server sent 3103 for every gather and 3107 for every craft; it now follows the recipe's type (`MasteryRules.ExpReason`). Chosen here: 3109 and 3110 for alchemy and cooking, by the same rule, not seen. The amounts were not compared.
  - Not done: 13 quest gains of one job's tutorial chapter (9044) are smaller than the quests' fixed exp in KMS2 (25 for 150, 17 for 700 and for 800, 527 for 1500). No rule fits them: not a share of the fixed exp, of the level's exp need or of the exp held, and the epic quest exp table is all 0. Another job's tutorial quests give their fixed exp.
  - Not done: the reward content exp, dark stream and buff exp still send 0, KMS2's reason for them is not known.
- **Map treasure chests:** the server placed no treasure chests on field maps. In KMS2 they are interact objects added with InteractObject Add (never in the load list), named `EventCreate_<block position>`, standing on the map's region spawn points (the name's block is the point's position cut to whole blocks toward zero, not rounded): a wooden chest (interact 11000004, `interaction_chestA_01`) on some of the points 2000-2999 and a golden chest (`interaction_chestA_02`) on every point 3000-3999 (93000-93999 on the maps numbered 0300xxxx, which show no wooden chest). Every 300 seconds KMS2 removes the chests left and adds a new set, an opened chest among them. The server does the same on every field map that is not a dungeon, a home or a map with combine spawn groups. Opening one sends what KMS2 sends, in its order: the drops of the object's global drop box, the state update, the result, the exp (wooden 1091; golden 1093 the first time the character opens that chest, 1092 after, at the field's level) and, after the object's hide time (10 s), the hidden state and the removal. A chest is opened once and counts for the golden chest trophy the first time the character opens it; KMS2 keeps the opened golden chest ids in the character's interacted object list, where the server keeps them too. A player who enters gets an opened chest's state after its Add. The objects of combine spawn groups and AI nodes are as before: added in the normal state and not updated.
  - The golden chest's interact id is not in the map data. It is taken from the map's golden chest trophy (the trophy whose name carries the map and whose condition counts interact ids): the n-th golden point by id holds the n-th id. This gives the id KMS2 sends for all 343 golden chests seen on the maps whose trophy counts a range. Three trophies count a list in two runs; KMS2 shows the later run first on the one such map seen, and the two others are taken to do the same. A map with golden points and no such trophy (3 maps) gets no golden chest.
  - Chosen here: a set holds a third of the map's wooden points, rounded up, picked evenly. KMS2 shows 2 of 5, 3 of 7, 6 of 11 and 4 to 5 of 15; its rule is not known.
  - Checked: on 63000002 the six wooden chests KMS2 sends stand exactly on its region spawn points 2002, 2003 and 2006 to 2009 of the KR data, with the names the block rule gives, so the server places them the same way. The map has golden points 3000-3003 but no golden chest trophy, and KMS2 shows no golden chest there; neither does the server.
- **Field interact events:** the interact objects of timeEventData type 4 (3,248 KR rows after the feature and locale filter) were never placed, so the 42 field missions that ask for one (72000011, interact 14000005 on 02000003, among them) could not be completed. The ingest now keeps these rows (`TimeEventTable.FieldInteractEvent`), filtered as the client filters them (IsElementAvailable: a row whose feature is off or whose locale is another drops out); the time fields accept the `2099-12-31-00:00:00` form 201 rows use, and a row whose times do not read is skipped and counted (none in KR). Each field that is not a dungeon, a home, a rank duel or arena field, or an instanced map other than one of channelScale (solo map 02000179 holds 4 KR rows; its chests stay the guessed ones) runs the rows naming its map (`FieldInteractEventRules`): a run starts at startTime and every cycleTime after, before endTime, late by a random part of randomTime; it removes what the row's last run left, then puts the row's interact object (its model, asset and states) on free points of the row: its targetSpawnPointIDs and the map's spawn points tagged with one of its targetSpawnTags. The objects leave lifeTime after the run. The field reads the rows at its first update, places the run alive then for what is left of its life, and stops them with the field. An object on a chest point (2000-3999, 93000-93999) behaves as a map treasure chest: opened once, hidden after its hide time and removed; a golden one counts its first open for interact_object (the map's golden chest trophies), while the field missions ask interact_object_rep, which counts every open. The guessed map treasure chests stay off the points a type 4 row of the map holds, unless the row never runs (no prob, or past its endTime).
  - The client reads only targetFields and interactID of these rows (the field mission list, tableexporter.cpp); the server table's own fields settle the rest, so the timing comes from them: cycleTime, lifeTime and randomTime (non-zero only on the Season shadow chest rows), and prob (a row without prob, all Season, CBT, Katvan shadow chests and two Kritias rare chests, never runs, as for the world bosses: no default is stated, the parser's prob has none and the client does not read it; 305 KR rows have none, no world boss row lacks one). eventDuration is the hour window of the day a run starts in (the end minute included, past midnight when the end comes first).
  - Chosen here: an object is opened once in its run, also on a point that is no chest point (no rule says when its resetTime would bring it back; of the KR rows, 6 on 02000138 would reopen after 30 s). The channel keeps the run each row last placed on a map, so a field disposed when empty and made again in that run does not place it twice. A golden chest point holds a row only when the row's interact id is one of the map's golden chest trophy ids (all 812 KR rows on golden chest points are; on 4 maps the rows give the ids in another point order than the guess). A run puts interactCount objects when the row has one, else variableCountByChannelCount of the points (rounded up, at least 1), never more than the points; the points are picked at random, none twice, from those no object stands on. A used object is not replaced before the row's next run; randomTime is read as the run's lateness, as World reads it for the world bosses, since the table has no regen field. Each channel's field runs its own set (individualChannelSpawn is 0 on every row). keepAnimate is not read.
  - Not placed, no rule in the client or server data (KR): field missions 40002798 (npc 25022805: its only spawn row, npcregionspawndata, has no main tag and a sub tag no mapspawntag row uses), 89000392-89000394 (interact 11000095: only the ended 2018 stamp event names it; no xblock, combine spawn, trigger, AI or type 4 row places it) and the trophies 92000120-92000122 (npc 27000051: only a term tag no map or event reads). Trophies 22200014 and 22200015 (interacts 11000008 and 11000009) have 228 type 4 rows (114 each), all without prob, so they never run (as above); which prob KMS2 used is not in the data. The other trophies r181 found unplaced have no spawn in any source either (npcregionspawndata rows without a main tag, or no row at all).
- **Rest exp spent as KMS2 does:** every exp gain added rest exp on top, up to twice the gain, and took only that bonus off the rest exp, for quests and chests as well as kills (and the bonus was sent but never added to the exp). In KMS2 a kill gets three times its exp while rest exp is left (a 11,454 kill gives 34,362 and the rest exp drops by 34,362), a kill with too little rest gets its plain exp, and the gains from quests, chests and other sources are never raised; in every case the rest exp drops by the gain, down to 0. The server now does the same (`RestExpRules`); the factor is 1 + RestExpAcquireRate / 10000 (20000 in the constants).
  - Chosen here: a kill gets the bonus only when the rest exp covers the raised gain. KMS2 shows the bonus with ample rest and none with rest below the plain gain; whether a rest between two and three times the gain gives it is not seen.
- **Rest exp at home:** the server never added rest exp, so the exp bar's rest part stayed empty. KMS2 sends ExpUp 1 (SetRestExp, read by `CSceneStateGame::OnExpUp`, scenestategame.cpp:4186) every 10 seconds while the player is at home, the same step for every tick at one level. The server now does the same: one tick after entering the home and every 10 seconds after it, rest exp grows by the level's row of exp base table 1 times 35001 / 10000000, rounded down and at least 1 (`RestExpRules.Tick`), and the new total is sent. Nothing is sent when the rest exp is already at its cap. Other fields give no rest exp.
  - Chosen here: the factor 35001 / 10000000. It is fitted, not read: it gives the KMS2 step at all 22 levels seen (lv5 to 86, e.g. 57 at lv27, 1401 at lv72), and 0.0035 exactly gives 1 less at lv72 and lv73. The server table `restExp.xml` is not used: its value / 12 gives the KMS2 step at lv27 to 55 only, gives 10 at lv17 and 2 at lv12 where KMS2 gives 8 and 1, and stays at 13740 from lv60 where the KMS2 step keeps growing.
  - Chosen here: the cap is NextExp of the level times RestExpMaxAcquireRate / 10000 (100000: 10 levels' worth). The client only shows (exp + rest) / NextExp up to 100% (`UIExpBar`, uiexpbar.cpp:223), so it gives no cap. KMS2 rest exp is seen at up to 7.02 times NextExp and still growing.
  - Chosen here: no tick outside the home. KMS2 also sends ticks on a few town maps, but only now and then, and the packets do not show what starts them.
- **Beauty:** a new item (sub-type 3) reads the long between the item id and the appearance; the fee of sub-types 3, 5, 21 and 22 is taken only after the change succeeded. Sub-types 17 and 19 extend the saved style slots by BeautyStyleExpandSlotCount1time for BeautyStyleExpandSlotPrice merets, up to BeautyStyleMaxSlotCount extra slots; 17 then saves the hair it names.
- **Beauty shop header:** the shop answer (sub-type 0) carries the shop's random flag in the byte after the coupon id (KMS2 sends 1 for the random hair shop 508 and 0 for the dye shop), then, after the two costs, the saved style slots the character owns and the styles saved in them (the client counts its saves up from the second byte and compares it with its slot maximum; KMS2 sends 30 and 0 for a character with no saved style). Both bytes were 0. The long after them stays 0: KMS2 sends a date there (20210812080000) whose source is not known; the client only sends it back in the random hair request. Also left: for shop 508 KMS2 sends coupon tag 17 where the data gives 21 (one sample).
- **Beauty fee sale (SaleBeautyShop):** the ingest reads the event (value1 the beauty shop's id, value2 the rate out of 10000, value3 a flag) and the event list sends it: the event's id, the shop, the rate, the flag as a byte. The shop's windows then show its style, recolor and item fees lowered (`gamerule.cpp:656`: price - price * rate / 10000 in unsigned whole numbers), and every fee of that shop, whatever its money type, is tested and taken at the same number (`EventSaleRules.BeautyShopPrice`); a voucher is not touched. The client keeps two rates per shop, picked by the flag (`gameevent.cpp:799`), and takes the higher; the flagged one counts only for a PC room player (`pc.cpp:1823`), and the server has none, so a flagged row lowers nothing here. In each slot a later event of the list overwrites the shop's rate (`gamerule.cpp:638`), so the server takes the rate of the shop's last running unflagged event by start, then id, and sends the login event list in that order (`EventSaleRules.BeautyShopRate`). The 57 stored KR rows name the shops 500, 501, 504, 505, 506 and 508 with rates 2000, 3000, 5000 and 10000, some on days of the week only, none with the flag, all ended (the last 2022-07-07; 11 later rows to 2023-03-09 are under feature 23_02, which the ingest leaves out). Chosen here: a rate is held to 0..10000; and when one of two running events of a shop ends, the client drops the shop's rate altogether (`gamerule.cpp:650`) and shows the full fee until the next login, while the server goes on with the other event's rate, so it never takes more than the window shows.
- **Meret cost wallets:** the client maps a shop or beauty cost type to its own currency (`FUN_1403baee0`, gameentitycommon.cpp:269; beauty through `CanPayFee`): 4 (Meret) to its 3, the plain merets; 9 (EventMeret) to its 10, the red merets when they cover the price, else the plain merets; 10 (GameMeret) to its 9, the red merets only (`HasEnoughCurrency` and `GetCurrencyAmount`, uicommon.cpp:972/1020). The shop took EventMeret from the plain merets, and the beauty shop tested and took both from the plain merets. Both now check and take the wallet the client checks, each in one step (`MeretCost`, `CurrencyManager.TryTakeMerets`); a lack shows the client's text for it (uicommon.cpp:1083-1105: 3 and 10 the meret text, 9 the red one; Beauty has no error code for the red one, so the beauty shop sends it as a notice). A meret market price of pay type 2 is red only (`FUN_14069c3e0`, meratmarketbasketinfo.cpp:217), as before, now taken in one step. The KR and NA clients never pay such a price: the market's check maps it to wallet 9 (`CanAffordProduct`, uimeratmarketcommon.cpp:365), whose test (`CClientPlatformBase::vfunc_41` @0x140c75c80, ms2clientplatformbase.cpp:328) needs feature 0x43 RedMerat and an admin level of 52 or more (the level OnAdmin mode 0 sets). feature.xml has RedMerat and RedMeratShop at 99 for KR and NA, above their Live settings (78 KR, 47 NA; on only for CN and TH), so a red row shows "not enough red merets" and sends no request whatever red balance the server holds; the red figure (feature 0x44 in the market, 0x43 in the meret tooltip) is hidden too. The server's red balance (the fifth wallet long of field enter, MeretUse 0x0117) is written as the client reads it. A UGC design's priceType is read as the client converts it (`ConvertPriceType`, ugccubecategorytable.cpp:445: 1 merets, 2 red, 3 red else merets, any other meso); type 3 was refused before, and the fee is given back to the wallet that paid it. The banner fee stays plain merets: the client makes it red else merets only under feature 99 (RedMeratUGCBanner), which KR Live does not run. No KR shop, beauty or design cost uses these types, so the old rule was never hit there.
- **Party search:** Remove sends sub-type 1, and the error's request type is one byte.
- **Ugc:** the confirmation request reads the client's two strings.
- **Ugc 0 (upload failed):** the client sends one byte when its picture upload failed. What the official server did with it is not proven. This server gives the fee of mode 1 back once, only for the character's own upload that was paid and has not been confirmed; a second mode 0, or one with no such upload, does nothing. A new mode 1 gives the unfinished one's fee back as well, so a retry costs the fee once. A banner's unfinished designs are taken off the slots they were put on, also when the character has left that field; the slots stay reserved. A design coupon comes back as a new item of the same id: the expiry and binding of the used one are not kept, and with a full inventory it is lost (logged). A mode 3 whose kind is not the upload's finishes nothing, so that fee can still be given back.
  - A design's confirmation (mode 3 for an item, furnishing or mount) that cannot make the design gives the fee back, once: the item's row is not written, or furnishing storage does not take it (no free slot, or its write failed). Before, the upload was finished and the fee lost. Chosen here: no answer is sent to the client, as before.
    - The fee also goes back when the paid upload's confirmation finds no staged design of that upload (none, or another upload's) or no design entry for its item, and when a paid banner upload's confirmation finds no banner slot staged for it. Before, the confirmation had finished the fee and returned, so it was lost.
- **Ugc 0 to the client (upload refused):** the client locks its upload when it sends mode 1 (`CUgcManager::LockForUpload`) and refuses every later one as already running until an upload reply or a Ugc 0 unlocks it (`FUN_14188f450`, `ugcmanager_4.cpp:3805`: a byte it skips, the error byte, then `UnlockUpload`). Before, a refused upload (lack of money, a full outfit bag, a bad design, banner or guild check) sent no Ugc 0, so every later upload of the session failed in the client until a relog. Every refusal now sends Ugc 0. Where the client has a text for the reason, its error byte: 0x13 lack of meso (in place of the message box the server sent before) and 0x81 a full outfit bag (before, no text). Chosen here: 0x8a, a case of the client's switch that shows nothing, for the rest; the server's own notices for those (lack of merets, a bad coupon, the guild right) stay. Chosen here: the skipped byte is the upload's kind.
- **Ugc guild kinds:** the emblem upload reads the client's guild id and needs the emblem right (64), as the client's button does. The client asks for no rank right before a guild house poster upload, so any member may put one up; the poster id must be one of the house's poster objects. A confirmation makes only the upload that the same session started the emblem or poster.
- **Ugc banner slots:** a reserved slot belongs to the character that reserved it (mode 19); an upload from another character is refused whole and costs nothing. The owner is kept in memory only, so it is lost at a restart, when the field is disposed (an empty field, after 10 minutes) and on another channel; a reserved slot without a design then has no owner and takes an upload from anyone at the usual price, and the first character that reserves its hour again (mode 19) holds it. The 24 bound below starts again with it. A slot with a design keeps the design's character. Server rule, not from the client (which sends the hours of one day, 24 at most, and shows no limit): a character holds at most 24 reserved slots without a finished design on a field (a design whose picture never arrived counts), and an hour that is over cannot be reserved.
- **Ugc design name, bag check, blueprint and profile picture:** a design upload (mode 1; item, furniture, mount) whose name is longer than the constant UgcNameLengthMax (14 characters, the limit the design window gives its text field) or holds a control character is refused before the fee; before, any name was stored. No lower limit is asked: the client puts UgcNameLengthMin (3) in the field's hint text only, and what the window itself refuses is not in the client source. The free outfit slot is asked only of an item design (a furniture design goes to the furnishing storage); before, a full outfit bag refused furniture and mount designs too. A blueprint confirmation (mode 3) is taken only for the preview the session staged on that blueprint and for a resource of the blueprint kind; before, any resource id put its path on the blueprint. Mode 11 (profile picture) is taken only when the text is the path this server's web answer gave for the sender's own character (`data/profiles/avatar/<character id>/<file>.png`, the file of letters, digits and `-`); before, any text was stored and sent to every player who saw the character. The web server now serves that file at the path it answered (`GET /data/profiles/avatar/<character id>/<file>.png`, the file the upload wrote under Data/profiles/<character id>); before, no route served it, so a client never got an uploaded picture (the portrait of the rank duel result, `UIPvPRankDuelResultDialog::SetPlayerProfile`, and every other portrait read the profile key through the download).
- **NameChange (0x98), unreachable:** the client sends it (item uid, a name) from an input box (`FUN_141405440`, invenslot.cpp:581) that opens only for an item whose UGC look has the state byte 2, the byte after the url (`CUgcItemLook::FUN_140893520`): the inventory use action of such an item (use type 15), a cube texture item without a UGC id and a blueprint item without a UGC id (`BlueprintImport`). The client never sets that byte itself (only `CUgcItemLook::Read` and copies of a read look do), and the server writes 0 there for every UGC item, so no KR item shows the box and the opcode has no handler (no item data sets the byte; it is server state). Not done: what the byte 2 stands for and what the answer to the name is.
  - Chosen here: the path shape is this server's own (the official web server's is not known), and a refused mode 11 gets no answer. The unused fields of mode 1 (amount, cost) stay unread for the price: the fee is the server's.
- **Design price sale (SaleUgcDesign):** the ingest reads the event (value1 the rate out of 10000) and the event list sends it: the event's id and the rate. The design window then shows the lowered price (`uiugcitemeditdialog.cpp:1007`: no rate, the price; rate 10000, free; else price * (10000 - rate) / 10000 in whole numbers), and the upload charges the same number (`EventSaleRules.UgcDesignPrice`). KR has 14 rows, all ended (the last stored one 2022-07-07, rate 8000; a later row to 2023-03-09 is under feature 23_02, which the ingest leaves out). Of several running events the highest rate counts, as the client keeps it (`gamerule.h:85`). Chosen here: a rate above 10000 is free.
- **Design market charge table (Ugc 21):** sent at every login after the field enter request, as KMS2 sends it: five rows of a step and a value (0/70, 5000/60, 10000/50, 100000/40, 200000/20). The client lists them (step + 1, 100 - value) in the tooltips of the design market's register and settle windows (`uimeratmarketcommon.cpp:999`), which show no tooltip without the table. A sold design is charged by that table (`UgcMarketRules`): a row's value is the seller's share in percent for a sales total from its step + 1 to the next row's step, so the charge is 30% up to a total of 5,000, then 40%, 50%, 60% and 80% above 200,000 (it was a flat 25%). KMS2 gave 210 for a design sold at 300 by a seller below 5,000.
  - Chosen here (KMS2's sales were all in the first step): the step is the one that holds the seller's sales total before the sale, the total being the one the settle tooltip marks (price times sales count of the account's listings); the share is rounded down to a whole number. A sale already recorded keeps its amount.
  - The sales count of a listing is written only by a sale. The seller's session holds the listings as they were at its login and saved them whole at logout, on the sales tab, on relist and on unlist, which put the count back, so a seller who was online during the sales stayed on the first step; those saves now leave the stored count, and the seller's copy takes the stored count when the listings or the sales are shown. The sale and the count are stored together, so a sale that cannot be stored (and is refunded) leaves the count as it was.
  - A sale is stored with its time. It was stored as 1970, so the seller's sales list showed that date and the payment as due at once (the wait is 10 days from the sale). A listing row whose blueprint column holds the column default `{}` is written with an empty builder name; it threw on the design shop page.
- **Design market register fee:** the fee of putting a design on sale (and of putting it on sale again) is worked out by the server the way the register dialog does it: the client script calcMarketRegisterFee by the account's listings on sale that are not music scores (none 0, one 30, two to eight 50, nine or more 100; the CN values under LOCALE=CN), nothing for a music score, plus UGCShopAdFeeMerat when promoted. The script is in the client's precompiled lua pack (not in the client source); under CN the client also counts listings in status 1, the server only the ones on sale (CN is not a target). The fee field of the request is read and not used: before, the server paid whatever it said, so 0 listed for free and a number below 0 gave merets. The fee is stored at once, and goes back when the listing could not be stored.
  - Chosen here: a request whose fee differs from the server's is charged the server's fee without a notice (an unchanged client always sends the same number).
- **Design market price range:** the price of a new listing and of one put on sale again is kept inside the register dialog's range: the constants UGCShopSellMinPrice and UGCShopSellMaxPrice, a design item's own range from the design table where it is above 0, and a music score's lowest price from the client script calcMarketSellPriceMinMaxMusicScore (15; 100 under LOCALE=CN; the two were the wrong way round before). A music score cannot be put on sale: the server lists only items with a design template, and the client's score market (feature UGCMarketMusicScore) is off in KR. Before, putting a listing on sale again took any price, also one below 0, which paid the buyer.
- **Design market refusals:** only a design whose look was made by the player's account can be put on sale (the dialog offers no other). Only a listing on sale can be bought: one taken off sale, past its sale time or with a price below 0 gets the purchase failure answer; before, any listing id could be bought. A purchase (design or premium) makes the item before it takes the price, and a design sale that cannot be stored gives the merets back; before, a product whose item could not be made was paid for and the handler threw.
- **Design market cancel and refund:** the client's meret market has no refund request at all (the modes it sends are 11 to 15, 18 to 20, 22 to 25, 27, 29, 30, 32, 33, 101 to 104 and 107). The cancel of the bill dialog sends nothing. The sales window sends 15 ("stop the sale", after a confirm box) only for a listing on sale and 14 ("delete") only for one that is not on sale; for one on sale it shows a notice instead. The server now keeps to that: 15 for a listing that is not on sale and 14 for one on sale do nothing (before, 14 deleted a listing on sale and 15 wrote the expired state again). Neither gives the register fee back, as the client shows none.
- **Design gifts by mail:** the meret market's gift (MeretMarket 30 with the gift flag, a receiver's name and a message) was refused with 0xA4 every time. The client offers a gift only on a design (`IsUgcProduct` sets the gift flag; the blueprint market is designs too), a design's `GetCurrency` is merets, and the bill dialog sends its pay type after the price (`BuyPacket::Write`: 1 meso, 3 meret, 9 red meret, 0x11 the product's own). The server now reads that pay type and delivers the design to the receiver's mailbox: a system mail with the client's gift texts (`s_meratmarket_gift_mail_sender`, `_title` with the item, `_content` with the buyer's name and the message), the item with the design's look and blueprint, and the sale stored for the seller as any sale. The mail and its item are written before the merets are taken, so a mail that cannot be written costs nothing; a payment or a sale that fails after that takes the mail back, and the merets are given back only once the mail is gone. A mail that cannot be taken back keeps the payment, so the gift is still paid for, and the server logs it at Error with the ids. A refused gift answers 0x1F with the client's own codes: 0xA4 for a product that is not a design, 0x87 for any pay type but merets, 0xA6 for no such character or one whose deletion has begun, 0xB3 for a character on the buyer's account, 0x91 for the buyer's own design, 0x88 for too few merets. The 0x1E/0x1F echo now carries the gift flag, receiver, message and pay type the client sent. A receiver online gets Mail 0x16 (`ReadGiftNew`: the buyer's name, the item id, rarity and amount, the design's url and title), which shows `s_mail_notify_recieve_gift`; the world passes it on in the mail notification (new optional field, no migration). Not done: the client's admin-receiver codes (0xA8/0xA9) and its block list check (the bill dialog checks its own list).
- **Meret Market pack variants:** a custom shop row (`shop_merat_custom.xml`) lists its bigger packs (as 5, 10 and 30 of the item) as `additionalQuantity` children; the ingest stores each child (ParentId set) before its parent, and the game server put a child under its parent only if the parent had come first, so a child read back ahead of its parent was dropped. The server now takes the parents first and then puts each child under its parent (`GameServer.PremiumMarket`), whatever order the table comes back in; the children of one parent keep the table's order. KR has 14 children; the MySQL json column returns the rows by key, which puts every KR parent ahead of its children, so none was lost there, but a child whose id sorts before its parent's, or any child under a store that keeps the ingest order, was dropped. No re-ingest needed.
- **Meret Market products by id (MeretMarket 32 and 33):** a chat link that names several market products sends their ids (`meratmarketman.cpp:661` premium ids as ints, `:675` design listing ids as longs, at most 20; `uimeratmarketdialog.cpp:3723`), and the client reads the answer 0x20 or 0x21 as a search result page (`OnSearchResultPage`, `meratmarketman.cpp:2498`: count, total, page, then a byte and a product per slot). The server left both unanswered. It now answers with the found products in the order asked, total = count, page 1; an id it does not find is left out, and a count below 0 or above 20 gets no answer. Only a product the market offers counts as found: a design listing on sale (status on sale and its sale time not over) and a premium product inside its sale window (start and end, 0 an open end). The search pages filter design listings by the sale time only and the premium pages and the single product lookup filter nothing, so this answer applies the filter itself; a purchase uses the same sale window check.
- **Meret Market listing refusals (MeretMarket 0x00):** a design listing (13) or a listing again (18) that the server refused got no answer, so the register dialog waited; the client reads MeretMarket 0x00 as its error reply (`CMeratMarketMan::OnErrorReply`; `ErrorPacket::Read`, commonfiles/meratmarketbasketinfo.cpp:2817: the request kind, the error, the product key as a premium id and a design listing id, a long and the blueprint flag) and shows the error's own text. A refusal now answers it with the request's kind, the item or listing id and the client's code, before any merets are taken: 0x9A (`s_meratmarket_error_register_disable_ugcitem`) for an item that is not the player's own design or is a survival match item (that was a separate message box), 0x98 for a design already on sale, 0x79 for 100 listings (the dialog's own cap, `FUN_140ebc4a0`), 0x82 without the design section of the category table, 0x7D for a price outside the dialog's range, 0x88 for too few merets for the fee (the client offers its charge box), 0x77 for a listing again of a listing it does not have, 0x8E for a listing again of one whose sale time is not over (the client offers the form only for those), and 0x68 when the listing cannot be written (the fee is given back). A music score has no design, so it is refused 0x9A before the already-on-sale test; the client's own test (`IsAlreadyListed`, uimeratmarketregisterdialog.cpp:702) also looks scores up by their music id (`FindProductByMusicUid`), which the server does not (listing a score is not done). A price outside the range was quietly moved into it; the dialog never sends one, so it is refused now. The other Meret Market modes the server does not send need nothing: 0x20/0x21 are the by-id answers above; 0x13 answers request 19, which the client sends only for a listing whose state after its sale end is 3 (the free resale box), and the server writes 0 there; 0x6E (refresh the home page, no body) has nothing that calls for it; 0x72 (`OnRandomCouponPick`, a random sale's pick) needs the random sale state, which the server does not keep (not done).
- **Change attributes:** the ForceFill sub-type is 8 and the debug remake sub-type is 7.
- **Change attributes sale (SaleRemake):** the ingest reads the event (value1 the rate out of 10000, value2 the item class: 0 all, 1 equip types 13 to 17, 2 types 12 and 18 to 21, 3 pets) and the event list sends it: the event's id, the rate, the class as a byte. The client keeps a rate per class, takes the higher of the item's class and class 0 (`gamerule.cpp`, GetRemakeSaleRate) and lowers the ingredient counts of the staged item's reply itself (`itemremakescrolldata.cpp:397`: the rate held to 10000 x IngredientFeeReduceLimit, then (count * (1 - rate / 10000) + 0.000001) in doubles cut to a whole number, at least 1), so that reply keeps the full counts and the change consumes the lowered ones (`EventSaleRules.RemakeIngredientCount`). KR has 23 rows of rate 3000 or 5000, all class 0, some in a part of the day only, all ended (the last 2021-09-09). Of several running events of one class the client keeps the highest rate (`gamerule.h:85`, Recompute), and so does the server. The limit is 0.5 when the constants lack IngredientFeeReduceLimit, as in the client (the KR table has 0.5). The client takes the higher of this rate and the character's fee attribute, ReduceRemakeOptionFee (169) for an item and ReducePetRemakeOptionFee (173) for a pet, as a rate out of 10000 (`itemremakescrolldata.cpp:361` and `:379`), and so does the server (`EventSaleRules.RemakeDiscount`). The item dialog shows the full counts with the sale line and its rate under them and uses the lowered counts only to test whether the player can pay (`itemremakeoptiondialog.cpp:776` and `:836`); the pet tab shows the lowered counts (`tabcontentspetremakeoption.cpp:645`).
- **Special attribute hot time (SAHotTime):** the ingest reads the event (value1 the special attribute ids, value2 their values, comma lists of one length; value4 the text of the HUD icon's tooltip) and the event list sends it: the event's id and the four values as strings. The client (`gameevent.cpp:664`) keeps a value of a rate attribute (valueType 2 in `table/specialabilitystringtable.xml`) as value x 1000 cut to a whole number and any other as the whole number, sums the running events per attribute (`gamerule.cpp:2896`) and adds the sums to the character's special attribute totals (`CEquipment::GetAbilityTotal`, `equipment.cpp:740`); the upper HUD shows an icon with the tooltip while one runs (`uiupperhuddialog.cpp:876`). The server adds the same sums to every player's totals while the events run (`StatsManager.AddHotTime`). KR has 83 rows, all ended; the last with values is 13100006 (attributes 1, 59 and 61, 1 each).
  - Chosen here: the rate attributes come from the played client's table, and without a client pack every attribute counts as a rate (the 12 the KR rows name are rates); a row whose lists differ in length, name an unknown attribute or add nothing is not stored; the PC room variant of the event is not sent; the totals follow an event's start and end (dates, days of the week, part of the day) within a second.
- **Dungeon clear bonus (DungeonBonusReward):** the ingest reads the event (value1 `<ms2><v dungeonID groupType mesoRate expRate itemRolls customDropBoxIds/></ms2>`, the root's first element as in `gameevent.cpp:996`; groupType is the group's name) and the event list sends it: the event's id, the room, the group, the two rates, the rolls and the box ids. The client keeps the running events per room and per group and merges them (rolls and rates summed, boxes joined, `gameeventcontext.cpp:786`), and shows only the items of the event boxes, marked as event items, in the room window's reward preview (`uidungeonroomdialog.cpp:976`); the rates and the rolls are for the server. A clear with a reward count left now applies the merged bonus of the running events that name the room or its group (`EventSaleRules.DungeonBonus`): exp and mesos plus `(amount * rate + 0.001)` cut to a whole number (the client's `ApplyExpRate` and `ApplyMesoRate`), the room's clear boxes rolled once more per roll, each event box rolled once, and the record's event flag set. KR has 128 rows, all ended (the last stored ones 2022-07-07; the 15 rows to 2023-02-22 are under feature 23_02, which the ingest leaves out): 87 with itemRolls 1 (82 by room, 5 by group), 40 with expRate 9, 1 with both rates and a roll at 1; none names a box.
  - Chosen here: a rate adds to the room's amount (a rate of 1 doubles it, as the row with all three at 1 reads); an event that names both the room and its group counts once; at most 10 extra rolls (`MaxDungeonBonusRolls`); a row that names no room or group, or adds nothing, is not stored. The result dialog shows the event line (see Dungeon clear result).
- **Item collection (CollectItemGroup):** the ingest reads the event (value1 `<ms2><info name/><result itemID count grade/><condition itemID count grade/>...</ms2>`, `gameevent.cpp:6474`) and the event list sends it: the event's id twice, the name, begin and end, the condition items and the result. The client's window lists up to 3 running events that have 2 to 5 conditions, ticks a condition the player has (the count of that id and rank in the bag or, for a count of 1, the id worn; `uicollectitemgroupeventdialog.cpp:292`) and sends EventReward 19 with the event id from its button. The server now handles 19: the running event of that id, not received yet (user value 2200 of the event), every condition held by the client's test; it gives the result (bag, else mail) and sets the user value to 1, which the window reads as received. The value is stored with the reward instead of at logout, and it follows the event's end when a later replay ingest moves the event, so the result is given once per run. An item row without a grade is read as rank 1, the client's default. The client has no reply case for 19 and refreshes on the user value, so a refusal sends nothing. KR has 22 rows, all ended (the last stored one 2022-04-14; the two rows to 2023-03-09 are under feature 23_02, which the ingest leaves out), badge upgrades from a lower badge plus tokens.
  - Chosen here: the condition items stay with the player. The client only tests that they are there, counts a worn piece and shows no cost; nothing in it says what the live server took. The claim is kept for the account (the client's user value list does not say whose it is), so one set of items pays an account once, whichever character holds it; a set that can be traded can still pay another account. Rows without a result, with an item without an id or a count, or outside 2 to 5 conditions are not stored (the window shows none of them).
- **Skill damage:** sub-types 7 and 8 write the client's fields; dot damage (sub-type 3) writes the amount as a long.
- **Npc damage to players:** an npc (not a pet) hitting a player now uses a factor of 2.85 instead of 4 in front of Rate x physical or magical attack x resistance / defense; players and pets keep 4. The factor was fitted to KMS2 hits of ordinary field mobs on players: 8 npc and player groups from 4 client builds (player levels 15 to 50, npcs 0 to 10 levels below), where the largest hit was 0.667 to 0.728 of what the factor 4 gives; within each npc the ratio did not move with Rate or the player's defense, and it follows the player's resistance as the formula has it, (1500 - resistance) / 1500: in KMS2 one npc skill on players of magical resistance 185, 255, 460 and 530 hits for 0.947, 0.790 and 0.736 of the first (the formula: 0.947, 0.791, 0.738), and with that term the constant stays between 2.78 and 2.89 for player resistances from 21 to 376. So only the constant was off. Chosen here: one constant 2.85 (4 x 0.71); 3 x 0.95 (the papRate for one player in the server table npcStatFactorByPlayerCount) fits these field hits equally well, but open fields name no factor id, so the table is applied on its own only where a room or instance field names one (next bullet); player hits on npcs were not measured and keep 4; some dungeon and boss npcs hit for much less in KMS2 than their stats give and are not covered by this constant. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Npc hit spread:** an npc's (not a pet's) hit is now spread evenly between 0.96 and 1 of the amount the formula gives; before, every hit of one npc skill on the same player was the same number. In KMS2, hits of one npc skill on a player whose defense and resistances stay the same vary: in the 7 such groups of 9 to 27 hits with a largest hit of 300 or more (98 hits), the smallest hit is 0.962 to 0.975 of the largest and the hits fill that span evenly. The 2.85 above was fitted to the largest hit, so the spread goes down from it. The same sessions agree with two parts that are unchanged: a critical npc hit is 1.5 of a normal one (36 groups, 41 critical hits at 1.46 to 1.53 of the largest normal hit, bar two on amounts under 25; the npcs' critical damage stat is 500), and at npc critical rate 62 against critical evasion 50 the critical share is 42 of 1,054 hits (4.0 %; the formula gives 4.9 %). The client only shows the amount it is sent, so the span is measured, not read. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Player hit rate:** the chance that an attack is not evaded is (accuracy - 10) / (evasion + 0.8 x accuracy) x 2, now plus up to 0.05 from the attacker's luck (0.05 x luck / (luck + 50)). The code subtracted that luck term and added the same term of the target's dexterity, but divided both as whole numbers, so both were always 0; the luck term now counts and is added, the dexterity term is left out (unmeasured; this changes nothing). No npc has a luck stat (0 of the 8,183 npc rows), so npc hits on players are as before. Fitted to KMS2 player hits on npcs (accuracy 82, luck 18 to 26, 8,682 hits): the misses at evasion 82, 86, 87, 91, 97 and 103 are 0.7 %, 2.6 %, 3.1 %, 5.2 %, 11.5 % and 13.0 %; the rule now gives 1.1 %, 3.7 %, 3.9 %, 6.7 %, 10.1 % and 13.3 %, without the luck it gave 2.4 %, 5.0 %, 5.6 %, 8.0 %, 11.4 % and 14.6 %. The size of the luck term is chosen here: it is the old term with its sign turned, and at low evasion KMS2 still misses a little less than it gives. Not settled: at evasion 97 the rate without the luck (11.4 %) is nearer to KMS2 (11.5 %) than the new one (10.1 %); the other five points are nearer with it. The critical rate needed no change: 736 of 10,126 KMS2 player hits on npcs are critical where the rule gives 761, and a critical hit is 1.25 to 1.27 times a normal one at critical damage 250.
- **Damage reductions:** a hit is now cut by the target's reduction of the skill's range type (Melee Reduction, special attribute 37, and Ranged Damage Reduction, 38) and of its element (the Ice, Fire, Dark, Holy, Poison and Electric Damage Reduction lines, 28 to 33). Nothing read them before. They are among the most common random item option lines: about 7,400 entries each for 37 and 38 and 6,600 for each element in itemoptionrandom, next to 19 to 47 buffs for each element reduction. They meet most monster hits too: of the npc and other non-player skills, about 5,600 of 9,000 are melee or ranged. Chosen here (the client never computes damage, so nothing in it gives the formula): the hit is multiplied by (1 - range reduction) x (1 - element reduction), each rate cut to at most 1 (a negative one adds to the hit, see Negative special attribute totals), after the rest of the formula and before shields; a hit of neither range type, or a physical or element-less one, meets no reduction. A line of 2.2 % takes 2.2 % of the hit. Not changed: constant and max-health hits, damage over time, the PvP and health-based reductions (55, 163) and the caster's leader and elite monster damage (10, 11). Live try: see the report.
- **Restore on kill:** the player whose hit kills an npc now gets back the health, spirit and stamina of the item option lines HpOnKill, SpiritOnKill and StaminaOnKill (special attributes 13 to 15), as flat amounts capped at the maximum. Nothing read them before. HpOnKill is one of the most common random option lines (7,151 entries in itemoptionrandom, 1 to 15 health); no item or buff in the KR data has the other two. Chosen here (the client only shows the stats the server sends): the last hitter only, and only while alive; the health goes in as regen does, with a stats update and no heal number.
- **Recovery number on kill:** each amount the restore on kill actually gives back (after the maximum cap) is shown over the killer with UiText (0x94), the client's recovery number: int actor object id, int type, int value, where type 4 is health, 7 spirit and 10 stamina (the client shows nothing for other types). Nothing sent UiText before. Chosen here: the client does not say which events send it, so only the restore on kill does (skill heals keep their heal packet). Also chosen here: it goes to the killer's session only, not the whole field. A full stat restores nothing and shows nothing.
- **DPS meter recovery and costs (DpsMode 0x56, DpsStat 0x86):** the DPS meter window sends DpsMode 1 when it opens and 0 when it closes (`UIDPSDialog`, uidpsdialog.cpp:86 and :98); the server had no handler and sent no DpsStat, so the meter's recovery block (natural spirit and stamina, skill spirit and stamina spent, effective and over-healing) stayed at 0. The client reads DpsStat only while its meter records (`CSceneStateGame::OnDpsStat`, scenestategame.cpp:4289): mode 0 a natural flag, type 4/7/10 and an amount (`CDPSMeterSystem::AddStat`, dpsmetersystem.cpp:994: spirit counts as skill spirit without the flag and natural with it, stamina only with it, health only without it); mode 1 an int it stores but never reads, spirit and stamina spent; mode 2 kind 0 or 1 and the health a heal actually restored, given or received (`AddHealEffective`, `AddHealedEffective`). The full heal amounts come with SkillDamage Heal, which the client already counts. The session now keeps the flag and, only while it is set, sends: mode 0 with the flag for each regen tick of spirit and stamina (the amount restored after the cap), mode 1 for each skill's spirit and stamina cost, and mode 2 for each skill heal of health, to the caster (kind 0) and the holder (kind 1), each only when its own meter is open. Chosen here: no mode 0 for health regen (the client counts none) and none without the flag (no server-side skill spirit or stamina other than heals, which SkillDamage already carries); mode 1's first int is 0; the flag lasts for the session, field changes on the same server included (the client sends it only when the window opens or closes), and a new session (a channel change) starts with it off, so an open meter carried over a channel change gets no DpsStat until it is reopened.
- **Heals received from others:** a skill heal from another actor now gives its target more health by the target's item option line Bonus Recovery from Allies (receivedhealincrease, special attribute 17, s_item_opt_sa_improve_recovery_regen_receiveheal), in thousandths, rounded down. Nothing read it before. It is on 491 random option lines (0.5 to 3.9 %), the target-side twin of the caster's Recovery Bonus that heals already read. The client never looks up type 17, so the server applies it (its knockback-reduction line 39, the most common unread one, the client applies itself). Chosen here: the whole health amount after the caster's healing invokes and before the stack count; a heal of the target on itself, spirit and stamina heals and potions are unchanged. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Skill range target choice:** a skill attack range's target choice is now ingested (`SkillMetadataRange.Select`, needs a re-ingest) and the server's target search applies it (`RangeTargetSelect`, from `FieldManager.GetTargets`, which picks the targets of player, npc and region skill attacks): 233 attack ranges on 61 skills. The client's search (`CBaseCollision`, basecollision.cpp, after the shape test and before the target count) filters by targetSelectType 2 and 3: a target passes when it holds the enabled buff targetHasBuffID (0: any; cast by the searching actor with targetHasBuffOwner) and, between two players, has the caster's non-zero party (targetSelectParty) and guild (targetSelectGuild). Mode 2 keeps only the targets that pass, none if none does; mode 3 keeps them if any does, else all; other modes (1 and 4 in KR) keep all and put the passing ones first once sorted. It then sorts by targetStatCompareFunction on the stat targetStatCompare (1 current ascending, 2 descending, 3 current/total ascending, 4 descending; the client loads the buff, party and guild fields only with a select type, the sort always, `Unk_RangeProperty::LoadElement`). Before, the server took the first targets in range: the party-only heals and buffs of 90000546 to 90000556, 90000605, 90000839, 10400152, 10400185 and 10600222 reached any player near the caster, 10600031 and 10200293 did not hit the targets with the caster's mark first, and the heals of 10400072, 10400153, 10400231 (lowest health rate first) and 10400212 (lowest health first) did not pick the most hurt ally. Chosen here: the guild test compares guild ids (the client compares the names); the stat sort is the client's swap sort, so equal values end in the order its swaps leave. Not done: the sensorProperty and detectProperty ranges (the four sensor rows of 10300193 to 10300255) are not ingested. Live try: see the report.
- **Skill range exclusions:** a skill range's `applyTargetIgnoreNpcRanks` and `targetHasNotBuffID` are now ingested (`SkillMetadataRange.IgnoreNpcClasses` and `HasNotBuffId`, needs a re-ingest) and the server's target searches apply them (`RangeTargetSelect.Excludes`, in `FieldManager.GetTargets`, the arrow step search and the bounce search): the targets they leave out are taken out before the target choice and the target count. The client's range loader reads neither (only its sensor reads a `targetHasNotBuffID`, and the server has no sensor), so there is no client rule to follow. Chosen here: an npc whose class (npcdata `class`, the client's npc rank: 3 and 7 elite, 4 and 6 boss) is in `applyTargetIgnoreNpcRanks` is no target, and neither is an actor holding an enabled copy of the `targetHasNotBuffID` buff, whoever cast it. The data folder has `applyTargetIgnoreNpcRanks` on 6 skills (50101303, 50101310, 50101311 and 50101314 list 3; 60170002 lists 3, 4, 6 and 7; 70000054 lists 6) and `targetHasNotBuffID` on a range on none (the client's skill data has it on 50003091 and 50003092, which the data folder lacks). Before, both were dropped and every npc in range could be hit.
- **Npc class target condition:** an effect condition's target `NpcRanks` is now ingested (`BeginConditionTarget.NpcClasses`, needs a re-ingest) and holds only for an npc whose class is listed (`SkillUtils`). KR: one use, class 7 on the on-hit proc of 90000499, which fired on every target before. No client reader exists; chosen here, with the class meaning of `applyTargetIgnoreNpcRanks` above.
- **Buff test of a condition's owner flag:** a skill or effect condition's buff test with `hasBuffOwner` (the server's `HasBuff.Owned`) now passes only on a copy of the buff that the condition's caster cast (`SkillUtils.Check`: the effect's caster for a skill effect, the buff's own caster for a buff's condition, and for the event skills of a buff (`BuffManager.TriggerEvent`: its stack, hit, attacked and other event payoffs, which run on the holder) the caster of the buff whose event fired); without the flag any copy counts, as before. The test compared the holder's object id with 0, so it never failed and any player's copy passed. The client only loads the flag (`SkillHasBuffCondition::Load`, 0x1407e6250) and never reads it, so its meaning is taken from the one field of its family the client does read, the range select's targetHasBuffOwner, which takes a buff cast by the searching actor (see Skill range target choice). KR data (pack and played client): 781 tests set it, in 38 skills (132 caster and 471 target tests) and 24 effects (84 owner and 94 target tests), on 51 buffs; 39 of them are casterIndividualBuff (one copy per caster on the holder; counted on the ingested flag, the other 12 are 10100271, 10200032, 10200182, 10200212, 10200221, 10200264, 10300182, 10400261, 10400301, 90000729, 90000752 and 120030061), every target test's buff but 10300182 among them. So when two players of a job share a target, one's follow-up no longer fires off the other's mark: Surprise Attack 10700091 to 10700093 (10700011, 10700111 on the target), Ember 10300222 to 10300226 (the burns 10300021, 10300222, 10300227), Chain Lightning 10300031, Plasma Chain 10600171, Sticky Monster 10600281, Bow Swing 10500051, 10800172, 10800272 and the npc skills 90050511 to 90050681; a caster test (Conviction Strike 10100171 to 10100173 and Divine Cuts 10100261 on 10100271, 10400212 and Greater Healing 10400231 on 10400261 and 10400301, Bloodlust 10200051 on 10200212) takes only the buff the caster gave itself. The event-skill rule keeps the stack payoffs of debuffs a player puts on an enemy, whose owner test looks for that player's own copy on the enemy: 10300271 at 10 stacks (10300275), 10800221 at 18, 10800281 at 3, 11000333 at 9, 90000729 and 90000752 (taking the holder there, the enemy, would fail them every time, alone too); for a buff its holder cast on itself (the hit payoffs 10600283, 10700301, 10800162, 10800223) the holder is that caster. In a region skill's bounce (`FieldSkill`) the hit target's `TargetAttack` makes the target the caster of an owner-placed effect, so an owner test there fails; the caster's own `ApplyEffects` on the next line applies the effect, so this only drops a second application. The stack count is compared on that copy. Not changed: `hasNotBuffOwner` (no server field; see Skill effects from the played client's skill data). No ingest change. Not tried live (the change needs two players on one target); OwnedBuffConditionTests covers it, the stack payoffs through `Buff.Stack`.
- **Buff test of a condition's not-buff owner flag:** a skill condition's `hasNotBuffOwner` (the server's `BeginConditionTarget.HasNotBuffOwned`) now makes its `hasNotBuffID` test fail only on a copy of that buff the condition's caster cast (`SkillUtils.Check`, the same caster `hasBuffOwner` compares); without the flag any copy fails it, as before. Either way only an enabled copy fails it, as in the range's `targetHasNotBuffID` exclusion (`RangeTargetSelect.Excludes`): the client's buff tests count only enabled records (`AdditionalCliList::HasEnabledWithId` and `HasEnabledWithIdAndCaster`, basecollision.cpp); before, a disabled copy failed it too. The data folder's rows do not give the flag (the data parser has no such field), so the ingest takes it from the played client's skill data (`MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML`, `PlayedClientActionAdditionals.MarkNotBuffOwned`), row by row: the played client's `<actionAdditional>` uses of the level go to the level's rows and an attack's uses to the rows of the data folder attack they are placed on (the on-damage ones to its on-damage rows), and there the n-th use of an effect id and level marks the n-th row giving that id and level when it tests the same buff with the flag (a use past the last such row marks nothing; KR has one use per row); the client's rows that the ingest adds carry it too. Without the played client's pack nothing changes. Chosen here: the client only loads and compares the flag (`SkillHasBuffCondition`, skilloverlapcompare.cpp), as it does `hasBuffOwner`, and never tests a condition with either, so it is read as `hasBuffOwner`'s counterpart. KR client data: on the effects 10300222 to 10300226 with `hasNotBuffID` 10300227, so another player's 10300227 on a target no longer blocks this player's 10300222.
- **Skill attack target order:** a skill attack's `direction` is now ingested (`SkillMetadataAttack.Direction`, needs a re-ingest; the parser does not carry it, so it is read from the skill files) and the server's target search puts its targets in that order before it takes the target count (`RangeTargetSelect`, from `FieldManager.GetTargets` for player skill attacks and npc casts). The client reads it with the attack (3 when not given) and its search sorts by it whenever no stat sort is given (`CBaseCollision::Detect`): 0 shuffles (the CRT rand Fisher-Yates of `ShuffleTargets`); for a box or frustum 1 and 2 sort by the target's signed offset along the search's right axis (1 the most negative first, 2 the most positive) and 3 and 4 by the absolute offset along its facing (3 nearest first); for a cylinder 3 and 4 sort by the squared distance from its center to the target's shape, for a hole cylinder by the distance to the target, on the plane; 5 and 6 sort by the cosine between the facing and the way to the target (5 the most straight ahead first). The search stands at the cast position plus the range offset turned to the facing (the client's MakeZRotation(pi - r): the offset's y runs along the facing, its x along the right axis) and faces the cast's facing plus the range's z rotation; an attack of `attackMethodType` 1 (its arrow hits; now ingested as `AttackMethodType`, from the played client's skill data with `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML`, as the data pack gives it for 2 skills only; taken by the attack's place in its motion when the played attack there has the same point, else the data pack's value stays (KR: 63 attacks, all 0 there), from the first `<level>` of each value (the client picks one by feature, `FUN_14028faa0`; KR has no value twice); KR: 8408 attack levels in 2850 skills, 486 of them in 232 skills with an offset or z rotation the search now leaves out, for example 10200282) takes neither and searches from the cast position with the plain facing (`SkillLevelData::FUN_1408043f0`). Each target's distance or offset is worked out once per search and moves with it through the swap sort. KR attacks: 1701 with 0, 1312 with 1, 241 with 2, 16036 with 3, 248 with 4. Before, the server took the first targets the field listed, so a skill with a target count below the targets in range hit whoever was listed first, not the nearest (or the farthest, or a random one). The client searches only for the casts it controls (`CMagic`, the owner's lock-on test) and never for an npc's, so npc casts get the same order here. Ties end in the order the client's swap sort leaves. Chosen here: for a player's cast of direction 0 the server shuffles with its own random source and still picks the targets itself, so when more targets are in range than the count, the ones the client shows hit can differ from the ones the server hits (as before, when the field's order picked them). The attack's area is placed the same way (`SkillUtils.GetPrism` for skill casts, npc casts and the debug view; the client's `DetectWithEulerRotation` in `FUN_1417e95a0`): before, the offset was added unturned, so an attack with an x or y offset hit off to one side for every facing but one. The swept shot of an npc arrow (`attackMethodType` 1, the client's `FUN_1417e9d20` box from the step's start to its end) sorts its hits by the attack's direction from the step's start, facing the travel, after the excluded targets and the ones off the step's box are taken out, and applies the range's target choice once, after that exclusion as the client does (before, nearest ahead first whatever the direction, and the choice was also made on the broad query first, so a mode 3 choice whose one passing target was then excluded hit no one). The corpse fallback of a player's attack searched 90 degrees off its direction; it now uses the same facing angle. Region skills keep the field's order: the client never searches for them (it only plays the region packets and shows `OnRegionSkillDamage`), so it has no order to follow, and keep their placement too (`SkillUtils.GetRegionPrism`: the offset added unturned, the area turned by the angle plus the range's z rotation), as no client code places them. Live try: see the report.
- **Charmed players hit their allies:** an additional effect's `forceApplyTarget` (BasicProperty) is now ingested (`AdditionalEffectMetadataProperty.ForceApplyTarget`, needs a re-ingest) and the server's target search reads it (`BuffManager.ForceApplyTarget`, in `FieldManager.GetTargetPool`). The client ORs it over the enabled buffs its player holds (`AdditionalCliList::FUN_140972a00`) into the target query of the player's skills (`CBaseCollision::FilterTarget`, basecollision.cpp): 1 also takes hostile actors, 2 friendly ones. KR has it on 7 effects, all 2 and all 5 s, the charms (with a -95 % attack rate) 40442043, 40500014, 40600014, 49165001, 49179005, 60093051 and 60144051, cast by npcs such as the Siren Harp (24003002, 29000212), the Snow Queen raid boss (21420330), Crow Magic (24002005) and the Desert Dragon (24002004). While it lasts, the holder's hostile skills hit the players around it as well as the mobs, outside PvP too; before, a charmed player's attacks reached only mobs and the charm was only its damage cut. A player killed this way off PvP dies as from a monster: no kill credit, no PvP reward, the field's death penalty. Bit 1 (no KR effect) adds the mobs to the holder's friendly skills. Npc and pet casters are not affected (the client reads it for its player only). The window ends with the buff. Live try: see the report.
- **Recovery debuffs:** an effect's StatusProperty itemRecoveryHPRate and healRecoveryHPRate are now ingested (needs a re-ingest) and change the health its holder gets from heals: a potion's heal (an effect of sub-type Consumable) is multiplied by 1 + the item rate, any other heal by 1 + the heal rate, the rates of the enabled buffs held added up times their stacks, rounded down and never below 0. Before, ingest dropped both, so the boss and dungeon debuffs that cut potions and heals did nothing. The effect tooltips give the sign and scale: 70000071 (both -0.1 to -0.95 by level) is "recovery by items and skills -10 %" and on, 50004800 (item -0.8 to -0.95) "recovery by HP potions -80 %", 50004803 (item -0.2, up to 5 stacks) the same at 20 % a stack, and 49113001 (both -0.5) "less health recovered by items and skills". The rates are on 12 effects in the KR data (21 levels and feature variants): 11 cut potions by 0.2 to 0.95, 3 of them (49113001, 70000071 and the PvP balance buff 90001002, both -0.9) cut other heals as well, and 90060009 raises potions by 0.2. The client reads both rates and never sums them (AdditionalCliList's status sum leaves them out), so the server applies them. Chosen here: telling a potion's heal by the effect's sub-type (146 of the 150 health heals the consumable skills cast are of sub-type Consumable, no job skill heal is); after the target's Bonus Recovery from Allies and before the stack count; spirit and stamina heals, health regen and kill restores are unchanged. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Monster rank damage:** the caster's Common Monster Damage, Elite Monster Damage and Bonus Boss Damage lines (special attributes 9, 11 and 12) now each raise only hits on hostile npcs of their rank, the ranks of monsterExpTable's columns: classes 3 and 7 (elite, dungeon elite) take the elite line, 4 and 6 (boss, dungeon boss) the boss line, any other class the common line. Before, the boss line also raised hits on class 3 and 7 elites, nothing read the elite line (17 constant item options and 15 set bonuses at 2 to 4 %, 7 buffs at 3 to 15 %), and the common line raised every hit, players included. A friendly npc and a player get none. The client never computes damage, so the split is chosen here from the server table's ranks; Leader Monster Damage (10) is on no item or buff in the KR data and stays unread. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Damage rate buffs:** a buff's physical or magical damage rate (OffensiveProperty papDamageR and mapDamageR, special attributes 177 and 178, and the invoke bonuses types 38 and 40 add to them) now raises the holder's hits of that attack type by the rate: the hit is multiplied by 1 + rate. Before, the hit was multiplied by the rate itself whenever it was not 0, so a +8 % buff left 8 % of the hit. The rates are on 178 effects (682 levels, 0.4 % to 50 %), among them job skill buffs (10100231 and 8 others), item and set effects and npc rage stacks; the effect tooltips read them as increases (110000115: 0.021 at level 1 is "damage dealt to enemies +2.1 %"; 110001009: physical and magical damage +2 %). The client reads only hitImmuneBreak from the effect's Offensive node and never computes damage, so the server applies the rate. A hit of either attack type takes the rate of the higher attack, as before. Not changed: the one negative rate in the data (70000095, -1) still does nothing, because a stat total does not go below 0 (the damage taken rates below are summed apart for that reason). Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Damage taken rates:** an effect's DefensiveProperty papDamageR and mapDamageR are now ingested (needs a re-ingest) and cut the physical or magical hits on the buff's holder: the hit is multiplied by 1 - rate, the rates of the enabled buffs held added up times their stacks, after the range and element reductions and before shields; a rate of 1 or more leaves the least hit (1), a negative rate adds to the hit. The effect tooltips give the sign and scale: 50000313 (0.99 both) is "damage taken from enemies -99 %", 50000316 and 50000317 (0.99) the physical and the magical half, 60132001 (physical 1) "blocks physical damage", 50000305 (magical -0.6) "magical defense falls 60 %" and 50003114 (-5) a frozen target at risk of instant death. The rates are on 73 effects (196 levels in the KR data, -5 to 2; 23 of the effects have a negative one), among them job skill buffs (10100151, 10100201, 10700161 and others). A hit of either attack type takes the rate of the type of the higher attack, as for the damage rate. The client reads only invincible from the effect's Defensive node and never computes damage, so the server applies the rate. Dots are cut too; constant and max-health hits are not. Not read: papDamageV and mapDamageV (flat values, only on 10800283, -60, with no tooltip). Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Hits only from named skills:** an effect's BasicProperty `attackPossibleSkillIDs`, `attackPossibleDotEffectIDs` and `attackPossibleEffectID` are now ingested (`AdditionalEffectMetadataProperty.AttackPossible`, needs a re-ingest) and applied (`AttackPossibleRules`): while an enabled buff with them is held, a skill hit lands only if its skill is listed, a damage over time tick only if its effect is listed, and either one if the caster holds the named effect. KR has them on 10 effects. Three are boss shields with a ShieldProperty and a dot list (50004546, 62100016, 62100024); their listed skills (90003005 and the like) are item-use skills with no attack, so in practice the listed dot is what breaks them. 62100153 and 62100156 have no shield and no dot list, only skills. 70002201, 70002361, 70002391 and 70002401 are invincible="0": the limit itself is what keeps their holder from being hit, except by the listed skills. 50004543 is hit only by holders of 90000874. A data typo is applied as written: 70002201 lists 9901027 and 99901028, and no skill 9901027 exists (99901027 does, the sibling of 70002361's 99901025 and 99901026). So a holder of 70002201 takes hits only from 99901028, and 99901027, which has an attack, never hits it (it still hits other targets). The client loads and compares the values and reads them nowhere else (additionalactionadditional.cpp:1080), so the server applies them. Chosen here: with several held, each must let the hit through; a refused skill hit is sent like a hit on an invincible holder (type 4, amount 0) and a refused tick as a 0 tick, and the shields stay. The caster's held effect counts only while it is enabled, as the fork's sums count only enabled buffs elsewhere. Only holders pay for the check: the limits are kept in `BuffSums`, and an actor with none returns before any lookup is built. Live try: not run (tests only); the ingested values are checked in a scratch db.
- **Final stats of additional effects:** the stats in an effect level's StatusProperty finalCap/Abp/Atp/EvpV/R and its FinalStatusProperty final<stat>rate/value rows are now ingested (needs a re-ingest) into the level's stat lines, as the client takes them: the played client's own level has a <SpecialAbility><v type value/></SpecialAbility> list (the last nonzero value of a type kept, additionalactionadditional.cpp:1227), turned into stats through table/specialabilitystringtable.xml (valueType 2 = a rate in thousandths, kept /1000 as the other rates: 90001005 hpRate 10000 is Health rate 10, as the unchanged 10900121 hpRate 10 is 0.01). Of that list only the types on the final row's attributes are taken (90000521 finalspvalue -30 takes sp -30, not the client's papRate 60 on the same level), and the client's mapping is not uniform: 70002231 finalmsprate -0.5 is msp -50 (flat), 73000001's is mspRate 500, 89000002 finalpap/maprate is finalAttackPower 252 plus pap/map 1, 70000173 finalhprate 50 to 500 per level and 90001002 one hpRate 29.25 for the pack's 55.2 to 455. A client level with no list adds nothing. The StatusProperty finals are never stats: the client reads no final name, and the KR pack's only nonzero ones are effect ids (40500027/40600027 finalCapV 50000017, 40500032/40600032 60011012), so without a played client they are skipped too instead of a CriticalRate of 50 million. The client keeps one value per type, so a client type of one attribute (hpRate, pap) is the level's whole stat of its kind: where the pack's level already has a rate or value of that kind on the attribute, the client's value replaces it (none in KR); a type over two attributes (finalAttackPower, wapRate) always adds (Chosen here). The ingest log counts the client rows dropped (not on a final attribute), the pack stats replaced, the StatusProperty rows left out (in both paths) and the pack values with a fraction cut to a whole number. 60 levels have finals in the KR data: 44 from the client's level, 16 from the pack's rows (Chosen here, where the client has no such level or only a Develop one: 70002541, 120030062 levels 1-10 and 89000014-18; a final rate becomes the stat's rate and a final value its value). finalAttackPower goes to the physical and magical attack rates (Chosen here: the client type has no server stat of its own). wapRate (90009501/2, -900) is the weapon attack min and max rates. The shared helper Maple2.File.Ingest/Helpers/ClientSpecialAbility.cs maps any client SpecialAbility type to server stats; PlayedClientAdditionalEffects.Entry.SpecialAbilities holds each played level's list.
