A/B of the KR ingest: only the Status of these 58 levels changes. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Owner event condition ignoreOwnerEvent (left unread):** an additional effect's condition `ignoreOwnerEvent` is ingested (`EventCondition.IgnoreOwner`) and nothing reads it. The client only loads it (m_nIgnoreOwnerEvent, additionalactionadditional.cpp:1599; the skill's ownerEventIgnore, m_bOwnerEventIgnore) and compares it when it tells two rows apart; no client code tests it, as the server runs the events. In the KR data every nonzero value is on an effect's `<owner>` condition (skills always have 0): 1,868 rows, most on event 6 (OnOwnerAttackHit, 1,695 rows on 363 effects), then 7 (51), 5 (48), 2 (26), 1 (25), 16 (16), 17 (4) and one each of 4, 10 and 18; 10500297 has the value 10500292 on event 0, a data slip. The same events also carry 0 (event 6 on 431 rows, 4 on 120, 10 on 99) and no effect mixes the two on one event. A 1 comes only with conditionSkill skillOwner 0 (the default), and 0 with skillOwner 0, 1, 2, 3 and 5, and both values are spread over class passives (10200031), item and option procs (900xxxxx, 1200xxxxx) with no id range of their own. Nothing names the owner it ignores (the effect's caster, a summon's master, a party member), so the server leaves it unread rather than guess: the owner checks run as before (`SkillUtils.Check`).
- **Bonus attack stat id:** the client's basic stat ids (`table/statstringtable.xml`) end 27 wapmin, 28 wapmax, 29 bap, 30 bap_pet, 31 pen, 32 rmsp, and the character panel's Bonus Attack row reads stat 0x1d (`UIPCInfoDialog::vfunc_86`, uipcinfodialog.cpp:965). The server's stat enum follows the data pack's StatValue order (dmg at 29, an unnamed 30, bap 33, bap_pet 34), and the Stat packet sent it as is, so a bonus attack (90009501: 4120) showed 0 and went out under an id the client has no row for. The Stat packet (the owner's full list and the type byte of a stat list) now writes BonusAtk and PetBonusAtk as 29 and 30; the server's Damage and its unnamed stat go out as 33 and 34 (chosen here: no client reader names those ids, and no server code reads those two stats). Every other packet field of a basic stat id carries the client's id too (`BasicAttributeIds.ClientStat`/`ServerStat`; the database, the ingest and the data pack keep the server's numbers, as 90051195's `statChangeResult="33"`, Bonus Attack from Accuracy, shows): item options (`CItemOption::Deserialize`, itemoptiondata_1.cpp:242), enchant options (`CItemEnchantInfo::Read`, itemenchantinfo.cpp:194), limit-break options (`UnlimitedEnchantInfo::ReadFromStream`, unlimitedenchantinfo.cpp:101), the enchant scroll lists (uienchantscrolldialog.cpp:372 and :409), the enchant dialog's staged and success lists (`OnEnchantInfoPacket`, uiitemenchantdialog.cpp:950; :1544), the merge option list (`CItemMergeOptionProxy::Read`, itemmergeoptiontable.cpp:245), another player's stat blocks (`CPCData::Read`, pcdata.cpp:101), the stat point list and its add request (`CStatPointManager::LoadStats` and `RequestAdd`, statpointmanager.cpp:66 and :124), the dps and floating recover type (scenestategame.cpp:4289 and :7878), the remake lock list (`Unk_RemakeStat::FUN_140619290`, itemremakescrolldata.cpp:854, kind 0) and the black market search key (`FUN_1401bce20`, blackmarketitemoption.cpp:262: group, percent, stat). The same panel shows a rate buff on weapon attack from the server's summed rates (`Stats.Total`: rates add, they do not multiply); with 110001006 (+2 %) held, 90009501's -90 % makes -88 % (a 112 average shows 13, not 11). The client shows the server's values and does not work this out, so how KMS2 combines two rates is not proven.
  - A basic stat id the client sends is one of its stat table's 35 (0..34; the table refuses any id past them). An id outside it drops the request: a stat point allocation is not answered, a kept option of an attribute change (0x0075 mode 0, 0x0076 mode 1) refuses the change with its server error, and a black market search option is left out. Before, the id was cast to the server's byte stat enum, so 285 named stat 29 and 256 stat 0.
- **Negative special attribute totals:** a special attribute's total (item option, buff, set and other lines in thousandths) is now the plain sum of its lines and may go below 0, as the client sums them (`CEquipment::GetAbilityTotal` adds the items', the buffs', the game rule's and the set items' lines with no floor). Before, each line added was cut to 0 at once, so a negative line was lost or counted in part depending on the order the lines came in, and the hit formula cut a negative rate to 0 again. The negative lines are in buff data: the Zakum and Turka boss shields (50006000 to 50006005, 50006007 to 50006012, 50006014 to 50006019) cut every element by 0.99 but one, which they take -5, -4 or -3 of, so a hit of that element now does 6, 5 or 4 times the damage instead of the full hit (the tooltip of 50006000: damage -99 % by the protection, but very weak to fire attacks); 40500047 (fire -4) and 50004500 (fire -300, held by npc 21400367) likewise. The Wizard's 10300213 (fire damage -0.08) and 90050331, 90050375 and 90050407 lower an element's damage, and 70000095 (physical and magical damage rate -1) now leaves its holder's hits at the least hit (1). The item option tables have no negative line. The uses that need a floor keep theirs (debuff duration, cooldown, kill and heal rates, the max-health cut and the least hit of 1). A basic attribute still stops at 0. No ingest change. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Max-health hit cut:** a hit by target max hp (a skill attack's or a dot's damageByTargetMaxHP, 210 skills in the KR data, mostly monster skills) is now cut by the caster's Reduce Damage By Target Max HP line (reduce_damage_by_targetmaxhp, special attribute 163): share = target max health x rate x (1 - line), the line capped at 100 % and a negative one ignored. The line is on the Berserker's Intimidation debuffs 10200061 and 10200064 (9 to 42 %, with the same cut to attack) and on 10200184 (50 %), so a monster under them loses as much of its health-share hits as of its attack; nothing read it before, and those hits took their full share. The target's own line does not cut. The client never computes damage, so the server chooses the formula. Constant-value hits are unchanged. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Hits down to a set health:** a skill attack's `constDamageByTargetHP` and `constDamageByTargetSP` are now ingested (`SkillMetadataDamage.ConstDamageByTargetHp` / `Sp`, needs a re-ingest). Each hit of such an attack takes the target's health down to the value (`DamageCalculator.DownTo`, in `Actor.ApplyDamage`, counting the earlier hits of the same attack) instead of the rate damage, never below it, so it never kills (the health change itself cannot kill either); shields do not take it, as for the max-health hit cut; a target already at the value or below takes a hit of 0, still a hit for the hit events; the damage record and the damage dealers get the health really lost. `constDamageByTargetSP` takes a hit player's spirit down to its value the same way when a hit landed (not when every hit missed or was blocked; npcs have no spirit). An invincible target, or a hit refused by attackPossible, takes nothing, as before, and a value of 0 or less is no such hit. KR data: 1 on the Zakum rage attack 50100116 (both) and on the Pink Bean curses 99999737 and 99999814 (health only); before, each was a plain rate-1 hit. The client reads both (skillattackdata.cpp) and counts an attack with `constDamageByTargetHP` above 0 as a damaging one (`SkillDamage::HasDamage`, which the damage numbers of `PCBase` check, pcbase.cpp), and never computes the amount. Chosen here: "down to the value", from the motion's effect name `Attack_Rage_HPSP` and the curse's tooltip (health drops to 1). Not tried live yet.
- **PvP damage and defense (left unread):** the PvP Damage and PvP Defense item lines (pvpdamageincrease, special attribute 54, and pvpdamagereduce, 55; flat special values on 490 and 389 constant item options of PvP gear, 4 to 84 and 7 to 189) are points, not rates. The client's character sheet shows them as whole numbers with no percent sign (UIPCInfoDialog's special stat row takes value / 10 % for every special stat but these two), and the KR special ability name table formats them {0:d} where the rate lines use {0:0.1f}%. Nothing in the client, constants.xml, the server constants table or the PvP mode buffs (ModePVPAdditionalEffect and its Red Arena, Crimson Tear Mine and rank duel siblings) names how points turn into damage, so the server leaves both lines unread rather than guess a scale; hits between players are unchanged.
- **Npc stats by player count:** the server table npcStatFactorByPlayerCount is ingested (rows whose feature is off and the file's empty rows are skipped). An npc spawned in a dungeon room with a playerCountFactorID, or in an instance field with an npcStatFactorID, gets the row for (factor id, npc class, player count): health, physical and magical attack, defense and critical rate each become stat x rate + value, cut to a whole number. No row for the id or the npc's class leaves the npc as it is, and a count above the factor's largest playerCount takes that largest row. The recorded hits agree: npcs in rooms with factor 1000 (papRate 0.95 for one player) hit for 0.95 of the field mobs' ratio, and those of instance field 1100001 (factor 2001, papRate 0.1) for 0.1 of it. The client only reads the two ids (dungeonroom.cpp, fieldwardata.cpp) and nothing in it says how the count is picked. Chosen here: the player count is the room's size when it was made (the party's members, 1 solo) or the players in the field at spawn, whichever is larger, since a room's npcs spawn before anyone enters; it is taken once, so npcs already spawned keep their stats when someone joins or leaves; a count with no row of its own takes the nearest lower row, or the lowest row when it is below all of them (factor 5001 starts at 4 players); the room's id wins over the instance field's, because a room id is set per dungeon room (one map can serve rooms of several difficulties) while the instance id is per map, and no room in the data has a field whose instance field names an id; player pets are never scaled, and neither is an npc a player summons with an item; npcs summoned by other npcs are scaled like the room's own. The npc damage factor 2.85 is unchanged. Needs a re-ingest (a server without the table refuses to start in Release, like every server table). Live try: not run (tests only).
- **AI select useNpcProb:** a select node with useNpcProb="1" now picks its children by the npc's own skill probs (npc xml `<skill probs>`, now ingested next to the ids and levels; needs a re-ingest), child i by the prob of skill i + 1, a child past the npc's skills never. Before, the node's prob list was used, which the parser fills with a single 100 when the attribute is absent, so such a select always took its first child: the 38 AI files with useNpcProb (AI_Default among them) (one branch per skill index under the top select, 2 or 3 of them) only ever used skill 1. Chosen here: the child-to-skill mapping is by position, as the branches are laid out in those files (two files, BossDungeon/BeetleBigYellow/AI_Default.xml and AI_DefaultNew_AttackAfterRunaway.xml, also name other skill indexes inside a branch); an npc with no probs (data ingested before this) keeps the old behaviour. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Skill autoTargeting turn:** an npc cast of a skill whose motion faces the target now turns to the target by the client's autoTargeting check (`PCBase::vfunc_101`, `FUN_141679800`): the target must be within a cylinder around the caster, radius autoTargetingMaxDistance (a plain ground distance) and height autoTargetingMaxHeight (150 when 0), and within a cone of autoTargetingMaxDegree degrees, the whole width, around the facing on the ground plane (dot >= cos(degree / 2 in radians)); all three must hold. Before, the degree went into Cos as radians, the squared distance was compared to MaxDistance, the height check took a ground axis, and the distance and height checks were ORed. The npc skills' blocks are almost all the default 210 / 0 / 0, so their turn changes little. Chosen here: a radius of 0, for which the client takes the skill's attack range, sets no limit; the cylinder is centred on the caster in both height cases and the target is taken as a point (the client centres it only for a set height and tests the target's collision volume). Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Npc standby facing:** a standby node with faceTarget="1" (AI_Default has one after each skill) now turns the npc to its battle target when the standby starts; before, the standby ignored the attribute and the next skill started on the old heading. Skills whose motion does not face the target (such as a lunge that moves the caster forward) then went past the player: one test field mob hit on 13 of 95 casts. In recorded KMS fights most field-mob casts near the player point at the player (about 80 to 90 of 100) and most engaged casts hit. Chosen here: only the value 1 turns the npc (3 and absent do not); the turn is the plain direction to the target, on the same spot the facing is kept. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Npc cast facing:** an npc's skill use now carries the npc's facing as its rotation, and the npc turns to its target (or to the AI's face position) before the cast is sent instead of after it. The client takes a cast's facing from the rotation's z (`CSceneStateGame::OnSkillUse`); KMS2 sends an npc cast with a zero direction and the rotation (0, 0, facing) (all of 4,155 npc casts), and a boss's cast at a player points at that player within 15 degrees in 290 of 381 first motions that face the target. Before, the rotation of every npc cast was zero unless the npc itself faced zero (the test for a missing rotation looked at the caster's instead of the cast's), and the control entry sent with the cast still had the old heading. Splash and cube skills an npc cast places take their rotation from the cast as well. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **AI skill node without faceTarget:** a skill node that writes no faceTarget (19722 of the 32397 skill nodes; the rest write 0, 1 or 3) now turns the npc to its target at the cast, as faceTarget="1" does; a written faceTarget="0" still leaves the facing to the motion's own faceTarget and the autoTargeting check. The parser reads both as 0, so the ingest tells them apart from the AI file (`AiSkillFace`; needs a re-ingest). KMS2 sends show the difference: a boss whose skill nodes write no faceTarget and whose motions have faceTarget="0" casts facing its target every time, turning right round between two casts 1.4 s apart, while one whose nodes write faceTarget="0" keeps its facing. Before, such bosses (most boss AI files) never turned at a cast. Standby nodes without the attribute (648) are unchanged: not known.
- **Guide objects:** the skill guide has no int before the entry count; a ball is created on field entry through the home action packet, not the guide object create, which refuses a ball.
- **Guild:**
  - Server-to-client sub-types 52 to 89 are shifted up by 2 to the client's numbers.
  - Experience, funds and member contribution use the client's sub-types (53 and 97).
  - Guild raid records: a guild member's raid rows (the member block's CGuildMemberRaid, commonfiles/guilddef_1.cpp:783: an int count, then raid id and value pairs) were always empty, and Guild 37 (`OnPacketMemberRaidCount`, client/ms2/guildmanager.cpp:2120: name, raid id, value) had no writer. The raids are the dungeon rooms of `table/guildraid.xml` (now ingested into the guild table: dungeonRoomID, clearRaidPoint, clearGuildFund, clearGuildExp, as `CGuildXmlData::GuildRaid` reads them, guilddef_2.cpp:433; KR: 26001001 to 26005001, clearRaidPoint 1000). A clear of such a room that gives the clear reward adds the room's clearRaidPoint to the member's value on the World server, which stores it (a json column on guild-member, migration AddGuildMemberRaids) and sends Guild 37 to the guild on every channel. The channel's call to the World runs off the dungeon field's thread, and the World saves only that member's row (a failed save keeps the value for the guild's next save). The raid page shows each value (a row is complete when the value is above 0) and the member's and the guild's sums as the raid score (client/ms2/uiguilddialog.cpp:2108, :2718); the client reads clearRaidPoint but never uses it, so the client does not tell a count from points. Chosen here: the value is points (clearRaidPoint per clear); the KMS2 rows seen are 1000 each, which fits one clear of points and no count of a weekly room. Chosen here: the records are cleared at the server's weekly reset, with the weekly contribution; the rooms' own cooldown is weekly too. Guild 61, which that reset already sends, falls through in the client's dispatcher to the handler of 62 (guildmanager.cpp:367, :2637), so it also clears the client's records and no 62 is sent. Chosen here: a clear that gives no clear reward (the room's reward count used up) adds nothing. Not done: what clearGuildFund and clearGuildExp multiply, and the entry rule of string 3841 (s_room_dungeon_require_guild_join_date: new members wait for the raid score reset). No source: the weekly raid rewards (`CGuildXmlData::GuildRaidReward`, guilddef_2.cpp:445: raidPoint, guildFund, rewardItems; `GetRaidReward`, :765, takes the row at or above a raid point). The client has the row reader but loads no file into it and nothing calls `GetRaidReward`. Needs a re-ingest and the migration.
  - Guild vs game (66200001_gd, InstanceType GuildVsGame): the field runs the script's guild_vs_game_* actions and conditions, set_user_value_from_user_count, set_user_value_from_guild_vs_game_score and user_value_to_number_mesh; its show_event_result and system sounds go to the tagged team. Each team's players get the result dialog (Guild 122: the blue guild, then the red one, each id, name, mark and score; the client finds its own side by guild id), the guild's exp and fund rewards of `table/guildproperty.xml` at the guild's level (added by the World off the field's thread, noticed with Guild 123 with what the World added: kind 1 exp, 2 fund; the fund stops at the guild's fund max, and nothing added sends no notice), guild coins as items, and each member the contribution of `table/guildcontribution.xml` (winMiniGame / loseMiniGame; now ingested, new world rpc AddContribution, which also adds to the week's contribution). The member contribution update now leaves out the check-in time when it has none. A win by default keeps the script's isWin false (the data as it is). Chosen here: in a match field the guild of the first ticket is the blue team (tag 1) and the other red (2), set before anyone arrives (a field entered without a match: the first guild to arrive blue, the second red); anyone else is on no team; a round goes to the team with more players left in the box, an even round to none; the number meshes show the ones at the start block and the tens at start + 10 (the xblock's placement), leading zeros shown. The exp reward is 0 at most guild levels in the data. After guild_vs_game_end_game no round, reward or contribution counts; a room plays one game (the script's create_field_game has no reset). The registration and match run (Guild 115/116/120, see below): two guilds with tickets get one match field, which both guilds' members enter from the entry prompt; a plain move to the map still makes a new field per entry (FieldEntryRules.NewFieldPerEntry). guild_vs_game_end_game ends the World's match (the guilds' open game is cleared, Guild 119 with 0). Not tried live.
- **Server list:** the error writes the string the client reads first.
- **Server list character count and RequestServerList (0x14):** the byte after a server's name is the account's character count on it, which the server list shows as charCnt (`UIServerListDialog::ReceiveServerList`, uiserverlistdialog.cpp:310, :483); the server wrote a constant 4, so every account showed four characters. It now counts the account's character rows, at the login's server list, at ServerEnter and in the list a Game server sends at field entry. RequestServerList has no body and had no handler: the client sends it when it leaves the character list, after a LoginToGame that succeeded (uicharacterlistdialog.cpp:592-601) and on the back-to-server-list button (:1644-1648). A server picked again from that list opens a new connection that logs in again (uiserverlistdialog.cpp:512); the login server now drops the account's entry at 0x14, so that login is not refused as already logged in while the old socket is still closing, and a session that closes drops the entry only while it still names that session. Nothing is sent back (the list shows what it already has). Chosen here: the count is of all the account's character rows, as the server has one world.
- **Birthday card (BirthdayCard 0x111 / c2s 0xa6) not built:** the card dialog shows the birthday month the character data carries (`UIBirthdayCardDialog`, `PC::FUN_141660030`); the feature HappyBirthday is level 99 in KR (Live 56; only CN has it) and the server holds no birthday.
- **Reconnect (0x05):** after a lost link the client can connect to the game server again and, in place of ResponseKey, send a reconnect request with its account id, character id, attempt, packet sequence, machine id and the context the server gave it (`CNetworkService::OnRequestKey`, `CReconnectionManagerCli::SendReconnectRequest`, reconnectionmanagercli.cpp:245). It does so only after the server turned reconnection on (Gvg group 43, the first option 1) and sent a context of mode 1 (Reconnect command 0); this server sends neither, so the client does not reconnect and shows the usual disconnect box. The request had no handler; it is now read and refused with Reconnect command 3, on which the client drops its reconnect state and, in the game scene, quits to the login scene with its own message (`OnReconnectPacket`). Nothing in the request proves the account, and a real resume would need the lost session kept with the packets the client has not had acknowledged (the client drops its copies on Pong), which the server does not keep.
- **Heartbeat and lost sessions:** the World server asks every session for a heartbeat each 30 s; the client only answers it (`CNetworkService::OnHeartbeatRequest`) and keeps no time limit of its own. A late answer no longer ends the session (the Release build cut any answer later than 2 s, so a client that stood still for 3 s with a heartbeat on the way was dropped): a held-up client is still there. A session ends when nothing at all has arrived from its client for three heartbeat rounds, 90 s (chosen here), checked when the next heartbeat is due, so the cut comes 90 to 120 s after the client's last packet and only after two unanswered heartbeats (a client that stands still for 100 s is cut only when a round falls into its last 10 s). That session's socket is reset, not closed in order: the client shows its lost-connection notice, or starts its reconnect, only when the socket closes with an error (`CGameSocket::OnClose`); after an orderly close it shows nothing and stays on the field with nothing reaching the server. `Session.Cut` resets; `Session.Disconnect` still closes in order, for a client that was sent its last packet and leaves by itself.
- **A World server that comes back:** a Game server now checks its World link every 5 s and registers again when the World no longer knows its channel: it asks for its own channel id (`AddChannelRequest.channel`, with an instance id so a second process cannot take a held id) and then reports each online session with the player update it sends at login, so the World's channel and player lists are whole again; a report that fails because the World is not there is tried again, and one the World refuses for a single session is logged and skipped. Before this a restarted World handed the next Game server the same id and knew none of the players until each changed map. Chosen here: parties are not kept across a World restart, so a member whose party the World no longer has gets the party-disbanded answer the client already handles; group chats, party search entries, dungeon matches and other state the World holds in memory are gone as before. No database change.
- **Two Game servers started together:** the World server marked a channel inactive when its health check got no answer, also while the channel was still pending (its Game server loads its data before it listens, about ten seconds), and gives an inactive channel on the same address to the next Game server. A second Game server started in that time got the first one's id and ports: it failed to listen (address in use) and exited, and the first one's link check was refused. A pending channel now stays pending for a start grace (`ChannelRegisterRules.StartGraceMs`, chosen here: two minutes) before an unanswered check ends it, and the inactive channel is looked up and taken under the registration lock; the health monitor also changes a channel's status under that lock, so it cannot set back a channel taken a moment before. A Game server that dies during its start holds its id for the grace: restarted inside it, it gets the next id and ports (no clash); after it, the id is free again. A served channel still fails over at once. No database change.
- **World start offline reset:** a World that starts sets every character's channel to -1. That update also moved the row's LastModified (the save conflict check), so after a World-only restart each character still online lost its first save to a concurrency conflict (channel 2 against -1). The reset now keeps LastModified, so that save goes through whole.
- **Saves during a stored reset:** while the World stores a daily, weekly or monthly reset, a save on the field thread does not wait for it. A purchase whose caller gives back what it took (a shop buy, a quest completion) is refused with that caller's usual error; none of these packets has a "try again later" code (the client's s_ugcmap_retry_later and s_blackmarket_notice_try_later belong to other windows), so the player sees the generic error and can try again a few seconds later. A field mission or stamp quest whose last counter filled in that window is completed 2 s later on the field's queue, or at the next field entry. The try again completes only the quest the session still holds: when the reset drops that day's mission (with the day's other open missions) before the try again runs, it is not completed, pays nothing and writes no row back, so the day's grant still comes; when the grant started it again, the new one starts with its counter at 0. KMS would have completed it before the reset, so a mission filled in the reset's last seconds is lost here (a known gap). Each step of a reset an online session takes runs on its own, so one that throws does not skip the rest; the step that threw is logged and not run again for that reset, and the next save stores what it left (a known gap: what it did before the throw stays, what it did not do waits for the next reset).
  - Checked, no change: the World's machine id write at an account's first login and the game entry's online and channel write store the whole account row as they read it, but the row version refuses the row when another write came between (tests AccountRowRaceTests): the other write is kept, the machine id is written at a later login, and the entry fails with a concurrency error (an exception on the session's thread, so the session is cut). The daily, weekly and monthly resets cannot come between at entry: the entry reads their times with a share lock first.
- **Item save cost:** a save (the timed save, a field or channel change, the logout) wrote every item row of the character (bag, storage, equips, furnishing storage) whether it had changed or not, and its EF change scan grew with the square of the rows: 480-560 ms a save for 1024 storage rows on a local MySQL, of which the database took 11-13 ms. SaveItems now keeps, for each item object, the column values a committed save of this server stored for it, and writes a row only when its values differ from those; a changed, an added (a new object: a load, a copy, a row made elsewhere) and a removed item (its owner-0 write) are written as before. The same 1024 rows now save in 19-42 ms; a save that writes all of them takes 150-200 ms. The values are kept only once the save's transaction commits, so a failed or rolled-back save writes the rows again next time; every other write of an item row forgets what any item object keeps for that row, so the next save writes it (a split lowering the source stack, a home save joining a returned cube to its storage stack or mailing a doll's item, the doll put-on, swap and delete, a move between owners, a mail deleted with its items, a black market buy or take-back, UpdateItem): without it a split merged back, or a joined stack placed again, matched the kept values and was not written, so an item was lost on relog or a furnishing doubled. Each row id has a write stamp (in a table of 2^18, set from one rising clock) that every write moves on, and a write outside a transaction moves it again once it is done; kept values count only at the stamp they were kept at, so two objects of one row (a home's cube and its copy) do not skip a write after each other's. Two saves of one object that overlap (two threads) keep nothing, and a commit whose answer was lost keeps nothing. What is stored and the all-or-none session save are as before.
  - The loads (login, storage, furnishing, a single item) keep each loaded row's values for its item as a save had stored them, so the first save after a login writes only what changed since: with 1024 storage rows it took 353-402 ms and wrote 1064 rows, now 83-90 ms and none (the rest is the other tables and the first run). A row is kept only when a save of the item as loaded would write exactly that row (LastModified left out; the times to the second, as the item holds whole seconds and the row the fraction of its insert), when no write of the row came after the load's query began (its stamp is not past the clock read before it), when the load is not in a transaction (it reads the transaction's own writes) and when the request did not already track the row (a tracked row comes back as tracked, not as the DB holds it). A load that fails keeps nothing; a new item has nothing kept and is written. Each session's first save logs `First save took N ms, M item rows written`.
  - Only rows of an account and its character that a session of this server holds are kept: the stamps are per process, so a write by another game server after the load (the save of the channel the player came from, still storing) would never reach them, and each save would skip the row while the database kept the other write (an item lost at the next login, or doubled against a trade). A session holds them from its entry when it took the account lock there (the old channel's save holds the lock until it is stored, and that session saves nothing after it); an entry that went on without the lock (refused after its five tries, or the world down) keeps nothing, and its first save writes every row as before. Rows of other owners (a pet's, a doll's, a mail's) are not kept. A row's stamp only rises: a write that took its clock value before another stored a later one leaves the later one.
  - UpdateItem (a blueprint's confirmed picture) wrote the row with owner 0, an unowned row deleted at the next world start unless a save of its owner came first; the row now keeps its owner.
  - A row an unchanged item skips keeps its LastModified: every write sets it to the minimum time, as before.
- **Which close a session gets:** every close that the client did not ask for and was not told of by a last packet is now a reset (`Session.Cut`): a socket error or a failed send, an exception on the session's threads, a wrong client version, the World server's disconnect request (the account logs in elsewhere, a GM ends the session), an equip or badge request that leaves the session in a bad state, a login that fails without an answer, and the older login session of an account that logs in again. The orderly close stays for a session that was sent a last packet that makes the client leave (the move to another server, channel or map instance, a disconnect notice) and for what the client asks for itself (quit, back to the server list). The first close decides: a reset that comes while an orderly close drains (the session's own threads end with one) no longer turns it into a reset. Login sessions get the same 90 s silence check as channel sessions, in the login server's heartbeat round. The silence check also needs two heartbeats that went unanswered since the client's last packet (chosen here), so that a session is not cut for the time the rounds themselves stood still (a World server restart, a round that was held up); rounds closer together than half an interval count as one. An error packet that the client shows without leaving (a failed channel change, move to the home or another map, return to the character list, login key or game entry) and the logout command are followed by a reset that waits 0.5 s after the send queue is drained (`Session.CutAfterSend`, the wait chosen here), so the client reads the error and then learns that the connection is gone; they were closed in order, which left the client on a dead socket.
  - Chosen here: command 3 rather than a failure code of command 2, so no code is made up; the request is handled on the game server only, where the client sends it; the server does not close the link itself (the client does on command 3) and does not act on the ids it read; reconnection stays off (no Gvg group 43, no context).
- **Field portal:** sub-type 3 writes the portal id before the two vectors.
- **Cube:**
  - The architect score, build budget and build permission packets (sub-types 28 and 56 to 59) write the client's fields.
  - The profile update writes four ints before the name and a trailing byte; plot extension writes only the error byte.
  - The vote confirm (sub-type 25) writes the error byte and, on success, the character id and value; sub-type 53 writes the four longs the client reads.
  - A refused edit is answered in the edit's own sub-type, as KMS2 sends it: place (10), remove (12) and replace (15) write the error byte and the object id twice (10 bytes), a refused lift (17) the error byte and the object id (6 bytes). The server answered every refusal through sub-type 2, which KMS2 does not use for these (186 refused edits and 20 refused lifts in KMS2 sessions, none in sub-type 2). A place on a taken cell is refused with error 2 (s_ugcmap_create_on_non_empty_area) instead of s_ugcmap_cant_create_on_place, and a replace by the item already on the cell with error 53 (s_ugcmap_cant_replace_same_cube), which the server carried out (68 such refusals, no such replace carried out). A cube that cannot be paid is refused the same way (error 96 or 97).
    - The 10-byte place answer with byte 1 is no refusal: it is the notice of a bought cube, sent between the storage packets and the place itself (KMS2 sends FurnishingStorage 3, 5, 2, this notice, FurnishingStorage 4, FurnishingInventory 1, then the place). The server sends the same packets in that order. Its FurnishingStorage 5 named the item id where the client reads the storage item's uid (`CSceneStateGame::OnFurnishingStorage`, scenestategame.cpp:2736), so the client found no item and skipped its gain notice; it now names the uid, as KMS2 does.
    - Not done: a refused rotate keeps sub-type 2: KMS2 sessions hold no refused rotate.
  - A build helper gets sub-type 55 after sub-type 53, as KMS2 sends it: the owner's interior level (a short) and the list of (trophy id, grade) pairs that cube items ask for and the owner has not cleared, in key order. In a home it may build in, the client locks a cube by these instead of the helper's own level and trophies (`MyPC::FUN_1415302e0`, mypc.cpp:4437; the tooltip, astooltip.cpp:2292); without the packet the owner's level is 0 there. The level is the home's decoration level, the value sub-type 39 gives the owner's own client.
  - A lifted weapon cube (a rock, a sign) is away until its respawn time: a second lift of it before then is refused (`s_ugcmap_no_cube_to_lift`), and a player who enters the field meanwhile gets sub-type 27 with each such cube's cell and the tick it comes back at, which hides it on that client as the lift itself (sub-type 17) does for the players present (scenestategame.cpp:10195). Before, the server kept no such state: a late player saw the cube and could lift it again at once.
    - Chosen here: sub-type 27 is sent only when a cube is away. KMS2 also sends it with a count of 0 (9 of 10), which changes nothing in the client.
  - The forfeit confirm (sub-type 7) carries a reason: 0x72 to an owner who gives the plot up, as KMS2 sends it, and 0x75 to an online owner whose plot ran out (the World's expiry job tells every channel; the owner is answered on whichever channel they are on, whether or not it has the plot's map loaded). The client shows its "already expired" notice only for 0x75 and the forfeit notice for anything else (scenestategame.cpp:9959); the server sent 0 on both paths, so an expired plot was announced as given up.
  - A plot purchase (request 2) and a plot extension (request 9) give the contract or extension cost back when the plot row cannot be saved. Before, the mesos or merets were taken first and kept when the save then failed, as it does for a plot another channel sold first (its row already has an owner) or for a refused write. The cost is still checked and taken in one step before the save, with the same error (`s_ugcmap_not_enough_money`, `s_ugcmap_need_extension_pay`), since the merets can also be taken on another thread (a partner's paid wedding hall) and a charge after the save could fail with the plot already saved. The refund is the same amount in the same currency, and a failed buy leaves the home's outdoor plot as it was. Chosen here: a meret refund does not take back the use_merat trophy count the charge raised.
  - The plot row and the cube deletes of a purchase, an extension and a forfeit are written in one transaction, and the row by one conditional update: a purchase only while the plot has no owner and has expired, an extension or forfeit only while the plot still has the owner and expiry read. Before, a purchase and a forfeit deleted the plot's old cubes and their maids at once and saved the row after, so a purchase whose save failed, such as the second buyer of a plot another channel had just sold, still deleted the cubes (the plot row's version refused the second save, but only after the deletes). An extension did not check the owner. Any of these that fails, a database error included, gets null with nothing written, which the refund above relies on. A commit that throws is logged as "commit outcome unknown" with the plot, owner and expiry, since it may still have been applied while the purchase cost is refunded. A plot cannot be forfeited once it has expired: the World's expiry job takes it back then. The server let a forfeit through up to three days after expiry, and the storage then refused it with a database error. The client has no rule of its own; the expiry error is the server's.
  - The concept of a plot purchase (request 2, its second int) is used. A player with no plot gets the model house dialog first (`UIRealEstateDialog::SendRequestCubeMode2`, uirealestatedialog.cpp:404-425); its Flash (uiugcmodelhousedialog.gfx) sends 0, 1 or 2 for its three model houses (m_concept0..2) and -1 for its empty house (m_empty), through `UIUGCModelHouseDialog`'s OnHomeConcept (uiugcmodelhousedialog.cpp:76-91). Model house c is exportedugcmap/(c+1).xml, the three labelled houses of the dialog. After a purchase, a buyer whose indoor home has no name yet gets that home made from the chosen house, as the dialog's other path (RequestHome 3) does; -1 makes the empty home. Before, the server dropped the int, so the house chosen in the dialog was lost. A home that already has a name is not changed, and nothing is placed on the outdoor plot. Chosen here: a concept outside -1..2 is taken as -1 (the empty home). Chosen here: an unnamed indoor home that already holds cubes (built through the plot's door) is also left as it is, since building a model house over it would delete them. RequestHome 3 keeps that wipe, as the client asks for a new home there: with a model house, the cubes already in the unnamed home are deleted; the empty home (-1) deletes nothing.
    - The cubes a model house deletes (RequestHome 3) go back to the owner's furnishing storage in the same save as the delete; before, they were lost. The client does not say they are lost: the model house dialog (`UIUGCModelHouseDialog`, uiugcmodelhousedialog.cpp, its confirm sends `MyPC::SendRequestHome` mode 3, mypc.cpp:3163) shows no warning, and its strings (`s_ugcmodelhouse_dialog_title`, `_type1`..`_type3`, `_desc1`..`_desc3`, `s_home_info_build_modelhouse`, `s_home_info_build_empty_modelhouse`) say nothing about the placed items. A home taken away gives its furnishings back to the furnishing list (system mail 50000001). A cube joins the first stored stack of its item and design, as a cube taken off a plot does, or takes a new storage slot when there is no such stack (at most 1024 slots); a cube whose stack is full, or that finds no free slot, is sent by system mail 50000000 (as a mannequin's items are). A maid cube's maid moves with it to the storage item or the mailed item. If the save fails, nothing is written: the cubes stay in the home and storage is unchanged. Chosen here: mailed cubes of one item and design share one mail up to the item's stack size; a maid cube gets a mail of its own. Chosen here: the home keeps its new name and size when the cube save fails. Not done: a cube whose item is unknown to the metadata is deleted without a return (as before). Another instance of the same home that is still open (another channel, or one left empty) gives no cube twice: its save of the old cube list writes nothing, since the model house deleted those rows (the save fails as a whole and is logged), and a pickup of an old cube there is refused, since the cube is no longer in the owner's furnishing list. An empty instance of the home is not reused once the model house has written its cubes (see below): the next entry loads the home again, so the owner who warps home after the model house sees its cubes. Before, that entry reused the old instance for up to 10 minutes: it showed the old cubes, did not show the template cubes, and the leave failed its save. Chosen here: an instance with players in it is kept as it is until it empties; a cube placed there in that time is not saved. A name given by SetName alone does not reload an instance: the entry reads the home's name and size from the database (LoadUgcMap), not from the instance. The free template cubes come once per indoor home: a model house is applied only while the indoor name is empty, every gameplay path that sets the name refuses an empty one, and forfeit and expiry change outdoor plots only. Only the GM command `reset-home` (debug permission) empties the name again.
    - The retire of a stale home instance (the model house applied since it was loaded) now also waits for a player whose map is still loading there: the spawn that sends the player to the field counts the player as arriving until the client's field entry or the player's leave, and the retire and the arrival take one lock, so either the player is counted or the spawn sees the field retired. A spawn into a field retired meanwhile asks the factory for the home again and moves the player there (its return map is pushed once). Before, an entry could be sent to an instance the factory had just taken out, and that instance was then disposed with the player loading into it. A retired instance not yet disposed when the channel shuts down is disposed with the factory; before, it was left undisposed. Chosen here: should the new load fail, the player stays in the retired instance and the failure is logged. Not tested: the spawn's second load (the tests have no field factory for a session); the retire's wait and the shutdown are.
    - A home instance is stale whenever its cube rows differ from the database's, not only when it was loaded before the home had a name. Each instance keeps the cube rows it loaded and saved, per plot. It is stale when the database holds a cube row the instance neither loaded, saved nor holds (rows a model house, an interior package or another instance wrote), or when it holds a saved cube whose row is gone. The factory reloads a stale instance that has no players. Before, an instance loaded while the home already had a name was reused after a model house: the owner saw the empty floor at every entry, and the owner's leave saved the old empty list, which deleted the template's rows from the database. A stale instance's leave no longer saves the indoor plot; it logs a warning instead. A cube placed or taken in a live instance writes or deletes its row at once, so the instance's own edits keep it current. Chosen here: an instance with players in it is kept until it empties, as before.
      - A cube's rotation also writes its row at once (HousingManager.TryRotateCube), and an instance is stale too when a cube it holds has another rotation in its row. Before, a rotation changed only the instance's cube and was saved at the leave: a stale instance's leave saves nothing, so its rotations were lost, and a rotation made in another instance (same rows) was not seen, so this instance's leave wrote the old rotation back. This holds for outdoor plots too. Chosen here: when the row cannot be written, the cube is turned back and the client gets the no-cube-to-rotate error.
      - Chosen here: when the row cannot be written, the cube keeps the exact rotation it had (a row can hold one outside 0..360). Before, it was turned back the other way, which gives the old value only modulo 360, and the instance was then stale against its own row.
      - The leave's save takes one copy of the plot's cubes under the plot's edit lock, in the same lock as the stale check and the read of the plot's rows, and writes the copy outside the lock (FieldManager.CommitPlot); the cube write never runs under the lock (only the rewrite of a rotation, below, does, as a rotation itself does). The swap after the write takes only the plot's edit lock, not the field's lock(Plots), so no database write runs under the field-wide lock; before, the rotation rewrite ran with lock(Plots) held. The rows each plot last saved are kept per plot in a concurrent map, since two plots' saves no longer share a lock. The save deletes only rows it read in that step, so a row written after the copy (a build helper's place meanwhile) is kept, and a copied cube whose row is gone when the save writes (removed meanwhile) is skipped. Under the lock again, a cube still held as copied takes its saved row; one changed or removed meanwhile is left as it is, and one turned meanwhile keeps its rotation, which is written to its row again (undone when that write fails). A remove between the save's read of the rows and its write (a build helper's delete is written at once) leaves that cube out: its update finds no row, so the save drops that update and writes the other updates again in the same try, and the cube is not among the saved rows (GameStorage SaveCubes; the new rows and the deletes are written only after the updates). A write that fails otherwise takes a new copy and writes again, at most 3 times. Before, the missing row failed the whole write, so a remove in each of the 3 tries made the leave throw (under load, a home leave could fail this way). A copied cube whose row another instance deleted is kept, so the instance is stale and reloads once it empties. Before, the save read the live cube list without the lock, so a helper's edit during a save could throw from it, delete the row of a cube placed meanwhile or write back the old rotation of a cube turned meanwhile, and the check and the save were two steps.
      - A row the instance took off the plot but could not delete (a place refused after its row was written, a remove, when the delete fails) is kept on the plot as a stray row: it counts as saved, so the instance stays current, and the next save deletes it. A stray row the save wrote again (a cube copied, then removed with a failed delete before the write) is deleted again right after the swap, under the edit lock, so the row does not depend on this instance saving again (a field disposed before its next save left the row, and the next load showed the cube beside its storage item); when that delete fails too it stays a stray and the next save deletes it. Before, the save cleared every stray it had read, and that row then stayed beside its item in storage (the cube twice) and kept the instance stale. Before, such a row kept the instance stale for its whole life (its leave saved nothing), and the next load showed its cube without a storage item.
      - Known limits. Chosen here: should the write fail 3 times in a row for another reason, the save fails as any failed save does: it is logged and the leave throws, so the rest of the player's field leave is skipped. Chosen here: a change to a held cube's fields made between the copy and the swap, other than its rotation and its portal settings (both carried over), stays on the cube the swap replaces.
    - A furnishing item collected from mail (and any other furnishing item added to the bag, from shops or rewards) goes into the furnishing storage whole, or is refused (s_mail_error_receiveitem_to_inven) and stays where it was. Before, only one piece was stored, the rest of the stack was lost, and the item was also lost when storage was full; and mail collect checked a bag tab for room instead of the storage.
      - The room checks before a payment (a shop buy, a treasure map, a pickup, a premium club or pet reward) ask furnishing storage the same way, all pieces or none; before, a shop buy with storage full took the mesos and lost the item. Where the Add can still fail after a check (an NPC script's present items, a treasure map's rewards, a UGC give-back coupon) the item goes by mail; a pickup that does not fit stays on the ground. Chosen here: the mail fallback, as other rewards in the fork use it; the client shows no message of its own for these.
      - A bag item of more than its stack size (a shop pack, a field drop, a reward) goes in as stacks of at most that size, all of it or none. The room is counted with the stacking rule the bag itself uses (same id, rarity, transfer, lock, binding and expiry), so a locked stack of the item is no room for it. Before, the split filled every stack of the same id and rarity, counted the slots after that and could stop half way: a shop buy kept the mesos and lost the rest, and a pickup (checked for one free slot only) dropped the whole amount again after the first part went into the bag. A pickup now needs room for all of it. If a write fails half way, the pieces given stay and only the rest is mailed (shop) or dropped (pickup). Chosen here: a shop item whose add still fails after the payment goes by mail. When a furnishing item from mail fails half way, the storage rows it already wrote are written back at once, not at the next storage save.
      - An item that already has a row (a mail attachment) and is more than its stack size is split off that row: each piece but the last is a new bag row written in the same save that lowers the attachment, and the last piece is the attachment itself, so its row moves to the bag. Before, the pieces were new rows and the attachment row stayed with the mail, so a collected mail gave everything again after a relog, without end. If a write fails half way, the mail keeps only the rest, in the database too. A mail is collected only when the bag has the slots for every attachment (one per stack size), not one slot per attachment. A box or capsule result after a failed write lists what it gave, not only the mailed rest.
      - A bag add that is written at once (a mail attachment, a split piece) changes nothing in the bag when its save fails: the stacks, the new entry and the item's amount and slot are put back and the add is refused. Before, the result was not read: the bag kept the item while its row stayed the mail's, so it came back on relog. A new item that tops up a stack is saved together with the stack in one write (its emptied row to no owner). A split of a row that is gone writes no piece.
        - Guild coins: the check-in's coin (Guild 15, after the World has taken the check-in) and the guild vs game's guildCoin reward come by mail when the bag is full; before, the bag refused them and they were lost.
        - Blueprints (RequestCube 63): a full bag refuses the blueprint with the bag's notice before anything is paid or written; before, the merets were taken first and the blueprint and its layout row were lost. The answer now carries the uid of the item in the bag (the client looks the item up by that uid); before, it carried 0, so the client could not find it and the upload worked on a copy.
        - Shop buy back (Shop 3): when the bag's write fails (the room is checked first), the entry is listed again with its id (Shop 7) and no meso is taken; before, the mesos were taken and the item, out of the list and not in the bag, was lost. Chosen here: listing the entry again, as a sale lists it. The buy back holds the bag lock from its room and meso checks to the meso take, so an item or meso change made under that lock on another thread cannot come between the check and the take; before, a spend in between made the take throw with the item already given. Its save takes the account lock after the bag lock is let go, the order a session save takes them in. Not changed: a meso change made without the bag lock can still come between them.
        - Meso changes are made in one step under the wallet's own lock (CurrencyManager.ChangeMeso, TryTakeMeso, AddMeso): other threads change the balance too (the field's thread: arcade, dark stream, mini game, trigger and dungeon rewards; the other trader's thread: a trade's hand-over and give-back) while the packet thread pays or is paid. Before, each `Meso += / -=` read and wrote the balance apart, so two changes at once wrote over each other: a payment undone (mesos made) or a gain lost. Every meso take runs on the payer's own packet thread, one packet at a time. The buy back now checks and takes its price in one step before the item is added, and sends the balance after the add (KMS2 order kept); a failed add gives the price back. Before, a spend between the check and the take made the take throw with the item given and no meso taken. Not changed: the other check-then-pay paths still check and take in two steps; only gains come from another thread, so the balance they checked cannot drop before the take.
            - The divorce filing's refund (a filing not stored) and the storage's give-back of a meso move that is not stored are one-step changes too; before, it was set from a balance read before, so a gain on the field's thread in between was lost. A give-back of a take made without its packet (buy back's failure paths) sends the balance; before, nothing was sent, so a meso packet sent in between (a gain on the field's thread) left the client showing the balance without the price. A give-back that would pass MaxMeso stops there and the part over it is logged (chosen here); before, the sum overflowed past long.MaxValue to a negative balance.
        - Merets, red merets, the other wallets (Treva, Rue, valor, mentor and mentee tokens, star points, havi fruit, reverse coins, meso tokens) and the fame points are changed in one step too (CurrencyManager.ChangeMeret, ChangeGameMeret, Change; QuestManager.AddFamePoints under the points' own lock). They are changed by other threads as well: the field's (rank duel valor tokens, trigger and dungeon rewards, quests completed by a condition update, currency items given there) and another session's (a wedding hall paid by the partner). Before, each `+= / -=` read and wrote apart, so two changes at once wrote over each other. Each site keeps its sign, clamp, packet and count: the gain's condition (get_karma_token and the like) and use_merat run after the wallet's lock is let go, in the order the setter used. The wallet's lock is a leaf (the bag lock is taken before it, never after). The wedding hall checks and takes the payer's merets in one step before its save (no packet), sends the Meret update and use_merat once the hall is stored, and gives them back with the balance when it is not; before, the balance was lowered and raised again by hand around the save outside the lock, so a meret change on the payer's packet thread in between was undone, and a spend there made the take after the save throw with the hall stored. Chosen here: the give-back's Meret packet carries a change of 0, as the take was never shown. Guild funds are changed by the World under its guild lock and were already one step.
        - The payer's own meret takes check and take in one step (CurrencyManager.TryTakeMeret): the shops and the meret market, beauty, taxi, chat, premium club, pet rename, skill tab and bag, storage and style slot extends, the housing budget and cubes, blueprints, UGC uploads and banners, the revivals, party recall, smart push, the attendance gifts, maid crafts and the NPC salary. Another thread (a wedding hall paid by the partner) can take merets between the site's check and its take; before, the take (`ChangeMeret(-cost)`) then threw. Now the site answers with its own lack-of-merets error. A site with work between its check and its take (a db row, a revive, a recall, a gift) takes the merets at the check without a packet, gives them back when that work fails, and sends the Meret packet and use_merat where the take was, so the packets of a paid action stay the same. Chosen here: a negative price is refused. The wallets' token packets (CurrencyToken) are sent under the wallet's lock, so they show the balances in order; before, they went after the lock, and a later balance could be shown before an earlier one. Chosen here: a token packet now goes before the gain's condition updates. A meret give-back stopped at the cap now logs the part it drops.
            - The build budget (RequestCube 56) takes a raise of its mesos in one step too (TryTakeMeso), after its merets. Another thread can take mesos between the budget's check and its take; before, the meso take (`ChangeMeso`) then threw and the merets taken just before were kept. Now the raise is refused with the budget's error and the merets go back (with a meret packet of change 0, as the wedding hall's give-back sends).
        - The mail a full bag falls back to (AddOrMail) leaves nothing behind when its write fails: no empty mail when an item row's move fails (the row keeps its owner and place), and no item row under the deleted mail when a new item's row write fails (the delete ran on the failed request and wrote the row again).
        - Trades: each handed item's row is moved to its receiver when the trade ends (a commit add); before, it stayed the giver's until the receiver's next save, so the giver loaded the item again on a relog in between. A hand-over or a window removal the bag refuses goes by mail, and an item that cannot be mailed either is logged with its uid (its row stays with its old owner).
        - The remake scroll takes the scroll first and the lock item only when the scroll's take succeeds. An expired scroll was not checked before the take, so the lock item was taken and given back as a copy with its uid: with a stack of one, its row stayed on the bag's delete list, and once the item left the bag (stored, traded, mailed) the next save of the bag deleted it. When the lock item's take fails after the scroll's (not reached today), the scroll comes back as a new item of its id and rarity, by mail if the bag is full (chosen here: not a copy with the old uid).
        - The enchant transform's outputs get their rows from the bag's add, or from the mail when the bag's write fails; an output neither takes is logged (chosen here: the transform stands). Before, each output's row was made first with no owner and a failed write threw after the gear and the meso were taken: that output and the ones after it were lost and no result was sent; the ignored result of the bag-or-mail add left a row with no owner.
        - A tree watering reward that neither the bag nor the mail can write (only a failed write) is logged with its item; its count is already up, so it is not given again. Before, the result was not read and the reward was lost with no trace.
        - A confirmed design that neither the bag nor the mail takes (a full bag and a failed mail write) is logged. Its row is the character's already, so it is not lost: the next login places or mails it. The result was not read before.
        - The bag's delete list (the rows a bag save writes to owner 0) was never emptied, and an entry stayed when its item came back: an item taken and given back as a copy with its uid had its row written to owner 0 at each bag save, which after the item left the bag (stored, traded, mailed) took it from its new owner. An add of an item with a uid, and a full remove from the bag, now drop that uid from the list. The list keeps what a save wrote until the write is kept: an entry is taken off once the save is committed (at once without a transaction, at the commit of the session save's or purchase save's transaction; a new DatabaseRequest.AfterCommit), so a failed or rolled-back save writes it again next time. Chosen here: entries are taken off by reference, so the same item put back on the list between a save and its commit is taken off too.
        - An item taken back from the trade window is written to the bag at once (a commit add, as the hand-over does). Part of a stack offered is split off as a row of owner 0; its return kept only in memory left that row and the lowered stack, so a crash before the next save lost the piece.
        - While part of a stack is in the trade window, its row stays the giver's, at no bag slot. The trade's end writes it to the receiver (or back to the bag on a cancel). A crash before then loads the piece again with the lowered stack, so the giver has the whole amount once. Before, the row was owner 0, so the crash lost the piece. A part split off for a sale or a drop is still owner 0. An offer the window stacks onto an item already in it (its bag slot is past the window's five, or that slot is taken) is written in one save: the item's new amount, and the offered row's (owner 0 when all of it joined the stack). Before, the offered row stayed the giver's, and the giver loaded it again after the trade. A failed write takes the stack back in the window and returns the offer to the bag.
        - A row written to owner 0 at once (a commit discard, or the undo of a row made for a failed commit add) that is not written goes on the delete list, so the next bag save writes it again. When both the add's save and the undo failed, the row stayed the character's at slot -1 while the caller mailed or dropped the item as a new row, and the next load gave the item twice. Chosen here: a crash before that save still leaves the row.
        - An entry of the delete list whose item comes back by a commit add is taken off only once that add's save is kept; before, it was taken off first, so an add whose save failed (and was undone) left the row the character's with the item in neither the bag nor the list. The bag lock is not held from the session save's bag save to its commit, so an item of the list given back in that time (an add without commit) had its owner-0 write committed while the bag held it, and a crash before the next save lost it; its row is now written back to the character right after the commit. Chosen here: a crash between that commit and the write back still loses the item, and a write back that fails is logged and left to the next save.
        - Bag changes made off the owner's packet thread were checked (field rewards, a trade's hand-over, a partner's wedding hall, mini games, GM items): each goes through a bag call that holds the bag lock. Two were not safe. A discard (the delete list's add) did not take the bag lock, while the field's thread adds a currency or medal item (which discards it under the lock) and the session save reads the list; an entry added at the same time as another could be lost, and its row stayed the character's. Discard now holds the bag lock. The lapenshard equip and the partner's wedding coupon stored the bag's take (SaveConsumed) under the account lock but let the item go from the bag (ConsumeStored) after that lock was let go; a session save in between (the field's timed save) wrote the bag as it was, so a last lapenshard's row went back to the character while the config wore it, and the shard was there twice after a relog. The bag now lets the item go at the commit, still under the account lock. Packets, counts and the all-or-none save are as before.
        - A blueprint whose bag add fails after the room check goes by mail as before, and request 63 is now answered with ok 0, which closes the client's upload dialog with its own notice (CUgcManager::FUN_14188ff10); before, nothing answered it. Chosen here: no extra notice says the blueprint was mailed.
  - Not changed: the remove-helper answer (sub-type 58) ends in KMS2 with the helper's name after the account id; the client reads the error byte and the id only.
  - The interior check-in (request 40) and a housing grade reward (request 41) are answered when refused, in their own sub-types 0x28 and 0x29 (one error byte, which the client shows with its build notice, scenestategame.cpp:10635 and 10645); before, the server sent nothing, or for a check-in in another player's home the same error in sub-type 2. A check-in already made today gets `s_ugcmap_cant_take_interior_gift_more` (84), a claimed grade `s_ugcmap_already_taken_interior_grade_gift` (86), and a check-in or grade whose reward cannot be made `s_ugcmap_take_interior_gift_fail_from_db` (85). A grade above the home's decoration level is no longer given: the mastery dialog offers a grade only once the level reaches it and it is not claimed (uimasterydialog.cpp:958), so such a request gets no reward and no answer.
    - Not sent, no server path gives them: sub-types 0x20 and 0x21 close the inventory window and mute, then unmute, the notices of bag, furnishing storage and pet inventory changes (`MyPC::FUN_141530700`, read at scenestategame.cpp:2721 to 2906), a bracket around some bulk item change no KMS2 session shows; 0x32 selects a cube by uid in the build palette; 0x3c stores a value for the local character that no matched client code reads; 0x3d with error 100 shows `s_err_ugcmap_construct_exp_overtime` (no more decoration exp today), and this server gives decoration exp only by the check-in.
  - Not sent, the played client has no reader: FunctionCube sub-type 8 and HomeBlock sub-type 0 (its reader takes 2 to 6; KMS2 sessions with sub-type 0 as their second or third packet never hold a sub-type 4 list, so they are of another build).
  - EnterUgcMap (the answer to a home visit, `CSceneStateGame::OnEnterUgcMap`, scenestategame.cpp:4581) has 12 modes, each a message box but 3 and 4; the server wrote 3, 4 and 6. Mode 3 (the password box) reads int, int, int, long, int, byte and mode 4 (wrong password) int, int, int, long: the writers wrote a long where the client reads two ints (the same bytes, now written as the client reads them). Now sent as well:
    - 11 (`s_home_visit_failed_private`) for a home that is closed to the visitor; it got 6 (`s_home_visit_failed_homeless`, "has no home").
    - 9 (`s_home_visit_failed_dead`) to a dead visitor and 8 (`s_home_visit_failed_disablemap`) from a map whose `homeReturnable` is off, unless the visitor stands in a home: the client checks the same two before it sends a visit from the friend list (uicommon.cpp:385, `FUN_1418fd330`), but the home's web page and the password answer send without them (`HomeVisitRules`).
    - Chosen here: 10 (`s_home_visit_failed_myhome`) to a visit to the player's own home from inside it, in place of `s_home_returnable_forbidden_to_sameplace`; the client names no case that sends 10. Only from inside: the friend list never sends the player's own account (`FUN_1418fd330` stops without a message), but the item billboard, the home's web page and a home's portal send without that check, so a request naming the player's own account elsewhere may be the way home. Standing in a home stands in for the client's exception for a player-designed map, whose state test is not known.
    - Not sent: 0, 1, 2 and 5 answer the apartment dialog (`UIApartmentDialog`, rooms of a building: no room, no such room, expired, no such building); nothing in the client opens that dialog and the server has no apartments. 7 (`s_home_visit_failed_common`) answers the client's player state checks, which are not known.
    - Not done: apartment room entry. The broker dialog cannot be reached in this client: its constructor (0x1418b57f0) and its room setter (`FUN_1418b6840`) have no caller and no pointer, and `CUIService::RegisterDialog` does not register it (it does register the real-estate board, 0x4e76), so the client never sends a room-entry move.
  - A placed cube's expiry. The third field of a cube record (the long after the cube uid, `HeldCube`) is the cube's expiry time in the client: the build palette fills the same field from a bag item's expiry (`ugccubepalette.cpp:798`, CItem's 4th field, which the server's item writer fills with the expiry). A cube placed from furnishing storage takes the storage item's expiry, keeps it in its row (`ugcmap-cube` column `ExpiryTime`, migration `AddUgcMapCubeExpiry`; rows from before it have 0) and sends it there. 36 furnishings expire 30 days after they are got (items from 50800001, `ExpirationDuration` 2592000, the same 36 in KR and NA; neither furnishing shop sells them). Not done: what happens when a placed cube's expiry passes (the server neither removes it nor tells the client), and a cube picked up into storage gets a fresh item expiry rather than the one it kept. A cube a home template or a package places is put into storage first and takes the expiry of that new storage item; a cube placed from a blueprint (planner) or a liftable keeps no expiry (0). The row is made before the cube takes the expiry, which reaches it with the plot's next save.
  - Checked, no change: the second int of LoadUgcMap (always 0) is the apartment key by which the client finds a plot's owner together with the plot number (`CubeMapInformationService::FindCubeOwner`). The plot owner list carries each plot's apartment number, which the server always sets to 0 (the ingest and home plots), so the owner is always found.
  - LoadUgcMap of a home writes the saved layouts and blueprints (the two lists at the end of the home block) only to the home's owner. The client reads them only when the owner id is its own account id (`CSceneStateGame::OnLoadUgcMap`, scenestategame.cpp:2172); every visitor got them too, as bytes the client did not read, with the owner's layout names in them.
  - Checked, no change: FunctionCube 2 (the list at field entry) and 3 (one cube) carry the cube state and a byte that the client reads only for a cube id it holds (`CFunctionCubeService::ReadCubeState`); for any other id the record ends after the id, which in the list would shift every later cube. The client holds each placed function cube under `4_<cell>`, the id the server sends (every KR install object is an item of subtype 5, 6 or 7, object code 4), but skips tree and animated cubes placed while the home is in the decor planner (plot mode 1). A planner field is a new instance whose cubes start empty, entered only by its owner, so the list never names such a cube; an Add or Update for one leaves only its own tail unread.
- **Home maids:** a furnishing with a maid id (`<maid maidID>` in its item row) places the maid npc of that row of `table/maidproperty.xml` at the cube when the home loads and when the cube is placed, and removes it with the cube and when the plot is forfeited or expires. Only a friendly npc is placed, without its AI (it stands on the cube); the npc is spawned and removed on the field thread. The field sends the maid (FieldMaid, CMaid::ReadFromPacket) before the npc, and the npc's FieldAddNpc carries the maid uid (the cube uid), by which the client finds its maid. A new maid starts at affinity level 1 and mood 0 and is hired for `PeriodOfMaidEmployment` days (30). The client keys a maid by the uid it knows the maid furnishing by: CMaid's id (FieldMaid and UserMaid, by which CFieldMaidManager and CUserMaidManager hold it), the npc's maid uid of FieldAddNpc, and the uid the maid dialog's requests send (Maid 0x62, the npc's maid uid) are all that one uid, and the maid item tooltip looks the maid up by the item's uid. A placed furnishing is known by its entry of the placed list (FurnishingInventory, the cube uid), a stored one by its furnishing storage item uid. So the maid row (`maid` table, no longer tied to the cube table: migration `MaidKeyedByFurnishing`) is keyed by the cube uid while the furnishing is placed and moves to the storage item when the cube is picked up (UserMaid remove of the cube uid, add under the item uid), and back to the new cube uid when that item is placed again: the maid keeps its mood, affinity, contract and profile, and the stored item's tooltip shows it. Each maid item keeps a storage entry of its own (maid items do not stack; one may take an emptied entry of the same item). Deleting a stored maid item from the storage deletes its maid; so do forfeiting or buying a plot and dropping a cube from a saved plot, which delete cubes without returning them to storage. Maid rows written before this migration are all keyed by placed cubes and stay as they are. The npc keeps the cube's position and rotation when it was placed; turning or moving the cube does not move it. The owner gets the list of their maids at login (UserMaid; the maid status window, the tooltip and the craft windows read it), a new maid when it is hired and its removal with the cube, and each change of a maid (mood, affinity, contract, profile) both as UserMaid update and again as FieldMaid to the field. The maid dialog saves the profile (Maid mode 4) for the owner only and only while the maid has no name, as the client's dialog allows; a banned word in any of its 13 texts drops the save. Its debug buttons (modes 5-10: affinity level, affinity exp, reset, mood up and down, contract end earlier or later) are taken from a player with the Debug admin permission only; the mood step of 10 is the server's (the client sends none). The maid's talk script works through the script functions: `maidMoodUp` adds mood (kept in 0..100), `maidAffinityUp` adds affinity exp (each level needs the exp of its `table/maidexp.xml` row; the top level is the lower of `MaidAffinityMax` and that table's row count, where the exp stays 0), and `maidPay` takes the salary of `table/maidsalary.xml` (mesos for salary type 0, merets otherwise; nothing happens when the player cannot pay) and extends the contract by `PeriodOfMaidEmployment` days from its end, or from now when it has ended. These run for the maid's owner only, and the script condition `maid_auth` passes only for the owner. Crafting is on the next line. Not done yet: gifts to a maid (the client has no request for them); the conditions `maid_ready_to_pay`, `maid_affinity_grade` and the mood, affinity and days-before-expiry times (`maid_expired` is on the line after Maid craft at once; the two times a CMaid keeps at +0x38 and +0x40 are not known, so the server sends the hiring time for both); the counted id list and the byte at +0x4c of CMaid (sent as empty and 1). Needs a re-ingest (the maid table with salaries and affinity exp, and the items' maid ids).
- **Maid crafting (MaidCraftItem 0x68/0xA9):** a maid of the owner's home crafts one recipe at a time. The ingest reads `table/maidrecipe.xml` and `table/maidrecipegroup.xml` straight from the XML. A maid's recipes are the group whose id is its maid id (the craft dialog looks the group up by the maid id), each with the affinity level it needs. A recipe names its ingredients (an item id, or an item tag), its WorkbenchType, a lead time per mood grade, the maid exp and mood it gives and its product (item, count, rarity). Requests: 4 craft (recipe id, maid uid), 6 receive (maid uid), 10 cancel (maid uid). They run on the field thread of the maid's field, only after the maid is found still placed there and owned by the requester. A craft needs a maid that crafts nothing, a level the recipe group asks for and a running contract (else result 0x12). With WorkbenchType > 0 (28-30) the owner must have a cube with that icon code on one of their plots of the field (CubeMapInformationService); no recipe costs mesos. The ingredients are counted as the client counts them: by id, at the item's rank as rarity unless it is equipment, or by tag at any rarity. Each count is cut by the ReduceMaidRecipe rate, to at least 1. The ingredients are taken and the lead time of the maid's mood grade is set (result 1, record add); short items or no workbench answer 8. A receive after the ready time (start + lead time; 7 without a craft, 0xd before it) gives the product, raises the maid's affinity exp by the recipe's exp at the MaidExp rate and its mood by the recipe's mood. The answers are record remove (3), result 2 and the reward page (mode 11: item, count, rarity, exp, then bonus, jackpot and merat, sent as 0). A cancel answers 4 and gives nothing back. The owner gets the records of all their maids at login (mode 0). A maid picked up or placed again keeps its craft under its new uid; deleting the stored maid item deletes it. The craft is kept in the maid row (migration `MaidCraft`). Chosen here: the record's first long (read nowhere by the client) and its last int (the gauge's total) are the maid uid and the lead time. A recipe with an ingredient that is no known item tag is dropped at ingest, and a group table whose two lists differ in length on any row is dropped whole (the client fails the whole table then). Locked and expired items do not count as ingredients. The mood is added on receive, not at start. The product's socket data is not used. A full bag answers nothing; the craft waits until there is room (the client has no message for it). A maid's home can be open in several channels, each with its own copy of the maid. So craft, receive and cancel write the craft as one update that tests the stored craft (recipe and start), and only act when that update changed the row. Otherwise the request gets the stored craft back (its record, or its removal; a receive also gets its result from it, never result 2 since it gave no product), so a second receive of one craft gives no product and a second craft takes no ingredients.
