The other pet item functions the server does not handle, AddPetEXP (10 KR items) and MultiRiding, are not in the client's item function table, so the client never sends their use. CashAttendEvent and SurvivalLevelExp are not in it either, but an item with any function falls through to the plain use request (`Unk_Inventory::FUN_140a63880`, inventory.cpp:1401, calls `RequestUseItem` when the item data has a function), so their use is sent and the server serves both.
- **Pet extraction scroll:** the pet extraction scroll (item function PetExtraction, 20302314 in KR; all 127 KR pets allow extraction) makes a pet skin badge of a pet. Before, the use only logged an unhandled function, so the scroll did nothing. The client's dialog (`UIPetExtractionScrollDialog`, opened by the dispatch @0x140d21a10) takes a pet item whose pet allows extraction, shows the badge it gives (the client constant gemPetSkinBaseItemID 70100000 with that pet id) and on yes sends the scroll's use with the pet's uid as text. The server checks the pet again (`s_petextraction_invalid` for any other item or text) and the room for the badge (`s_petextraction_inventoryfull`), uses one scroll, adds the badge with the pet id and answers PetSkinBadge 0 with the pet's uid, the badge's uid and the pet id (uipetextractionscrolldialog.cpp:224). The pet is kept: the client's answer reader looks it up and shows its success notice only while it finds it.
- **Treasure map items:** the one TreasureMap item (30001025) was refused. The client appraises it in its own dialog, opened by two npc scripts (one paid, one free): it asks the cost (TreasureMap mode 2: item uid and the free flag), the server answers with ItemScript mode 2 (uid and cost; a negative cost reads "cannot appraise"), and the appraisal is a plain item use with the text "free" or none, answered with ItemScript mode 3 (success flag, item id, rank). The server now does this: the item's parameter names two individual drop boxes (`dropId` paid, `freeDropId` free), the use rolls every group of the box (the free box 330001824 has more than one), takes one map and gives every rolled item, and the answer shows the first one (the dialog shows one); without a box, with an empty roll or without room for every rolled item (checked before the map is taken) the map stays and the client gets the failure answer. KMS2 has no use of the item, and neither the client nor the data holds the cost (the client shows what the server sends), so the cost is 0, chosen here. The free flag is the client's and is not checked against the npc script, and the parameter's `groupId` is not used. No test on a client.
- **Festival items (FestivalEvent):** the festival items (4 in KR, parameters fieldID, gameEvent, itemUseType and dropItem) only logged an unhandled function. The client (item use FUN_140d1f030) checks the field, the named game event and the running Festival event's use type, then sends the use; the server now checks the same, takes the item and opens its drop box (an IndividualDropItem box, so its items go to the bag or by mail when it is full). The Festival game event is ingested (value1 and value2) and sent as the client reads it (GameEventFestival: two u8, FestivalContext m_18 and m_1c). Every KR Festival row (831001-831004) ended by 2021, so none is live today.
  - Chosen here: the item is taken before its box rolls, as OpenItemBox and OpenItemBoxWithKey do (ItemBoxManager), so a box whose every entry is filtered out (level or job) gives nothing and the item is still gone. Only the Lullu boxes roll first and keep the box when nothing comes out; making the festival box do that is left for a change to all the boxes together.
- **Blessing Charm (WeddingExpItem):** the charm (20001703) did nothing. The client has no rule of its own for it and sends the plain use. The server now adds the wedding reward table's weddingExpItem exp (350 in KR) to the marriage's exp log in the user's line (s_wedding_dialog_exp_9 the groom's, _10 the bride's, "Spouse's Blessing Amulet use") and takes the charm. The ingest had keyed the reward table by the parser's numbers, so no row sat under its server type; it is now keyed by name.
  - Chosen here: the row's limit is month, and the item's description says it can be used again 30 days after a use, so a partner's next use is refused for 30 days after their own last one, and the charm is kept.
- **Survival items:** a Royale Experience Tonic (SurvivalLevelExp, 8 items, 20301747-20301791, `<v SurvivalExp="N"/>`, 1,000 to 50,000) only logged an unhandled function. The client registers no function for it, so its item use (`UseItem`, unit_140d1ae30 @0x140d1bd60) sends the plain use. The tonic is now taken and its exp added as a game's exp is (`SurvivalRules.AddExp`, the level rewards of the levels reached), then the record (Survival 0x17) with the exp gained. Chosen here: at the last survival level, where the exp stays 0, the tonic is kept. A medal item (SurvivalSkin, 3 KR test items, group 2 type 9) becomes a survival medal when it is added and never reaches the bag. A second medal of an id held had its end (seconds since 1970) added to the held one's stored end; now a `durationSec` medal adds its time to what is left (from now once expired) and an `endDate` one keeps the later end. This changes only the stored end: the medal list (Survival LoadMedals) sends every medal's end as `long.MaxValue` and no server code reads the stored end, so a medal never runs out in play, before or after. The data gives medals a period, so they should; sending the real end and dropping an ended medal is left for a change of its own, as the KR client has no reader of the medal list to show which end it reads.
- **Item functions left as they are:** OpenWebPage (4 items): the client opens the page itself and sends nothing. ItemExtraction (2 items): the use only opens the extraction dialog, whose requests the ItemExtraction handler serves. EnchantSuperProtect (31000205, parameter rank 5, count 9 and 14): the client sends the scroll's uid with enchant mode 4 and says a failure keeps the stage; the server's failure never lowers the stage and reads no protector uid, so nothing is taken. OpenUGCEvent (20300990, the OX quiz host ticket): it opens the home OX quiz event (the 0x90 reply's sub types, the host's /ugcox commands, the promote and portal buttons), a feature of its own that the server does not have.
  - More left as they are: SurvivalScan (30001099 "Position Tracker", `<v duration="10000" isAnnounce="false"/>`): a survival match item (by its name and duration, a 10 s position track); the client sends the plain use, but no reader of a packet that would show the tracked positions is known, so nothing is sent. OpenPetNutrient (8 companion candies, 33000175-33000182, 7-day and 30-day, each with one additional effect of +20 to one stat, group 90000326-90000329, no duration): the client registers no function and sends the plain use, and neither the client nor the data says whom the effect goes to (the player or the summoned pet) or how the 7 and 30 days apply, so they still do nothing. MultiRiding (39 ride items with a skill, for example 50600208 and skill 80000208): the client uses a ride item through its skill (the ride path, not an item use), which the ride handlers serve. AddPetEXP (10 pet fusion books): the pet fusion takes their exp (`PetFusionRules.BookExp`), not a use. Client (10 items, parameters `ugcevent,camera,70x`, the OX event's top view): the client keeps them for the home OX quiz camera (unit_140dfef30), part of OpenUGCEvent above.
- **Guild quest rewards:** the guild quests (73000001 and on, `GuildQuestDaily`) give the guild exp, guild fund and guild coins of their completeReward (`guildExp`, `guildFund`, `guildCoin`, for example 120, 20000 and 8). The server gave only the player's exp: the guild rewards were a TODO. The coins are now given as the guild coin item (30000861, as the check-in gives them) with the other reward items. The exp and fund go to the World server, which adds them to the guild, and every member gets the new values (Guild 53) and the player the contribution notice (97). This exp counts for guild_exp (the line below). Chosen here: the fund stops at the guild level's fundMax, the ceiling the client's donation check keeps it under; a player without a guild gets the coins but adds nothing to a guild. A picked-up guild fund item (90000021) adds its amount to the guild fund the same way (it went to Reverse Coins before); without a guild it adds nothing.
- **Guild quest npc bonus:** a guild quest's reward gets the bonus of the guild's quest npc level, as the client's reward line shows it ("+x%", `CGuildXmlData::GetQuestRewardFactor`, questmasktable.cpp:915). `table/guildquestrewardfactor.xml` is ingested (the rows of enabled features, levels 0 to 299); the player's exp, the guild coins, the guild exp and the guild fund are each value * (1 + (factor - 1)) in float, for a factor of 1 or more. No quest npc, or a level the table lacks, gives no bonus. The `UserMeso` factor goes to the meso of a `relativeMeso` (the line below).
- **Guild quest meso:** every guild quest (the 40 daily `GuildQuest` and the 20 weekly `GuildWeeklyQuest` quests, the only quests with a `relativeMeso` in KR data) has `money="0"` and `relativeMeso`, so it gave no meso; `relativeMeso` was not ingested. The client's reward line (`FUN_1407622b0`, commonfiles/questmasktable.cpp:855) shows the meso of the reward contents row the value names (`table/rewardcontent.xml` rewardID 1003 or 1004: mesoTableID 2 or 3 of `rewardcontentmesotable.xml`, the player's level row, times mesoFactor; `RewardContentsTable::GetReward`, rewardcontentstable.cpp:213), with the guild npc's `userMesoFactor` bonus, instead of the fixed money. The quest metadata now carries `RelativeMeso` (the row id) and the server gives that meso (`RewardContentTable.RewardMeso`, `QuestMesoRules`); at level 1 a daily guild quest gives 1,000 and a weekly one 3,000. After either amount the client multiplies by the multicontrol `questRewardMeso` factor of the quest type when it is not 1; the server reads it into the constants (`QuestRewardMesoFactors`), and no pack of KR or NA has such a row, so it is 1. The quest's own meso rate (`GetMesoRate`) is 1 here, as the server sends every global rate as 1.
- **Quest mentee coins and wedding missions:** `menteeCoin` (KR: 100 on 35 quests) was ingested and never paid; the client's mentoring page shows it on the reward line (`FUN_140762be0`, commonfiles/questmasktable.cpp:1000, no bonus), and it now gives the mentee token, capped at its max and counted for get_mentee_token. `weddingExp` (5 on 119 KR quests, 118 of them in the played client: the wedding missions the wedding page lists, client/ms2/uiweddingpage.cpp:414) was not ingested. It now adds an entry to the marriage's exp log, which the marriage's intimacy sums. The client's log has two lines for it (`s_wedding_dialog_exp_11` and `_12`, the groom's and the bride's wedding mission, `UIWeddingAffinityDialog::OnLoadComplete`, uiweddingaffinitydialog.cpp:87), so each partner who completes a mission adds their own entry: both partners completing the same quest add it twice. The groom is the first stored partner, as for a filed divorce, and each partner is sent the two lines in its own order. Only a marriage counts it (also with a divorce filed); a single, engaged or divorced player gets nothing and no error. Both partners are sent the new log (the other one through the World server on another channel). Needs a re-ingest; no migration (the log is a json column).
- **Wedding trophies:** four wedding conditions that no code reported now count. wedding_complete (23100428 "Complete {0} weddings") counts for each of the couple and wedding_guest (23100446 "Attend {0} weddings as a guest") for every other player in the hall room when the vow completes (trigger WeddingVowComplete, once per ceremony). wedding_hall_change (23100425 "Change your wedding hall reservation {0} times") counts for both of the couple when the partner accepts a hall change, as wedding_hall_reserve does for a reservation. wedding_divorce (23100447 "Divorce {0} times") counts for a partner who reads the marriage divorced after holding it undivorced: the mutual divorce, the agreed one and the end of the cooling off each have both partners read it again (on another channel through the World server). A partner offline then is not counted: at login the marriage is read already divorced, with nothing to tell it from an older divorce. The client registers the four as types 0x13d, 0x13f, 0x140 and 0x141 (commonfiles/conditionutil.cpp); it reports none of them itself.
- **Guild exp condition:** the guild exp a member's own check-in, donation or other contribution adds counts for guild_exp (70300016, 74000029 "Obtain 100 guild experience").
- **Guild exp from massive events:** the trigger action give_guild_exp (13 maps: the massive event maps 61000001-61000022 when their game starts, the individual arenas Red Arena 65000001, Crimson Tear Mine 65000002 and Treasure Island 65000003, and the wedding mini game room 84000007_wd, all `type="2" box_id="0"`) did nothing. The scripts' own note on it says the type is the id of a GuildGainExp table, 2 being the massive event, and box 0 the whole map; that table is not in the data. Chosen here: type 2 pays each guild with a member in the box (or map) once, the lose (taking part) mini game exp and fund of its level's `table/guildproperty.xml` row (the level by exp, as in "Guild level and member cap": `GuildRules.PropertyAt`) (`loseMiniGameGuildExp`, `loseMiniGameGuildFund`: at level 1 2000 and 400000), since the action runs before anyone wins. As the pay is chosen, it must not be farmable: only a map whose instancefield row is of type massiveEvent pays (the 61000xxx maps, opened by the schedule, not by a player). The arenas, which a player can queue for again at once, and the wedding room have no such row, and give_guild_exp pays nothing there. The first member found asks the World server, which adds them as for a guild quest reward (the fund stops at fundMax) and sends every member the new values; other types pay nothing (tested: once per guild at its level, nothing for another type, an arena, a lobby or a wedding hall).
- **Home bank condition:** a home bank call from the home dialog counts for home_bank (70400061, 74000003 "Use the safe inside your home once"); the inventory's premium bank button does not.
- **Home invite:** the inviter got the result 3 notice ("waiting for an answer") as soon as the invite was delivered. KMS2 sends the inviter nothing then (2 of 2 delivered invites); the next packet is the accepted or declined result, so the notice is no longer sent.
- **Home open type:** the last byte of the home profile (Cube UpdateProfile, the house info's open type that the Home window shows as Access) was 0 (public) for every home and every region, so a home with a passcode showed Public while a visit asked for the passcode. It now comes from the passcode: none private, empty public, set password; a passcode change sends the profile again.
- **Home options (restrictions, background, lighting, camera, size):** each client enforces the restriction rows of the home it stands in itself (jump, climb, skills, instruments, potions, ground and air mounts, PvP: an enabled row stops everyone at setting 0, everyone but the owner at 1 and everyone but the owner and the owner's party at 2, `CubeMapInformationService::FUN_1410c3cd0`). It gets them from the home info at field entry and from ResponseCube 0x2a and 0x2b (RequestCube 42 and 43, `UIHomeOptionDialog::SetPermissionRow`; background, lighting and camera are 51, 52 and 54). The field entry reads the home from the database, but a change of the rows, the background, the lighting or the camera was saved only when the owner logged out, so a player who came in after a change got the old rows and was not held to the new ones. These changes are now saved at once. They and the size changes (RequestCube 37, 38, 44 and 45) also changed the sender's own home wherever it stood and were sent to the players of that field, so a visitor's request reached the home it visited with the visitor's settings. They are now taken from the owner only, in a field of their own home (`HomePermissionRules.MayChange`), and a row outside the client's 9 or a setting above 2 is dropped (`HomePermissionRules.Enable`, `Set`).
- **Home portals and channel change, checked against KMS2 with no change:** a channel change is answered by the move to the other channel (KMS2 also sends its reconnect context first, which stays off here, see Reconnect); a home warp, a visit with its password request and wrong password answers, and the invite accept have the layouts KMS2 sends; a home cube portal with no destination cube gets no answer, as in KMS2 (2 of 2).
- **Home dice roll and the block list:** the roll line of a home (HomeAction 11, the `roll` command) carried the roller's account id where the client expects the character id. The client drops the line when the roller is on its block list, by name and by that id (`CSceneStateGame::OnHomeAction`, the buddy map keyed by character id as for chat), so the id check never matched. It sends the character id now.
- **Dungeon reward condition:** a dungeon clear that still had a reward count left (the clear reward is given) counts for dungeon_reward (70700002, 40001940); the achievement rows that name a dungeon stay uncounted.
- **Furnishing purchase condition:** the mesos paid for a furnishing bought as it is placed count for buy_cube (74000028 "Spend 5,000 mesos placing furnishings in your house"); meret purchases do not.
- **Attribute change condition:** each paid attribute change counts for item_remake_option on gear (70600029 "Attempt to change an item's attributes through Ophelia") and pet_remake_option on a pet, the two kinds the client registers apart.
  - The change records count too: item_remake_option_record on gear (23200025 "Change the bonus attributes on a single item {0} time(s)", grades 1, 10 and 20) and pet_remake_option_record on a pet (23200039 "Re-roll a single pet's bonus talents {0} time(s)"). They count the changes of one item, so each change reports that item's change count (the item's TimeChangedOption) and the trophy keeps the highest, as the gear score trophy does; a second item's changes do not add to the first's.
- **Remake scroll without an option value:** an attribute change scroll whose row has no option value (table/itemremakescroll.xml `addOpValue` 0, `RollValueType.None`) is refused with error 11, invalid scroll data, as the client refuses it (`FUN_140616170`, itemremakescrolldata.cpp:283, after the item types, rarities and levels); before, the server used it. The value itself (1 normal, 2 the higher end) only picks the scroll's description text in the client (`UIItemRemakeScrollDialog::GetScrollDescription`), and no rule reads it, so the roll is unchanged. The KR table's 166 rows all have 1 (144) or 2 (22), so no shipped scroll is refused.
- **Pet fusion condition:** each pet taken as a fusion fodder counts for pet_enchant (70600026 "Use 3 pets as fusion catalysts"; 90000795 names the fused pet). Exp books are not pets and do not count.
- **Pet fusion exp condition:** the exp a fusion's fodders give counts for pet_enchant_exp (70600033, 70700075 "Acquire 10,000 pet experience through fusion").
- **Pet evolution conditions:** an evolution counts for pet_evolution_by_rank with the rarity reached (23100383-23100386 "Evolve normal pets to rare" up to legendary, codes 2 to 5), and the points evolve points add count for pet_evolution_point_by_rank with the pet's rarity (23100387-23100390 "Gain {0} evolution point(s) with a normal pet", codes 1 to 4). Neither was counted.
- **Pet album and housing pet conditions:** a pet new to the Pet Album (summoned for the first time) counts for pet_collect with its grade as the target (23100276 "Collect {0} pet(s) in the Album", target 1: any grade) and for pet_first_collect with its item id as the code (23100377, code 61100100); a better grade of a pet already in the album adds nothing. On a housing pet cube, the owner's feeding counts for nurturing_eat (92000118, 92000148) and a visitor's play for nurturing_play (92000095, 92000117, 92000147); either counts the growth points the pet gained for nurturing_growth (92000096 "Contribute 100 growth points"). Their code is the pet's stage-1 npc, the first id of the object's pluralNpcID (the client's pet npc by level), which the ingest now keeps with the nurturing data; a cube without it uses its item id. Re-ingest to fill it.
- **Housing pets without a feeding gift:** the ingest made a pet cube only when its object listed both rewardItem and rewardItemByFeeding, so objects 50400283 and 50800001/2/3/5/6 (Rainbow Slime and Pumpkin Devil variants, e.g. the cube of item 50400309) were plain cubes. As in the client, which reads the nurturing node of any object and takes each reward only as 3 values, a cube with ControlType Nurturing and growth stages is now a pet, and a missing reward is item 0. Feeding such a pet drops nothing and a visitor's play sends the owner no gift mail; the rest of feeding and play is unchanged. Re-ingest to fill it.
- **Maid conditions:** a received maid craft (with or without merets) counts once for maid_get_item with the product item as the code (23500009-23500013 "Obtain ... from a ... maid {0} time(s)"). A closeness level-up, from a received craft or the maid's script (maidAffinityUp), reports the level reached for maid_affinity, which keeps the highest level reported (23500014 "Raise an assistant's closeness to level {0}", 2 to 10); the debug buttons count nothing. A saved maid profile counts for maid_profile (23500015).
- **Pet taming condition:** the feeding that fills a wild pet's taming gauge counts for pet_taming (70600025, 70700073 "Pacify a hungry monster"; 90000792 names the pet).
- **Vibrate object condition:** each hit on a vibrate object counts for vibrate_object with the object's `materialTag` as the code (70400059, 74000024 "Hit a tree 20 times": code "wood"; the trees carry "wood", the rocks "stone"). The map data now keeps the tag (format 3), so the maps need a re-ingest (the `map-data` table).
- **Ranked dungeon clear condition:** a clear of a room with a rank table counts for dungeon_rank_clear with the room id as the code (93000174 to 93000182 "Clear the "Duel on the Deck" Fortress Rumble" and the other four Fortress Rumble rooms, 2409x005).
- **Item move condition:** a moved item counts for item_move only on the box the row names; the target is a box id, not a minimum, so a cube placed on a higher-numbered box no longer counts (93000061-93000063 use boxes 905, 906 and 907).
- **Dungeon rank condition:** a ranked clear counts for dungeon_rank in its room when its rank reaches the row's target (0 F to 5 S+, the client's DungeonRankRequire::GetRank on the room's rank table): 22210001 "Earn S+ Rank in the fortress rumble" (5), 22210045 "Earn A rank" (3), 93000173 "Spar with" the captain (0, any clear).
- **Design condition:** an item, furniture or mount designed from a template counts for item_design with its UGC kind as the code (70400058, 74000032 "Craft 1 template in the workshop": codes 1, 2 and 7; 90000560 "Use a template to design your own outfit" has no code).
- **Dungeon rookie helped condition:** a rookie (no veteran record of the room) who clears a room with a veteran counts for dungeon_help_beginner_helpee (70210090 "Complete a dungeon as a dungeon rookie with the aid of a dungeon veteran").
- **Monster skill hit condition:** a hit by a monster's or the map's skill counts for skill_damage_npc with the skill id as the code (70400009 "Get attacked by a seedling at the Maple Arcade" 49230005, 21233002 "Get trapped in the $npc:23000018$'s water prison" 99999981 and 36 other achievement rows).
- **Death by a monster skill condition:** the hit by a monster's or the map's skill that takes the player's last health counts for skill_die with the skill id as the code (11 achievements: 21233007 "Deadly Whirlwind" 70000500, a region skill of Bastei Peak; 21233011 "Spinning Laser" 50100394, 50100405, 50100406, skills of Devorak). Chosen here with no KMS2 sample of such an update: the client only puts skill_die in the group of skill_damage_npc. A death by a damage-over-time effect or a fall is not counted.
- **Boss assist bonus condition:** a dealer who is sent the field boss assist bonus notice counts for npc_assist_bonus with the boss as the code (20 achievements, one per field boss, 23000013 Doondun lv15 to 23000094 lv55). Chosen here with no KMS2 sample: the one assist bonus KMS2 sends in our sessions is for a boss no achievement names.
- **Auto fishing condition:** a catch made while auto fishing is on (the auto flag of the catch packet) counts for auto_fishing (23100181 "Sleepfishing", 10/100/1000, no code). Chosen here with no KMS2 sample: the client puts it in the fishing group, whose other conditions count catches; a count per auto fishing purchase is the other reading.
- **Epic chain conditions (tests only):** the KR epic chain's runtime conditions from lv10 to lv40 are checked against their own rows: the 34 trigger codes (60100010 AudienceWithEreb to 50001675 ClearPP, each sent by its map's set_achievement), item_move 30000119 into liftable target 600 on 02000084 (60100045, the box keyed by its block), emotion hello x3 on 02000076 (50001557), swimtime 20 on 52000133 (50001583), dungeon_clear 25004001 counted once (50001601), and the quest script move of 50001603 (scripts 104 and 200, function 1) to 52000136 portal 1. All pass; no server change was needed. ServerTableMapper.MergeQuestScriptFunctions is public so the test can feed it rows.
  - Chosen here: the rows are copied into the test (no data folder or database read); the item_move target test stays the server's >= on the target number, which the one chain row (600) meets.
- **Jump and fishing map conditions:** a jump is reported with its map as the code, so a jump quest that names a map (7 KR quests, the daily missions 70400081 and 70400103 among them) counts jumps on that map only; before, any jump counted. The target of fish and fish_big is the map of the catch and must be that map: all 27 KR quest targets and 5 trophy targets of the two are map ids, and they were read as a minimum, so a catch on any map with a higher id counted. No lv13-30 quest of the KR data (94 epic, 18 world, 375 daily missions; alliance missions start at lv50) has a condition type that is never reported or whose code cannot match. stay_cube is a time condition (the client's condition table groups it with playtime, stay_map and survive_cube, and the daily mission 70300068 asks for 60 on grass): each second on a cube counts one, in any state, with the map as the code and the cube's material as the target; before, it counted walked metres on grass, snow or glass only. The second is taken at the once-per-second lookup of the block under the player, which runs on the client's state syncs. survive_cube is the same kind of time condition (the client shows both as seconds over 60): each second alive in a liquid counts one, with the map as the code and the liquid as the target (trophy 22210016 asks for 60 in lava; lava is a fluid cube, which stay_cube skips). It is taken in the same once-per-second lookup, only on fields that have a fluid cube. Chosen here: the liquids are those of the player's cell and of the cell under the feet, so standing in lava counts as well as swimming in it, and the count is a plain sum (a death adds nothing and resets nothing). The code of music_play_instrument_time is the instrument's category, not its id: the music_play_instrument_mastery trophies name the categories 1 to 30 (instrument ids go to 96) and that condition is reported with the category. Chosen here for both, no KMS2 sample.
- **Mission point condition:** a completed quest counts the mission points of its reward for mission_point (70900001 to 70900003 and 70900011 to 70900013, 100 to 900 points; the daily missions give 30 each).
- **Club join condition:** becoming a member of an established club counts for club_join (89050036 "Join a club"): on accepting an invite, and for the leader and each founding member when a staged club is established. Counting the founders is chosen here, as for guild_join: the client only registers the type.
- **Mini-game clear condition:** the reward a player-opened mini-game gives its winners (trigger MiniGameGiveReward of UserOpenMiniGameExtraReward) counts for useropen_minigame_clear with the field as the code (70200061 "Complete any Mini-game and claim your reward", fields 81000001 to 81000003). The players in the winner box of the EndMiniGame action, who still have their game record, count for minigame_clear with the field as the code (trophies 23400007, 23400008 and 23400039 name the fields 61000004, 61000006 and 61000010); KMS2 sends the 23400008 update as the player reaches the Crazy Runners goal, just before the winner buff (3 sessions). Chosen here: the report sits in EndMiniGame and not in the round end or the reward action, which the scripts of these fields run in the same step for the same box.
- **Mini-Game M extra reward:** the extra reward of a player-opened mini-game (MiniGameGiveReward of UserOpenMiniGameExtraReward, reward content 1008) was paid on every clear. It is now paid at most userMassiveExtraRewardMax times a day (constant 5; the three fields' scripts say "5 extra bonuses a day"), counted per character with the day's reset like the pet fusion count, and once per game: only a player who was in the box at StartMiniGame has a game record, leaving the field drops it, and the record keeps that its extra reward was given (other reward content is paid per call as before; all 15 MiniGameGiveReward actions in the scripts come right before an EndMiniGame on the same box, which drops the record, so no game pays twice). The count is stored before the reward; when reward content 1008 has no row, nothing is counted and no notice is sent. After a paid clear the player gets PlayerHost 5 (the "you got a bonus Mini-Game M reward" notice); a game start with rewards left sends PlayerHost 4 ("get a bonus reward by clearing"). Both read two uints a and b and print a and b - a as the daily count and the ones left, so the server sends the maximum and the maximum plus the ones left. Chosen here: when mode 4 is sent (at the game start), as no capture shows it. The useropen_minigame_clear update stays on every clear, past the cap too.
- **Mini-game round start:** start_mini_game_round sent MassiveEvent 0 (the round banner) for every player with a game record. KMS2 sends nothing there: the banner comes only from set_event_ui_round, once per round (Trap Master: one banner and one countdown per round, 21 s apart), and the fields whose scripts never call set_event_ui_round (61000004, 61000006, 61000010) show no banner at all. The client replays the banner on every MassiveEvent 0, so the fields with both actions showed each round twice. The action now sends nothing.
- **Event banners without a box:** set_event_ui_script sent its banner (MassiveEvent 2) only to the boxes of its box_ids; 949 of the 1,526 banner actions in the scripts name no box and went to nobody (two more have an empty box_ids, which failed the action). KMS2 sends those to every player of the field (Trap Master: the three opening banners, whose actions name no box). No box, an empty one and box 0 now mean the whole field; "!id" stays everyone outside the box.
- **Round banner offset:** set_event_ui_round passed its v_offset and arg3 in swapped order, so the fourth int of MassiveEvent 0 (which the client hands to the banner's setStage) was always arg3, 0 in every script, and the v_offset 120 of the ten round banners of 51000001 was dropped. No KMS2 packet of that field was seen; the value is the script's.
- **Negated box list:** the 51000001_dg tutorial (Spring Farm) writes user_detected with box_ids "!100" in 2 conditions, where every other arcade script ends a round with the negated condition name on the box; the box list parse threw on it, so the tutorial trigger did not load. A box list starting with "!" is now the negated test on the boxes after it, as the scripts' "!" means "not in the box" in event UI box lists (and the client's trigger reader turns a "!" before a condition name into its not flag, `TriggerConditionCli::Create`, `triggeractioncli.cpp:273`). A test parses the tutorial and every action and condition form of the 51000001-51000006 triggers.
- **Trophy count condition:** each grade a trophy reaches counts for trophy_point with the trophy id as the code (89040018 "Obtain 10 trophies", trophy 23100112; id range 20000000 to 29999999).
- **Decoration score condition:** a cube placed in a home reports the home's decoration score for interior_point to the owner (89050037 "Raise Decoration Score", trophy 23500024 "Reach 1,100 decoration score"): the counter is the score itself. The counters of this type keep the highest score instead of adding (chosen here). Not counted: meso_donation (93000073 "Donate mesos"), since nothing in the KR server or client data says where mesos are donated; donation_type is counted by the donation box (see Green Hoods item donation).
- **Play time, shop and sitting trophies:** three counts no code reported are now reported. Play time (trophy 23100001) counts 1 for each full minute logged in, across maps; KMS2 sends one step every 60 seconds. Mesos paid in an NPC shop count for shop_buy (23200004), the price of each purchase; Treva and Valor Tokens count the same way for shop_buy_karma_token (23200014, "spend Treva") and shop_buy_honor_token (23200012, "spend Valor Tokens"): the client names currency 4 karma and 3 honor (its take messages, client/ms2/uiworldshareinfoeventlistener.cpp:264), the ones the shop pays as Treva and ValorToken. The other currencies do not count; their shop_buy conditions (lu, habi, reverse coin, mentor, mentee, star point) have no KR trophy or quest. Time in an emotion counts for emotiontime by the emotion's name (23100016: Sit_Ground_A, Sit_Chair_A), read from the emotion skill id of the player's state record; it is reported once five or more seconds are due, as KMS2 sends 5 every 5 seconds.
- **Kill streak trophy:** each monster kill now reports the player's kill streak for killcount (trophy 21210014, grades 10, 50, 100, 500 and 1000 kills in a row), and the trophy keeps the longest streak, so it sends an update only when a streak passes it. No code reported this condition, and the chain kill counter was never called. The streak follows the client's Lua calcKillCount (the rule behind its kill count display, UIKillCount): a kill 5 s or more after the last one starts again at 1; a sooner kill adds 1 when the monster is level 50 or above or within 5 levels of the player, and leaves the streak as it is otherwise. The server's copy of that function had both of the other cases wrong (it kept the streak after 5 s and reset it on a level gap). KMS2 sends the streak's length the same way: in 13 sessions all 520 kills that kept a streak came under 5 s after the kill before (longest 4.97 s) and all 20 that broke one came later (shortest 5.98 s). Chosen here: every dealer of the kill counts it, as for the kill trophies, and the streak carries over a map change. Not done: the streak's bonus exp rate (calcKillCountBonusExpRate, 1% from 21 kills to 15% over 100); the KMS2 kills past a streak of 20 gave 12 exp each, too little to show 1%.
- **Meret, home and first clear trophies:** three trophy conditions that no code reported now count, each as KMS2 sends it. use_merat (trophy 23200041, 500 merets): every meret balance change that takes merets off adds the amount spent; in KMS2 sessions all 9 meret packets with a negative change were followed at once by this trophy's update, up by that amount (a 100 meret purchase moved it from 88412 to 88512), also when the merets came off the second balance of the packet. home_goto (trophy 23500019, account-wide, 1, 10 and 100 times): going to your own home adds 1, reported just before the move and only when the move is made (a refused move counts nothing, and neither does the guide's go-home step sent while already standing in the own home); KMS2 sends the update right before the migrate packet in all 6 of its updates, 4 after the go-home request and 2 after the server sent the player home with no request, and none of the 16 other moves to a home map (among them visits, the designer, the model home and an invite) had one. dungeon_first_clear (6 trophies, each listing 2 or 3 dungeon ids): the first clear of a listed dungeon adds 1, reported right after the dungeon record update that shows 1 clear, as in all 4 KMS2 updates. Chosen here: game merets count for use_merat like merets (KMS2 has no sample of a game meret purchase), and the guide's go-home step counts for home_goto as one of the moves without a request; every meret balance change that takes merets off counts as spent, so a negative GM currency command counts too and a fee that is later given back stays counted (KMS2 has no sample of either).
- **Trophy grade, guide quest and home decoration trophies:** four more conditions a level 13 to 30 player meets are now reported. They were picked by how often KMS2 sends updates for conditions this server never reported: hero_achieve_grade 25 updates in 21 sessions, quest_revise_achieve 16 in 5, interior_level 9 in 3 and interior_exp_offset 6 in 6; the other unreported conditions with more updates belong to level 50 content. hero_achieve_grade (trophies 21240001, 21100019, 23100290): each grade reached on a trophy adds 1 for the trophies whose code range or list holds that trophy, reported after the grade's trophy_point update; in KMS2 14 of 14 grades reached on a trophy in 21240001's range were followed by its update. quest_revise_achieve (22210017, 22300102): completing a quest on the trophy's code list adds 1, after quest_clear; every KMS2 update follows the completion of a listed quest. interior_exp_offset (23500023): a home check-in adds the decoration exp the home took from it, as in KMS2 where the counter rises by exactly the home's exp gain (450, 600, 950). interior_level (23500020 to 23500022): a home check-in reports the home's decoration level and the trophy keeps the highest, sent after the exp update; the grades are the levels 1 to 10. KMS2 trophies end at the home's level (2 at level 2, 4 at level 4), but KMS2 gets there in steps of 1: the one check-in that raised a home from level 1 to 3 moved the counter to 2 only, and on another account the third grade came 188 seconds after the second, by a step no session shows. Chosen here: report the level at once instead of in steps, so no grade is left unreachable and a home older than this change catches up at its next check-in; a new home reports level 1 when it is created, because KMS2 accounts hold the first grade before their first check-in and no session shows when it was given; a home name may not be empty, since an empty name would create the home again at level 1. Also chosen here: quest_revise_achieve does not remember which quests it counted, so on the account-wide 22210017 the same guide quest completed by a second character counts again (not seen in KMS2 either way); interior_exp_offset adds the whole check-in score when the home took any of it, including the check-in that reaches level 10 where the home keeps no exp, and the GM home exp and level commands report nothing; 23100290 counts every grade reached on its four listed trophies, so four grades of one trophy finish it (KMS2 shows updates for two of the listed trophies only, never such a case), and a grade on a trophy outside the Combat, Adventure and Life categories reports no hero_achieve_grade (not seen in KMS2).
- **Locked trophies:** a trophy with locking="1" in the achieve XML no longer counts (181 of the played KR client's 2101 achieve files have it, before the feature and locale test). The client shows such a trophy locked (UIAchieveDialog::IsAchieveLocked). The KMS2 evidence is thin: the one first clear of a dungeon listed by a locked dungeon_first_clear trophy (22200455) sent no update while the 4 for unlocked trophies did, and of 36,887 trophy updates over 340 trophies in its sessions 1 is for a locked trophy (21100009, a job change trophy). That one update does not show a count from play: it came during the login of a debug client session, with no job change before it, and carried a counter of 10 for a condition that asks for 1. So the rule is chosen here from the one withheld update and the client's display, and that one update stands against it. The ingest now keeps the attribute (AchievementMetadata.Locked), so this needs a new ingest (the achievement table gains a Locked column). With a played client pack given, that pack's flag stands alone for every trophy it has a row for (one that passes the feature and locale test), so a trophy the data pack locks and the played client shows open counts; the data pack's flag is used only for a trophy the played client has no row for. A KR ingest has 157 trophies locked from the data pack alone and 164 with the played KR client pack (which has 1,900 trophies), 22200455 and 22200457 among the added ones; no trophy the data pack locks is open in that client. Chosen here: a trophy with several rows is locked only when every row that passes the feature and locale test is; a locked trophy a character already has keeps its progress and is still listed. The GM command `player trophy <id>` (GM accounts only, as every command) now says so: for a locked trophy it answers that the trophy is locked and takes no progress, and for a trophy that has no row after the command that its condition was not met; both were a success without a line before. On success it prints the trophy's grade and counter.
- **Trophy claim with nothing due:** KMS2 gives no answer to a claim (Achieve 3) of a trophy whose reward grade has no reward due; the trophy was sent again (Achieve 2). A claim that gives a reward is answered as before (a title: UserEnv 0, then Achieve 2). The rest of this pass matched KMS2: the login list (Achieve 0, then Achieve 1 in parts of 60), no answer to the favourite toggle (Achieve 4), and the navigator's claim (Quest 0x18) answered with the quest's complete packet and then the items.
- **World champion trophies (parked):** worldchampion_damage (80000005-80000008) is never raised. The server runs no world champion season (WorldChampion 8 answers with the client's no season notice; every season in WorldChampionBase.xml has ended), so there is no raid damage to count.
- **Limited bundle trophies (parked):** limited_bundle_buy (the account trophies 92000104-92000107, 92000112-92000115 and 92000151, "Buy the ... Package") is never raised. A limited bundle is one of the meret market's advertised premium products: the client gets them only in the answer to MeretMarket 103 (`CMeratMarketMan::OnAdProducts`, client/ms2/meratmarketman.cpp:2129), lists them in the market's limited tab and pushes them to the ad dialog (`UIAdPushDialog::AddBundle`). The trophies' codes (3000147-3000150, 3000153-3000156, 3000194) are those products' ids, which no table of the client or server data holds (shop_merat, shop_meratGroup and shop_merat_custom have none of them), and KMS2 answered every ad list request with an empty list (see Known not to work), so there is no bundle to buy.
- **Metadata database older than the server:** the Game, World, Login and Web servers now stop at start with "Metadata database is older than this server" and the table names when the metadata database lacks a table or a column the code reads (one statement per table that names every mapped column and reads no row). Before, the server started and each query of such a table failed: with the trophy table of an ingest from before the Locked column that is the login of every character that has a trophy (its trophies are read then) and every count of a trophy condition. A new ingest writes the columns: the ingest recreates the database when the model's tables changed since it was written, and also when this same check finds a table or column missing while the model is unchanged (a column dropped by hand, a restored older dump); before, such a database kept its schema version, a plain ingest left it as it was and only `--drop-data` repaired it. Known limit: the check sees missing tables and columns only. A change in how a value is written inside an existing column (new move types in a JSON column, for example) is not seen by the server or the ingest; such a change needs a re-ingest, as its own bullet says.
- **Trophy list at login:** each row of the list now carries the trophy's current grade where a constant 1 was sent. The client sets up the trophy's condition and the goal of the grade before from that grade (`CAchieve::Init`), and KMS2 sends the current grade.
- **Trophy reward claim:** a claim gives the reward of one grade, as in KMS2, and the reward grade then moves over the finished grades whose reward needs no claim; the client gets one update. Before, one claim gave every due item and title.
- **Dismantle condition:** each item broken at the dismantle counts for item_break, by the amount broken (74000011 "Dismantle 1 item", 10002700 dismantles 10).
- **Green Hoods item donation:** the donation box (interact 10001313 on map 02000422, open during the Green Hoods missions 93000071, 93000072 and 93000149) opens the dismantle dialog with type 2, the client's UIItemBreakDonationDialog. It was handled as a dismantle: the items gave onyx and counted for item_break. Now, in type 2, an item is staged (by hand or by auto-add) only when CItemBreakManager::TryAddItem would take it: CanBreakItem, and an item of type 1 (CItemData::FUN_14053cbc0) or with a skin type (CItem::HasSkin); any other is refused with nothing staged, and the confirm checks the staged slots again, so such an item is never taken. The confirm takes the items and gives nothing, with no room check: the dialog's result handler reads no reward list, and no reward preview is sent (the client gives it to the break dialog only). Each donated item counts for donation_item (trophy 22500042 "Philanthropist") and for donation_type with the item's type as code (the missions' code "1,0,0"), by the amount donated, and never for item_break. meso_donation (93000073 and the alliance missions 93000139 to 93000142) is still not counted: no meso donation is in the data.
- **Item sockets on outfits:** the socket pages of the item socket dialog list items from inventory tabs 0 and 1 (gear and outfit) and the equipped gear and outfits (`GetItemsInCategory` 0 / 1 and `CollectShownItemsInLayer` 0 / 1, ItemSocketSystemPageSocketUnlock and the other pages), so an outfit with sockets (e.g. 11200084, skin type 2, socket row 9001) is one of their items. The server looked only in the gear tab and the equipped gear, so the unlock stage (0x88 mode 2) of an outfit got no answer and the page stayed waiting. Unlock, its stage, gem equip, unequip and upgrade now find the item in the gear or outfit tab or among the equipped gear and outfits (`ItemManager.GetSocketItem`), and the unlock takes its ingredient items from both tabs. A stage for an item the server cannot find answers s_itemsocketsystem_error_invalid_target, the message the client shows when it cannot find the item itself; any reply ends the page's wait.
- **Gemstone socket conditions:** a gem put into or taken out of a socket also counts for equip_gemstone_puton / equip_gemstone_putoff on equipment and skin_gemstone_puton / skin_gemstone_putoff on an outfit, told apart by the item's skin type as the client's socket code does (70600031 "Insert a gemstone into a socket" is equip_gemstone_puton).
- **Socket transfer (ItemSocketSystem 12):** the open, empty sockets of one outfit move to another outfit with the same socket count and none open; it was read and not answered. The rules are the client's (itemsocketsystemutil.cpp): the source (FUN_140627e80) and the target (FUN_140628050) both need a skin type, an equip subtype the socket system takes (`CItemData::FUN_14053e940`: 12-18, 20 and 22 on an outfit), sockets, no gem, no expiry unless the item has a socket row, and `CItemTransfer::CanTransferSocket`. That asks the item's socket row to open fewer sockets than it holds, or the item to have no socket row (`CItemSocketTable::FUN_14062d980`), and the transfer block's socket transfer flag (m_b42, the byte the server always sent as 1). The source also needs no gacha dismantle id. The source comes from the outfit tab or the equipped outfits, the target from the outfit tab only, as the page lists them. The cost is `GetSocketTransferCost`: 200 of client tag 0x58 (SkinCrystal) for each open socket. A bad pair answers s_itemsocketsystem_error_invalid_target and a short balance s_itemsocketsystem_error_lack_price. The client has no transfer code in its error list, and checks both itself first. The answer is mode 13 with both socket blocks and the target's transfer block. The target's flag is cleared, so it cannot pass the sockets on (chosen here: the source keeps its flag; KMS2 sends the flag as 0 on many fully socketed outfits, which the socket row already keeps from a transfer). The flag is kept in the item row; a row saved before reads it as 1. Mode 17 is the GM debug transfer and is not run.
  - The subtype the rule asks is the client's (`CItemData::FUN_14053e940` reads the client's item data), which for equipment is the two id digits after the first (GearScoreRules.SubType, as the gear score uses). The server pack gives most equipment subtype 0 or 16: the Northern Fur Earrings 11200084 have 0 there and 12 in the client, so the client offered them and the server refused the pair with invalid_target, which the client shows as "The selected target is not in your inventory.". The ingest still keeps the server pack's subtype: taking the client's for every item would also move the emotes and the fishing and music items out of the tabs the server picks from that subtype.
- **Transfer flags at item creation:** a new item now gets the client's socket transfer flag (m_b42) from its socket row, as `CItemTransfer::InitFromItem` sets it: m_b42 = `CItemSocketTable::FUN_14062d980` of the item's own socketDataId row at the item's grade, else grade 0. An outfit with its own 9001-9008 row (12220268 included) starts able to pass its sockets on. The client reads no drop row there (drop items are made by the server). Chosen here: when the sockets come from a drop row's socketDataID, the same rule is asked of that row, so an outfit dropped with rows 1002 or 1006 (sockets already open) starts unable to pass them on. Before, every item started with the flag set. A socket row's openType is ingested: with openType 1 the client opens no socket at creation (`GetInitialSocketCount` reads fixOpenCount only for openType 0), and the server now does the same, also in the transfer check. The socket system (feature 0x2e, ItemSocketSystem) and the transfer page (0xa7, ItemSocketTransfer01) are ingested from the feature table; with either off a transfer is refused, and with 0x2e off no outfit can give its sockets. KR has both on and no openType 1 row. The no-dismantle flag (m_b3F, the transfer block's byte before m_b42) is now sent, saved and read back: a drop row's disableBreak sets it, and an extraction result gets it with no socket transfer, as the client's preview shows. The dismantle follows `CItemBreakManager::CanBreakItem`: a gacha dismantle item passes; any other needs enableBreak and no m_b3F. This needs a re-ingest (openType and the two features).
- **Pet capture conditions:** a pet captured with a trap now counts for pet_catch_id with the pet's item id as the code (the Kritias fauna quests from 94000006, "capture $item:61000153$"), and for pet_catch_grade with its rarity (70600034 "Capture a rare pet", code 2). The rarity is still the server's own pick (taming rank / 25 + 1). It also counts for pet_catch_category with the pet's `petproperty` `collectGroupId` (70600035 "Capture $petCollectGroup:2$ pets"); the pet ingest now keeps that group, so this one needs a re-ingest (before it, the group is 0 and no row counts).
- **Alliance missions (daily and weekly alliance quests):** the client starts a daily alliance quest (type 9, `repeatable` 2), or a weekly one without a `groupID`, only when it is in the list of Quest 0x1F (daily) or 0x20 (weekly) (`CQuestManager::vfunc_1`, client/ms2/questmanager_2.cpp:555; the reader at :1145), and the server never sent either list, so the alliance quest window offered none of them. The server now sends both lists at login, again at the daily reset, and again when the quest a method names in `reloadOnAccept` or `reloadOnComplete` is started or completed. The lists come from `table/famepickmethod.xml`, read as `CFameTable::LoadPickMethod` (commonfiles/famegradedata.cpp:571) reads it: the `<environment>` of the server's locale, or else the one without a locale, and of it the methods whose feature is on. A method of type `RankAllianceCombination` picks each rank's `pickCountByRank` from its alliances together, an alliance at most its `maxCount`; any other type picks each rank's count for each alliance. Only quests the character can start are picked, and the missions of the list's repeat already started in its period (done or not) are picked first and use up the counts, so a level up or a new gear score within the period does not add missions past the counts. The quest ingest now keeps a quest's `alliance`, `rank` and `groupID`, and `table/famedef.xml` gives the officer NPC kind of each alliance. When a list's period ends (the day; the week from the `usePeriod` weekday), the held missions of that list without a `groupID` that are not completed are deleted (the client's `RemoveQuestsByTypeAndState` keeps the ones with a group), and the list is sent with its drop flag set so the client removes them too (`RemoveQuestsByTypeAndState`); at login such held missions are deleted before the quest list is sent. A started mission must be in the list (Quest error 3 otherwise; the GM bypass skips it). The reset runs on the session's own queue, and takes the period that starts within two hours of it as the new one, so a reset timer that fires a moment early, or an hour early after a clock change, still starts the new day. A completed quest is no longer deleted by Quest 7 (expired) or Quest 2 (abandon): Quest 7 drops only a quest in progress that has an end time (a `usePeriod` weekday or minutes, or an `eventTag`; `FUN_14074b090`), and Abandon refuses a completed quest, so the completed row keeps a mission from being started and rewarded twice in a period. Chosen here, as the client does not show how the original server picked: each list is a shuffle of all the missions of its repeat, seeded by the character, the start of the period and the method, so it stays the same through the day or week and across logins with no stored state. A data ingest is required: the Release server does not start on data ingested before this change (the fame table has no row), and the quest fields it reads (`alliance`, `rank`, `groupID`, the `usePeriod` minutes) are new; no migration.
- **Alliance mission fame and mastery grades:** a quest's `<require alliance="..." fameGrade="N">` and `<require masteryType="N" masteryGrade="N">` were not ingested, so the server picked alliance missions of any grade for the daily and weekly lists; the client does not accept a quest whose alliance grade (`GetGradeByPoint` of the alliance's fame points) or mastery grade (`CMasteryTable::Grade`) is below it (`CQuestManagerCommon::CanAcceptQuest`, commonfiles/questdata.cpp:576), so a grade-1 player's lists were mostly dead rows. In KR 408 live alliance missions need a fame grade (191 daily and 122 weekly, the KritiasExped sets spread over grades 1 to 5) and 32 daily ones a mastery grade. The quest ingest now keeps the four fields, and starting a quest checks them (a 0 alliance, type or grade checks nothing), so the lists pick only missions the player can take, accept all (Quest 34) skips the others, and a plain start of one is refused. A mission started in the period stays held: it is kept in the list until the period ends, as the client's list is fixed for it, and uses up its rank's count; one that was listed but not started is left out at the next login, since the client would not accept it. A grade reached within the period shows in the lists at the next login or reset. Needs a re-ingest; no migration.
- **Navigator and level-guide reward claims (Quest 0x18):** the reward button of the daily mission page and the level guide (`UIDailyMissionDialog::SendQuest`, client/ms2/uidailymissiondialog.cpp:321; `UILevelUpGuideDialog`, uilevelupguidedialog.cpp:306 and :380) sends Quest 0x18 for a quest the client holds as complete. The server completed it when it held the quest and its conditions were met, and otherwise sent nothing, so the button did nothing and no line was logged. A refused claim is now logged with its reason (no quest data, not held, completed, not in the played client, conditions unmet, inventory full), and the client gets the quest error (Quest 0, `ShowQuestErrorMessage`, questmanager_2.cpp:1187). A `level` condition (no codes, no party count) is now counted when the quest starts if the player is already at or above its level, as the level-up counts it; before, a navigator mission started at level 10 or above stayed at 0 until the next level-up.
  - Chosen here: the refusal answer is error 2 (`s_quest_error_consume_fail`), as the client names no claim error; an inventory-full claim gets only the inventory error Complete already sends, and a claim of a completed quest (a second click) gets no answer.
  - Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Alliance mission accept:** the alliance window's accept (NpcTalk 8 with mode 0, `UIAllianceQuestDialog` onAccept) started the quest at once, and the window kept the row on "Accept" until it was opened again: the window fills its rows only when it opens, and a quest start does not refresh it. The client's own answer to NpcTalk 8 is the NpcTalk 8 reply (`UITalkManager::OnTalkPacket` mode 8: talk type, quest id, script state, page, button, read as mode 2), which loads the quest's talk and opens the talk scene (`ReloadScript`); the talk's accept button sends Quest 2 as any quest talk does, and the window is opened again when the talk ends. The server now answers with the quest script's first start state (100 to 199) and starts the quest on that Quest 2. A quest without a start script is still started at once. Complete (mode 2) is unchanged: the quest's completion closes the window.
  - Chosen here: the start state is the script's first state from 100 to 199 whatever the held quest's state, as the window offers only missions that can start.
- **Alliance fame points:** the alliance mission window's reputation (`GetFamePoints`, the grade line and the points) never moved, because the server kept no fame points and never sent them; the window showed 0 after a mission whose reward said +700. The client fills them only from UserEnv 10 (all, a count and per alliance a short id and int points) and 11 (one alliance), `CSceneStateGame::OnUserEnv`; the unused UserEnv writer sent mode 10 with the shop's reputation numbering, which is not the client's alliance id. Completing a quest whose complete reward sets `useMainFamePoint` now adds to the quest's alliance the points the client shows as the reward: Lua `calcQuestRewardFamePoint(rank, grade, useMainFamePoint, alliance)` (alliances 1 to 5: 2000/1500/1000/800/700 for S to D, doubled with a flag of 2; otherwise 230/200/140/100/90, ten times with a flag of 2), and sends UserEnv 11 before the quest's completion; login sends UserEnv 10. The points are kept per character in the new `FamePoints` column of `character-unlock` (`AddFamePoints`), and `useMainFamePoint` is now ingested, so this needs a re-ingest and the migration; until the re-ingest no quest gives fame.
  - Chosen here: no upper limit (kept at int.MaxValue); the client's grade table is not used to cap; quests without an alliance give none.
- **Alliance fame in every locale:** completing a rank C mission of a main alliance (93000001) moved the reputation by 100 while the reward line said +800. The server's `calcQuestRewardFamePoint` gave the large table only in KR and CN, as the upstream server script does; the client's own script (`luapack`) has no locale test, so the client shows 2000/1500/1000/800/700 for alliances 1 to 5 in every region. The server now uses the client's script, so the points match the reward line in NA too.
  - Chosen here: the client's script, not the upstream server script, is followed; no re-ingest or migration is needed, and points already given are left as they are.
- **Fame log (fame board history):** the fame board's history stayed empty, because the server kept no fame log and never sent one. The client fills it only from UserEnv 12 (all: a count, then per entry an int log id and a long time in epoch seconds) and 13 (one entry, with the notice `s_fame_point_update`), `CSceneStateGame::OnUserEnv`; the board lists the entries whose `table/famelog.xml` row has the shown alliance, with the text of the log id and the date of the time (`UIFamePointBoardDialog`). In KMS2 a character's list holds no log id twice, every log is of a grade its alliance has reached (never above the alliance's current grade), and UserEnv 13 follows UserEnv 11 after 2 of 19 fame gains, each time with a log of the alliance that gained and of its grade at that time. The ingest now reads the log rows (`id`, `alliance`, `grade`) and the grade rows of `table/fame.xml` (`alliance`, `grade`, `requirePoint`) with the client's feature and locale filter into the fame table; the character keeps its logs (migration `AddFameLogs`, a json column of the character's unlock row), the list is sent at login after UserEnv 10, and a fame gain from a quest may add one log.
  - The client keys its list by the time (`uifamepointboarddialog.cpp:163`), so two logs with one time show as one line. A new log's time is the clock's or, when that is not above every held time, the highest held time + 1.
  - Chosen here: one fame gain in ten adds a log (the KMS2 share; what decides it there is not known); the log is a random one of the alliance's rows at its grade after the gain (the client's `GetGradeByPoint`) that the character does not hold yet, none when all are held; the time is the server's clock. Needs a re-ingest and the migration; characters start with an empty list.
- **Fame grade trophies:** the 44 trophies of the `fame_grade` condition (22500001 and on: the Sky Fortress ones and an alliance's grade trophies, such as Militia Volunteer 22500019) never counted, because nothing reported the condition. Their code is the alliance, by the client's fame numbering (22500001-04 take any of alliances 1 to 5, 22501001-04 any of the Kritias three 6 to 8), and their target the grade, 2 to 5; the client takes a condition's fame grade from the points (`CQuestManager::vfunc_o10_13`, questmanager_2.cpp:501: `GetGradeByPoint` of `GetFamePoints`, commonfiles/famegradedata.cpp:640). Each fame change (`AddFamePoints`) now reports every grade from 1 to the alliance's grade after it, so a gain that passes two grades counts both; the condition keeps its highest count, as `item_gear_score` does, so a grade already counted sends no update again. The client does not re-evaluate the condition at login or when the trophies load (`CAchieveManager::OnPacket`, achievemanager.cpp:499, only takes the server's state), and the server does not report it at login either: a character that held the fame before this change gets its grade trophies at its next fame gain. The four Resistance trophies (22501048-51, code 9) are left out: the server has no alliance 9 and the fame table has no grade rows for it. No re-ingest.
- **Sky fortress move (Quest 0x0E):** the move to the sky fortress needed `FameContentsRequireQuestID` completed. The client sends it only after `CanEnterSkyFortress` (questmanager_2.cpp:1723): the quest the sky fortress season requires (`GetSeasonRequireLvBySeason(1)`, famegradedata.cpp:855: famedef.xml `<season name="SkyFotress" requireQuest>`, 91000013 in KR) is rewarded, the player is not on the target field already, and off the home field 62000000 the player is not dead (`FUN_1409312f0`, accountsessionstate.cpp:971). The seasons' require quests are now ingested into the fame table and the server checks all three (a dead player's 0x0E or 0x0F is refused without an answer). A table stored before them falls back to the constant (the same quest in KR). Chosen here: the session flags `FUN_1409312f0` also tests (the client's own load state) are left to the client. Needs a re-ingest for the season table.
- **Sky fortress mission attack (Quest 0x0F):** the world map's mission attack was only logged. The client sends it (`SendQuestMode15`, questmanager_2.cpp:1766) when the move to that field is allowed (`CanEnterSkyFortress`), the field's field quest in progress has an alliance (`FUN_1416d0800`: the first quest in progress among the field's field quests; a quest the played client lacks is skipped) and fewer than `FameContentsMissionAttackCount` (10) attacks were made today, and counts the attack itself. The server checks the same three and moves the player to the field. Trophies: a counted attack raises `mission_attack_system` (22500041, whose description counts uses of the sky fortress sortie system) and a prepared Quest 0x0E move raises `skyfortress_system` (22500040, whose description counts rides on the warship); neither condition has a code or target. Today's count is kept with the character's daily counts (no migration: the cooldown JSON column), sent at login as the second int of Quest 0x15 (the client's `m_refreshCount`), and cleared at the daily reset with the new SendOp 0x010B (`ResetRefreshCount`). The reset runs on the session's queue, where the attacks are counted, and a reset timer that fires before midnight waits for it, as the daily quest reset does. On 0x010B the client also updates its quest menu and mentoring window and opens the daily mission window. Chosen here: the player arrives at the field's default spawn (the packet names no portal); a refused attack gets no answer and is not counted; an attack is counted once the move is prepared (a prepared move that then fails at the field entry still counts; a move that cannot be prepared does not); attacks already counted after midnight when the reset runs are kept; 0x010B is sent at the daily reset; held quests are taken in id order; the content shutdown flag 0x53 the client checks first is not checked by the server (as for the other shut contents). With no attack left the client sends nothing: its box still opens and shows 0 left, and on Yes `SendQuestMode15` stops at its own count and shows the system notice `s_sky_fortress_warn_attack` (`ShowSystemNotice`, not a chat line), so the server gets no 0x0F and has nothing to answer (a live try: the 11th sortie logged no refusal).
- **Mission rank trophies:** the 13 trophies of the `quest_alliance_by_grade` condition never counted, because nothing reported it: 22500034-38 ("complete N D Rank missions" to S, any of alliances 1 to 5), the Kritias 22501017-21 (alliances 6 to 8) and the Georg 22501045-47 (alliance 9, B to S). Their code is the rank letter, as the quest XML gives the rank (`rank="D"`), and their target the alliance. Completing an alliance mission (quest type 9) now reports its rank letter with its alliance, from the rank index the ingest stores (S 0, A 1, B 2, C 3, D 4; a quest without a rank reports nothing). Each trophy counts its own rank only: the descriptions name one rank ("Complete {0} C Rank mission(s)", string/en/achievedescription.xml), so a higher-rank mission does not count the lower trophies. The client only registers the condition (`AddCondition("quest_alliance_by_grade", 0x3d, 0xb)`, commonfiles/conditionutil.cpp:579) and has no case for it in its own condition checks (achievecondition.cpp), so the server's report is the only source. The Kritias missions in the data are S, A or B only, so the Kritias C and D trophies (22501017, 22501018) stay out of reach. No re-ingest.
- **Job advancement trophies:** the 12 trophies of the `subjob_change` condition (90100000 "Change jobs", any of 101 to 1101; 90100001-11 one per class, such as "Change jobs to Saint Knight", code 101) never counted, because nothing reported the condition. Its code is the advanced job, the `changeSubJobCode` of `changeJob.xml` (`CChangeJobTable`, commonfiles/changejobtable.cpp:77; `AddCondition("subjob_change", 0x48, 0xc)`, commonfiles/conditionutil.cpp:586). The job change at the end of the advancement quest now reports it with the new job, next to the `job` condition. The client does not evaluate the condition itself and does not re-check it at login (`CAchieveManager::OnPacket` only takes the server's state), so a character that advanced before this change does not get these trophies. No re-ingest.
- **Completion tickets when the client's region is not LOCALE:** using 25 tickets on a rank C mission (cost 20) got Quest error 7, the box that says the tickets could not be used. The client offers the tickets when the quest's `property` has `AvailableCompletionTicket` and its own feature `FameCompletionTicket` is on, read in the column of the client's own region (KR unless the client is started with `-locale`). The ingest also tested that feature, but with the server's `LOCALE`, and the feature is off in the NA column, so under `LOCALE=NA` no alliance quest took tickets while a KR-region client offered them. The ingest now sets the quest's ticket flag from its `property` alone; the cost stays the Lua `calcFameCompletionTicketCount` of the rank, the same in the client's script. The server now reads the region in the client's hello (`ResponseVersion`: int version, short feature level, short region; the region was read as one byte and dropped) and logs a warning at each connection whose region is not `LOCALE`, since the client then gates features, item data and texts by another region than the ingested data. Needs a re-ingest.
  - Chosen here: the feature test is left to the client, which asks it before it sends the request; a connection with another region is still accepted, with the warning only.
- **Talk choices in the played client's order:** a choice the player picks in an NPC or quest talk reaches the server as an index (NpcTalk mode 2, `CSelectableDistractorCinematicComponent::vfunc_1`, client/ms2/cinematiccomponent.cpp:721) into the choices of the client's own script content in the order it shows them, and the server answers with the goto of its choice at that index. The played client's scripts can list them in another order than the data pack: in the KR client's `script/npc/npcscript_final.xml` the Colosseum MC (11004288) script 20 lists Continue first and Start Over second, the data pack the other way round, so Continue started a new game and Start Over continued. With `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` set, the ingest reads that pack's `script/npc/npcscript*.xml` and `script/quest/questscript*.xml` as the client does (`LoadTalkScripts`, commonfiles/talkscript.cpp: one element per id among those that pass the feature and locale test, a `randomPick` script without content not kept) and drops a choice of an npc or quest `<script>` whose goto names ids of which none is a kept script of that npc or quest (`CCinematicContent::vfunc_3`/`vfunc_4`, commonfiles/scriptcontent.cpp:301), as the client does after loading; each content of a script the pack has then takes its choices in that order. The log has one line with the count of contents changed. Not set, nothing changes. Chosen here: of several available elements with one id the first is taken (the client's feature selection between them is not followed); a content the pack lacks keeps the data pack's choices. Takes effect with the next ingest.
- **Dungeon rooms with limited entry:** a room the played client's own room table marks `limitEnter` (`DungeonRoom::Load`; `GetLimitType` 2) can be entered only while its reward record has a count left, as the client's `DungeonRecordCli::HasCountLeft` tests it. Otherwise the room window shows its cooldown icon, and the server answers the create with RoomDungeon error 4 and the room id; the client then shows that record's expire date (`DungeonRecordManagerCli::OnError` case 4). The server's NA room table has no `limitEnter`, and its `cooldownType` is set on 101 rooms as the reward reset, so the flag is read only from the pack named by `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` (6 rooms in the KMS2 dev client); without it no room is limited. Chosen here: the record checked is the reward room's (`unionRewardID` when set), only the creator's record is tested for a party, a missing record has a count, and the client's extra-reward-count event (`IsExtraExhausted`) is not modelled. The record's own `CooldownTimestamp` is not what the client shows; the client's expire is `UnionCooldownTimestamp`.
- **Extra reward tickets:** a room the played client's room table gives an `extraRewardTicketTableID` (34 rooms in the KMS2 dev client, none in the NA data) can buy one more reward count with the ticket items of its coupon row (`table/dungeonrewardcoupon.xml`, read from the pack named by `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML`). RoomDungeon 8 charges the room's `extraRewardTicketBaseCount` times the coupon's rate at the count bought so far (`DungeonRoom::GetExtraRewardTicketCost`; the last rate past the list), by item tag, and adds one to the record's bonus count (`ExtraSubClears`, the client's `GetBonusCountNow`), which raises the weekly reward count until the record's expire; the reply is the record update and the client's added-count notice. Refused with the client's texts: no ticket or a cost of 0 (error 5), the coupon's `maxExtraCount` reached (error 7), too few tickets (error 6). A room create with the use-ticket flag (the client's confirm box for a `limitEnter` room without a count left) buys one count the same way and then creates the room. The bonus count now resets with the weekly period and no longer with the daily one, since the client expires it with the record's main expire. Chosen here: the party leader pays for a ticket entry; the cost, the maximum and the count are the reward room's (`unionRewardID` when set; the client's ticket-entry box prices the entered room, and the data's union pairs have the same base count); a coupon without `maxExtraCount` stops only at 255 (the record's byte); a later row of a room or coupon id replaces an earlier one; a purchase in an expired period starts the next period; a count bought for a ticket entry stays if the room cannot be created afterwards.
- **Dungeon rank score:** a clear of a room with a rank table now fills the two scores the client's room window shows from the record (`DungeonRecord.WriteTo`): `LifetimeRecord` (the client's m_60) and `CurrentRecord` with its expiry (m_68, a timed value). From the client: the room's rank table is its `rankTableID`, else its group type (`DungeonRoomData::GetRankTableID`: normal 1, raid 2, chaos raid 3, reverse raid 4, fame challenge 5, world boss 6, others none), the score cap is that table's `maxScore` (`DungeonRankRequire`, dungeonconfig.xml), a season rank room is a chaos raid whose `limitPlayerLevel` is at least the constant `DungeonSeasonRankMinLevel` (`IsSeasonRankRoom`; 60 in KR, 99 in NA, JP, TH and TW), and the season is the first `table/dungeonseason.xml` row of the locale and features whose period holds the time (`DungeonSeason::GetCurrentSeason`; now ingested as a table, so this needs a re-ingest). Inferred, not found in the client (the server fills these values): the run score is the sum of the run's mission scores at the clear, capped at maxScore; `LifetimeRecord` keeps the best score, -1 until the first clear with a rank table; in a season rank room `CurrentRecord` keeps the best score of the season week (0 once its expiry has passed), and its expiry is the season's next `dayOfWeeks` at the time of day of the season's `startTime` (00:00 for the KR seasons 1, 2 and 1001, 08:00 for the current 1002, which starts 2018-07-05 08:00), clamped to the season's end. `dayOfWeeks` is read with Sunday 1, the client's `CMsTime::GetDayOfWeek` convention for `limitDayOfWeeks`; every row has 6, which is Friday so read, and two of the season starts in the table fall on a Friday, but the client code that reads the field was not found, so the day is not proven. The expiry goes in the record's `CooldownTimestamp` (the long the client reads as m_68's expiry; the DB column keeps its name). The room cooldown that was written there before is no longer written: nothing on the server read it, and the client's room expire is `UnionCooldownTimestamp`. The score counts on every clear, also one without a reward count left. Not tried live.
- **Dungeon helper reward:** a veteran who clears a room with a rookie now gets the helper reward, instead of the old TODO. The checks: the room has a `dungeonHelperRequireClearCount`, and the player's record of the room is a veteran's (RoomDungeon 17) with at least that many clears, as the client's `DungeonRecordManagerCli::CanClaimHelperReward` (dungeonrecordcli.cpp:509) tests it. Another player of the clear must be a rookie, which the client's expert notice (`s_room_dungeon_record_notify_change_expert`) gives as the rule. The reward takes one count of room 1003's record while it has one left (`DungeonRoomRules.HasCountLeft`; the helper window shows that room's weekly count, `UIDungeonHelper::GetHelperRewardCountText`), and adds the result dialog's helper line (`DungeonBonusFlag.Helper`). It also gives the items of the room's `findHelperRewardDropBoxIds` and sends the record update with the client's reward notice (`GiveDungeonHelperReward`). The boxes are read from the pack named by `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` when it is set (box 330002188 on 23 rooms in the KMS2 dev client), else from the server's rows, which set none in the NA data. Without a box, the count and the dialog line are still given, with no items, and the items need the box in the server's drop tables. Takes effect with the next ingest. Chosen here: a rookie is a player whose record of the room is not a veteran's (a player with no record counts too); only the players still in the field at the clear count. The veteran state and clears are taken before this clear counts. The helper keeps the normal clear reward. With no count left there is no helper reward and no notice. Room 1003's sub count advances with the main count, and an expired period starts the next one, as a clear does. The boxes are dropped both ways (individual and global, at the room's level), as the room's unlimited boxes are.
- **Dungeon helper bonus (FindDungeonHelperBonus):** the ingest reads the event (value1 the bonus) and the event list sends it: the event's id and the bonus. The client adds up the running events' bonuses and shows the event mark on the room window's helper line while the sum is above 0 (`uidungeonroomdialog.cpp:841`); it does nothing else with it. KR has 9 rows, all ended (the last 2021-09-22), all with bonus 1.
  - Chosen here: the helper reward's boxes are given once more per bonus (`EventSaleRules.HelperRewardTimes`, at most 10 times), still for one count of room 1003's record. The client gives no rule; the KR rows' notes describe the events as one more helper coin, or twice the coins.
