The entry's fields are the client's StateHitData (StateHitNPC): the stay after the sequence = the attack's hitPauseTime, the push time = pushduration (0 when the npc did not move), the lift height, and a byte for the get-up sequence = pushDown. KMS2 packets: hitPauseTime in 2,875 of 2,967 entries, Damg_A 1,929 / Damg_B 1,921, the byte set in 12 of 12 pushDown attacks, never a hit state for class 3 and up (0 of 127) or hitImmune 98/99. The state goes out once; the rest of the hold is state 0 with sequence -1, as KMS2 sends it.
  - A hit without a push reacts only on an npc that stands or walks (KMS2: 546 of 583), not during its cast or the hold of the last hit (63 of 807); a hit with a push reacts there too and ends the cast (848 of 868). The push moves the npc pushdistance away from the point the damage packet gives, on the ground (KMS2: distance 50 moves 40-50 units, 150 moves 110-150, direction within 26 degrees in 476 of 535). It runs straight over the navmesh and stops at the first wall or ledge. The hit is queued by the thread that dealt the damage and applied by the field thread in the npc's next update.
  - The hold follows the client's StateHitNPC: it ends at the later of the sequence's end and hitPauseTime plus the push time, and the get-up sequence (Damg_C) is added for a pushDown hit. Chosen here: the lift is pushUpDistance (0 in every KMS2 entry seen); the entry carries the end position and no velocity; pushprob below 1 is rolled per hit; push types other than 1 push the same way; no reaction while spawning, stunned or in a jump arc, or for an npc without the two sequences (its cast goes on).
  - Super armor keeps the reaction off, by the client's own test for players (SuperArmorRules): the larger of the cast's stateAttr superArmor (while it casts) and the enabled effects' MotionProperty superArmor, each only where its superArmorApplyFieldType lets it count (0 anywhere, 2 on PvP maps, other values off them). Level 1 gives way to a push or a grab (attack grabTargetType 1), 2 to a push with pushUpDistance or a grab, 3 to nothing, 4 to a push or a grab; superArmorBreak goes through all of them. The push itself follows its pushApplyField as the client's SkillDamage::GetPush does: 1 takes it away on PvP and home maps, 2 everywhere else. Class 3 and up still never react, armor or not. Ingest: the skill's superArmorApplyFieldType, the attack's grabTargetType and the effect's superArmor and superArmorApplyFieldType.
- **Boss phases:** nothing on the wire marks a boss phase: KMS2 bosses stay in states 0, 1, 2, 17, 18, 23 and what changes with their health is which skills they cast and which effects they put on themselves. Those follow the hpOver / hpLess conditions of the npc's AI tree (1,092 of 1,120 KMS2 casts of health-limited skills are inside the tree's health bounds, the rest follow a cast that started inside), which the AI here evaluates the same way; nothing changed.
- **AI skill reach:** the `trace` node walked to the skill's detectProperty distance and the `skillRange` condition passed within that distance + 10. That distance is 0 in most npc skills (147 of the 198 skills of the lv10-30 field bosses and elites), so those npcs walked onto the target and their range branches never passed. Both now use the skill's reach as the client reads it when it picks a target for a skill (PCBase::vfunc_101): a cylinder whose radius is the level's autoTargeting maxDistance, else the first attack's range value (the range distance, or the end width for a hole cylinder, `SkillLevelData::GetAttackRangeValue`), and whose height is autoTargeting maxHeight, else 150. `runaway` takes the same reach when it names a skill (`AiSkillReach`).
  - Chosen here: the circle is flat with a height window of the cylinder height above or below the caster (the client either centres the cylinder or stands it on the caster's feet), and keeps the old 10-unit slack for a walk that stops on the edge. The centre stays on the caster: the client moves it by the attack's offset unless the attack method is 1, which our data does not hold (6 of those 198 skills have an offset).
- **AI vibrate groups:** a map's vibrate objects with a `hideGroupId` (the Group3_0 rocks of maps 02000037 and 02000526, groups 1 to 9) start hidden, and the map data now keeps the id, so the maps need a re-ingest (the `map-data` table); the map data now starts with a format version, and the game server stops at startup with "Rerun Ingest!" when it reads map data of another format. The client never reads `hideGroupId` and shows every object, so a player entering the field is sent HideVibrate for each hidden one: CEntityVibrator::StartFall fades it out, takes its collision away and holds it. `showVibrate` (36 nodes in 8 AI files, `groupID` 1 to 9) sends ShowVibrate for the group's hidden objects, which releases the hold: the object fades in and drops onto its place in 0.5 s (the Balrog file's comment: drop rocks). `hideVibrateAll` (27 nodes in 10) sends HideVibrate for every shown one, and its one `isKeepBattle` keeps the battle. A group already shown is not sent again.
- **AI room time:** `modifyRoomTime` (13 nodes in 13 AI files) adds `timeTick` milliseconds to the room's timer (80000 and 300000) or takes them off (-30000, -90000); the time does not go below 0, which the client reads as the timer's end. `isShowEffect` (11 nodes) makes the client show the added or removed seconds with the timer's effect (RoomTimer notice type 1); without it the time moves silently (type 0). A field without a room timer has nothing to change.
- **AI interact objects:** `createInteractObject` (2 nodes in 2 files) adds an interact object where the npc stands: AI_Gigantika_SubBoss leaves scrap metal when it dies (`interactID` 10003001, `lifeTime` 20) and AI_KritiasSlime_MiniPuzzle_RunAway a golden chest (12000072, `lifeTime` 120). The client's SSG_INTERACT_ADD reads the fields a map interact object sends (name, state, kind, row id, position, rotation, model, asset, normal and reactable animations, scale, a flag) and takes kind 1 (mesh) or 8 (billboard) only; both rows are type 1. The object is sent as an MS2InteractActor with the node's `kfmName` as its asset, as the combine spawn table's chests are, and a player entering the field is sent it too. Nothing in the packet ends it, so the server sends Remove after `lifeTime`, read as seconds like a summon's `lifeTime` under 1000 (a server reading).
- **GM object create (InteractObject 9):** the client's object create dialog (`UIObjectCreateDialog::OnObjectCreate`, a debug window; GM accounts only) lists the interact object table and sends the picked row id, four strings, a count and the GM's position and rotation. The server adds a mesh interact object of that row there and sends SSG_INTERACT_ADD to the field, as for `createInteractObject`; a row the table lacks is dropped with a log. Chosen here: the four strings fill the model, asset, normal and reactable animation of SSG_INTERACT_ADD in their order, since the client does not name them; the count is read and not used (one object per press); the object stays until the field closes.
- **AI battleEnd:** the ingest put an AI file's battleEnd tree into its battle tree, so it ran on every battle pass and never at the end. It is now its own tree, with the file's `onlyDead` (the parser reads no attribute of battleEnd; the ingest reads it from the file): 452 of the 545 trees are onlyDead. At the npc's death both kinds run, through at once: a node that starts a move or a skill does not wait for it. A battle that ends with the npc alive runs only a tree without onlyDead (the AI files' own comment: with onlyDead it runs only at the death). `isKeepBattle` (2 files) is not read. Needs an AI re-ingest; until then the old data stands (the battleEnd nodes in the battle tree).
- **AI createRandomRoom** (2 nodes, the ShadowGate npcs' onlyDead battleEnd: `randomRoomID="3"`, `portalDuration="30"`): at the npc's death an Event portal opens at the npc for portalDuration seconds, to a room of `table/Server/room_random.xml` (a new server table: needs a re-ingest, and the Release server stops at start without it). The randomroom row's prob is read out of 10000 (row 1 gives 10000; row 3 gives 1500, so about one death in seven opens one), then a room by weight (row 3: 29 rooms of weight 100). The portal shows the room's nifAssetName and the client's countdown from its end tick (the client's portal add of type 3). The room is made at the first entry with its roomdurationTick on the room clock, and when it runs out the players go to their return field. It takes maxUserCount players through the portal (s_move_err_member_limit after that), and with autoClosing (read as: the portal goes when the room is full) the portal is removed. An ended room closes its portal. The prob scale and autoClosing are read from the values, not from the client (the server picks the room, the client has none of it). The gameEvent RandomRoomProb (which raises a row's prob) is not built. A bonus map portal now leads into its room too; it went to the map's shared field.
- **OpenMassive portals** (17 items, all with fieldID, portalDurationTick and maxCount): the portal an item opens now leads to a room of its own, made when the first player enters (the user enters at once), and takes `maxCount` players through it (1 for 4 items, 4 for 1, 20 for 4, 50 for 3, 70 for the 5 wedding hall items). A player past the limit gets s_move_err_member_limit, and the full portal is removed. The room has no clock and goes when it is empty. Before, every portal led to the channel's shared field of the map, and maxCount was never read. The client never checks maxCount (UIMessageBox::HandleOpenMassiveButton tests only the spot, the player's state and the portal count nearby), so the server holds it. The target maps are indoor event maps that the cash taxi cannot reach. The wedding hall items' `weddingParty` flag is still not used: no client code or data says what it changes. The random-room portal code is shared; random rooms work as before (each room_random row has a roomdurationTick above 0).
- **AI target state:** the `state` condition (229 in 59 AI files: `holdme` 169, `grabTarget` 60) tests the target player's state against this npc. The client's state sync names the other actor of both states. A player who holds the nearest actor in reach (state 32, gosHold; AI_DevilHugeRed, the Blackbean car) sends the held actor's object id in the sync's optional field 5, and a player that an npc's skill of grabTargetType 1 pins to one of the npc's grab nodes (state 43, gosGrabTarget; AI_Conder_FR_Lv1) sends the grabber's id in field 0. `holdme` passes while the target holds this npc and `grabTarget` while the target is in this npc's grab; holding or being grabbed by another actor does not count. Npcs take neither state, so an npc target never passes. Both used to test the state alone; the client sends the id, so the check is the client's and not a reading.
- **Grab release:** a player an npc's grab skill holds (state 43) is let go only by the client itself when the grabber's object or node is gone, and a dead npc stays in the field for its dead time, so a player held by an npc that died hung on its corpse. At the npc's death the server now sends SkillDamage 7 with the npc's object id and the players whose last state sync shows them held by it (`GrabReleaseRules`); the client puts each one still held by that npc back into state 1 (scenestategame.cpp:7128). The writer existed and was never sent. Chosen here: the release is sent at the grabber's death, to the whole field; no data or client code says when the server sends it.
- **Dead targets:** a monster lets go of a target that died and does not pick a dead player or npc as a new target; it takes another living target in sight, or leaves the battle and goes back to its patrol, and the player is a target again after the revive. Before, it kept casting at the dead player until the revive. The live game drops the target about a second after the death; here it is dropped at the monster's next update. A monster whose AI holds the battle (isKeepBattle nodes, in 518 AI files) stays in battle and keeps a target that is only out of sight, but lets go of a dead one too.
  - After the death: KMS2 monsters that chased a player stop at once, stand or shuffle near the body for some seconds and then run back to their spawn point (about 13 to 19 s after the death in the one clean case; no heal is sent for them). Here the monster's walk to its target is cancelled when the target is dropped, so it no longer walks on to the body, and it then runs back to a point within its move area of its spawn point as before. A tombstone is not an actor and is never a target.
  - Chosen here: no wait near the body before the run back, and a dead target is dropped under isKeepBattle (before, such a monster kept casting at the body). A monster made with the GM `npc` command has the GM's position as its spawn point, so it goes back to where it was made.
- **Buffs after a revive:** a death takes every buff without `keepOnDeath`, also the lasting ones a player holds from passive skills, gear and set effects, character abilities, prestige, lapenshards, the premium club, the guild and the insignia. Only a field entry gave them back, so after a revive in the same field the player kept the lower stats (a character ability's +31 max HP) until the next map. A revive now gives them back before the HP fill, so the player comes back at the full total; a revive that sends the player to the map's revival return map gets them from that field entry as before.
- **Invincible after a portal:** a player who arrives by portal now gets InvincibleEffect (0x83, no body), and the client shows the invincible effect for `UserPortalInvincibleTick` (5000 ms); the server takes no monster or player damage on that player for the same time, in the same window as the revival invincibility (`UserRevivalInvincibleTick`), and a new window never shortens a running one. Like the client's, the window ends only when the time is over, not on a skill or an attack.
  - Chosen here, from the constant's name: map-change and same-map portals (also a home portal cube, a room portal, a dungeon exit or lobby return, and a portal that returns the player); not a login, a revival, a GM or trigger move, and not a portal that migrates to another server.
- **Passive skill buffs follow the tree:** a passive skill's buffs were put on at every skill apply, reset, auto distribute, field entry and revive, but never taken off: the removal was left out. A reset, an apply that unlearns a passive or an applied tab with another tree kept the old passives' stats until the next field entry or death, and a lowered passive kept its old level even then, as a buff held at a higher level is not replaced (10200101 at level 2, lowered to 1, stayed at level 2). The client applies no passive itself: it shows the buffs the server sends, and after a reset, an apply or an applied tab it sends the whole tree (`UISkillBookTreeDialog::RequestJob`, uiskillbookautodistributedialog.cpp:1180; the tab apply also sends RequestTree with no tab first). The server now keeps the passive buffs (id and level) the tree gave and, before it puts the new tree's passives on, takes off each one the new tree does not give at the same level (`SkillBookRules.StalePassiveBuffs`). Not done: a buff that a passive buff's own effects add is not followed. Live try: as a berserker, learn the passive 10200101 to level 2, apply, then lower it to 1 and apply: the buff shows level 1; reset the tree: the buff goes away at once.
- **Dead player moved to another field:** a player moved while dead (a GM warp or a trigger move) got a new, living actor in the next field at 0 HP, still drawn dead but with no tombstone the revival dialog could use. Such a player now arrives with full HP and alive. Chosen here: the client gives no rule for this case.
- **Tombstone hits:** the hits a tombstone takes are hitPerDeadCount (5) for each death in a row, at most maxDeadCount (3) deaths: 5, 10, 15. A death is in the row when it comes within `timeResetDead` (180 s) of the death before it; KMS2 counts on at gaps of 14 to 175 s and starts again at 188 s and more, measured from death to death (a revive in between does not move the time), also for a character below the death penalty level. Before, the total followed the death penalty count, which only fields with a death penalty raise and which lasts an hour. Chosen here: the row is kept by the game server process, so a move to another channel starts it again; a row that has run out is dropped at the next death on that process. Chosen here: constants without a `timeResetDead` row count as 180 s (the KMS value), with one warning; the client has no such constant.
- **Tombstone packet on a revive:** the tombstone packet's int after the two hit counts is 1 only when the packet reports a hit by another player; KMS2 sends 0 when the player revives from the dialog. With nothing left and the int at 1 the client plays the tombstone hit effect and, for its own player, an event voice (`PC::FUN_14165d300`), so every dialog revive (safe, meso, meret) played them here. The last byte (a revive by a pet in the one KMS2 sample) stays 0.
- **Meso and voucher revive (Revival mode 2):** every test now comes before anything is taken, and the mesos or the voucher go only once the revive has happened. Before, a request from a living player took the mesos or a voucher and raised the daily count for nothing. Refusals: not dead, a noRevivalHere field, a disableRevivalHere buff, no voucher held, the daily count used up (mesos only), too few mesos. The price is now the client's (`UIRevivalDialog::RefreshMesoButton`): the Lua price times 1 - min(`MesoRevivalFeeReduceLimit`, the character's meso revival fee reduction), and 0 in a home field; before, the reduction was ignored, so a character with it was asked for more than its button showed. A limit row of 0 means no reduction, and a missing row counts as the client's default 0.5. In a home field the revive is free and the client shows its button whatever the daily count is, so the server does not test the daily count there; chosen here: that free revive does not raise the count (the client does not settle it). The client's button also refuses while the meret revival debuff is on the player and enabled, at any stack count (not in an infinityMeratRevival field), and so does the server now. Not done: the client's price takes a meso revival event flag of the game rules, and this server runs no such event. A voucher revive raises the daily count here; the client only shows the count the server sends and KMS2 has no voucher revive, so which is right is unknown.
- **Daily revive count across a login:** the day's paid revives (at most 3, `GetMesoRevivalDailyMaxCount`) were saved to the character's config row but never read back, so a return from character select, a channel change or any new login started the day at 0 again and three more paid revives worked. The count is now loaded with the rest of the config and sent in RevivalCount at field entry, the only place the client learns it (`CSceneStateGame::OnRevivalCount`); the daily reset still clears it. The count is kept per character, where the server already stored it; the client keeps only the number it is sent, so per character or per account is chosen here. Live try: die and use the 3 paid revives (the button then asks for merets), go to character select and back in with the same character: the button still asks for merets; after the daily reset it shows Uses Left: 3 again.
- **Meret revive (Revival mode 1):** the tests are one rule with a case each (not dead, a noRevivalHere field, a dungeon that disables it, a disableRevivalHere buff, the locale's LimitMeratRevival, the debuff on the player at any stack count, a ticked coupon box with no coupon, too few merets); the coupon or the merets go only once the revive has happened.
- **AI feature condition:** the `feature` condition (4 uses, all `FameChallengeBuff_01`: AI_Conder_FR_Lv1, AI_Mason_FR_Lv1, AI_Bliche_FR_Lv2, AI_Schatten_FR_Lv2, each followed by a `true` branch with the plain skills) is decided at data import, as the client's feature manager decides a feature: on when its feature.xml level is not above the feature_setting.xml level of the locale. An off condition is left out, so the boss takes the `true` branch. A feature with no column for the locale reads as level 0, which the client skips (unknown, so off) but the parser's filter keeps on; the import follows the client (see Feature level 0). With the KMS data under KR the feature is on (35, Live 78), so a KR import gives the same AI data.
- **NPC facing without a direction:** `Transform.LookTo` normalized the ground-plane part of the direction before its own check for a zero one, so a direction straight up or down, a zero one (a target on the npc's own spot) or a NaN one set NaN axes. Scale is read back from the axes, so the npc kept the NaN facing through every later turn. Its attack prisms are built from that rotation and hit nothing, and its skill moves (`move0`) asked the navmesh for NaN points every tick ("Invalid point <NaN, NaN, NaN>" with "Failed to find valid position"). The npc still traced the player, so it stood on the player and followed them without ever hitting. Such a direction now keeps the current facing.
  - Chosen here: keep the facing in `LookTo` itself, so every caller (walk segments, standby, skill face target, flying) is covered, rather than a check at each call site.
- **NPC path end on the ground:** an npc's path ended at the raw target point, not at that point's place on the navmesh. A mob chasing a player in a jump, or one on something the navmesh leaves out, walked up the last segment and stopped at its attack range in the air. The path's last point is now the target's closest point on the last poly, as Recast's own path smoothing does.
  - Chosen here: snap the end in the path smoothing (now `AgentNavigation.SmoothPath`, testable on a hand-built navmesh) rather than at each walk step.
- **NPC moves off the navmesh:** an npc's walk step or skill cast move to a point with no poly within the search box (200 across, 400 up or down) was taken as it was: Detour's nearest-poly search reports success with poly ref 0 and the point itself when it finds nothing, and the npc's position update only checked the status. A mob walking or lunging past a ledge kept the off-mesh point, and a NaN point got through as well. The update now needs a poly: off the mesh the npc stays where it is, and a NaN point is logged once with the npc's id. The jump and pet warp fallbacks to the raw point, and path finding's own off-mesh handling, are unchanged.
  - Chosen here: check the poly ref in the npc's position update (`AgentNavigation.SnapToMesh`) and leave the field's nearest-poly lookup as it is, since path finding and patrol points read its ref 0 themselves.
- **NPC directions of zero length:** after the facing fix, the other places that turned a zero-length offset into a direction were guarded the same way: the cast's face-target turn and its target direction (a target on the caster's own spot), the path walk over a zero-length segment (a repeated path point), moving away from a goal on the npc's own spot, the flying walk's velocity over a zero-length tick, a walk in a zero direction, the Euler angles of a rotation whose sine rounds past 1, the axis angle of a quaternion, and a vector length clamp of a zero vector. Each keeps the value it had (facing, cast direction, path progress, velocity) or refuses the move, where the old code produced NaN.
  - Chosen here: guard at each site rather than in one helper, since what to keep differs per site; no log, as a target on the caster's spot is normal skill data.
- **Patrol waypoints off the navmesh:** a ground patrol waypoint with no navmesh poly near it was skipped with a warning. Of the 4,869 ground patrol steps in all maps, the one such waypoint is a train's last stop in 80000015_bonus (MS2PatrolData1201A, waypoint 1201B): its track is ground the navmesh is not built from, and the trigger waits for the train in box 124 at that stop before it takes the train away and sends the next one, so the train never arrived and the loop stopped. The npc now goes to such a waypoint in a straight line at walk speed, as for an air waypoint. A waypoint with a poly but only a partial path is unchanged (the other train there, MS2PatrolData1200A, still stops short of its box 122).
  - Chosen here: fall back only when the npc stands on the mesh and the goal has no poly (`AgentNavigation.GoalOffMesh`), not for partial paths (186 of the 4,869 ground patrol steps in all maps end short, both trains' first steps among them).
- **Item option job:** an item's constant and static option values (str, dex, int, luk, hp, ndd and the rest) are scaled for one job, which the server took from the job limit's first entry. The client takes it from the recommended jobs: the lowest one that is not 0 or the beginner, else 0 (`ItemOptionValueRef::GetRecommendedJob`, commonfiles/itemoptiondata_2.cpp:1355). The two differ when the limit is empty or lists other jobs: weapon 15400118 has no limit and recommends the rune blader, so its values were rolled for job 0. Intelligence also takes whether the item recommends more than one job (the striker's mage value), which the server always set; it now follows the recommended jobs as the client's `recommendJobs.size() > 1` does. No data change: both columns were already imported.
  - Chosen here: the job limit is not used as a fallback, as the client has none (no recommended job gives job 0).
- **Feature level 0:** every table's feature filter follows the client's feature manager: a feature whose feature.xml level for the locale is 0 (no column) is off, where the parser's filter kept it on (0 is not above a setting of 0). Rows gated by such a feature are left out, and where the parser picks between rows by feature level, the row without a feature is taken. Data with a column for the locale and no level 0 is unchanged: a data set with all six locale columns (572 features, Live NA 47) gives the same NA import, table by table. The KMS data has a KR column only, so under any other locale every feature is now off, as in the client.
- **Feature levels from the played client:** with `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` set, every table's feature filter uses the features the played client turns on, read from that pack's `table/feature_setting.xml` and `table/feature.xml` the way `CFeatureManager::Init` reads them (`LoadFeatureSetting`, commonfiles/featuremanager.cpp:757: the `<setting>` whose type is the server type, without case, at the locale's attribute; `LoadFeature`, :787: a feature with no level for the locale is skipped, the rest are on when not above the setting). Before, the filter used the data folder's tables, whose Live KR level (56) and feature list are another client version's; the KR client's own Live level is 78. A data folder alone (no played client) is filtered as before.
  - Chosen here: the server type is Live, the type the client takes on the Nexon platform, and `MS2_FEATURE_ENV` (Dev, Qa, DevStage, Stage or Live, without case) picks another; an unknown value stops the ingest. The client reads its server type once at startup (the `-env:` argument, or Dev when it does not run on the Nexon platform, which the patched client does not), so a played client started without `-env:Live` runs at Dev (KR 99, 131 more features, Develop among them) and differs from a Live ingest. When the played pack has no level for the locale at that type (the KR client has KR and CN columns only, so NA has none), the ingest stops with a message instead of turning every feature off. A played pack without the two tables leaves the data folder's features. Live try: not tried live.
- **Region table rows the played client lists:** under a `LOCALE` other than the data folder's region, a row of `table/<region>/dungeonroom.xml` or `chapterbook.xml` whose id has no row for the locale is now kept when the played client's own copy of the table (`MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML`) lists that id in a row whose feature is on at the locale's level; ids the client does not list stay dropped. The NA data folder tags most rows of the two tables locale NA with no KR row, so under KR the filter kept 6 of the 101 dungeon rooms (none between level 10 and 30, and not the union room) and 0 of the 27 chapter books; with the KR client it keeps 69 rooms (63 added, 32 the client does not list) and all 27 books. The kept row is the data folder's row with each value the client row sets taken from the client row, its feature included (clear boxes, `playerCountFactorID`, `dungeonHelperRequireClearCount`, `subRewardCount` and the others); values only the data folder's row has stay. Chosen here: where the two differ, the client's value, as the player sees it. A client value the parser cannot read (the client writes `playType` as a number) keeps the data folder's value, and the ingest prints which. Every other locale-filtered table the ingest reads was checked: none loses an id this way (`individualitemdrop_*` has rows for both locales and is unchanged). An id the data folder has no row for at all is now added from the client row alone (37 dungeon rooms, among them 29002001 and every room of 20 client features such as Zakum01, TreasureMap01, DungeonOS01/02 and the DungeonOpen70 CH3-CH7 rooms; 2 chapter books, 28 and 29). A row the data folder has for the locale now takes the client's value too, where the client row sets the field (its feature aside, so the filter's pick stays); a field the client row does not set, such as `cooldownType`, stays the data folder's. With the KR client that changes one value: union room 1007's `rewardCount` 20 to 10. The union rooms 1006 (30 clears a week, 10 a day) and 1002 already came from the client row. The ingest prints each changed value and each client-only id. Needs a re-ingest. Not tried live.
- **Dungeon rooms whose fields the server cannot make:** a room is now made only when its lobby and every field have map data, a navmesh file and map entities (`FieldEntryRules.UnmadeDungeonField`), all checked before any field is made. Before, only the navmesh was checked; a field with no map row was skipped with a log line, so the lobby was made without it and the player entered a room whose field never loads, and a map without entities threw after the lobby existed. A refused room answers the create with move error 0x1E, which the client shows as `s_move_err_FailCreateDungeon` (`GetFieldMoveErrorMessage`, from `OnGameToGame`) and stays in the field; a dungeon match gets no room. Of the 37 KR rooms added from the client row alone, 6 run (20007001, 23004003, 23018003, 24090004, 29004001, 29005001; their reward boxes are in the drop table). 14 are refused for a missing navmesh (the rooms on lobbies 50000015 and 50000016, and 29002001, 29008001, 29070001); a navmesh set built for the KR maps lets them be made. 8 are refused because a field has no map row in KMS2's map data (23046002, 23070003, 23071003, 23080003, 23081003, 23100001, 23101001 and 23110001; fields 02000526-02000529, 02000559 and 02200001). The other 9 (230460031 and the like) are reward-count rows other rooms name as `unionRewardID`, with no lobby or field, and are refused as well. Tested on made-up maps; not tried live.
- **Chapter book skill points with one value:** a `skillPoint` reward value is `amount,rank` or the amount alone; the ingest read only the first form. The KR client's `chapterbook.xml`, whose rewards the books now take under KR, writes 7 of its 18 skill point rewards as one value (one with the type written `SkillPoint`), so the KR ingest stopped at the chapter book table. The client reads the value as a list of numbers and the rank only when a second one is there (`CChapterBookData::Read`). The ingest now does the same: one value is that amount at rank 0. Tested with a full KR ingest against the played client; needs a re-ingest.
- **Dungeon clear boxes from both reward lists:** a clear with a reward count left gave only the room's `rewardUnlimitedDropBoxIds`. It now gives the `rewardLimitedDropBoxIds` too, the boxes the client's reward preview lists (`DungeonRoom::Load`), then the unlimited ones, each box once and box 0 skipped (`DungeonRoomRules.ClearDropBoxIds`). Under KR, 27 of the 69 rooms list a limited box that their unlimited list lacks; for 14 of them the unlimited list is the NA pack's (box 10 and a 3300019xx box that holds no KR item), so the clear gave no room box. 21 of these 27 boxes hold items under KR; the six FameChallenge boxes (24091005-24095005) hold none. Each box (and each helper reward box) is rolled for the player as an opened item box is, not given whole: the group's drop count, then entries by weight, by the player's job for a smart drop group, with the gender, level, quest and map tests (`ItemDropManager.PickGroupEntries`). Given whole, box 21001001 (three groups: one job-matched gear item always, two more at 5% each) handed over all 55 entries, every job's weapons and armour.
- **Interact object, fishing and trigger spawn boxes rolled:** these gave an individual drop box unrolled. An interact object took one entry at random from all of a box's entries, ignoring groups, drop counts, weights and job, and forced the object's `objectDropRank` on it as the item grade; of the 440 box references on 433 KR interact objects, 36 are a single entry given only by chance (310000018: 30%), 25 have more than one group (310000226: three meso groups, of which one was given) and 17 are smart drop gear boxes (a random job's item). Its global drop boxes kept only the last box's items. A trigger item spawn (`CreateItem`, `SpawnItemRange`) gave every entry of its box: box 23000080 on two field maps handed out 236 gear items, box 390000015 its 0.5% group every time. They are now rolled as an opened item box is (`ItemDropManager.PickGroupEntries`): an interact object and a fishing spot for the player, a trigger spawn, which has no player, by the entries' own weights with quest entries and other maps' entries skipped (`PickBoxEntriesWithoutPlayer`); a smart drop group without weights gives a spawn nothing. No KR fishing spot has an individual drop box, so fishing gives the same as before. A key-opened item box (`OpenItemBoxWithKey`) opened several at once rolled once and gave the same items for every box; it now rolls each box. Quest, chapter book, achievement, attendance, event and reward content rewards are fixed item lists, given whole as their data says (a quest's job items only for the player's job).
- **Quest-only global drops:** an entry of `globalDropItemSet` with `constraintsQuest` was never dropped. KR has 39 such sets, all in drop box 1 (on 904 npcs) and each for one map, and each item is what an item_exist quest counts (10001270 needs 15 of 30000043 from the monsters of map 2000136; 10001310, 10001330, 10001460, 10001540 and 20001350 have no other source of theirs), so those quests could not be finished. The entry now drops for the player it is rolled for when a started quest of theirs still needs it: an item_exist condition that names the item and this map, below its count. The client has no drop code, so the server table decides. Chosen here: a roll with no player behind it (a trigger spawn's or a dungeon reward's global box) never gives one; npc death, hit and corpse drops, interact objects, breakables, fishing and item boxes are rolled for their player.
- **Quest-only individual drops:** a row of `individualItemDrop` with `constraintsQuest` dropped for every player. KR has 432 such rows (353 items) in the boxes of monsters and interact objects; the ingest read a quest id from the wrong part of the row's reference text (most rows have none) and kept 0, and nothing checked it. The row now drops, as a quest-only global drop does, only for the player it is rolled for when a started quest of theirs still needs the item: an item_exist condition that names the item (and this map, when it names maps), below its count (50001652 needs 30000102, the only row of box 721000216 of monster 21000216, on map 2000019). 183 rows are for enabled item_exist quests; the other rows serve only disabled quests or no quest and no longer drop. A box rolled without a player (a trigger spawn's) skips the row. The client has no drop code, so the server table decides. The metadata changed: a server needs a re-ingest; a table from an older ingest reads every row as not quest-only and drops as before.
- **item_exist counts the held items:** an item_exist quest's counter is the count of the item the player holds, as the client shows it (CQuestManager::UpdateQuestProgress counts the item's inventory tab and keeps no progress the server sends for item_exist). The server added the whole stack on each pickup (one more on a stack of 5 counted 6 more), never counted what was held at the start, and never counted down, so a player who held the items before the accept, or for a repeat, saw the quest done while the server refused to complete it, and one who sold or dropped them could still hand it in. KR has 601 live quests with item_exist (176 of them repeat). The count is now taken again at the start, on login and on each add, remove or use of the item, up to the condition's value. The client takes no item back at completion, so neither does the server; trophies keep their own item_exist count.
- **meso_donation and equip_exist count what the player has:** the funding quests 93000073 and 93000139-93000142 (meso_donation, 5,000 to 200,000 meso) and the event outfit quests 95000000, 95000001 and 95000003-95000008 (equip_exist, 6 of 12 listed items) never moved: nothing on the server counted either condition, so the client showed them done while the server refused to complete them. The client counts both itself (CQuestManager::UpdateQuestProgress, questmanager_2.cpp:1400): meso_donation is the meso the player has (case 0x49), equip_exist the number of distinct listed item ids among the worn items (case 0xad, CQuestManager::FUN_1416d3af0 at questmanager_2.cpp:2309 over CEquipment::CollectShownItems at equipment.cpp:850, which takes each worn item once; types from conditionutil.cpp:588 and :630). Both now count as item_exist does: at the start, on login, and on each meso change or equip and unequip (gear and both outfit tabs), up or down, up to the condition's value. Not known: whether a funding quest takes the meso at completion. Its completeReward has money="0", there is no consume or reset field, and its quest and npc scripts (npc 11003536) have no event, so the server takes none. Also not known: the client skips a worn item whose item-stream bool after the glamour forge count is set (CItem::FUN_140529620); the server always writes it false, so every worn listed item counts.
- **Trigger user values per trigger:** each trigger keeps its own user values, under its trigger model id. `user_value`, `wait_seconds_user_value` and `add_user_value` use the trigger's own; `set_user_value` writes the trigger its `trigger_id` names, or its own without one; the AI nodes TriggerSetUserValue/TriggerModifyUserValue and the npc script functions write the trigger their id names (every one of them gives one). The field had one table for all its triggers and dropped the ids. The client has no user value code, so the trigger data decides: 47142 of the 47356 trigger sets with a trigger_id go to a trigger that reads the key (92 name no trigger of the map), and many scripts of one map keep a counter under the same key (WaveTime in each mob-wave script of 02000378), which one table mixed up. The sets this makes dead were read against their scripts and no map stalls on them: the 02000471 clear keys and InteractClear (also sent to warpcheck and elite_02, which do not read them), 02000311 Buff_01, the Timer and SpecialTimer resets of 02020019 to 02020027 (nothing starts those timers), BossPhase 2 to object1 on 02020061 and 02020062 (after the dungeon ends), 02020111 Lapenta_Attack_Guide (the boss AI feeds the guide), 51000003 Round_06 (main moves the rounds on timers; normal_projectile_02 keeps its round 4 pattern), the id-less EventStart (six maps) and ExtraEventOff (82000002, 82000003) sets, and the 92 sets to an unknown id, which now do nothing. No data change: the ids were already imported.
- **spawn_npc_range random pick:** `random_pick_count` N spawns N different spawn ids of the range at random; without it the whole range spawns. The field spawned the whole range every time. The client has no trigger code, so the trigger data decides: all 61 placed actions that give it pick fewer than the range holds, and every id of those ranges is a placed one-npc spawn point, so the vip dungeon rounds of 02000385 and 02000393 put 64 monsters on the field every 10 seconds where the script asks for 3 or 4 (3 of 9 on 02000392, 02000432 and 52100002; 1 of 4 and 1 of 3 on 52010038; 3 of 20 on 99999908). These are timed waves that wait on no monster_dead of the range. The 58 actions without it still spawn the whole range. `is_auto_targeting` is still not used. No data change.
- **monster_in_combat:** the trigger condition is true when a monster of one of the listed spawn points is in battle. It skipped every monster with a spawn point id, so it was never true: the nine shipped uses (maps 02000066, 02000254, 02000313, 02000332, 02010055) wait on it to start a boss fight's timer, barricade or next state. Both it and `monster_dead` look at the friendly npcs of a spawn point as well as the monsters: 02000066 fails its waves on `monster_dead` of spawn point 99, the friendly barrier the players guard, which was never found among the monsters, so the script failed at the first wave and its `move_user map_id="0"` sent everyone back to their return map about 25 s after entering. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Monsters and friendly npcs fight:** a monster takes the battle npcs (friendly 1) of the field as targets, and they take the monsters, so the friendly npcs the scripts wait on with `monster_dead` can die. Friendly npcs only fought players' targets before, and monsters only players. Npcs carry an attack and a defense group (npcdata `npcAttackGroup`, `npcDefenseGroup`; the client only reads them, the fight is the server's); taken as bit sets, an npc of the other side is a target when its defense group shares a bit with the attacker's attack group. In the KR data monsters are 1/2 and battle npcs 2/1; the npcs of 2/4 (a cannon, a guarded object) are not hit by monsters, and the few monsters of 4/1 and 4/2 hit only those. A talk npc (friendly 2) is never a monster's target; pets are left as they were. Area skills hit the same: a monster's hostile skill hits players and the npcs it fights, a friendly npc's hits the monsters it fights. Three spawn points keep the old behaviour, an npc `monster_dead` does not wait on, as no monster can walk to them on the navmesh and nothing else ends them: 02000213 spawn point 201 (its first scene) and 02000349 and 52000076 spawn point 1098 (the fog). On 52010038 the core dummy (2097) now stands; its death is the fail, which came at once before. An npc tagged `no_die` (npc propertyTags; 392 npcs in the KR data, 183 of them friendly 1) is held at 1 health by damage and dots, so the story allies a script waits on with `npc_detected` (52000030 box 102, spawn points 1001 to 1014) stay; the client has no rule for the tag, so holding it at 1 is chosen here (the GM kill still kills it). A script marker, an npc of the Dummy model whose bar the client does not show (no `big_hp_bar` or `always_display_hp_bar`), is no monster's target: 02000350 runs each dark stream wave until its marker of 22 health goes (`monster_dead`), and 02000299 waits on four markers that the time machine scenes destroy. A Dummy with a bar is an object to guard and is hit (02000334 spawn point 200, its death the game over). A monster's AI node that targets Friendly picks among the monsters (its own side), as before; only a friendly npc's Friendly node is held to the groups. A friendly npc that a script walks along a path (trigger `MoveNpc`) keeps to it until the path ends: it takes no monster as a target on the way and lets go of one it has, and after a fight it walks on along the path instead of back to its spawn first. Since monsters and battle npcs fight, such an npc left its path for any monster in sight and the scripts waiting on its arrival (`npc_detected`) were held back, or never went on if it died: in the KR data 29 maps move a friendly npc where monsters spawn and 13 wait on its arrival (02000349 and 52000076 spawn points 2002 to 2006, boxes 127 to 136; 52000016 spawn point 101, boxes 9004 to 9006; 52010017 spawn point 1005, boxes 103 to 105; four of the walkers are not `no_die`). Chosen here (the fight between npcs is the server's): monsters may still hit the walker, and a monster on a script's path and an npc on the path it spawns with fight as before. Live try: not run (tests only). No data change.
- **npc_detected with negate and no spawn point:** the trigger condition ignored `negate` when it named no spawn point (any npc in the box). It now turns the answer around there too, as it does with spawn points. No trigger of the v1.3.2 data pack uses that form (208 uses, all with spawn points, 6 with negate), so nothing shipped changes. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **set_effect stagger:** a set_effect action changes the first effect of its list after `start_delay` ms and each next one `interval` ms later. The field changed them all at once and did not use either value. The client has no trigger code, so the trigger data decides: 43 of the 12,566 placed set_effect actions give either value, all in two quest dungeons. 52000150 turns on four rows of five effects with start delays 0, 200, 400 and 600 and interval 200, which makes one diagonal wave. 52000151 lights seven chains of 5 to 12 effects one at a time, 100 ms apart. All other placed actions give 0 for both and still change every effect at once. An effect that is already in the asked state is left alone when its turn comes. No data change.
- **quest_user_detected job code:** a quest_user_detected condition that gives `job_code` counts only players of that job; without it every player counts, as with user_detected. The field ignored the job, so the first branch that matched the quest won. The client has no trigger code, so the trigger data decides: 58 placed conditions give a job code, on 52000025 to 52000028 and 52000188. On 52000188 one state checks the same quest and state once for each of the 11 jobs, Knight first, and sends each job to its own scene, so every player got the Knight scene. On 52000025 to 52000028 each job code comes with that job's own job quest, so nothing changes there. No data change.
- **npc emotion sequence lists:** a set_npc_emotion_sequence that names a comma list (`Attack_01_C,Attack_Idle_A`) plays each name once, in order, and then the npc goes back to idle; a name the npc's rig lacks is skipped and logged. The field looked the whole list up as one name, logged "Invalid sequence" and played nothing. The client plays a trigger emotion list on the player the same way, one name per sequence end and idle after the last (StateTriggerEmotion::EndOfSequence @0x1417c47d0); an npc's animation comes from the server, so the server walks the list. 28 placed actions on 11 maps give a list (for example 52000099, 52000137, 52000150). A single name plays as before. No data change.
- **typing-game rounds:** the three dungeons with the typing game (02000533_bf, 02000535_bf, 02000555_bf) now play it. widget_action TypingGame Start sends the client its typing game (Trigger Ui 12: digit count and time in ms), and the client's answer (Trigger Ui 12 with int 1 or 0, from UITypingGameDialog SuccessGameProgress / FailGameProgress) becomes widget_value TypingGame Result; only the first answer to a started game counts. The answer was read as a guessed "Round" widget type and set GameClear or GameFail at once, the typing UI was never sent, and both widgets were the do-nothing base widget, so the game logic stopped before round 1. The client has no round widget, so the Round rules come from the scripts (SettingFinalRound 1, SettingAllowedFailCount 3, SettingRoundInitIfFail 0, three round states with harder digits and times): every result moves to the next round, one win clears, the third fail ends the game at round 3; RoundInitIfFail would send a fail back to round 1. widget_value conditions such as "= 2" or ">= 3" are now read with their operator; a value with no answer yet matches nothing. If no player answers (everyone leaves the field during a round), the round waits.
- **npc_is_dead_by_string_id:** the condition holds once a monster whose npc row carries that basic `stringID` has died on the field and none with it is still alive. The ingest now keeps the stringID (15 npc rows; the six Gigantika variants 23001001-23001006 share `Gigantika_01`). The one placed use is 02020051_bf's 104_end_1, which spawns one variant at random and waits on the boss death or the 600 s timer: the field always returned false, so killing the boss did nothing and the event ran until the timer. A row ingested before this change has no stringID and keeps the old behaviour (the timer ends it). Needs a re-ingest.
- **patrol_condition_user:** the action sends the players who carry its `additional_effect_id` along its patrol, as move_user_path does for every player; without an effect id every player moves. In 02020310_bf and 52100302_qd (with the test map 99999843) four interact objects each give one route buff (73000005-73000008), and field_5 sends each buff's holders along that route's MS2PatrolData_01-08; move_1-move_4 then wait for a holder in box 10001. The field only logged the action, so no one rode a route. `patrol_index` is not used (it repeats the path number).
- **Interact object npc spawns, npc_to_patrol_in_box:** an interact object's `spawn` list (59 KR objects, all mesh objects, placed 899 times on 21 maps: 51000001_dg, open fields such as 02000059_bf, 02000017_bf and 02000172_bf, the four survival maps 82000000-82000003 and 52000119_qd) spawned nothing. The client reads the list (`CInteractObjectData`, interactobjectdata.cpp:241) and never uses it, so the spawn is the server's. Rules of this server: each use rolls once in [0, 10000) and spawns the first entry whose running sum of `prob` passes the roll, so a single entry of 3000 spawns on 30% of uses and nothing on the rest; the entry's `count` npcs stand at random spots within its `radius`, put on the navmesh, or on the object itself with no radius; each leaves after `lifeTime` (milliseconds; 1000 and up, read as a summon's). `npc_to_patrol_in_box` (96 triggers on the survival maps, 201_boat-224_boat, run every 200 ms) only logged an error; it now puts each npc of `npc_id` in the box on the path, and a boat already on that path keeps its place on it. Its `spawn_id` is `interactObject` in every use: the npc a dock's object spawned (11000046 spawns boat 11400001 for 11 s on 100% of uses).
- **Air patrol speed:** an air waypoint was flown at half the patrol's speed times the npc's run speed, so the dock boats of 82000000 (patrols 201-224, speed 6, npc 11400001 with run 400) flew at 1,200 u/s and left the screen within about 3 s of a dock's use. The client plays the speed the server sends (each NpcControl entry gives the velocity the npc moves by until the next, CNpc::ApplySyncBlock and CNpc::vfunc_53), so the speed is the server's. An air waypoint is now flown at the patrol's speed times 100 u/s whatever the npc's own speeds (`PatrolRules.AirSpeed`): the boat flies its path 201 at 600 u/s and arrives after about 11 s, its spawn's lifeTime. Ground patrols are unchanged.
  - Chosen here: 100 u/s per patrol speed point. The dock boats' spawn lifeTimes (6.5-16 s) fit their rise and path at about 600 u/s plus the two waypoint waits (1 s and 1.666 s), and the balloon ride's speed 8 patrol of 22,415 units fits its 32 s window at 800 u/s.
- **Combine spawn groups on copy maps:** start_combine_spawn took its groups only from the combine spawn table's rows for the field's own map, so 82000000 (a copy of 82000001: the same 34 triggers, spawn points and region spawns) and 82000006 (a copy of 82000012) spawned none of their boxes and monsters: the table lists their groups under 82000001 and 82000012 only. Group ids are unique across the table, so a group the field's map does not list is now taken from the map that does (`CombineSpawnRules.Find`). On 82000000 the 336 groups give 278 monsters and 248 boxes, the counts the groups' combineCount allows. The first such group a field starts also turns its map treasure chests off, as they are off on the map it copies. A group with a combineCount above the spawn points it can pick, or with none, no longer loops or throws, and a reset only fills the gap to combineCount. The groups no table lists (82000002/3, 80000017-20, 99999840/1) are still skipped.
- **Combine spawn group points spawn only through their group:** every npc spawn point with a regen time filled itself at the first field update and refilled every 10 s, so on maps whose points belong to combine spawn groups all of them were up at once: 82000000 put up all 1188 points (749 + 439 monsters) before the survival game started, where its groups keep 278 up, and a point could refill outside its group. A spawn point named by an npc group of the field now spawns only when its group picks it (start_combine_spawn, then each group reset); other points are unchanged. The field's npc groups are its own map's groups or, on a map the table lists no group for, the groups its own trigger scripts start that `CombineSpawnRules.Find` takes (every point they name on the field). Of the 17 maps with group points, at load 9138 monsters before and 984 after (the points outside any group); their triggers then start groups that pick 1274 points. On 02020061 four points of group 483, which no trigger starts, no longer spawn (`CombineSpawnRules.GroupSpawnIds`).
- **Quest-masked npcs at their partner's point:** the ingest reads table/questmasknpc.xml as `CQuestMaskTable::LoadFile` keeps it (field, npc, enableInMask, the start/end quest windows; a repeated npc replaces its mask, a mask without a range or with start quest 0 is dropped, `<mask>` rows filtered by feature and locale). The client only hides or shows npcs that exist (`CProxyGameObject::UpdateQuestState`), so an enableInMask=1 npc that no spawn of its field places is now spawned at the first spawn point of the field's enableInMask=0 npc with the same windows, and the client shows one of the two by quest state. Chosen here: the shared point (no source gives the official one). In KR this places Karl 11004779 at the regular Karl 11000074's point in 02000065 (the lv75 Apocalypse epic, 10003408 on, was blocked); the other unplaced masked npcs have no such partner and stay unplaced. The copy takes no spawn id, so a trigger that names the partner's spawn id acts on the partner alone. A talk to a masked npc that the player's quests hide (the client sends none, so only a forged packet) is refused. Needs a re-ingest: without the table row the server does not start.
- **Quest npcs of the played client:** with `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` the ingest takes each quest's start and complete npc from the played client's `questdata/questdata.xml` (the `<start npc>` and `<complete npc>` of its feature-enabled `<quest>` rows, 0 when missing; `PlayedClientQuests.Npcs`) instead of the data folder's `quest/*.xml`. The client puts a quest's "!" and "?" on these npcs and offers the quest in their talk only (`CQuestManager::GetNpcQuestState`, client/ms2/questmanager_2.cpp:1884), so the server's talk and completion now follow the same npc. The log line of the quest mapper counts the changed quests. KR data folder against the KR client tried live: 7 differences, all in the lv75 Apocalypse epic (EpicApocalypse01): 10003408 completes at Karl 11000074, not 11004779, and 10003409, 10003410, 10003411 and 10003418 start (10003409 and 10003410 also complete) there. The played client has no 02000065 mask, so 11004779 is not spawned there and before this the talk to 11000074, which showed the "?", gave only the plain line. Without the setting nothing changes and the previous bullet's partner spawn still places 11004779. Needs a re-ingest.
  - Chosen here: a map with groups of its own takes no other map's points, as 02020062 has the point ids of 02020061's group 483; and a map without takes only the groups its triggers start, as group point ids are small and reused: 02000402 (points 501-504), 52000200 (501-508) and 63000050 (201-208) hold the ids of other maps' groups but start no group, so their points fill themselves as before. 82000002/3 start only groups no table lists, so theirs do too; they and 02000532 and 99999906, which share such ids, are not ingested (their map features are off).
  - A group refills its picks only at its resetTick, not on the points' own regen. The field war groups of 02020061-02020063 (Fieldwar01) have 18000000 ms (5 h) for their waves, which the battle triggers start one after another and stop at the object phase of a round of 180 s timers: a killed wave monster stays dead for the round and the next wave comes from the next group. Only the started groups with 20000 ms (482, 488 and 494) refill within a round, every 20 s.
  - Chosen here: a group from another map is taken only when every spawn point (monster groups) or region spawn (box groups) it names is on the field; any other group is skipped.
  - Chosen here: a fill stops at the group's distinct points and after 100 draws per spawn it still has to put up (`SpawnGroupRules.DrawsPerSpawn`), logging a warning when its draws run out; a pick already up is drawn again and a point of weight 0 is never drawn, so the old loop could run for ever on the field thread. Of the 756 KR groups one names fewer points than its totalCount (02020051 group 1003: 38 for 37 points, all 37 go up) and none needs a weight-0 point to reach it.
- **Item spawn points with a shared id:** 02000360_bf and 63000033_cs (the same entities) carry EventSpawnPointItem14 and EventSpawnPointItem15 both with spawn id 14, and loading the map entities threw on the second, so neither field could be made. Chosen here: the point whose entity name sorts first keeps the id (EventSpawnPointItem14), whatever order the rows come back in, and the other is left out (`MapEntityStorage.AddEventItemSpawn`); the burn-item trigger's create_item of ids 1-87 skips 15, which no point has. No other KR xblock fails to load.
- **Trigger coverage, settled:** dungeon_variable and set_dungeon_variable are built, and start_mini_game_round sends nothing on purpose (see Mini-game round start); a coverage run counts them as done.
- **room_expire:** the action sends every player in the room back to their return map, as move_user with map 0 does. Only the wedding rooms use it: the photo studios 84000003-84000005_wd (after the 300 s timer 4000 or the exitstudio user value, guide summary 28400138 shows for 5 s, then the room expires) and the minigame rooms 84000006_wd and 84000007_wd. The field only logged the action, so the players stayed in the room after it ended. Each of these maps has itself as its enterReturnId, so entering one saves the room as the newest return map and a plain return would load the room again: the room's own return entries are dropped first (the default return map when none is left). The server has no way into these rooms yet (no studio or minigame entry from the hall), so this runs only for a player sent there another way. The player list is copied first, as each return removes a player from the field.
- **AI nodes not built yet:** these AI nodes still do nothing; each names what is missing.
  - `ride` (24 nodes in 3 files: `type="slave"`, `isRideOff`, `rideNpcIDs` 23000097, 23000098, 23000105, 23000106; the Blackbean boss summons a car npc and gets on it). Missing: the client has no way to put an npc on another npc. Its ride (ResponseRide mode 0, CNpc included) always makes a new Ridee from a riding table row named by the ride action's first int (ReadRideOnAction types 0 to 3, then GetRideeData). The four car ids are npcs (npcdata 230, kfm 23000097_B_CarParadeBlackbean) with no riding row, and the npc sync block has no rider or vehicle field. So what the server should send for this node is not known.
- **PvP maps:** in a map of type 9 an item's enchant and limit break count for defense and weapon attack only, and defense keeps 0.159 of them, as the client's own totals do. A limit break's special options (melee and ranged damage) count in every other map and not there.
- **Open PvP on pk fields (Outlaw's Overlook, Mushtopia):** a field whose map has pkMode set is open PvP, the client's rule (`PC::vfunc_122`, pc.cpp:3112): another player is hostile unless it is the player itself, both are in the same party, or either holds a buff with BasicProperty pvpOnOff (now ingested, `AdditionalEffectMetadataProperty.PvpOnOff`, needs a re-ingest; the client ORs it over the enabled buffs, `AdditionalCliList::FUN_140972a00`, and the server keeps it in the buff sums, so a hit reads one bool). KR has it on 70000032 (the shadow world's move protection) and 70000029. Before, players never hit each other on these fields. The KR pk fields are 03000059 (Outlaw's Overlook), the Mushtopia maps 82000000, 82000001, 82000006 and 82000012, and the test maps 99999919, 99999920 and 99999970; the 19 shadow world maps of continent 202 (03000003 and on, type 11) set pkMode 1 in their base row, but their KR environment row sets 0 and wins under KR (checked in a KR ingest). Under NA the base row stays, so NA has 20 open-PvP fields on continent 202 (the 19 shadow world maps and 03000059) and the squad maps 82000002 and 82000003 are pk fields too. The rule's vetoes (party, pvpOnOff) also hold over a field game on a pk field, so a pvpOnOff holder is no enemy in the Maple Survival game on the Mushtopia maps either. A kill on a pk field outside a field game counts shadow_world_kill for the killer and shadow_world_die for the victim (trophies 21300001 and 21300002), and sends the field the kill log (PlayerKillNotice 0, killer and victim). Chosen here: while a field game runs (a PvP zone, survival, rank duel or Hide and Seek) only its own rule makes players hostile and counts kills (the client's rule knows no field game), so the waiting area before CreateFieldGame is open PvP as in the client. The killer's and the victim's chat lines are the client's own, printed from the kill log (`UIPvPKillLogDialog::AddKill`, strings 0x973 and 0x972); the server sent PlayerKillNotice 1 notices for them too, which doubled each line, and no longer does. PlayerKillNotice 1 carries the streak notices instead: the client shows its text as a system notice with the System_KillSlayer sound (`CSceneStateGame::OnPlayerKillNotice`), and no client code uses s_shadowworld_player_kill_slayer or _ruler (client ids 2411, 2412), so the server sends them, at the counts of the server constants userKillSlayerCount (5) and userKillRulerCount (10), only on a field of the shadow world continent (202: under KR 03000059 and the test maps 99999919 and 99999920; the Mushtopia maps have no continent and get none), as the strings are the shadow world's. Chosen here, as the free-for-all's slayer and ruler: the streak is the killer's pk kills since its last death in the field, the field gets the notice with the killer's name when the streak reaches a count (the ruler first); userKillDuration (10000) is not used, nothing says what it times. A hostile skill's targets on a pk field are the mobs and the players the rule makes hostile (`GetTargetPool`, which bounces and steps share), and a friendly skill (a heal or a buff) reaches only the players the rule does not make hostile, as the client's target filter takes a friendly target only while the hostility test gives 0 (`FilterTarget`, basecollision.cpp:784); chosen here: in a field game off a pk field a friendly skill still reaches every player, as before. A region skill's hit counts for its caster. Not done: no item or currency drops at a pk death (the client source names no pk death drop); the death is the field's plain death. A damage-over-time tick of another player that takes the last health is that player's kill, as a hit is (kill log, trophies, streak); chosen here, the kill credit is the server's and the client source has no rule for it. Before, such a death credited no one and did not count toward the streak. A kill by a pet's skill still credits no one. A death on 03000059 has no tombstone and no revive box and ends in a revive about 9 s later: that is the field's own rule, autoRevivalType 1 with autoRevivalTime 9 in its field data; on a type 1 field the client opens no revive dialog (`MyPC::vfunc_137`, mypc.cpp:1351) and plays the revive animation instead of the tombstone (`StateDead::vfunc_4`, statedead.cpp:122), and the server revives the player at the end of autoRevivalTime (FieldPlayer.AutoReviveDelayMs; the client only tests the time for > 0, so the length is the server's reading of the data). The 5 s 70000032 after each such revive is the server constant shadowWorldBuffMoveProtect: a revive in place gives the field-entry buffs again (Buffs after a revive), and a ShadowWorld region field's entry gives shadowWorldBuffHpUp and shadowWorldBuffMoveProtect; chosen here: kept, so a revived player is not hit again at once (the client source has no revive rule for it).
- **PvP queue requests (Pvp 0x61):** the rank duel and the individual arena have a queue and a match, see "Rank duel" and "Individual arena". An arena whose round the server does not build gets the client's own way out of the queue, Pvp 10 (state 0; `CSceneStateGame::OnPvp` @0x141732350; the client has no text for a refused arena register).
  - Not built, with what the client sends: guild PvP register 1 and unregister 2 (guild window; the client checks the season, the championship flag and the member's right first), 3 and 4 (`CGuildManager::SendPVPMode3`/`4`), the result windows' close 104 and 110. None has a refusal the client reads, so they get no answer. The guild match packets (Pvp 114, 117; TeamPvp; Gvg 2 and up; FieldWar 0; LegionBattle) are not sent. A duel between two players in a field has no request of its own in this client: UserBattle (0x2F) is a player's in-combat flag.
  - Guild PvP (the guild championship) cannot be reached with the KR client, so it is not built. The guild window sends the register (Pvp 1) only when its guild season is current (`Unk_GuildSeason::FUN_1404c6160`: begin < now < end), the GuildChampionship game event is on (`CGameRule::IsGuildChampionshipOpen`, set by `GameEventGuildChampionship`) and the member has right 0x100 (uiguilddialog.cpp OnRegistPVP; only the client's debug mode skips the checks). The guild season is the client's own SeasonData_GuildPvP row of now (`CGuildInfo::UpdateSeason`, SeasonRankType 2), and the played KR client's table/kr/seasondata_guildpvp.xml ends 2017-05-30, so the register box always refuses and no server answer changes that (the server pack's table/na copy runs monthly seasons to 2030, which the KR client does not read). The GuildChampionship event is refused by the client too: `GameEventGuildChampionship::OnStart` (gameevent.cpp:3109) sets the flag only with feature 0x110 (GuildPvP, level 100 in feature.xml), and no server type reaches 100 in feature_setting.xml (KR Live 56, Dev and Qa 99; `CFeatureManager::IsFeatureEnabled`, featuremanager.cpp:845), so a sent event would not open the page either; unregister (2), join (3, `CGuildManager::SendPVPMode3`: guild PvP state 2 and the player's guild as the target) and the champion check (4, `SendPVPMode4`) all follow a register. What the client would settle if it were reachable: Guild 0x27 (the guild's PvP state byte), 0x28 (matched bool; a 30 s join box or the champion guild confirm), 0x29 (score, wins, losses), 0x2a (battle result: won bool and the other guild's name); Pvp 4 (the target guild, `SetTargetGuildAndNotify`), 'r' (guild A and B: id, name, mark; `UIPvPGuildTeamDialog`), 'u' (`UIPvPGuildTeamResultDialog::SetGuildBattleResult`); TeamPvp 0x9A 's' (round, end time, an int, the round's character ids, then each guild's id and score) and 't' (the round's winning guild, 0 a draw); zone type 4 is set only by the guild arena 64000001 (boxes 102, 103, 112, 113, 10, 11, 1, 3).
  - Out of scope for KR (no current guild season in the client's data, feature 0x110 off; the event, 99000037, is in the data but not ingested): Pvp 1 to 4 both ways, and the trophy conditions of the guild arena and the championship (in `ConditionType`, never raised): guildpvp_kill, guildpvp_die and guildpvp_win (21300009-21300011, code 64000001, the guild arena) and guild_championship (21300012, and 90000006 with target 1, 20 times). The KR data has no guildchampionship.xml, its seasondata_guildpvp.xml ends with season 7 (2017-05-30), and `CGameRule::IsGuildChampionshipOpen` (uiguilddialog.cpp:1010, guildmanager.cpp:222) is set only by the GuildChampionship game event (`GameEventGuildChampionship`, gameevent.cpp:3113, gamerule.cpp:2608).
- **Individual arena (Pvp 9, 10, 11, 12; set_pvp_zone types 1, 2, 3, 5, 6):** The Crimson Tear Mine (65000002) holds its players on a platform above the mine (box 102, the player spawn's box; the map's buff script gives box 102 effect 70000040 every second, which keeps job skills off) while its zone is box 101, the whole map, and no script action takes them down: when a free-for-all zone starts, each participant still in the box of the map's player spawn (other than the zone's own box) is moved to a random navmesh spot within 1500 (chosen) of the map's portal inside the zone and outside that box (65000002: portal 2 on the mine floor). The arenas' exp rows (redDuelWin, redDuelLose, bloodMineRank1 to 3, bloodMineRankOther) are read at the player's level, as the rank duel's; before they gave nothing ("Unhandled ExpType"). The arena window's register refusal "Battle applications have been temporarily disabled" (string 0x429) is the client's own check for additional effect 100000007 on the player (UIIndividualPvPDialog, UIRankPvPDialog); the server sends no such state, and no server code, script or table gives that effect. the KR client has no way to open the arena window (`UIIndividualPvPDialog`, 0x4ed1, the only sender of Pvp 9): no OpenDialog of 0x4ed1, no game menu entry (the menu's individualbattle opens the rank duel window 0x4f04, gameeventmanagercli.cpp), and no link to IndividualPvPDialog in the client's tables or scripts; the Trigger UI state packet (0x14, `CTriggerService::FUN_141862d30`) only shows or hides a dialog by name. So the queue below is reached only by a client that opens the window. the arena window's register (9 with the arenaType of table/individualarena.xml, now ingested), unregister (10) and the individualArenaJoin box's yes (11, `CPVPManager::SendCancelRequest`) go to a second World queue next to the rank duel's; a character is in one of the two at a time. From the client: register is answered with Pvp 9 and the arena (state 1, its queued notice), unregister and every refusal with Pvp 10 (state 0), a match with Pvp 11 and the arena (state 2, the yes/no box with its 25 s countdown, `CSceneStateGame::OnPvp`). The join moves the character into a new room of the arena's fieldID (PvpMatch move). The round is the map's own script: the Crimson Tear Mine (65000002) waits for its players, plays its intros and sets a PvP zone (set_pvp_zone 101, prepare 5 s, 300 s, effect 90001002, type 2), then moves everyone out once pvp_zone_ended. The zone's type is the client's PvP mode (CPvPInfo m_nMode): 2 is the free-for-all the client ranks and rewards (PvpZoneField); types 1 and 3 are below. From the client: each participant gets its PvP point, team and mode (Pvp 0x64, `CPvPInfo::ReadPointTeamMode`; two players with points above 0 that differ are enemies on a map without PK mode, `PC::vfunc_122`/`vfunc_116`); the ranking rows (0x67 character, score, kills, deaths, an int the free-for-all order does not read; 0x6a damage), cleared first (0x66); the kill log (PlayerKillNotice 0 with the killer and the victim, `UIPvPKillLogDialog::AddKill`); at the end each participant's rank (0x68, the result dialog) and the win or loss pose (0x65). The result dialog's reward is the client's: PvpFFAReward<rank>Count (plus PvpFFAAdditionRewardRate per kill) of PvpFFARewardItem for ranks 1 to 10, PvpFFAReward10Count after (`UIPvPResultDialog::FUN_141344210`); the server gives that, and the exp of the client's commonexptable rows bloodMineRank1 to 3 and bloodMineRankOther. A kill is worth ModePvPScoreKill (100) and a death ModePvPScoreDead (-50) (server constants); the order is the client's (score, kills, fewer deaths, damage, then the higher id; those still in the field first). The zone's effect (90001002) is given to the participants when the fight starts. The arena row's rewardItemId1 (40100023) is given with reward1Winner to first place and reward1Loser to the others (the arena window shows both).
  - Chosen here: a match takes the arena's playerCount of the longest waiting characters as soon as that many wait, or all of them (at least 2) once the longest has waited 30 s; a match nobody joined in 60 s, or any match after 20 minutes, is dropped. The participants are the players in the zone's box when the fight starts, with points from 101 up (as KMS2's one recorded free-for-all numbers them), team 0; a death counts as a kill only for the player that hit the victim last within 10 s; every participant gets a rank; the arena row's winner reward goes to the zone's winners (the free-for-all's first place, the Red Arena's champion, the winning team), the loser reward to the others. The zones' rewards (tokens, items, exp) are paid only in an arena match's field (apart from the one punch round's tokens, below): the guild and test maps that set the same zones (66000003, 66000004, 99999905) run them without rewards. All three arenas are queued (their zones are built, see below). A register for another arena while queued moves the character to it (its wait starts over); a character of a made match that has not joined 60 s after the match (a no on the box, or the box ran out) is let go with Pvp 10 and can queue again, the match goes on for the others. When every match character has left an arena field before its zone ended, or the field goes, the World lets the match go. A death counts as a kill only for a player that hit the victim in the last 10 s; a participant that comes back is shown to the others with its point again; a participant of a running zone revives with the server constants' PvP revive buff (ModePvPReviveBuff, level 1) and invincible time (ModePvPInvincibleTime, the longer of it and the plain revive's). Logging in on, or a plain move to, an arena map goes to the return field, as for the rank duel maps.
  - The arena window after a match: the client's arena state (`CPVPManager`, the window's Register, Unregister or Enter button, `UIIndividualPvPDialog`) leaves 2 (the match found) only on Pvp 10, and nothing sent it to a character that joined; so after the match the window showed Enter, whose join found no match, and a register was refused by the client's own state check (string 0x428, the same text as 0x429) until a relaunch. A match character now gets Pvp 10 when it enters the match field (`ArenaMatchField.Enter`). Chosen here: the entry (nothing in the client shows when the original sent it); a register from inside the match field is refused with Pvp 10 as before. Maple Survival keeps its own window state.
  - Pvp 12 (`CGameRule::ReadValues`: a count byte, then key and value ints): the arena window shows the values as arenaType and wait in milliseconds (`UIIndividualPvPDialog::UpdateWaitTimeLabels`; 0 the unknown wait, above 10 minutes its own text, arena 2 skipped). Chosen here: sent after each arena register and unregister, every arena of the table with its longest current wait (0 for an empty queue: the client keeps a value until it is sent again). The kill streak notice (Pvp 0x69, a system notice with the killer's name) takes the client's string ids 2421 s_pvp_ffa_double_kill, 2422 triple_kill, 2423 slayer_kill and 2424 ruler_kill (`CommonString`, commonstringtable.cpp); chosen here: a kill within pvpFFALongComboTick of the killer's last grows its combo (2 a double, 3 or more a triple kill), and the killer's streak since its last death reaching pvpFFASlayerCount or pvpFFARulerCount gives those notices instead.
  - Red Arena (zone type 1, the client's mode 1): "the hero who survives all of the random face-offs to the very end" (its script's text). From the client: the PvP string category's s_dualprepareplayer (10), s_dualstartcombat (11) and s_dualwin1 to 9 ("{0} wins! / a 2nd time / ... / defeated all challengers"), sent as MassiveEvent texts of kind 3 (`StringIdInterface::FUN_141024e60`); each fighter its own point in mode 1 (101 and 102, see Rank duel). The zone's box is the stage, not where the players wait: 65000001's script (enter.xml) holds everyone on the entry grate (box 101) and sets the zone on box 102, the stage around portal 1. So the participants are every player in the field when the fight starts (before, the players in the empty stage box: the zone ended at once with no champion), and each face-off's two fighters are moved onto the stage (MoveByPortal near the map's portal inside the zone's box at the box's centre height, else the box's centre: 65000001's portal 1 lies under the stage floor, 2875 against 3300, and box 102's centre, 3350, is just above it; 150 either side along X (chosen), put on the nearest navmesh point when the navmesh has one there, else sent as is: a move the navmesh check refused was dropped without a word, so nobody reached the stage); a face-off the time ends sends both back to a player spawn. Chosen here: the first face-off is two of them at random, each next one the winner against a random participant that has not fought; announced 3 s before it starts, at most 30 s long; a fighter's death or departure makes the other the winner (s_dualwin with its win count, 1 to 8), the time running out leaves both out; the zone ends when no challenger is left (s_dualwin9 for the champion) or its 120 s end; the champion gets the win pose, redDuelWin exp and the arena row's winner reward, the others the loss pose, redDuelLose exp and the loser reward. The script's end banners (enter.xml after pvp_zone_ended: WINNER, event ui type 3, to box 102, then Bonus, type 6, to everyone off it) go by position only, and a fighter beaten by a death lies on the stage until the map's auto revival (3 s) moves it to the player spawn on box 101; so pvp_zone_ended waits until no beaten participant in the field is dead, at most 10 s (chosen), while the poses, exp and rewards come at once. Before, the last loser got the champion's WINNER (client: the WinLose packet, Pvp 0x65, only plays the pose, `CSceneStateGame::OnPvp`). The redDuel exp (commonexp.xml: redDuelWin factor 25, redDuelLose 5, exp table 1 at the character's level) is logged per participant.
  - Treasure Island (zone type 3, the client's mode 3): two teams by PvP point, red 201 (0xc9) and blue 202 (0xca), as the client's name tags, minimap and team tables key them.
