From the client: the team result dialog's reset (Pvp 0x6b), the scores (0x6c), each player's row (0x6d, shown as kills and deaths), the result with the winning team and the reward count shown to the player (0x6e, of the client constant pvpBtiRewardItem: pvpBtiRewardWinnerCount or LoserCount), the red team's gauge (0x6f, tenths of a percent), "the <team> is capturing the <north/center/south> flag" (0x70) and "<name> plundered the <team>'s treasure" (0x71). The server gives the reward the dialog shows and the btiTeamWin or btiTeamLose exp. Chosen here: the zone's extra boxes (1, 2, 101, 102, 103) are the red treasure, the blue treasure and the north, center and south flags; the participants (the players in the zone's box when it starts, in random order) are dealt red, blue, red...; a flag goes to a team whose living players hold its box alone for 5 s and gives it 1 point every 5 s; a living player that enters the other team's treasure box plunders it for 5 points, once per entry and at most once per 30 s (a dead body neither plunders nor holds a flag); a participant that comes back gets the team result dialog's reset (0x6b) last; a kill gives 1 point; the higher score wins, equal scores are a draw (team 0, the loser's count to all); the gauge is the red team's share of both scores.
  - One punch round (zone type 5, the client's mode 5; 61000035, the MassivePVPDollshouse event map): 61000035_me's script sets zone 701 (prepare 5 s, 240 s, no effect, type 5) once at least 4 players are in box 701 (fewer: it times out and moves everyone out), its buff script keeps the ride transform 99910220 on the box. The zone is the free-for-all's (points, rows, kill log, ranks, poses, pvp_* conditions with code 61000035) in mode 5, which the client's minimap ranks as mode 2 (`uiminimapentity.cpp`, `CPvPInfo::IsOnePunchMode`). From the client: the reward is PvpOnePunchReward<rank>Count (9, 8, 7, 6, 5, 4, 3, 3, 2, 2) of PvpOnePunchRewardItem (30001533) for ranks 1 to 10, the tenth's count after, with no kill addition (`UIPvPResultDialog::FUN_141344210`, `CPvPInfo::GetRewardItemId`); the server gives it on 61000035 although that is no arena match's field (only 61000035 sets type 5), with no blood mine exp. Farm guard: the tokens need at least 4 participants when the fight starts (the script's own minimum) and go only to participants still in the field at the end. 61000035_me_item's round sets type 6: the same zone in mode 6, which the client's result dialog gives no count (0), so no tokens. Chosen here: kills and deaths score ModePvPScoreKill and ModePvPScoreDead as in the free-for-all (the data has no one punch player score); type 6 runs the zone at all. A full bag drops the reward with a warning, as the free-for-all's. Not built: PvpOnePunchScoreNpcKill (-50; the data does not say whether it is for killing one of the round's dummies, spawned with score 100, or for dying to an npc) and PvpOnePunchUserOpenRewardItem (20301204, the reward of the player who opened the event; no opener is known).
  - The fight's start countdown: MassiveEvent 8 shows the client's s_pvp_start step message and counts its seconds down with the count sound (`UIMassiveEvent::FUN_1419f6790`, uimassiveevent.cpp:301, ShowStepMessage type 2). Nothing sent it. Chosen here: when set_pvp_zone sets a zone of type 2, 3, 5 or 6 with a prepare time, the field gets 8 with the prepare seconds (65000002: 5 s; 61000035: 5 s); the Red Arena (type 1) keeps its own s_dualprepareplayer and s_dualstartcombat texts. Not sent: MassiveEvent 4 (a round countdown with a client string id, the same step message as mode 1), since the trigger countdowns carry the script's own text, which is mode 1, and no server event has a string id for one; 5 and 6 (a string-id text with the win sounds or the fail sound, ShowMessage 2 and 1), since no client code names the event or the string they carry, and the Red Arena's s_dualwin texts already go as kind 3 texts (mode 3).
  - Not built: the combo ticks' short tick (pvpFFAShortComboTick, 1000 ms next to the long tick's 10000 ms in the server constants): no client code reads either tick, the client only shows the four notices by string id, and the two notice levels (double, triple) are already covered by the long tick, so what a 1 s window would add is a guess and it stays unused; zone type 4 (the guild arena 64000001: its script takes the teams from the guild PvP register, which the KR client cannot reach, see the guild PvP line above; set_pvp_zone logs it at Debug and the script waits in its end state), the arena row's second reward item (no row has one). The guild maps 66000003 and 66000004 and the test map 99999905 set zones of types 1 and 3 too; they run there the same way (no arena match, so no arena reward).
- **Rank duel (Pvp 14, 15, 16):** a 1v1 matchmaker in the World server and a match field per match. From the client (`CPvpRankDuelComponent::OnMessage`, pvprankduelcomponent.cpp; `UIRankPvPDialog`; `UIPvPRankDuelResultDialog::FUN_140f90b10`): register (14) is answered with Pvp 14 (state 1, the unregister button); unregister (15) with Pvp 15 (state 0); a match with Pvp 16 true (state 2, the s_rank_duel_matching_complete box with its 15 s countdown, whose button sends 16, the join; the box has no refuse button, and when its countdown ends the client sends the same 16 itself: `UIMessageBox` OnPopupTimerComplete gives the listener message 2002, and `UIRankPvPDialog::OnMessage` answers it for the rankArenaJoin box with `RequestCancelMatch`. So a duelist who never presses Enter Now still joins 15 s after the box, as the client does); a refusal or a match that ends without the character with Pvp 16 false (s_rank_duel_matching_fail, state 0); a register from inside a rank duel field with Pvp 17 and 0 (s_rank_duel_ui_error_in_arena_field). The join moves the character into the match field (a new migration type, PvpMatch, with the channel, map and room). In the field: the round intro Pvp 27, the fight start 26, the win 28, lose 29 and draw 30 intros (the HUD UIEventIntroduce, uieventintroduce.gfx). Each int is how long the intro stays, in ms: the movie's addMovie arms setTimeout(onTimeOut, ms), which plays the intro's "out", so 0 shows nothing. 27 has two ints that the client passes to onRound the other way round; onRound draws the first as the round number and times the movie with the second, so the wire has the time first, then the round. Chosen: round 2.5 s, fight 1.5 s, win/lose/draw 3 s. Then the result dialog 18 (the winner, then per side the name, character, rounds won, rating, rating change and valor tokens) and Pvp 15 so the character can register again. The rank duel maps are the ten whose environment feature is PvPRankingDuelMode (65010001 to 65010010); one the server can load is picked at random. During the fight the two duelists are hostile targets of each other's skills (the server picks the targets); nobody else in the field is.
  - Chosen here (the client and the data do not settle them): the queue pairs by rating: the longest waiting character first, with the waiting character of the closest rating inside the gap its wait allows, which starts at the server constant rankDuelPvpMatchingMinGap and grows by it every rankDuelPvpMatchingTime seconds (the shipped values, 10000 and 2, pair any two at once); the World tries again every 5 s. The match field is made on the first channel, as dungeon rooms are. One round. The fight starts 3 s after both are in and lasts 180 s; both standing at the end is a draw (intro only: the result dialog fills nothing without a winner). A duelist who dies in the fight, or leaves the field before the result, loses; one who has not come 40 s after the field was made loses to one who has; with nobody there the match is void. A match nobody joined within 45 s, or any match after 15 minutes, is dropped by the World, and a character that never joined gets Pvp 16 false. 10 s after the result the duelists go back to their return field (on the match's channel, as after a dungeon), to the spot they joined from when that was the return field: the field they stand in, not the character's saved map, which an instanced field (a dungeon hall, a channelScale map) leaves at the field it was entered from, so a join from a hall had kept the hall's spot for the town (`DungeonHallRules.KeepsReturnPosition`, as the hall exit). A queued character leaves the queue on logout; a login on a rank duel map goes to the return field. A plain move to a rank duel map never lands in a running match, and a match room that is gone is not made again: a rank duel arrival whose field cannot be made enters as a plain arrival through the same fallbacks (the return field, then the default one). A duelist who arrives after the result (a late join, a slow load) gets Pvp 15 and goes back to its return field at once (once: the exit 10 s after the result leaves it alone); a match field that has sent its duelists out takes nobody. A register or a join is refused (Pvp 16 false) while dead, from a dungeon, from a room of a solo, dungeon lobby, event, game maker, guild event, guild PvP, guild vs game, ranking PvP, Maple Survival (solo or squad) or field war instance (open fields, channelScale fields, homes, guild houses and wedding halls are allowed) and, for the join, from a rank duel field; a register of a character that is queued already keeps it queued (state 1), one in a match gets Pvp 16 false. A join whose move fails gets Pvp 16 false (the match counts the character as not come). When the channel of a match field goes down the World ends its matches at once.
  - Rating, grades, rewards and season (a re-ingest for the grade table and the migration AddRankDuelRecord): each character keeps its rank duel record (the tables rank-duel-record and rank-duel-job-record): the PvP season it belongs to, the rating, the season's best rating, the valor tokens won this week and the season's wins and losses by the opponent's job code. From the client: Pvp 22 is the season (league), the rating, the season's best and the weekly tokens (CPvpRankDuelComponent::OnMessage 0x16; the dialog shows the weekly tokens against its grade's weeklyRewardMax, UIRankPvPDialog::UpdateOwnRecord); Pvp 23 is the rows of three ints the client reads as job code, wins and losses (0x17 through CIngredientInfo::Read; the statistics page keys its rows by job code 1, 10 to 110 and sums them for the season's wins); the grade of a rating is the highest grade of table/pvprankingduelmode.xml whose gradeRating is at most the rating, grade 1 below it (CRankingArenaDataTable::GetGradeOfRating); the current season is the row of SeasonData_PvP (SeasonRankType 1) whose span holds the time, 0 when none does (CSeasonDataTable::Refresh), and the client starts its rating display over at 1000 when Pvp 22 names another season (RefreshLeague). Pvp 22 and 23 are sent at each game server entry, as KMS2 does, and after each result. A result moves each duelist's rating by its own grade's winnerRating or loserRating and gives its grade's winnerReward or loserReward in valor tokens (90000006, the result dialog's reward), as far as weeklyRewardMax leaves room this week, and the exp of the client's commonexptable rows rankDuelWin and rankDuelLose at the duelist's level; the result dialog shows the new rating, the change and the tokens. The weekly tokens go back to 0 at the weekly reset.
  - Chosen here: a record of another season starts over (rating and best 1000, no job rows) when it is read; the rating does not go below 0; a duelist who left or never came gets the rating change but no tokens and no exp; a draw changes nothing; the table rows of pvpExpTable.xml (server side) are not used, the client's commonexptable is. With the played KR client's season table (its last PvP season ends 2023-01-12) the season today is 0 on both sides.
  - The duelists are each other's enemies in the client too: when the fight starts each gets its own PvP point (Pvp 0x64; points 101 and 102, team 0, mode 0. Not 1 and 2: a PC with a point takes it as its team (`PC::vfunc_116`, 1 without one), and the client's hit test for skill targets (basecollision.cpp @0x1409ff8d0) never hits a target of team 2 and lets an attacker of team 2 hit anyone, so with 1 and 2 only the second duelist could hit and nobody could hit it. The Red Arena's face-offs use 101 and 102 too), back to 0 at the result, and the server constants' rank duel arena buffs (ModePVPDuelRankArenaAdditionalEffect, level 1) are given at the fight's start (when is chosen here). A result's two records are written in one save: when it fails neither changes and nobody is rewarded. The weekly reset sends the rank duel window's record again. The season at the end's own second is still the ending one, as the client holds it (it moves on only past the end); the played client's season file is read as the client reads it (every row, a season listed twice keeps its last row).
  - Watching a rank duel: when a duel's fight starts the World offers it to the players outside a PvP field (Pvp 0x19, no body; KMS2 sends it to players in their homes too); the client shows its s_rank_duel_matching_complete_to_observe box only in state 0 with its observe option on, and the box's yes sends the join (Pvp 16, `CPvpRankDuelComponent::RequestCancelMatch`). A join from a character with no match of its own moves it into the duel's field as an onlooker: Pvp 0x13 false (the client puts its player at the origin with state flag 1) and the duel's observer dialog (Trigger dialog kind 5, `CTriggerService::FUN_141862110` read by FUN_140743f50: per duelist the character, health, total, rounds won and rating, then a camera step); at the result the dialog closes (character ids 0) and 0x13 true, and the onlookers go out with the duelists. The Red Arena's other players get the face-off's observer dialog (kind 2, FUN_140743ca0: per fighter the character, health, total, wins) when it starts, closed at its result; the client follows the fighters' health itself (`PC::vfunc_74` for players with a PvP point). An onlooker takes no part: its skills reach no player and nobody's skills, friendly or hostile, reach it; it gets the duelists' points (Pvp 0x64) on arrival, so its client follows their health into the dialog. The offer goes only to players whose join would not be refused (not dead, not in a dungeon or another refused room). Chosen here: the duel watched is the one whose fight started last; the dialog's ratings are read once, when the fight starts; the camera step is 0; an onlooker that arrives after the result goes back at once; the dialogs last the face-off's or the fight's longest time.
  - Not built, and what is missing: best-of rounds (no round count in the client or the data: 0x1b only passes the counts it is given; resetting health and placing the duelists between rounds is not settled), the perfect intro (31, when it is shown is not settled), the rank duel ranking board and season-end rewards.
- **PvP quest and trophy conditions:** the pvp_* conditions (the client's conditionutil.cpp registers pvp_win, pvp_kill, pvp_die, pvp_participation, pvp_win_score, pvp_win_time, pvp_win_with_buff, pvp_win_perfect and pvp_win_with_grade and checks none itself) were never raised, so trophies 21300003-21300041 and the rank duel quests (70400006, 70700062, 71000133, 89010057) never moved. In the KR data no pvp_win, pvp_kill, pvp_die, pvp_participation or pvp_win_perfect row (94) has a target; the four kinds with one are checked against it. The Red Arena, the free-for-all, Treasure Island and the rank duel now raise them, with the map of the fight as the code (the data's codes: 65000001-65000003, 61000035 and the rank duel maps 65010001-65010010; a row without a code, 21300014, counts on every map; `PvpConditions`, `ConditionUtil`). pvp_kill and pvp_die come with a death in the fight (a kill only for the player that hit the victim last within the kill-credit window, 10 s: the free-for-all and Treasure Island through their ranking, the Red Arena face-off and the rank duel for the other fighter's last hit, so a fall or a trap is no kill; `KillCredit`); pvp_win goes to the free-for-all's first place, the Red Arena's champion, the winning team and the duel's winner. Treasure Island's win adds pvp_win_score with the lead in points and pvp_win_time with the fight's seconds, each holding at the target or below (21300018: a win by at most 50 points, target 50; 21300019: a win inside 5 minutes, target 300). A rank duel win adds pvp_win_perfect at full health (21300036), pvp_win_with_buff once per effect held (21300035, target 90001010, the rage effect that 90001009 casts below 25% health on map category 9) and pvp_win_with_grade with the grade of the new rating when the record was saved (21300037-21300041 ask for grades 3 to 7, gold to grand champion, the ids of the grade name strings and of pvprankingduelmode). Chosen here: participation counts when the fight starts, a higher grade holds a grade target too, a draw is no win, and a rank duel counts only once its fight started: a walkover (the opponent never came by the join deadline, or left in the waiting or countdown phase) raises no condition, and an opponent that left during the fight gives pvp_win alone, with no perfect, buff or grade (no proof the fight was earned; `RankDuelWinBy`). Treasure Island runs its whole 600 s with no early end, so its pvp_win_time (300) cannot be met yet; the guildpvp_* conditions need guild PvP, which the KR client cannot reach.
- **World:** a channel change is not shown to buddies, party, guild, club, group chat and spouse as a logout and a login. While the migration token is valid (30 s), a failed heartbeat of the channel being left does not mark the player offline. The span equals the heartbeat period, so a client that is closed during a channel change is shown as logged out by the second heartbeat after the change began: after 30 s at the earliest and about 60 s at the latest (the commit that added this says "within about 30 s").
- **Network:** a session is closed gracefully after the last send, so the last packet is not lost to a reset.
- **Regen period:** a player's health, spirit and stamina regen tick came one field update later than the interval each time (3.05 s instead of 3.0 s for health on a 50 ms update), because the next tick was counted from the update that took the last one. The ticks now stay on the interval grid: the next tick is due one interval after the last one was due, and after a pause of two intervals or more (the stat was full) the grid starts again. The first health tick comes RecoveryHPWaitTick plus one interval (4.0 s) after a hit, as before without the extra update. The interval floor (100 ms) is unchanged.
- **Stamina refill:** after a stamina use the first refill comes one StaminaRegenInterval (500 ms) later, as in KMS2. Before, RecoveryEPWaitTick (1000) was added on top, so the first +10 came 1.5 s after a dash. Chosen here: RecoveryEPWaitTick is not applied to a use; the client does not read it and what the constant is for is not known. A spirit cost holds no regen, as before.
- **Npc spawn effects:** an npc spawned by the field gets the additional effects its npc data lists (additionalEffect codes and levels), each cast by the npc on itself, in a buff packet right after the spawn is announced. A code the effect data does not have is left out. Field monsters get their under-60 % HP super armor effect this way.
- **Splash delay:** a splash sub-skill that is not immediateActive fires first at Delay + Interval after its region is placed and then every Interval; an immediateActive one fires at once and does not use Delay. An onlySensingActive region still starts looking one Interval after it is placed (chosen here).
- **Bounce chain:** an arrow attack with a bounceCount of the Range (1) or Chain (2) type now hits its bounces. Before, the target packet was clamped to targetCount and the damage went to the first hits only, so Triple Splitter (targetCount 1, bounceCount 4 and 5) hit one mob. The server builds each chain as the client does (`StateSkill::PickAttackTargets` and its bounce step): from every first hit, up to bounceCount more hits, each the nearest object in a cylinder of bounceRadius and 150 high around the last hit that is not yet in the chain; a Chain with bounceOverlap that finds none takes a random object around the last hit other than the last hit and starts the chain list again. In KMS2, Range and Chain skills hit up to 1 + bounceCount mobs, one damage per hit. The packet clamp now allows targetCount x (1 + bounceCount).
- **Npc bounce chain:** an npc's arrow attack of the Range (1) or Chain (2) bounce type now bounces too. The client builds a chain only for its own player's casts (`StateSkill` bounce step), so for an npc the server builds it the same way, from every first hit (a hit in the attack's range, or the actor a swept shot hits), once per cast. The target packet carries the chain after the path's record when that record names its target: one record per bounce, linked to the one before it (prev uid) with the bounce number as its index, which the client's target packet hangs under its parent and draws as a shot from it. Every chained actor takes the attack's hit once, through the same damage, evade, invincible and hostility rules as the first hit; one that died between the target packet and a swept shot's hit takes none. A bounce takes full damage: the arrow data has no per-bounce rate and the player path applies none. In KR, 45 npcs carry 18 such skills.
  - Chosen here: Pierce (3) and Boomerang (4) are not built on the server; their extra hits come from the arrow's flight, one target packet per contact, as before (KMS2 sends one hit per Pierce packet). An object hit twice in one chain (an overlap chain coming back) is dealt once, since the damage record is per object.
- **Region fire without a target:** a region skill's fire now sends its region damage packet (SkillDamage 5) whether or not it hit anything, and no target packet (SkillDamage 0) before it. The client plays the region's hit effect when the packet arrives, before it reads the targets (`CSceneStateGame::OnRegionSkillDamage`), so a region that reached nobody showed no fire at all. In KMS2 sessions 39,357 of 47,834 region damage packets name no target, and 8,320 of the 8,477 that do have no target packet before them. A region whose attack bounces sends as before.
- **Cube skill damage (tile packet):** a cube skill's fire (SkillDamage 6, `CSceneStateGame::OnSplashDamage`) now goes out as one packet for each target, with no target packet before it, a skill uid of 0, the point one block below the cube and the unit vector from that point to the target; it was one packet for all targets after a target packet, with the caster's position and facing. All 1,785 KMS2 tile packets name exactly one target, have uid 0, a position on the block grid and a direction of length 1; 1,763 carry no damage entry (blocks that only give a buff). In all 1,785 the map has a cube with the packet's skill one block above the sent position (only 213 have one at the position itself, stacked cubes), and the direction is the unit vector from the sent position to a position the target reported within the last second in 325 of 326 packets that name the session's own character.
- **Region damage targets:** each target of a region damage packet now carries the region's point it was reached from, cut to whole numbers, where it carried the caster's position, rounded. In KMS2 packets all 12,682 targets carry one of the points of the region's add packet (12,638 of them cut, not rounded). Chosen here for a region of several points: the point nearest the target. A target of an attack with damage count 0 (a region that only gives a buff or heals) is named with no damage entry, in KMS2 (2,159 of 2,160 such targets; every target of an attack with a damage count has one entry) and here, where this was already so; a byte test now holds it. The direction after the position is the unit vector from that point to the target in all three axes, zero when the target stands on the point (KMS2 packets, targets that are monsters standing still: 6,960 of 7,001 directions, the other 41 within 5 units of the point; 1,225 are zero, all on the point); it was the caster's facing.
- **Regions fired by a hit (splashActiveSkillID):** a region of a skill whose level has `splashManualActiveProperty` `onlyManualActiveSplash` (`SkillMetadataLevel.OnlyManualActiveSplash`) no longer fires on its own: it stays `splashLifeTick` ms (the first motion's `motionProperty`, now ingested as `SplashLifeTick`) and fires once each time a hit of an attack of the same caster names its skill in `splashActiveSkillID` (`SkillMetadataAttack.SplashActiveSkillIds`), at most once per `splashInvokeCoolTick` ms (`FieldSkill.Invoke`, `FieldManager.InvokeSplashes`, from `Actor.TargetAttack`). KR data: 10400292, the region the priest's 10400291 places (fireCount 0), whose fire gives the recovery buff 10400291 around it; the heal 10400071 and 10400072 names it. Before, the region fired once when placed and was gone at once. The data pack's skill data has no `splashActiveSkillID`, so it is read from the played client's skill data (`MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML`, by the attack's place in its motion when the played attack there has the same point; the ingest prints how many). The client loads `splashLifeTick` and `splashInvokeCoolTick` (skillleveldata.cpp:1177-1178), `splashActiveSkillID` (skillattackdata.cpp:597) and `splashMaxCount` (skilloverlapcompare.cpp:872) and shows no use of them; it has no `splashManualActiveProperty` or `onlyManualActiveSplash` at all, so that flag is server data only. Chosen here: the rule is taken from the data's names. Chosen here: a hit at the very tick the cool time or the life ends still fires; the region's own fire is the plain region fire (targets, damage packet, effects). Not done: `splashMaxCount` (at most 2 regions of 10400292 at once) is not ingested; `splashSkillIDs` of the property is empty in KR. Needs a re-ingest.
- **Effect level from the linked skill (linkSkillID):** a skill effect's skill with a `linkSkillID` (`SkillEffectMetadata.Skill.LinkId`, already ingested) now takes the caster's learned level of the linked skill, for a buff (`Actor.ApplyEffect`) and for a region skill (`FieldManager.AddSkill`); before, it took the level the data names. For the 108 buff rows that is level 1 at every level of the casting skill. For the 12 region rows of 10400186 (a sub skill of 10400181) it is 10400186's own level (level N names 10400187 at N), so the region followed 10400181's level and now follows 10400281's. The client loads the field (skillactionadditional.h:19, skillleveldata.cpp:346) and shows no use of it, so the rule comes from the data: the recovery buff 10400291 heals half of Healing Prayer 10400071 at each level (the Holy Place text says 50% of Healing Prayer's heal), and the damage rates of region 10400187 at levels 1 to 3 (2.4, 2.68, 2.96) are those of 10400281's text (240, 268, 296%). KR: 120 rows in 10 skill files; the links are 10100272 to 10100271, 10300269 to 10300261, 10400291 to 10400071, 10400302 to 10400301, 10700194 to 10700191 and 10400187 to 10400281. Chosen here: an npc caster, or a player who has not learned the linked skill, keeps the data level. No ingest change.
- **Cooldown length:** the server starts a skill's cooldown at the length the client starts it (`CSkillSystem::FUN_1417664e0`): the skill's cooldown less its share for the reductions the held buffs give the skill's cooldown group (SpecialEffectProperty type 4, `groupID` and `reduceRate`) and, unless the skill ignores cooldown reduction, for the caster's cooldown reduction rate (special ability 35, in thousandths). Before, the full length was kept: a holder of a group reduction (two buffs in the KR data, for the item skill groups 90000001 and 90000005) was refused the item's next use until the full cooldown had run, and the cooldowns sent on a field change were the full ones. The job skill cooldown check counts the reduced length too, instead of leaving out every cast of a caster with a cooldown reduction rate. Needs a re-ingest for the group reductions; without it they count as none.
  - A skill with recharge uses gets one use back at each cooldown's end, and the cooldown runs again from there while uses are still out, as the client counts them (`CSkillSystem::Update`). Before, the uses never went down while the record was kept, so the cooldowns sent on a field change could carry too many uses. The uses left out and the length of each recharge cooldown are carried through a channel change (the world server settles them for the time between), so the uses do not all come back on the new channel.
  - A cast of a skill that takes its caster off the ride (`unrideOnUse`) is refused while the caster is on their own item ride: the client asks to get off first and casts once it is off (`MyPC::FUN_141525580`). A skill of the main types 1, 4, 7, 11 to 15 and 17 to 19 counts as one whatever its `unrideOnUse` says (`SkillData::IsUnrideOnUse`). The server's data pack has no main type, so it is read from the played client's skill data (`skilldata/`, ingested with a played client given; 492 skills of those types in the KR client). Without a played client no skill has a main type and only `unrideOnUse` counts. Chosen here: a passenger and a ride a buff gave are not refused.
  - Chosen here: a group reduction with a probability under 1 is not ingested (the client rolls for it at each cooldown; the KR data has none). A cooldown of 1 s or more that a reduction brings under 1 s is not checked. The job skill cooldown check holds a cast to the length the client starts, with the rates the server counts (set items are counted); the game rule's share of the caster's rate (`CGameRule::FUN_1404774a0` in `CEquipment::GetAbilityTotal`) is not counted and not allowed for, so a caster who has one could be refused up to that share early (no extra tolerance, so a forged cast is not taken seconds early). A group reduction is read once per effect, the first one, as the client keeps one. The client keeps a buff's invoke effects on a skill as skill options 1001 to 1004 (`CEquipment::FUN_1411e1250`, filled by `AdditionalCliList::CollectInvokeEffectStats`; the played client's own effect data writes them so), which the server's data folds into its own types: 1001 is the cooldown rate and 1002 the flat cooldown seconds (both the server's type 1, rates and values, e.g. 11100301 takes 4 s off 11100062 in both), 1003 the spirit cost (the server's 56) and 1004 the skill damage.
  - A recharge skill's cast is refused while no use is left until the next one comes back, and within its `defaultRechargingCooldownTime` after its last use (`CSkillSystem::CanUseRechargeSkill`; the gap is now ingested, 6 of the 17 recharge skills give one, 0.2 to 1.5 s). With a played client given, its recharge counts and `rechargeShareOrigin` are taken from the client's skill data (16 skills; 10700272 and 10700274 count their uses on their origin skill, which the server does not follow, so they are not checked). Chosen here: the same slack as the cooldown check, a use counted back from 500 ms before it comes back and the gap from 3/4 of it on; not checked for a skill that uses an item. The client starts each recharge cooldown at the reductions held at that moment; the server settles the chain only when it looks at it, so when the length the client would start now is shorter than the kept one (a group reduction or cooldown reduction gained mid-chain) the shorter one is kept from then on and the running step ends sooner by the difference (chosen here, toward letting the cast through); a reduction lost keeps the kept length.
  - The cooldown invoke effects of the held buffs count too (invoke type 1, the client's skill options 1001 and 1002): the client takes their flat seconds and then their rate off each cooldown it starts, recharge ones included, unless the skill ignores cooldown reduction (`FUN_1417664e0`: (cooldown - seconds) - cooldown x rates). Before, the server kept the full length and left every cast of a skill such a buff shortens out of the cooldown and recharge checks; they are now checked at the shortened length (82 effect levels in the KR data, 16 of them with flat seconds). Chosen here: the effects held when the server accepts the cast are the ones counted, the client counting those held when it starts the cooldown.
  - An effect's `RemoveRechargingCount` gives a skill recharge uses back. The KR data has one: 10600280, 2 uses of Focused Fire 10600181, which its p0 hits put on the caster for each target they land on that holds the caster's own mark 10600281 (`target hasBuffID 10600281 hasBuffOwner 1`: the debuff that skill 10600281's hits put on a target, whose interval skill puts 10600282 on the caster), while the caster holds its own 10600282 (`caster hasBuffID 10600282 hasBuffOwner 1`). No client code reads the row; the client learns of it from 0x139 alone (skill, min, uses back: `CSceneStateGame::FUN_14173fc40`, scenestategame.cpp:8959, which takes the skill's recharge origin and ends the cooldown when no use is left out, `CSkillSystem::FUN_141765e70`). The server now settles the skill's recharge (the cooldown records are kept under a lock, since the field thread now changes them too), takes the uses off those out (never under none; at none it drops the cooldown, as the client ends it) and sends 0x139. Before, it neither read the row nor sent the packet, so the charge gave no use back. Chosen here: the effect is applied once for each marked target a p0 hit lands on (targetCount 8), so a hit on 8 marked targets sends 0x139 8 times (16 uses; the floor at none keeps it harmless), as the server applies a hit's effects per target; whether KMS2 sent one 0x139 per target is not known. A start counts at the effect's first copy and at each reapply that restarts it (resetCondition 2, 10 s) or adds a stack; min is 0, as no data gives another; the record kept is the skill's own, as its casts keep it (the client moves to the origin itself). The data pack does not have 10600280, so the ingest takes an effect only the played client has from its `additional/` data. Each level row is written as the pack's row (`PlayedAdditionalRows.ToPack`) and read by the pack's own serializer and the one `Build`: `<additional isDebuff mainType category skillGroupType>` to `BasicProperty` (buffType 2 for a debuff, buffSubType 1 << mainType); `Basic`, `Motion` and the other children of the same class to their `...Property` rows as they are; `Condition` to `beginCondition` (its owner tests on `owner`, its id lists as child rows); `Additional` to a `conditionSkill` with its tests in a `beginCondition`; `Skill` to a `splashSkill`. An effect is taken over only when every child of every level is in the gated set and every attribute has a pack name. The gated set (22 children) is what an A/B proved: each shared level's played row, mapped this way, against the pack's metadata and against the played client's own legacy row (`additionaleffect/`); a child is in when every difference from the pack is one in the data (the client's two rows differ there too). Out: `Skill`, as the pack sets nonTargetActive on 39 of 867 shared levels and the client's row has no such attribute; `Status`, not mapped. `SpecialAbility` is read as on a shared level (below) when every level's list maps and no level has final stats; the list becomes the level's stats. For KR, 123 effects (370 levels) are taken over, 61 of them with a `SpecialAbility` read, and 14 left out (6 for `Skill`, 4 for `SpecialAbility`, 4 for `Additional` owner-range attributes the pack has no name for, 1 each for `ModifySkillCooldown` and `Status`; a few for more than one). The gate was proved on the KR played client only: the A/B (`AdditionalEffectMapper.Compare`) prints its counts and does not change the set, so another locale's client needs its own A/B run before its takeovers are trusted.
  - A level both data sets have now takes the played client's row too, part by part (`PlayedAdditionalRows.TakeOver`): the client loads only its `additional/` rows, so where the pack's row differs the client runs the played row. Each part of the gated set that differs (`Basic`, `Motion`, `Condition`, `Additional`, `CancelEffect`, `ImmuneEffect`, `ModifyEffectDuration`, `Recovery`, `DotDamage`, `Shield`, `Consume` and the others in the set) is taken from the played row mapped as above; a part with an attribute that has no pack name keeps the pack's part, and a level with no part taken stays the pack's metadata. A pack field that no played row writes (the client schema has no such attribute, such as `beginCondition allowMapleSurvival`, the caster's `hasNotBuffID` and some buff levels) keeps the pack's value, and a part that differs only in such fields is not taken (`PlayedAdditionalRows.KeepPackOnly`): 183 item and event levels keep the Maple Survival permission the pack gives them. These fields are an explicit list (`PlayedAdditionalRows.PackOnly`), not whatever the played rows happen to leave out. The ingest prints the names kept, the listed names a played row writes (none in KR) and the pack attributes with a value that are neither listed nor written (in KR only the `Offensive` and `Defensive` damage values, whose parts are never taken). A level whose played row has a child the ingest does not read (`Offensive`, `Defensive`, `Spread`; `PlayedAdditionalRows.Holding`) stays the pack's whole, since the client runs that child with the rest of the row. The played `<SpecialAbility>` list is read through `ClientSpecialAbility` (as for the final stats): where its stats differ from the pack's, they replace the level's stat lines (the rest of its status stays the pack's), so Shadow Stance 10800211 now lasts 60 s with +3% physical damage at level 1 instead of the pack's 12 s, dark damage and 6% shield. The level stays the pack's whole while one of its types has no server stat (KR: aevp, cad_ignore_limit, cap_ignore_limit, cap_ignore_limit_extra, defensive_damageV, reduce_do_heal_hp; 112 levels; see below), while it has final stats, or where the client's list adds nothing and the pack has stats (17 levels). `improve_stat_wap_u` (shown by the client as a max weapon attack percent, valueType 2) is the max weapon attack rate, as the pack writes it (90050836-90050838 `Rates.MaxWeaponAtk` 0.08/0.16/0.08), not the special stat of the same option index, which no server calc reads. `atpToBap` and `strToInt` (rates, shown as bonus attack from accuracy and intelligence from strength) are the stat conversion (`Status.Conversion`, Accuracy to BonusAtk and Strength to Intelligence at the rate, as `StatsManager` applies it); a level holds one, and a second conversion type has no stat. The pack writes the same two rows as `statChangeBase/Result/Rate` (2.2 on 90051195, 0.7 on 10900041; the client reads no `statChange` attribute), so the data does not change. The client's loader skips a type whose table row has value type 0 (`additionalactionadditional.cpp:1227-1250`; the field read there is `SpecialAbilityInfo::m_nValueType`), so such a type adds nothing and holds nothing. A played `<Status>` holds the level the same way where it differs from the pack's status property on the 12 attributes the client reads (`PlayedAdditionalRows.StatusRead`), either way: 10700193, 10700196 and 10700281 keep the pack's timing until their compulsion event is read, and a pack level with a compulsion event where the played row has no `<Status>` (10800041, 60027031, 60065031) is held too (Chosen here: the rule is the same both ways, so the pack's compulsion stays with its whole level); `resAspR` and the `statChange` attributes, which the client does not read, hold nothing. The ingest prints these levels and the types without a server stat. The rest of `Status` (with the final stats of **Final stats of additional effects**), the splash skills (`Skill`) and the effect's other properties stay the pack's. Only with `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` set; without it the output is the same as before. For KR, 2593 of 13877 shared levels take parts (Basic 1680, SpecialAbility 557, DotDamage 332, Additional 132, Condition 112, Shield 97, CancelEffect 93, Recovery 60, Motion 50, ImmuneEffect 3, ModifyEffectDuration 1, Consume 1), and 184 are held (SpecialAbility 112 for a type without a server stat and 17 for stats only the pack has, Status 28, Offensive 24, Spread 24, Defensive 3). Among them: Focused Fire's caster buff 10600282 now lasts 10 s and cancels the charge 10600280 (the pack's lasted 100 ms and cancelled the mark), and the mark 10600281 no longer casts 10600282 every 10 s; Ion Cannon 10600212 loses the 30% max-HP shield its client row does not have (the row has only children the ingest reads); the Soul Binder shields are the client's fixed hpValue instead of a max-HP rate; 24 debuff levels (10500261, 10600201) whose Basic `SubType` goes from 6 to 2 (the client's mainType 1, a status debuff) now have their duration shortened by debuff-duration reduction (`Buff.ReducedDuration`), as status debuffs are. The takeover was proved on the KR played client only (the same gate as above), so another locale's client needs its own A/B before it is trusted.
  - Six played SpecialAbility types have no server mechanic, so the levels with them stay the pack's whole: the client only shows them (its effect tooltip, character info and damage meter read every type through `CEquipment::GetAbilityRate`/`GetAbilityTotal`), no client calc reads them, and the client's scripts have no critical, evasion or heal formula. `cap_ignore_limit` (a value, critical rate past the cap; 1 row, 10700181), `cad_ignore_limit` (a value, critical damage past the cap; 24 rows, 10900161, 10900162, 10400243) and `cap_ignore_limit_extra` (a rate, extra critical chance; 12 rows, 10800041): the server's critical rate and damage have fixed caps (`Lua.CalcPlayerCritRate`, `CalcCritDamage`) and no stat outside them; the pack has the same levels as plain CriticalRate/CriticalDamage stats or, for 10800041, a compulsion. `aevp` (a rate, evasion that accuracy does not lower; 63 rows on 11 effects from 10700182 to 10700295): no server stat. `reduce_do_heal_hp` (a rate, lowers the heals a skill does; 12 rows, 10400251 at 900): the server has no stat that lowers a caster's heals, and the pack has no stat on these levels. `defensive_damageV` (a value, no name in the client's strings; 12 rows on the debuff 10800283, -45 to -100): no flat damage-taken stat. Not applied: how far past its cap each critical type goes, what the evasion rolls against and what the heal cut applies to are not in the client.
- **SkillCancel:** a client's cancel of its cast (Skill 4) is sent on to the other players only, no longer back to the caster. KMS2 answers none of 2,797 cancels to the client that sent them. The cancel the server sends when it ends a state skill still goes to everyone, the owner too (KMS2: 1,252 of 1,275 state skills seen end with one), and the cancel still clears the cast's motion time and stops its attack packets after 2 s.
- **First buff proc:** a buff with dotCondition 0 (16,680 of the effects) now procs as soon as its delay is over: a dot, recovery or splash lands on the add, and an interval-0 dot (one proc, interval = duration + 1000) procs at once instead of after its end. dotCondition 1 (617 effects) still procs one interval after the delay. In KMS2 the first dot of a dotCondition-0 buff comes with its add, the first dot of a dotCondition-1 buff one interval later. This also fires an immediateActive buff splash (rune shields) on the add.
  - Chosen here: dotCondition 2 (2 effects) is read as 1. The client's rule that a dotCondition other than 0 with an interval below 1 makes the effect no dot (5 effects) is not applied.
- **Buff proc count:** a buff with dotCondition 0 procs duration / interval times, rounded down and at least once. Before, it procced once more: a potion (100 ms long, interval 100 ms) healed twice, as did every instant skill heal built the same way, and a dot ticked once too often. In KMS2 such a 100 ms effect heals once, an 8 s dot with a 1 s interval hits 8 times (at 0 to 7 s) and a 25 s effect with a 1.5 s interval 16 times. dotCondition 1 buffs are as before (first proc one interval in, last at the end).
- **Buff applied again:** applying a buff its owner already has restarts it when its reset condition is 0 or 2 and it has a duration: the update carries a new start and a new end (before, the old start with a new end), and the procs run from the beginning. A dotCondition-0 buff procs again at once and a dotCondition-1 buff one interval later, each with its full count. Before, only the end moved and a buff that had used its procs never procced again, so an aura heal of 1.1 s that is applied again every second healed once instead of every second. In KMS2 packets 4,960 such updates of single-stack buffs carry a new start and end, and 954 of 954 re-applications of a dotCondition-0 heal or dot are followed by a proc within 0.3 s; an 8 s dot applied again runs its 8 hits again. Reset condition 1 keeps its ticks (no KMS2 update seen for one), and a buff without duration keeps its start. Not proven: the restart of a dotCondition-0 buff that has neither heal nor dot (its tick skills run again here), which the packets cannot show.
- **Debuff duration on players:** a debuff put on a player is shortened by the player's gear options, as KMS2 sends it: a status debuff (sub-type 2) by special attribute 36 (debuff duration reduction) and a motion debuff (sub-type 8) with stun 0 or 1 by special attribute 34 (stun duration reduction). The option totals are per mille and the duration is rounded down: with 15 on boots and gloves a 4000 ms stun is sent as 3940 and a 1000 ms one as 985, with 5 a 1500 ms one as 1492, with 15 of attribute 36 a 5000 ms status debuff as 4925, and a player's own status debuff is shortened too (9999000 ms sent as 9719028, a rate of 28). The options were read from the worn items for the 15 cases; for the 5 and the 28 the items were not sent, only the rate fits. Motion debuffs with stun 2 or 4 were sent at full length by the same players, so they stay full; status debuffs are untouched by attribute 34 and stuns by 36. Debuffs players put on npcs are sent at their data length (4,615 of 4,642; the other 27 are one buff sent longer), so nothing changes there. Before, neither attribute was read. Chosen here: the reduction comes after the duration invoke values, and it also applies to a duration the caller gives.
- **Debuffs that keep their duration:** an effect level's `ignoreReduceTimeCondition` (136 levels on 114 effects) is now ingested (`IgnoreReduceTime`, needs a re-ingest), and such a debuff keeps its full duration: the holder's stun and debuff duration reductions do not shorten it (`Buff.ReducedDuration`). The client loads the flag with the level's basic properties and reads it nowhere else, so the server applies it. 56 of the levels are debuffs the reductions reached (50 status, 6 motion with stun 0 or 1): mostly boss mechanics (50000203 and its kin, 10 s status debuffs; 40501011 to 40501015; 40500031 and 40500038, motion debuffs with stun 0). Before, a geared player shortened them.
  - Live try: see the report.
- **Buff stack count:** a buff is added with one stack, and each further application adds one up to its maxBuffCount. Before, an add carried the count its caller gave, 0 from every caller but skill effects (their overlap-count condition), saved buffs and the debug command, so such a buff was sent with 0 stacks and never grew; the meret revive debuff never reached the count that closes the meret revive. Every KMS2 add carries a count of 1 (23,417 adds, maxBuffCount 1 to 10); with a higher maximum each application adds 1 (57 updates) and at the maximum only the time restarts (33). The client starts a buff at 1 and multiplies the buff's status values by the count (`AdditionalCliList`, additionalcli_2.cpp:1110). A buff without a maximum (16 effects) keeps 0.
- **Shield value and removal by caster:** a hit a shield buff takes now reaches the client: the update carries the shield flag with the buff flag (the client reads the shield value only under flag 2, `AdditionalCli::DecodeModifyPacket`; KMS2 sends flags 3 for these). Before, the update had flag 1 only and the shield bar stayed full until the buff ended. Removals that mean "this buff" (death, leaving the field, entering a pvp field, a cancel by id or category, a broken shield) now name the buff's own caster. Before they named the holder, so a buff held once per caster (casterIndividualBuff, 209 effects) that another actor had cast stayed through all of them.
- **Values of a stacked buff:** a buff's values count once per stack. The client sums a buff's status values, its consume and resistance rates and its invoke values and rates times the stack count, and a KMS2 dot does the same damage per stack (a dot of 0.8 % of the target's health hits for three times that at 3 stacks). The server now does so for the stat totals (values, rates, special values and rates), the resistance rates, the invoke values and rates and a dot's health, spirit and stamina amounts (the not-kill cap comes after), and rebuilds the stat totals when a count changes, before the skills that reaching the maximum starts. The invoke values are summed from the buffs held when they are read, so the same buff from two casters counts twice, as in the client, and removing one leaves the other's. A recovery is multiplied the same way; that is chosen here, no KMS2 heal of a stacked buff is known. A buff without a stack maximum has count 0: its status, resistance and invoke values count 0 times as in the client (none of the 16 such effects has one), and its dot or recovery amount once (chosen here; the 16 are recoveries). A stat refresh applies the worn items' and gems' effects again; an effect the wearer already holds is left alone, so a refresh adds no stack (168 item and gem effects have a maximum above 1, 24 of them with status values and 8 with invoke values). Shield values and the stat conversion rate are not multiplied.
- **Invoke effects on effects:** the client counts an invoke effect by its key, the first of `skillID`, `effectID`, `skillGroupID` and `effectGroupID` it has, and only with a type of that kind (`AdditionalCliList::CollectInvokeEffectStats`, additionalcli_2.cpp:851; specialability.cpp `FUN_14081d8a0`, `FUN_14081d8e0`): a skill's types are 1001-1004 (the server's 1, 2 and 56), an effect's 3, 4, 5, 23, 26, 38, 40 and 58, and every other type is dropped. It reads the skill ones for cooldowns, spirit costs and the DPS meter only; what an effect's type changes is the server's. The ingest now takes the invoke rows and the effect groups from the played client's additional data (`additional/<nnn>.xml`, `PlayedClientAdditionalEffects`) at every level it has, skipping a level whose feature or locale is off as `AdditionalData::Load` does (additionalactionadditional.cpp:714; the first level left gives the effect group): the data pack's skill damage rates differ on 264 levels (10100241: 0.03 for 0.23), its type 38 and 40 rows have no value, its 24 duration rows of the client's type 4 are type 3 at half the time, and it has no effect groups: the client files 10 effects (120 levels) under `groupIDs`, where the pack's `group` is 0 or another number (10900222: 10900062 for the client's 10900241), so none of the 85 effect group invoke levels matched. A level the client has without an invoke row has none, which drops 39 of the pack's invoke levels (10700031: type 3 on 10700102, 24 levels; 10300281: type 3 on 10300283, 12 levels of JobChange_02; 10300253 on 10300257; the type 34 rows of 90050853 and 90050855), as the played client does; the server never counted effect invokes before, so none of them is weaker than it was. The 244 pack invoke levels the client does not have keep the pack's row. Before, the server matched every invoke on a skill or skill group only, so no effect invoke counted. Per type:
  - 3 and 4 (duration of the named effect, a rate and milliseconds; 53 and 36 levels, e.g. 10400281 lengthens 10400182 by 10 s): counted, from the caster's buffs (chosen here: the invokes are the caster's job effects), before the gear's debuff reduction.
  - 38 and 40 (offensive physical and magical damage of the named effect, in thousandths; 90050806 and 90050841 on 10 items each, 90050829 on 8, 90050827 on 1): the holder's physical or magical damage rate rises by the value times the invoke's stacks while it holds the named effect too (90050806 at 5 stacks adds 10 % to 90050805's 2 %). Chosen here, the client having no stat of its own for them.
  - 58 (healing of the named effect or group; 97 levels, rates 0.04 to 0.4, 10400301 raises its group 10400301 by 4 %): the caster's rate raises the health a buff's recovery gives, rounded down, before the stack count; spirit and stamina heals are unchanged (chosen here).
  - 20, 21, 34, 35 and 57 (90050324 on 10200081, 90050853 and 90050854 on 90050852, 90050351 on skill 10500061): the client drops these types, and the played client has none of these rows (90050324, 90050854 and 90050351 are not in it, its 90050853 has no invoke), so they are not counted.
  - 5 (dot damage of the named effect; 161 levels on 15 effects, all a rate and no value, e.g. 90050003 raises 90050002's rate 3.1 by 35 %; every one names an effect with dot damage): the caster's rate raises the dot's damage rate, rate + rate times the invoke rate, as the client's DPS meter counts the caster's own tick (`CDPSMeterSystem::OnDotDamage`, dpsmetersystem.cpp:1145, its only reader of the type). Before, a dot never took them. Chosen here: the server reads the dot caster's own buffs, so a dot cast by a player's summon or pet takes no invoke; the client reads the local player's own invoke also when the attacker is the player's summon (`pMe->FUN_1416628b0(5, effectId)`), but only for the meter.
  - Not done: 23 and 26 (evasion and critical evasion of the named debuff, 11100252 on 11100242, -1 and -10 a level) are matched but not applied: the client has no reader of them. 11100242's own evasion and critical evasion now lower an npc's (next bullet).
- **Skill effects from the played client's skill data:** the client gives a skill level, and each attack of its motions, the effects of its `<actionAdditional>` rows in `skilldata/<nnn>.xml` (the skill's `m_vAdditional`); the server applies the ones the data pack's `skill/` rows give as `conditionSkill`. With `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` set, the ingest reads that pack's `skilldata/` and adds every row whose effect id and level no row of the level gives already (anywhere in the level: an effect the pack gives on the level or on one attack is not added to another attack): a level row to the level's effects, an attack row to the data pack's attack at the same place in its motion when both have the row's point, else to the first attack of its motion with that point (to its on-damage effects for `dependOnDamageCount`), with the row's target, owner, overlap count, link skill, probability and battle-ride flags (`allowBattleRidingState`, and `onlyBattleRidingState` as `OnlyOnBattleMount`, read as the client's bools: `TiXmlElement::QueryBoolAttribute` sets a value starting with true, yes or 1 in any case and nothing else, so a 2 is not set; `PlayedClientActionAdditionals`). KR: 103 effects in 15 skills, for example 10700001 on a p0 hit of 10700181 (probability 0.07) and 10200061 on a p0 hit of 10200062. Not added: rows naming an effect id and level the data pack has no effect for (350 rows, for example 10200102..10200108 on 10200101, and 10400263 level 2 on 10400212 and 10400231) and 3 attack rows with no attack of their point. A row's `beginCondition` is read as the client reads it (`SkillBeginCondition::Load`, `SkillHasBuffCondition::Load`): its flags and probability, and a `<caster>` and a `<target>` buff test (`hasBuffID`, `hasBuffCount`, `hasBuffOwner`, `hasNotBuffID`, `hasNotBuffOwner`), given to the effect as the data pack's rows give theirs (the buff at any level, its stacks equal to the count when one is given, the owner flag as the pack's `hasBuffOwner`; a test naming neither buff is none, as the client's `HasContent`). A row whose condition has another field or child is not added (KR: none). KR: 2065 rows have a buff test, and none is added: the level's pack rows give 1997 of their effects already and 67 name an effect the data pack does not have. Not set, nothing changes. Chosen here: `useTargetCountFactor` has no reader in the client source, so the plain probability is kept; `hasNotBuffOwner` has no field on the server's condition, so the buff the actor must not have counts whoever cast it (every KR row sets it to 1). Takes effect with the next ingest.
- **Skill level cooldowns from the played client:** the cooldown the server counts for a cast and sends in its skill cooldown packet is now the played client's own level cooldown where the client's level gives one (`skilldata/<nnn>.xml`, the `cooldown` attribute of `<level>` in milliseconds, read by `SkillLevelData::Load`; `SkillLevelData::GetCooldown` is what the client's skill slot counts down and its tooltip shows), with `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` set (`PlayedClientSkills.ReadLevelCooldowns`, the first `<skill>` of an id and the first `<level>` of a value). Before, it was the data pack's `beginCondition` `cooldownTime`, which differs for 95 KR skills (502 levels: 405 shorter in the client, 97 longer), for example Sticky Monster 10600281 (40 s, the pack 60 s) and Holy Symbol 10400051 (120 s, the pack 180 s): the client's counter ended first and after a relog it showed the server's longer one. The rates on top (cooldown group, the caster's reduction, invoke effects) are unchanged. Chosen here: a level the client gives no `cooldown` keeps the data pack's value (the client counts 0 there; KR: 181 levels in 16 skills, all but one under half a second, for example 10200201 at 0.1 s, and 10100061 level 1 at 5 s). Without a played client nothing changes. Needs a re-ingest.
- **Buff stats on npcs:** an npc's stats now take the status of the buffs it holds, counted as a player's are (values, rates, special values and rates, times the stack count) on the npc's own stats (its data's, or the room's player count factor of them), and are rebuilt when such a buff is added, changes its stacks or is removed. Before, only a player's stats were rebuilt, so every job skill's debuff on a monster changed nothing in the damage a hit reads from it: 10100181 (defense -30 %), 10200061 (attack -9 %), 10800161 (accuracy -20), 11100242 (evasion -3 and critical evasion -20 a level) and the other debuffs of the job skills, and an npc's own buffs left its stats alone. A change of the npc's maximum health is sent to the field (Stat, health only), its health keeping its share as a player's does. Chosen here: the client keeps no stats of an npc but its health and attack speed (`StatsPacket`), so how KMS2 counts them is not known; the player's rule is taken. A movement speed debuff now slows an npc's walk too, which reads its movement speed. A debuff never takes an npc's maximum health or movement speed under 1 (an npc with no speed of its own keeps 0): 9 effects reach a movement speed rate of -1 alone or by their stacks (49289072, 69000504 at -0.5 x 3), which left a walk with speed 0 that never arrived, and health rates of several buffs together could take the maximum to 0, which killed the npc with no killer. A living actor whose health share rounds down to 0 when its maximum falls keeps 1 (chosen here; a rebuild is no hit). Its health then keeps the share of a maximum of 1, so it is full again when the buff comes off: the client has no npc stats to compare with, and the player's share rule is kept (only health rates of -1 or less together do this; no one buff in the data does).
  - Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Disabled buffs:** a held buff whose begin condition does not hold (an HP band, a weapon, a buff it needs; `Buff.Enabled`, checked each tick) now counts for nothing: not in the stat totals, the invoke damage bonus, the resistance, invoke, invincible and stun sums or the stat conversion, as the client's sums skip a buff without m_bEnabled (`AdditionalCliList::FUN_140972a00`, `CollectInvokeEffectStats`). The stats and sums are rebuilt when the condition starts or stops holding, and a buff added with its condition failing is disabled before the first rebuild. Before, its stats counted the whole time it was held, so the job passives of an HP band gave both bands at once in every fight (10100232: physical and magical res +150 at 30 % health or less, held with 10100231's damage +10 % at 70 % or more). A disabled shield takes no hit, as the client's shield total skips it (`AdditionalCliList::GetTotalShieldValue`; 61100121's 1200 while its owner holds 61100120, 90060012's 30 % of health while 90060013 is held). The compulsion rates (block, evasion and critical override) are summed over the enabled buffs held, each buff id once; the client never reads them, so this is chosen here as in its other sums (10300242's critical override on three skills while 10300250 is held; 60027031 and 60065031 on the caster's health). Before, a compulsion counted from its add until a removal by id, so it also outlived an expiry. A revive in place is refused only for an enabled buff with disableRevivalHere, the flag the client takes from the same enabled-only loop (`FUN_140972a00`, additionalcli_2.cpp:1065; none of the 3 such buffs in the data has a condition). A buff added with its condition failing keeps its procs (dot, recovery, splash) for when the condition holds: before, it never procced again for the rest of its life. Its timer now ends a buff that is disabled at its end: before, a timed buff disabled after its add ended only through a proc it could not run, so it stayed held. Chosen here: a buff that comes back on does not pay the procs it missed; its next proc is the first of its schedule at or after that tick, and a buff of one proc still gets it. When that slot falls after the buff's end, it does not proc again (`Buff.ResumedCanProc`); before, a buff back on after its last slot outlived its end and procced once more. Not done: a buff the client suspends on a battle mount is covered only by its AllowOnBattleMount condition.
  - Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Range count conditions:** a begin condition's target count in range (`targetCheckRange`, `targetCheckMinRange`, `targetInRangeCount`, `targetFriendly`, `targetCountSign`; 60 levels on 27 effects, all owner conditions) is now ingested and checked: the condition holds only when the count of living enemies (friendly 1) or allies (friendly 2) between the minimum and the range around the checked actor compares with the wanted count by the sign (`SkillUtils.InRangeHolds`, `FieldManager.CountAround`). The client loads the fields into its overlap condition and sets it when a range and a sign are given, but evaluates nothing, so the server checks it. Before, the fields were dropped and those passives applied whatever stood near. The tooltips give the meaning: 90050886 (900, 4 or more, friendly 1) is combat power +520 with 4 or more hostile targets within 9 m, 90000525 damage +7 % with 3 or more enemies within 6 m, 10500111 (400, 0, equal) spirit regen with no enemy near. A buff turns on and off as actors come and go (the enabled check runs each tick).
  - Live try: not run (tests only).
- **PvP zone conditions:** a begin condition's `applyPvPZoneType` (4,823 levels on 1,699 effects) is now ingested and checked: 1 holds outside PvP zones only, 2 only in them, 0 anywhere (`SkillUtils.PvpZoneHolds`). A PvP zone is a map of type Pvp (arenas and battlefields), the maps where `clearEffectFromPVPZone` already applies; the holder's map is checked, for npcs too. The client loads the field with its effect level data and reads it nowhere else, so the server checks it. Passives come in PvE and PvP halves of the same buff (10100151, damage rate 0.25, type 1; 10100152, 0.125, type 2, both added by one skill), and 90050886's tooltip says it is off in arenas and battlefields. Before, both halves held everywhere at once. The enabled check runs each tick, so a held buff turns on or off on entering such a map.
- **PvP zone cooldown clear:** a skill's stateAttr `clearCooldownFromPVPZone` (442 skills, nearly every job skill) and an effect's BasicProperty `clearCooldownFromPVPZone` (2 effects, 10500072 and 10500272) are now ingested: entering a map of type Pvp (arenas and battlefields) ends the cooldown of each flagged skill (sent as a cooldown ending now, so the skill is ready at once) and of each flagged effect (`SkillCastRules.PvpZoneClears`). The client loads both flags and reads them nowhere else, so the server applies them. Other cooldowns run on, and no other map clears any. Before, a skill used just before entering an arena was still on cooldown inside.
- **PvP zone cooldown hold:** the town cooldowns that a PvP-zone entry ends are held and come back on the way out, so the zone's clear is not a free reset (before, a warp into an arena and straight back made a flagged skill ready). The first Pvp entry of a stay holds each cleared cooldown (with its recharge uses); a move to another Pvp map keeps that hold. The first entry of a map of any other type, by any way out (match end, early leave, portal, warp, a relog straight into town), puts back each held cooldown that has not run out, unless a use inside ends later or, for a skill with recharge uses (15 of the flagged skills, such as 10400231), has more uses out: the record with more uses out wins, the later end only between equal counts (`PvpCooldownHold`). The skill's last town cast comes back with it, so the server's own cooldown check of a job skill (made on its last cast) holds the restored cooldown too; like the last casts themselves, that one is not kept over a channel change or a relog. The disconnect save reads the hold from another thread, so it takes a copy under the hold's lock. Skill holds ride the World's player config cache like the other cooldowns, with the same time-away rule; a World restart loses the hold as it loses the cooldowns, so nothing comes back then. The 2 flagged effect cooldowns are held in the session only: effect cooldowns (`BuffManager.CooldownTimes`) are not cached over a channel change or a relog at all.
  - Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Skill cooldown reset (SkillResetCooldown 0x43):** the GM command `resetcooldown` (Debug permission) ends every skill cooldown and recharge record and the last casts the cast checks use, and sends 0x43, which has no body: the client ends every cooldown of its skill system and drops its recharge uses (`CSceneStateGame::OnSkillResetCooldown`, `CSkillSystem::FUN_141765de0`). Before, nothing sent 0x43. The cooldowns a PvP zone holds stay held.
  - Not sent otherwise: no KR item or rule ends all skill cooldowns. The cooldown reset stone (item function `ResetAbilityCoolTime`) resets the character ability reset time, not skill cooldowns, so 0x43 stays a debug packet.
- **Heal critical rate:** a heal crits at the caster's critical rate against the target's critical evasion, the rate a hit uses, and a crit heals for 1.5 times the part that scales with the caster's magic attack. Before, the evasion was taken as 0, which ran the rate to its 40 % cap for every caster. KMS2 heals of a caster on itself crit 93 times in 1,380: 42 of 566 at luck 18, critical rate 53 and evasion 50 (formula 6.2 %), 35 of 554 at critical rate 60 (6.8 %), 8 of 230 at evasion 62 (5.0 %) and 8 of 24 at critical rate 305 (26 %). Taking the target's evasion and not the caster's is chosen here; every sample is a heal on the caster. A KMS2 skill heal is its recovery rate times the caster's magic attack rounded down, raised by the caster's recovery bonus (the special attribute s_item_opt_sa_improve_recovery_regen_doheal, from item options and buffs) and rounded down again, then times 1.5 on a crit: KMS2 heals of five priests on themselves fit to the point at a bonus of 0, 0.5 % (an earring's option), 2.3 % (a codex's option) and 5 % (a codex's 1 % and the passive 10400141 at level 1, 4 %), e.g. rate 0.9 at magic attack 272 heals 245 and crits for 367, rate 6.08 at 809 heals 5,163. Before, the bonus was not read and those heals came out 0.5 to 5 % low. Reading the caster's bonus and not the target's is chosen here from the attribute's name; the bonus a target gets from allies' heals (..._receiveheal) is not applied, no sample has it. Value heals (potions, 40 heals) never crit.
- **Invincible buffs:** a skill hit on an actor that holds a buff with the invincible flag (107 buffs have it) is sent as damage type 4 with amount 0 and takes no health; a damage over time tick on it is sent as Normal with amount 0. The flag was read from the data but never used, so those buffs did nothing. The client names type 4 "Invincible" and type 8 "InvincibleReflection" (GetGradeName, dpsmetersystem.cpp:177). In KMS2 every hit on a holder of such a buff has amount 0: 28 hits of type 4 (27 by monsters on a player, 1 by a player on a monster), 9 hits of type 8 on monsters and 7 ticks of type 0; no type 4 lands on an actor without such a buff. The buff's invincible value decides the type: all 9 hits of type 8 land on holders of a buff with value 2, all 28 of type 4 on holders of one with value 1 (26 buffs have 1, 90 have 2, 5 have 3, one 150), and the same is sent here (`InvincibleReflect`, ingested); chosen here: the other values give type 4, and a holder of both kinds gives 8. A GM's kill toggle still kills.
- **Death resistance:** an enabled buff with `deathResistanceHP` keeps its holder alive at 1 health: a skill hit or a damage over time tick takes the holder no lower than 1, and heals go in as before (`BuffManager.DeathResistance`, the same hold the npcs tagged `no_die` have). The flag was ingested but never read, so the Berserker's Raging Soul (10200221, 8 s, 12 levels; the other buff with it is 51000002) did not keep anyone alive. 51000002 (unlimited) is held only by the training dummies 51000001 to 51000003, 52000005 and 52000006, from their own npc data (`<property buffs>`); no skill, item, trigger or other effect gives it in the KR data, so no player can get it, and a dummy that cannot be killed is what it is for. The client only gathers the flag from the enabled buffs (`m_nDeathResistanceHP`, the highest value, `AdditionalCliList::FUN_140972a00`) and reads it nowhere, since the server decides deaths; chosen here: the KR data has only the value 1, which is the health kept, and other health losses (a fall, a skill's health cost, a GM kill command or kill toggle) are not held. Live try: as a berserker, cast Raging Soul and let monsters hit you at low health: the health stops at 1 and you do not die while the buff is on; after it ends, the next hit kills.
- **Skill cast checks:** a player's cast is refused before anything is paid when the player is dead and the skill does not allow that, and, for a skill that uses up an item (potions, food, tonics), when the skill's own condition fails (level, stats, battle mount and the rest) or it is on cooldown. Before, none of this was checked and the cooldown was only stored, so a cast packet from a dead player still used up the potion. A refused cast gets SkillUseFailed mode 5 with the cooldown, on which the client ends the cast and sets the skill's cooldown (`CSceneStateGame::OnSkillUseFailed`); a cast whose cost cannot be paid now gets the same answer instead of none. The cast is sent to the field once (KMS2 sends one SkillUse per cast; before, a player's went out twice), and a tick sync of a cast does not take its item a second time. A cast is also refused when an enabled buff the caster holds lists the skill in its `skillUseDisableIDs` (the client does not send such a cast: cast check answer 0x1f); the list is new in the ingest, so a server needs a re-ingest for it. A skill whose `requireMapCodes` name fields is refused in any other field (answer 0x17), also when it uses no item. The spirit cost follows the client's formula (`CSkillSystem` 0x14176b960): the consume `spRate` of the enabled buffs held, times their stacks, is added to the cost rate, so a buff with -1 makes casts free; before, the server charged the full cost and refused a cast the client could send. The item of an item skill is looked up before the other costs are paid; before, a cast naming an item the player does not have still paid its spirit. A cast is also refused when its skill's main type (`mainType` of the played client's skill data, 2 for the job skills) has its bit in the skill-type masks: the `skillUseDisable` of an enabled buff held (a bit mask, OR'd over the buffs, new in the ingest, so a re-ingest is needed) or the field's `skillUseDisable` list (a list of main types, ingested before but never checked). Both are taken from the played client's data, the buff masks from its `additional/*.xml` (`<Motion skillUseDisable>`) and the field lists from its `table/fielddata.xml` (the available environment's `<property skillUseDisable>`); the data pack's differ, and the server refused casts the client sends. KR ingest: 29 buff levels with a mask (31 in the pack; 5 levels taken from the client: 71000051, 71000068 and 71000074 block main type 1, basic attacks, in the pack and nothing in the client; 49018002 has 12 in the pack and 6, types 1 and 2, in the client), as 50002100 with 4: no job skill; 68 fields with a list (the same 68 in the pack; 64 lists taken from the client: the pack adds 8 and 9 almost everywhere, 5 in the wedding fields 84000006 to 84000046 and 4 in 61000023 to 61000034), as 02000387 with 2 and 51000001 with 1, 2, 3, 5. Without a played client the pack's masks and lists are kept (`PCBase::GetSkillUseDisableMask`, answer 0x1e; the client does not send such a cast). Both buff checks (0x1e and 0x1f) apply to a cast's first motion only: the client sends the next motion of the same cast without its check (stateskill.cpp:2977), so a mask that lands between motions does not stop a cast the client goes on playing. A Use counts as a next motion only when the server holds an earlier accepted motion of the same cast (the same cast id and skill, a lower motion point: the client sends the next motion with the cast's own id, `StateSkill::FUN_1417ee120`); before, any Use with a motion point past 0 skipped both checks, so a crafted packet cast a disabled skill. Live try: in 02000387 a job skill does not go off and the log shows no cast of it; in any other field the job skills cast as before. Left out, with why: the stamina skill in a field without `useEPSkill` (no KR field sets it to 0), `requireEffect` and `hasNotAdditionalEffectGroup` (not in the ingest; no skill of the KR skill xml read here uses them) and stun (the client's cast check has no stun test; it is part of its state handling).
  - Chosen here: the cooldown check covers item skills only, the skill's own cooldown and that of its cooldown group (the red, orange and white potions share one, the elixirs another); a use up to 1.5 s before the cooldown's end is taken, since the client keeps the cooldown itself and KMS2 takes a second potion 4.0 s after the first on a 5 s cooldown. Whether KMS2 refuses an earlier one is not known. Job skills have their own cooldown check (next bullet); any other skill's cooldown is only stored.
- **Job skill cast checks:** a cast of a skill that is in a job's skill tree is refused when the caster's own tree lacks it or holds it at a lower level than the cast names (a sub skill counts at its main skill's level; the awakening rank counts only for an awakened job). A skill in no job's tree (mount, lift-up, a skill a buff gives or a skill changes to) is not checked. A job skill is also refused when it comes too early after its last accepted cast or after the last cast of a skill of its cooldown group: in KMS2 the casts of a skill with a cooldown of 1 s or more never come sooner than the cooldown by the cast's own tick, and shorter cooldowns are cut by the attack speed and the cast motion's speed. The client starts a cooldown that is not shown in the UI (`useCoolDownShowUI` 0) at the cooldown divided by the cast motion's speed (`MyPC::FUN_141525ff0`; the speed of the motion checks below: `SequenceSpeed` x attack speed, `SequenceSpeed` alone for move type 3) and applies no cooldown reduction to it; KMS2 casts of such skills come no sooner than 0.9 of that time at attack speed 100 to 117. A buff's cooldown reset clears the skill's last cast. A cast's costs (meso, spirit, stamina, health, item) are all checked before any is paid; before, a cast short of spirit had already paid its meso. A refused cast gets the same SkillUseFailed mode 5 answer and pays nothing; refusals are logged at Debug.
  - Chosen here: a cooldown of 1 s or more refuses a cast more than 0.5 s before its end, a cooldown under 1 s a cast sooner than 0.75 of the client's time for it: the cooldown divided by the speed of the last cast's motion (the higher attack speed of the last cast and the present one; under 100 counts as 100). `useCoolDownShowUI` is not ingested: a cooldown under 1 s stands in for the flag 0 (in the KR data the two agree but for four skills with a shown 0.4 s cooldown, which are only taken sooner). So 10500001 (0.45 s, motion speed 1) is taken from 337 ms at attack speed 100 and from 309 ms at 109, where the client's own cooldown is 412 ms; before, a motion faster than 1.33 (10500173: 0.25 s at 1.5, 166 ms in the client) was held to more than the client's time. The time between two casts is measured as for the motion check (lag and clock rules below), so a client cannot lengthen it with its tick. A cast whose health cost (the skill's rate of the current health) would leave no health is refused.
  - Not checked by this cooldown check: a skill with recharge uses (it has its own check, in the recharge bullet above); combo windows (the client's animation keys `combo`, `selfcombo`, `charging_combo`, `inputcombo` in StateSkill) and changed skills (the client switches by a buff or by the running cast's output skill, `MyPC`), which the server cannot time. The last casts are not kept across a login.
  - Hands and spirit: a job skill whose condition lists hand weapons (skill `basic` `condition` `weapon lh rh`) is refused unless one row fits the weapons held, a hand of 0 fitting any; one with a spirit threshold (`condition sp`) is refused with less spirit than the threshold cut by the spirit cost reductions (`CSkillSystem` cast check `0x14176a8c0`, answers 0x1c and 0xb; the held weapons as `PC::UpdateWeaponAction` reads them, the left hand empty when it holds the right hand's two-handed weapon). The ingest takes the weapon rows from the played client's skill data where it has them: the data pack differs for 2 skills (11000146 needs a right hand 55 in the client and none in the pack, 10100061 a left hand 41 in the pack only). The weapon held is now the client's weapon action: a hand whose item the character may not use counts as none (`PC::CanUseItem` as the server keeps it: expired and not a pet, a premium-club item without premium club, bound to another character), the left hand is dropped when the client's weapon action table (`table/weaponaction.xml`, `CWeaponActionTable`, the new `weaponaction.xml` table, so the metadata needs a re-ingest) does not allow it with the right hand (subtype 30, the client's `Blunt_` actions, takes none), and a skill with weapon rows is refused when the table has no action of the two hands (the rows then fit the action's hands).
