Not done: the client also refuses for a disabled hand slot (no data disables slots 4 or 5), checks the item's other `CanUseItem` limits (PC bang, wedding, socket gems) and takes an outfit tab weapon (layer 5) first; the server reads the equip tab's hands only. The client has no stamina threshold: the pack's stamina 10 on 10500153 is not checked.
- **Tick sync cost:** a tick sync of a running cast pays the cast's spirit and stamina again only for a skill that continues while held (`ContinueSkill`), as KMS2 does (such a skill loses its spirit cost at each sync, and a sync it cannot pay gets SkillUseFailed mode 5); no other skill pays at a sync, and meso, health and item are paid once per cast. Before, every sync paid the whole cost again.
- **State skill refusal:** a state skill (climb, swim and the like, StateSkill 0) the server does not take, because it names an item the player does not hold, a skill it does not know or a cost that cannot be paid, now gets SkillUseFailed mode 6 with the state the client named. The client ends that state when it is still in it and takes the cooldown as in mode 5 (`CSceneStateGame::OnSkillUseFailed`); before, the server answered nothing and the client stayed in a state the server never started.
- **Attack packet checks:** the client names a cast's attack points, attacks and positions; the server takes them only inside what KMS2 clients send, and drops anything else without damage (logged at Debug, never a disconnect). A target packet pays damage only for an attack uid that an attack point packet of the same cast declared for that attack point, and each declared attack pays once (a bouncing arrow 1 + `BounceCount` times): KMS2 target packets always name such an attack, once. An attack point is taken once per cast; a skill that continues while held (`ContinueSkill`) sends it again with the iteration one higher each time, as KMS2 does, and every other iteration is dropped. The target area starts at the impact position, which is held to the cast position, and the cast position to the player's own; before, a client could place the impact anywhere in the field and send a target packet any number of times. The targets themselves were and are picked by the server from the skill's own range.
  - Chosen here: a cast position more than 600 from the player's position is replaced by the player's position when the client synced in the last second (KMS2: within 507; median 0.9, 99 in 100 within 191); an attack point packet must carry the cast position (KMS2: always the same; 1 unit allowed); the impact must lie within the larger of 3 times the attack's range distance and that distance + 750 of the cast (KMS2: within 730 and 3.0 times; 99 in 100 within 450 and 2.3 times); an attack point packet declares at most 20 attacks (KMS2: 0, 1, 3, 4, 10 or 20); attack packets are taken up to 5 s after the cast (KMS2: at most 3.55 s; 99 in 100 within 0.82 s). For a held skill the 5 s count from its last taken attack point packet: while held, the client sends a tick sync and then the attack point packet of the next iteration (KMS2: at most 0.58 s apart), and the attack point packet is the one that restarts the time, so a hold pays for as long as its iterations keep coming.
  - Not checked: the time from the cast to an attack point against the motion's keyframes (KMS2 packets come as early as 0.4 of the keyframe time and earlier), the cube magic path packet, the direction of the target area, and a cast position when the client has not synced for a second. State skills take no attack packets.
  - Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Target packet hits:** a target packet now pays the objects the client names in it, each as the client's writer sends it (`CMagic`, magicdata.cpp): a region's hits once per object up to the attack's target count, only its own target when nonTarget is 0, once per point group between two tick syncs; an arrow's up to max(1, bounceCount) different objects (piercingAttackMaxTargetCount below it when every path sets one and none re-pierces), again after each piercing interval, and once more on a boomerang's way back. An object pays only inside the area the writer searched: a region's range at the owner's position the packet carries (when within 300 of the server's player position, as the client syncs it right before it writes, magicdata.cpp:1840; the server's otherwise, logged) turned to the packet's facing, an arrow's collision box over the packet's step, each grown by 150; an arrow's points must lie within its paths' reach of the cast or of the owner's position now (the client starts the magic at the owner's fire node when it spawns, after a dash or leap of the motion, magicdata.cpp:1408, and a boomerang homes on its owner on the way back; not checked for a path with a fixed fire point), which also replaces the impact bound for an arrow whose paths have a distance. A boomerang that hits an object again without re-piercing is taken as on its way back, also when the number did not show it.
  - Logged only, never refused (a check that cannot tell lag from a cheat): an arrow step longer than 0.25 s of its fastest path (the client does not clamp a step, magicdata.cpp:980, so a frame hitch makes one as long as the hitch), and an arrow point that moved faster than its path since the arrow's packet before (arrival times are the server's, so a packet the link held up makes the next look fast). An arrow hit's number (the count of objects it hit before) is not checked against the server's count, as a packet the server dropped makes it skip; it only tells a boomerang's way back. Not done: a re-piercing boomerang whose first hit of an object the server dropped can take a later number for its way back early, and its real way back then counts both ways' objects toward its most. A packet with no hits (the client clamps a crowd to piercingAttackMaxTargetCount and still sends) uses up none of the arrow's packets.
  - Not done: an invincible target's deflection (magicdata.cpp:1975, the arrow flies on sideways at 1.2 times its speed for the time left) and a magic that follows a control guide (`FUN_1414607e0`) are held to the reach like any arrow; a magic whose owner is not the player (a summon's or a pet's) gets no sync before its region packet (:1840), so its area and facing are taken at the player's: no such player magic is known; not seen refused in KMS2 packets, not verified. Each packet allocates its found-actor array and walks the field's actors once per named object (fine at KMS2 sizes).
- **Attack speed and movement checks:** a cast is refused when it comes before the motion of the last accepted cast lets the next skill in; the answer is the same as for a cast on cooldown (SkillUseFailed, nothing paid). The time is the motion's `inputcombo` key (`selfinputcombo` for the same skill again; without it `combo`, else `end`) divided by the motion's speed. The client makes that speed in Lua (`calc_pc_asiR`) from the motion's `SequenceSpeed` and the attack speed stat; the Lua body is not known, and speed = SequenceSpeed x attack speed / 100 is fitted over KMS2 casts at attack speed 100 to 121. A skill that is active at once (`ImmediateActive`) is taken at any time. An iteration of a held skill is dropped, like any other refused attack point packet, when it comes sooner than the motion's loop (`loopend` - `loopstart`, at the same speed) after the one before. An attack point packet declares at most the attack's `targetCount` attacks, 1 for an arrow that needs no target (`NonTarget` 1), and never more than 20; before, any count up to 20 was taken. All 8,130 KMS2 casts and 11,341 attack point packets pass these checks.
  - Chosen here: a cast is taken up to 100 ms before the key time; a key after the loop of a held skill's motion is counted without the loop's length, since a hold that ends leaves the loop, and a cancelled cast is not counted at all (the cancelled cast itself takes no attack packet later than 2 s after its cancel, and each such drop is logged at Debug: KMS2 still answers one that comes up to 0.87 s after it with damage, the latest of 23 such casts); the time between two casts is measured by the lag and clock rules below; the higher of the attack speed at the last cast and now counts, and an attack speed under 100 counts as 100. A motion of move type 3 is played at its own speed whatever the attack speed, as the client does (`StateSkill::GetMotionSpeedScale`); the move type is ingested with the motion, so this needs a re-ingest (without one every motion counts as scaled, which only shortens the time). In the KR data one motion has it (10500153 level 1). An iteration is due 0.9 times the loop - 20 ms after the one before (KMS2: 0.91 to 1.02 times a 500 to 600 ms loop; 270 ms in the middle on a 250 ms loop, single ones 51 to 579 ms), and may arrive up to 500 ms before it is due; one that comes sooner still draws the rest from the lag budget below, and the due times after it come sooner by what was drawn. The due times run on from the first iteration, so the iterations a stall held up are taken together, and the hold as a whole is not faster than its loop; an iteration that comes too early pays nothing and the next ones are still counted. The highest attack speed seen during the hold counts; it is kept per session, not per cast, so two holds that overlap share it (left so: it only lets more through).
  - Setting: the timing checks (the motion check of any cast, the cooldown check of a job skill's cast, the held-skill iteration timing and the attacks an attack point packet declares against the attack's `targetCount`) refuse only when the Game server runs with the environment variable `MS2_CAST_CHECKS=enforce`. The default is log-only: the cast or packet is taken, and what would have been refused is logged at Information ("Cast not refused (log only)", "Attack point not dropped (log only)"; each at most one line per session in 10 s, with the count since the last line). The default stays log-only until a live try with lag has passed. The other checks (skill tree, dead caster, item skills, positions, packet order, the 5 s window, more than 20 attacks in one attack point packet) refuse in either mode.
  - Lag and clock rules (every number chosen here): a cast that is on time by its arrival (the server's clock between the two casts) is taken whatever its ticks say. The client stamps a cast with its copy of the server's tick (`GetServerTick`: its own millisecond clock on a base), and sets that base to the server's number whenever a time sync answer arrives, without any correction for the time the answer took (`SetServerTickBase`, `CNetworkService::OnTimeSyncResponse`); an answer that is held up moves the ticks after it back, so two honest casts can lie closer by their ticks than they were. A cast that is early by its arrival (packets held up by the link or by a stalled client arrive together) draws the time it is short by from the session's lag budget: 2 s, of which 1 s comes back in 10 s. The cast's own tick may lie up to 2 s short of the time needed after the last cast's tick, or before it: a stall that holds the casts holds the time sync answer as well, and the first cast after it then carries a tick set back by the time of the stall (a stall of 0.3 to 2 s with casts 0.5 or 0.7 s apart is taken in full). A tick further back than that is refused and draws nothing. An empty budget refuses, so one stall of up to 2 s passes, a client that casts faster than its motions by its own ticks, or with ticks that fall, gets the same 2 s once, and a client that sets its ticks ahead gets 2 s once and is held to the arrival times after that; before, every pair of casts had its own allowance (0.5 s on a cooldown, 1 s on a motion), which a client with ticks set ahead got on every cast. In KMS2 sessions no cast is early by its arrival, so none draws. The ticks are also measured against the server's clock: when the ticks of a session's casts have run more than 1 s ahead of the server's time since the lowest point kept (it follows a fall of up to 1 s in 30 s; the first 5 s of a session are not measured), the clock counts as fast for 30 s: this is logged once at Information ("Fast clock"), and the session's casts draw nothing in that time. The measure starts at the session's first time sync, from the tick the server sends with it, not at the first cast: a first cast that a stall held up does not make the ticks after it look fast. A cast tick more than 2 s before the one before it counts as a fast clock too (a held-up time sync answer moves honest ticks back by the time it was held; no KMS2 cast tick goes back at all). The position syncs have their own measure with 2 s. In KMS2 sessions cast ticks run ahead by at most 341 ms and sync ticks by at most 1,080 ms after the first 5 s (up to 9.7 s within them).
  - Movement: a position leaves the 1 s window by its own tick only when it also arrived 1 s ago, or a second lag budget of the same size gives the time missing (positions held up together draw once); with a fast clock nothing is drawn. So ticks set ahead no longer shorten the time a distance is measured over. A tick that lies before the last sync's no longer empties the window: the positions kept are moved back with it, the new one counting as later than the last by the time between their arrivals, so syncs whose ticks keep falling are measured by their arrivals. The moves have their own budget so that this check, which only logs, cannot empty the one the casts draw from. A player whose position syncs lie more than 1,800 units apart on the ground within 1 s (by the syncs' own ticks; the height is not counted) is logged at Information with both positions and the state, one line per player in 10 s with the count since the last. Nothing is refused or moved back. The 2 s after the server moves the player itself are not measured. KMS2: half of all 1 s spans within 346 units, 99 in 100 within 748, 999 in 1000 within 1,011, the furthest 1,726; walking is 350 a second at movement speed 100.
  - Not checked: mounts (their speed is not told apart), the Lua bodies (the speed of a motion, of a move), the time from the cast to an attack point, a motion without a sequence on the player's rig, and a held skill whose motion has no loop keys.
  - Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Damage of attack type All:** the target's resistance is now its physical or magical resistance, by the higher of the caster's two attacks; before, the target's own attack stat was read as the resistance.
- **Stat rebuild death:** a stat rebuild (StatsManager.Refresh: level up, equip, buffs, pets, job change, field entry; a battle mount's static stats) cleared every stat to 0 and added the new values back, Current with them. The rebuild runs on the thread of whatever caused it, often a session thread, while the field thread's Actor.Update kills a player whose Health.Current is 0, so a living player could die in the middle of a rebuild. Each stat now keeps its Current through the rebuild; Base and Total change as before, and damage or healing in that time is clamped at 0 only.
- **Stat rebuild heal:** the rebuild also filled every stat's Current to its Total, so any buff with stats gained or lost, an equip or a pet summon was a full heal. KMS2 does not: a hurt player keeps its HP across a buff gained or lost that leaves the maximum alone, and when an equip, a pet or a buff changes the maximum, the HP keeps its share of it (Current x new maximum / old maximum, rounded down, both ways). A level up is the one fill: KMS2 sends HP and SP at their maximum right after it. The rebuild now ends that way: every stat keeps its share, Health and Spirit fill on a level up (also the GM level command), and a dead player stays at 0.
  - Chosen here: SP, stamina and the other stats follow the same share rule (only HP was measured); a stat with no Total before the rebuild starts full; a battle mount's static stats scale the same way.
  - Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Spent attribute points:** the points a player spends on str, dex, int, luk, health (10 a point) or critical rate (3 a point) are now added on every stat rebuild, after the base values and before the items. Before, they were added to the totals once, so the next rebuild (an equip, a buff, a level up, a pet) dropped them until the next login. A spend or a reset answers with the allocation and then the whole stat block, as KMS2 sends; before, only the one stat was sent.
- **Attribute point reset:** KMS2 answers a reset with the allocation (AttributePoint 1), the notice and then the stat block; the stats went before the notice. A reset with nothing spent is not answered (the client sends none, `CStatPointManager::RequestReset`).
- **Attack and resistance from stats:** physical and magical attack and physical and magical resistance come from str, dex and int: the base value from the base stats, the total from the total stats (spent points, items, buffs), each rounded down, as in KMS2. Before, only the base stats counted, the resistances were 0, and a beginner had no attack. The client computes none of these; the coefficients are fitted to KMS2 values and reproduce every known one for the job (count in brackets): physical attack 0.63 str + 0.175 dex, at least 1, and physical resistance 0.11 str + 0.165 dex for beginner, knight, berserker and rune blade (39); magical attack 0.567 int for wizard, soul binder and rune blade (33), 0.4725 int for priest (9), 0.35 int, at least 1, for beginner, knight, berserker and striker (23) and for thief and archer (3); magical resistance 0.11 int for wizard, priest and soul binder (21), 0.22 int for rune blade (21).
  - Chosen here: magical resistance 0.55 int for beginner, knight, berserker and striker (the known values allow 0.539 to 0.555) and for thief and archer (fits their 3 known values); striker's physical resistance 0.11 dex + 0.165 str (fits its 5 known values, which do not settle it alone); a rate bonus on str, dex or int counts before the attack is derived.
  - Chosen here, kept from before: striker's physical attack (fits its 5 known values), the casters' physical attack (wizard 2 of 11, priest 8 of 9 known values) the physical attack of archer and thief and every attack of heavy gunner and assassin (too few known values); these jobs' unsettled resistances stay 0.
- **First login of a new name:** the client may open two login connections at once. With auto register both found no account and both tried to create one, so the second failed on the unique username, its login failed with gRPC Unknown, that connection dropped and the client stayed on the login screen. The World now creates accounts one at a time and looks for the account again first, so the second login is checked as one for the account the first made (password included). A create that still fails on the unique username reads the account back the same way.
  - Chosen here: one lock for all account creates in the World, not one per name; a create is rare.
- **Client time zone:** the client shows its clock and counts every reset (the alliance mission window's "Time until Reset", quest and dungeon resets) in the zone TimeSync 0 and 1 give it (`CMsTime::SetTimeZone(zone, dst)`, client/ms2/networkservice.cpp:767; its local time is UTC minus the zone plus the dst hours), and the server sent 0, so a client counted to UTC midnight while the server's day (daily reset, alliance missions) runs in its own local time. TimeSync now sends the seconds west of UTC of the server's local zone at that time (daylight saving included, dst byte 0); LoginResult 7 (restricted), which also sets the zone, sends the same. LoginResult 0 does not set a zone in the client and is unchanged.
- **TimeSync 4 not built:** the client's TimeSync mode 4 (networkservice.cpp:783) only puts the server time and a signed number into the HUD's version text (`UIUpperHudDialog::SetVersionText`, uiupperhuddialog.cpp:650; with the WaterMark feature also the s_upper_hud_version label); nothing in the client says what the number is, and WaterMark (feature 201) is off on KR Live (level 99), so the server does not send it.
- **Rock-paper-scissors (Microgame 0x0114):** the RPS event's game between two players now runs. Banner (mode 0), invite (1), the invitee's ack (0xC) or auto-reject (6), accept (2), decline (3), cancel (5), card (7) and reward claim (10) follow the client's MicroGameManagerCli and its sender UIs. A game is one round; both cards in, each player gets mode 0xB (draw 0, win 1, lose 2, own card, a card 0..2), loses one ticket (the event's GameTicketId), and gets user value 1800 (play count) plus 1 and the play_rps and play_rps_win / lose / draw conditions, with the event's ticket as the code (the 11 trophies of these results each name a ticket item as their code, 92000139 names 20302424, and KMS2 sends their updates at the result; before, the code check dropped every one of them). A claim needs the reward's play count and an index not yet in user value 1801 (a comma list, as the client parses it); the items go to the bag or the mail, and mode 0xA lists them. Both values expire at the next local midnight, as the attendance values do. A player leaving the field ends the invite or game, and the partner gets mode 6. The event writer also missed the item count in each reward (RpsContext::Read), which shifted every field after the first reward. Chosen here: the server uses the first active RPS event (the client keeps one); the third byte of mode 0xB is the partner's card (the client checks only that it is 0..2); the 300 distance check (MicroGameMaxDistance) uses the positions the server last got; a player without a ticket when the second card comes in ends the game for both with mode 6, with no count and no ticket taken, and a ticket that cannot be consumed does the same (the partner's ticket is then not taken; when it already was, the error is logged and the ticket is given back); mode 6 reasons 1 and 3 (blocked in this field) are never sent, as no server data for such a field is known; an invite nobody answers ends after 60 s and a game without both cards after 60 s, both players getting mode 6 (server limits: the client's request box declines by itself after MicroGameRequestDuration, 20 s by default); a player can invite again 5 s after the last invite; a claim whose items can neither be created nor bagged nor mailed stays open (each failure is logged), and with at least one item delivered (or none to deliver) it is marked claimed and mode 0xA lists the delivered items; the player's event values take a lock, as a game writes the partner's values from the other player's handler and logout saves them. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Couple dance (Microgame 0x0114, modes 0xD to 0x19):** the CoupleDance event and its dance between two players now run. The ingest reads the event (value1 <rewards>: the daily rewards by order and the good-end bonus; value2 <gameData>: the dances, the round count, the field and the eight steps) as CoupleDanceContext::LoadReward and LoadDance do, and drops it where they fail; the event list writes it in the CoupleDanceContext::Read layout (the event id twice, as GameEvent::vfunc_4 reads it first). Every mode carries the event id. Banner (0xD), invite (0xE), the invitee's ack (0x10) or auto-reject (0x14), accept (0xF), decline (0x11), cancel (0x13), answer (0x15) and reward claim (0x19) follow MicroGameManagerCli and its sender UIs. Accept sends mode 0xF to the inviter and mode 0x12 with one stage per round to both; each round both answers in, each player gets mode 0x16 (round, good, own dance, partner's dance). After the last round each player's user value 2101 (a comma list, one state per daily reward) gets its first open entry set to 1, or 2 on a good end, and the couple_dance_event condition counts up only then, so it stops with the day's daily rewards; a claim of a step in state 1 or 2 gives that daily reward (plus the good-end bonus for 2) to the bag or the mail and sets 3 or 4. The value expires at the next local midnight. A player leaving the field or cancelling a running dance ends it, and the partner gets mode 0x14 with the event id the invite was made for. A player is in at most one RPS or couple dance invite or game. Chosen here: a round is good when both chose the same dance index, and the good-end count is reached by the good rounds (the client only counts the good flags it gets); the stage of each round is a random dance index (the client puts it among the three dances it offers); when all daily entries are used, a finished dance records nothing (no condition count) but can still be played; the server takes the active event with the sent id, and keeps the invite's id: a later mode of the same invite or dance with another id is ignored, and an answer with another id or for an event that ended ends the dance for both with mode 0x14; the ingest also drops an event whose round count does not fit the byte or whose good-end count does not fit the short the event list writes; the 300 distance check uses the positions the server last got; the event's field id is read and sent but not checked; answers for another round, a dance index outside the event's dances or a second answer are dropped; an invite nobody answers ends after 60 s and a round without both answers after 60 s from its start, both players getting mode 0x14 (server limits; the client's request box declines by itself after 20 s, and its code has no choice countdown); a player can invite again 5 s after the last invite; there is no reward notice packet (the client has none for this event); a claim marks the step received only when at least one item reached the bag or the mail (or the reward has no items), and logs each item it could not create or deliver; no ticket is taken. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **RPS and couple dance rewards, given once:** a reward item goes to the bag only when the bag has room for all of it (the pieces the existing stacks cannot take, rounded up to slots at the item's slot maximum); otherwise the whole item goes to the mail. Before, an add over the slot maximum filled the stacks and then failed, and the mail sent the full amount again. A mail whose item cannot be created is deleted, so a failed claim no longer leaves an empty mail on each retry. A reward row with no items is marked claimed. When the inviter's RPS ticket was taken and the invitee's could not be, the inviter gets the ticket back (bag or mail). The time-limit check of both games returns at once when no invite, game or cooldown is open. Chosen here: the invite cooldown stays per game (an RPS invite does not hold back a dance invite); a reward that does not fit the bag is mailed whole, not split. Live try: not run (tests only).

## Maple Survival (Mushking Royale)

Mushking Royale needs the restored client (the MS2Recomp branch restore, which brings back the KMS client's Maple Survival manager and windows) and the client's own survival tables (table/na: maplesurvivalopenperiod, survivallevel, survivallevelreward, survivalpassreward, survivalpassreward_paid, maplesurvivalskininfo), which the KR client pack does not ship; the client loads them as loose files. The ingest reads the server data's table/na copies as they are, with no locale or feature filter (a data pack without them leaves the table empty: no season, no levels, no pass). The stock KR client is not affected: it never sends op 0xC2, and the server sends the survival lobby and queue state only when `MS2_SURVIVAL_CLIENT=1` is set (otherwise the entry sends the record and medals as before).

- Requests (op 0xC2, the restored client's): 0 queue solo, 1 and 6 join, 2 cancel, 3 refuse, 4 register the party as a squad (the chief of 2 to 4 online members), 8 equip a skin, 0x12 lobby refresh and level reward claim, 0x22 buy the paid pass (the season's passPrice in merets), 0x23 claim pass levels; 5 (season record) is not served, as the client never sends it (`CMapleSurvivalManager::RequestSeasonRecord`, maplesurvival.cpp:382, has no caller; the season 0x16 and the record 0x17 come with each lobby send); SpectateMushtopia 0xAB u8 0 from a fallen player watches a living one (answer 0x11A mode 1, the client's observer state); u8 2 + the character when the observer switches whom it watches (the client turns its camera first): the server checks the pick like u8 0 and answers only when its pick differs (the character fell, left or is no participant), moving the observer to the first one still playing. Chosen here: the answer to a switch (no client code says the server answers one). Tested: MapleSurvivalTests.SpectateAnswer.
- Queue: the World's arena queue (arena 100); a match takes whole units (squads, solo players) in arrival order up to `MS2_SURVIVAL_PLAYERS` players (default 20, the survival script's count; 1 to 20; the script's 20 is read as that count in a match field). A squad is never split: a unit that would take the match past its count waits for the next one, and a match holds at least two units. A member's logout or channel change, a party leave or kick, or a disband takes its squad out of the queue, and every member gets the unregister (Party 0x37 false) and the idle state. The match field is a new room of 82000000; the map's own scripts run the round (boats, field game, storm).
- Game: the players of different squads are enemies; a death or leave ends a player's game, the kill goes to the last hitter within 10 s; a squad's rank is set when its last member falls; the last squad standing wins. The storm (SurvivalContents widget): a safe circle at the field's centre shrinking in 8 steps of 60 s (SurvivalEvent 0, then 4 ready, 6 countdown, 5 start and 1 new radius per step), 5 % of the total health a second outside it; the game's end stops the steps and clears the zones (SurvivalEvent 3), since the client prints the storm lines of 4, 5 and 6 whatever the game's state. SurvivalEvent 2 (one zone's node and markers removed) is not sent: the game has one zone, and 3 clears it. Results (Survival 0x11) carry the rating, rank, participants, kills, survival level and exp, levels and time as the result window reads them (its level bar takes r6-r10 as a1-a5: r8 the exp inside the level after, r9 the level before, r10 the level after, the bar's total from the client's own survivallevel); a game gives survival exp (levels by survivallevel, level rewards at each level), and a season rating in the survival-record table (migration AddSurvivalRecord; squads under their own season key).
- Kill log status (Survival 0x1a, 0x1b): the kill log's status line (`UISurvivalKillLogDialog::RefreshStatus`, survivalkilllogdialog.cpp:227) calls the movie's setStatus with 0x1b's two ints and 0x1a's int; the movie (uisurvivalkilllogdialog.gfx) shows the first in the survivor field and the third in the own-kills field (not when it is 65535), and does not read the second. The server now sends 0x1b with the survivors to the field at the start and after each fall, and 0x1a with the player's own kills to each participant at the start and to the killer after a kill. Before, it sent the survivors in 0x1a, so the own-kills field showed the survivors and the survivor field stayed empty. Chosen here: 0x1b's second int is the match's participants and its flag byte 0 (no client code read for them).
- Skins and lobby level (restored client only; the KR client gets the medal list as before): a new medal sends Survival 0x1f (its slot, 0 tail, 1 riding, 2 gliding, the skin and its end, `long.MaxValue` as in the medal list 0x1e) and 0x21 (the slot and the skin, for the client's s_maple_survival_learn_skin line; the restored client does not read the int, GMS formats an item name into the line, so chosen here: the skin); the client appends the skin to its slot (maplesurvival.cpp:616). Before, the whole list was sent again and the line never showed. A renewed medal still sends the list. A Royale Experience Tonic now also sends 0x15 (the exp inside the level, the level, and the level whose reward was given last, the level itself as in 0x12); the record 0x17 refreshes only the pass window, so the lobby showed the old level until its next send. A game's result already sends the whole lobby.
- Trophies and quest conditions (21300042-21300099, 23200042): survival_enter at the game's start; with each participant's result, the match's counts: survival_kill (target the kills, at least), survival_rank_with_kill (code the rank and target the kills, both at most: 21300058 "Place 5th ... without defeating anyone" holds for 5th or better), survival_npc_kill (monsters it helped defeat), survival_breakable_object, survival_item_get (code the item, target the amount that entered the inventory in the match; the starting kit is given before the game counts), survival_kill_use_skill (code the killing hit's skill, target its kills) and survive_map (the seconds from the start to the fall or the end, code the map); for the winning squad survival_win_without_interact (no interact object completed), survival_win_without_npckill and survival_win_use_one_skill (every kill by the one skill, its code). Each kill raises survival_total_kill_use_skill and survival_total_kill_use_single_skill (code the skill) and, when the killing hit landed in the same field tick as the hit behind the killer's previous kill, survival_double_kill (Chosen here: the client has no window for "simultaneously"). Buying the gold pass raises survival_buy_gold_pass. Not raised: survival_kill_outside ("outside your field of vision" is the client's camera).
  - Parked: survival_kill_outside (21300070, a kill of a player outside the killer's view) is never raised. The client only registers the name (commonfiles/conditionutil.cpp:793); nothing in it defines the view range, so no kill can be told to be outside it.
- Items: 44 items carry `survival="1"` (28 PS skill items 20000733-20000993, 30001099/1102/1103/1104, the gliders 404000xx and the mounts 5060xxxx). The client never reads the flag (`ItemProperty::Load`, commonfiles/itemdatastruct.cpp:1122) nor the map's `<survival>` element (82000000-82000003, 82000006, 82000012; 82000002/3 are not in the KR ingest: userDrop, userAdditionalDrop, plusInven, plusInven_Basic, plusInven_Zero), has no survival bag or tab, and the Survival packet has no item command: a picked-up item is a normal inventory item. The ingest now keeps the flag (`ItemMetadataProperty.Survival`) and the element (`MapMetadataProperty.Survival`). Chosen here (`SurvivalItemRules`): an item with the flag is a match item unless it also comes from outside the match, so 30001099 (an npc script collects it), 30001102 (box 320000565 of interact object 32000021), 30001103 and 30001104 (event and promotion boxes), 40400067 (GameEvent) and 50600212 (a game shop) stay normal items. Entering a field without a survival element deletes the match items from the inventory, as does entering a survival map whose plusInven_Zero is 1 (read as "the match starts empty"), except the re-entry of a participant into the same field instance while its game still runs (it fell when it left, so it watches; a channel change lands in another instance and clears), with the normal remove packet and one log line per item; a logout or crash inside a match is covered at the next field entry. A match item cannot be traded, sold to a shop, listed on the Black Market or the Meret Market (the "cannot register this item" box), or put in storage or a pet bag (player mail carries no items). The 18 SurvivalContents03 items (PS items 20000980-20000993 such as 20000985, and the glider and mount skins) are off for KR Live and stay out of the KR ingest, so 26 of the 44 are ingested and 20 of them are match items. Chosen here: when the game starts, each participant gets the plusInven_Basic box (311000042: 20000885, 20000895, 20000744) in the inventory, once per participant per game, so a rejoin gets none; an item that does not fit lands at the player. A participant killed while the game runs drops, once per death and with no owner (userDropOwnership is 0), its match items (userDrop=1; never the kept items above) and one roll of the userAdditionalDrop box (311000039, four PS items) at the body; the items despawn like other field drops. Leaving the match drops nothing.
- Chosen here (nothing settles them): the storm geometry, timing and damage; start rating 1000 and +30 / +10 / -10 by rank; game exp 1000 + 300 a kill + 200 a squad ranked below; level rewards given at each level-up.
- CreateFieldGame MapleSurvivalTeam (the Countdown state of the squad maps 82000002/3) starts the field's survival game as MapleSurvival does, when none runs. Chosen here: the same rules (the players of different squads are already enemies; no data or client code gives the squad game other rules). Tested: MapleSurvivalTests.FieldGameStartsSurvival.
- Not built: squad-only matches (no queue sends players to 82000002/3), re-invite, the season window and rankings, revival of squad members. Known gaps: a squad register (mode 4) refused for no party, not the chief, too many members or an offline member answers only Party 0x37 false, with no reason shown; CreateFieldGame MapleSurvival on 82000000 entered outside a match (no arena) makes a game whose script still waits for 20 players.

## Database migrations
The servers do not migrate the game database when they start. After an update that adds a migration (`Maple2.Server.World/Migrations`), run `dotnet ef database update` in `Maple2.Server.World` (the ingest runs the same command) before the servers start. Without the `home-block` table of `AddHomeBlockTable` every login fails, because the home block list is read at login. Without the `nurturing-daily` table of `AddNurturingDaily` feeding a housing pet or playing with one fails (the daily record of feedings and plays is kept there; the daily reset empties it). Without the `dark-stream-record` table of `AddDarkStreamRecord` the end of a Dark Stream run fails to read and keep the best score, and the web ranking's Dark Stream boards fail. Without the `boss-clear` and `boss-clear-member` tables of `AddBossClear` the clear of a raid, chaos raid, reverse raid, lapenta, turka or fame challenge room with a BossRanking fails to keep its row, and the web ranking's boss and fame challenge boards fail. Without the `Damage` column of `AddBossClearDamage` every boss clear fails to keep its row, and the web ranking's boss, fame challenge and world boss boards fail. Without the `colosseum-record` table of `AddColosseumRecord` the end of a Colosseum run fails to keep the best run, and the web ranking's Colosseum board fails. Without the `ExtraCooldownTime` column of `AddDungeonExtraCooldown` every dungeon record read fails. Without the `home-commend` table of `AddHomeCommend` every login fails (the last commendation time is read at login), and so do loading a home and the web ranking's house board. Without the `maid` table of `AddMaid` every login fails (the owner's maids are read at login), and so do removing any cube and loading a home with a maid furnishing (after a re-ingest). Without `MaidKeyedByFurnishing` a picked-up maid cannot move to its storage item (the key to the cube table refuses it) and is deleted with its cube, as before. Without the `FamePoints` column of `AddFamePoints` every character load fails (the column is read with the rest of `character-unlock`). Without the `account-event-value` table of `AccountEventValue` every login fails, with the event replay switches on or off, because every login reads the account's event values: run `dotnet ef database update` before the servers start on this commit. Without the `arcade-record` table of `AddArcadeRecord` the end of a Spring Farm arcade game fails to keep the best score, and so does the arcade lobby's score list (PlayArcade mode 9). Without the `buddy-notice` table of `AddBuddyNotice` every login fails (the friend notices are read at login), and so do accepting or declining a friend request and removing a friend while the other player is offline. Without the `guild-expel-notice` table of `AddGuildExpelNotice` every login fails (the kept guild expels are read at login), and an offline member's expel keeps no notice.

## GM level
The client has one GM level byte. The server sends it from the account's permissions (`AdminLevel.Of`), in the Admin packet at field entry and again when `perm` changes the permissions:

| Account permissions | Level |
|---|---|
| Admin | 255 |
| GameMaster (all of its flags; finer flags on top do not matter) | 50 |
| anything else, also single flags such as Warp or SpawnItem | 1 (the client's own value for a player) |

The level only switches client features on (debug keys, filter skip, debug buttons). The client can raise it by itself, so every GM action is still checked on the server.

The GM grade byte in the party, guild and buddy views comes from the player info the World server keeps. A `perm` change is sent to World as a player update and goes on to the channels with the other profile fields, so those views follow it the next time they are sent. The field view and the Admin packet follow at once.

GM chat commands the client's own GM windows and hotkeys send (each needs the permission in brackets):

| Command | What the server does |
|---|---|
| `/MoveField <id> [flag]` (Warp) | move to the field |
| `/AddField <id>` (Warp) | a plain move to the field, the same as `/MoveField` |
| `/ClearInven` (GameMaster) | empties every inventory tab; worn items and the item of a summoned pet stay |
| `/ToggleInvisibleFromNpc` (GameMaster) | monsters do not pick the player as a target |
| `/ToggleDeathPower` (GameMaster) | each hit kills the monster it lands on; friendly NPCs and pets take normal damage |

The two toggles last for the session (they stay on across field changes and go off at a channel change, a relog, or when the account stops being a GM).

## GM-only packets
Some packets and packet modes are sent by the client only from a GM command, a debug hotkey or a developer dialog (the client tests its GM level or its debug mode before it writes them). A handler for one of those starts with `GmCheck.Allow(session, OpCode, mode)` (`Maple2.Server.Game/Util/GmCheck.cs`): the account needs the GameMaster or Admin permission, as set with the `perm` command. Any other sender is dropped with one warning (account id, opcode, mode) and gets no reply. A new handler or mode of that kind must start the same way.

The client's GM create windows are handled, each behind that check (a plain account changes nothing):

| Packet | Window | What the server does |
|---|---|---|
| 0x15 RequestItemCreate | item create | Needs SpawnItem as well. Creates the item (id, amount, rank) into the inventory by the normal add path. A rank outside 1-6 or an amount below 1 is refused. A stackable item gives one stack at most; other items give one per free slot; a currency id (meso, meret, experience orb and the like) gives 100,000,000 at most per request. A full bag answers with the inventory-full notice. An unknown id is refused. The period, level, enchant and option fields are read and not applied. |
| 0x24 RequestNpcCreate | NPC create | Needs SpawnNpc as well. Spawns the NPC at the position and rotation the window sends (1 to 50 per request, within 3000 units of the sender). One field instance holds at most 200 living GM-spawned NPCs, counted together with the `npc` command, which follows the same two limits. The NPC belongs to that field instance and goes with it. The pet button (pet flag set) is read and not handled. |
| 0x5C RequestAdditionalEffect | effect control | Mode 0 puts the effect (id, level) on the sender; the packet names no target. Mode 1 (reset) takes off the buffs this window created in this session that are still on. A buff the sender already had, or got again later from a skill or an item, stays. |
| 0x50 SetDebugMode | debug mode: minimap click (mode 2), NPC control (mode 4), debug guide commands (mode 7) | Mode 2 (a click on a minimap entity while the debug mode is on) moves the sender to the entity's position when the field accepts it. Mode 4 names an NPC by object id: picking the NPC is answered with DebugMode 0x0087 kind 6 (its skill list) and kind 5 (the window's text: movement state, AI file, battle target, physical and magic attack, defense, critical rate, health and its total; which texts retail put there is not known, these are the server's own choice; no AI trace is kept, so tracking is off; the extra list carries the NPC's AI values by key, at most 32; the AI file text is marked while the AI is switched off), and kind 5 is sent again after a health button, an AI on or off button, the AI file button and the extra button. The health buttons set its health (1 to 100 percent, or a value; never below 1 or above the total, so the window does not kill), the additional effect buttons put an effect (id, level) on it or take every buff of that id off. The skill button makes the NPC cast the first skill of the list once, facing the sender (the loop count and the rest of the list are not played); stop skill cancels the cast. That cast is a normal NPC cast: it hits players in range exactly like the NPC's own skill, so it is a GM tool to use with care. AI off (command 5) stops the NPC from choosing what to do next until AI on (command 4): its AI and idle steps are skipped, what it already does runs to its end, and its battle target is still kept up to date. The switch is logged with the account and lasts until the NPC leaves the field. The extra button (command 0xB) sends kind 5 again; the client has no reader of its own for that button, so what retail answered is not known. The AI file button (command 0xD) sends the path of an xml file picked under the client's Data/Xml/AI folder (uinpccontroldialog.cpp:576-591). The server takes the part of the path below that folder (or the bare file name) as the AI's name; a name it has no AI for, a text that is no xml name or one with a `..` part changes nothing and is logged. The NPC gets the named AI at its next update on the field thread: the AI it ran is dropped where it stood, its AI values and battle target stay. The change is logged with the account, lasts until the NPC leaves the field, and kind 5 is sent again with the new name. That the file replaces the NPC's AI is read from the button's name and its one argument; what else retail did with it is not known. The other buttons (battle setting, traces, assist, current additional) are read and logged, as are modes 0 and 9. Mode 7 (the debug guide window's multi execute button) carries 1 to 32 lines of command and argument, each text at most 256 characters; anything else is dropped. The lines are sent back as DebugMode 0x0087 kind 7 with the sender's name to the sender only; a client with the debug guide window open enters each line in its own chat, so every line comes back as that account's chat command and passes that command's permission check. No other client gets them, also not another GM in the same field: one GM cannot make another client run commands. The client hands every packet to every open window (networkservice.cpp:719, uiservice.cpp:1386), and its NPC control window reads kind 7 as an AI file name and an id and opens an AI debug window for it (uinpccontroldialog.cpp:233); the client sends nothing when that window closes, so once the sender has picked an NPC with the debug camera (mode 4, select) the lines are no longer sent back for the rest of that session (logged), until it logs in again or changes channel. Who retail sent kind 7 to is not known from the client; sender only is this server's own choice. |
| 0x89 CharacterAbility mode 3 | ability window (chat command `LearnAllAbility`) | Learns every ability of the table that the sender has not learned and sends the window's load packet (see Ability window above). |
| 0x9A ItemRepeat | item create, repeat tool | Needs SpawnItem as well. The create window's request plus a repeat count (the window's default is 100). The server rolls the item's options (id and rank as the window sends them) that many times and answers with 0x101 ItemOptionSimulator: mode 0, then mode 1 packets with the item id, the rank and the rolls (the option block of an item), then mode 2, on which the client writes the rolls to `../Debug/itemOptionRepeat.xml`. No item is given. A rank outside 1-6, a count below 1 or an unknown id is refused. Chosen here: at most 1,000 rolls per request, 100 per mode 1 packet (the window sets no maximum). |

The item and NPC windows need the same permission as the chat command that does the same thing (`item`: SpawnItem, `npc`: SpawnNpc), on top of the GM check. GameMaster alone holds neither flag, so a GameMaster account gets them with `perm` or is an Admin.

The client's debug mode (the GM buttons of the enchant, remake, exchange scroll and other dialogs) is toggled by key action 600001, the ` key in the client's default key file; the client turns it on only at GM level 50 or more. The key table the server sends holds that bind for a GM account only (added on ` or Scroll Lock when the saved table lacks it, taken out for a plain account). Every packet those buttons send is behind the GM check or has no handler.

A field with `enterAdminLevel` above 0 in its map file (8 test fields in the data: 80000000 and seven 99999xxx ids) is closed to plain accounts: every move into it is refused, and so is taxi travel to it (walk, car, rotors, meso call and the air taxi item), with the client's own message box, `s_field_limit_admin`. A plain character saved in such a field logs into its return field. A GM account passes. The value is read at ingest, so it needs a re-ingest.

The client reads no reply of its own for the first three; it shows the normal item, NPC and buff packets.

## Known not to work

The client sends 22 sub-types that the server does not handle yet. They are listed here per system, with the number of unhandled sub-types:

| system | count | system | count | system | count |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EventReward | 7 | Guild | 1 | Wedding | 0 |
| RequestItemEnchant | 3 | RequestCube | 3 | HomeAction | 2 |
| Quest | 2 | RoomDungeon | 1 | Party | 0 |

These systems have one unhandled sub-type each: FurnishingStorage, InteractObject, Ugc.

Guild handling is switched on (`HandlingOn` in `GuildHandler.cs`). The Guild count above uses this measure: the 39 sub-types that the client writes (one writer each in `CGuildManager`), minus those with a `case` in the switch of `GuildHandler.cs`. That leaves 1: 65 (emblem). A second measure, not in the table: no sub-type is left with a case that only reads the body or writes a debug line and changes nothing. The count was 6 before and the total 47; neither measure gives 6 today.

Guild rank rights, as the client tests them before it sends: invite 2, expel 4, notice 8, change a member's rank 32, emblem 64. The notice and emblem buttons of the guild window are enabled from rights 8 and 64 (and for the leader), so a rank with rights 3 has both grey and sends nothing. The server sends a changed rank (29, with the rights) and a changed member rank (21) to every member in the form the client reads, and the client stores them and hands them to the window; an open window showed the new rank name, the new member rank and the invite button only after it was reopened in the live try. That part is inside the window's Flash file and is not answered by a packet.

Guild 68, 87 and 88 are handled (they had a case that only read). 68 stores the "listed" flag and the focus bits of the guild; the sender needs the invite right, as in the client, and every member gets the new values. 87 (search by focus) and 88 (search by name, 2 characters or more) answer only a player without a guild, as the client only sends them then. The server's own rules, not read from the client: only listed guilds are found, a focus search finds a guild that shares at least one focus bit (the client's "any" value finds all), a name search finds names that contain the text, and at most 90 guilds are sent (10 pages of the window). The three trophy numbers of a search row are sent as 0. The third number after the member count is the guild's experience, which the client turns into the level; the fork sent the focus there before. Existing guilds become listed with the migration `AddGuildListed`.

Guild 82, 83 and 84 (join requests) are handled, with the lists 85 and 86 and the notices 45 to 48. 82: a player without a guild asks to join a listed guild; one open request per guild and at most 10 open requests (the client's own limit, error 37). The answer is 82 with the id of the new request (KMS2 sends the id the request's row in 86 starts with; the client only tests that it is above 0; it was the guild id) and the guild name, and the members of that guild who hold the invite right get the new row (45). 83 withdraws an own request; the id is matched against the guild ids of the player's requests first and then against the request ids, because the client code names it a guild id and the request window holds both. Those members get 46. 84 is sent by a member with the invite right, with the request id (the accept page row's "requestSn") and accept or refuse; it is matched against the request ids of the own guild only, not against character ids. On accept the applicant joins as with an invite, all other requests of the applicant are removed (46 to those guilds), and the members with the invite right get 47; the applicant gets 48 and, when online, the guild. On refuse the applicant gets 48 too, which only drops the request from its list and shows no text (`OnPacketMyJoinRequestRemoved`, guildmanager.cpp:3006), and the client's reject mail: a system mail from `s_guild_search_join_reject_mail_sender` with `_title` and `_content`, each with the guild's name, written by the world once the request is removed and announced to the applicant's channel when online. 85 lists the open requests of the own guild (invite right), 86 the own requests of a player without a guild. In the request row the server now writes the account id before the character id: the accept page names the fourth id "charSn" and uses it for the profile picture. The requests are rows of the existing `guild-application` table; no migration. The client has no separate flag for automatic acceptance: the value the request page shows is the "listed" byte of Guild 68 (`CGuildInfo` +0x154, set with the focus bits), so a request always waits for an answer. A guild that is not listed refuses a request with "guild not found". A player who joins a guild by an invite, or creates one, loses the open requests too (46 to those guilds): the member row and the removal are one save, so a request made before never outlives the join. The member limit is checked and the member added under one lock for both ways in.

Guild 71 (mail): a member with the mail right sends a title (1 to 100 characters) and a text (1 to 2000); every other member gets it from the sender as a mail of kind 2, and the sender gets 71. Kind 2 is what KMS2 gives a guild mail (a mail between two players is kind 1); the client lists kinds 1 to 3 on the player tab with the user mail icon and opens the sender menu only for kind 1 (uimaillistdialog.cpp:987). It was kind 1. The client has no waiting time between two mails. The server has one, as its own rule: one guild mail per sender in 60 seconds (one packet writes a mail for every other member); a mail sent sooner is refused with guild error 45, "Please try again later." (the client has no text for a mail sent too soon). World keeps the time, so the limit holds on every channel; the turn is taken before the mails are written, so a failed write still costs the sender the 60 seconds, and a World restart clears the times. Guild 113 (donate): the body is the count of donations. One donation costs the `guildDonateMeso` constant and adds the same to the guild fund. Refused, as in the client: a count under 1, a member who joined today, more donations in a day (UTC) than the guild level allows (`donationMax`), a fund over the level's maximum, too little meso. The member's count and time are stored and sent back in 113; the guild gets the new fund (51). Order: the channel takes the meso first, World adds the fund and saves the guild and the member at once, and the meso comes back only when World answers with a refusal (a failed save is one, and undoes the fund and the count). When the call to World fails without an answer the meso stays taken and is logged, since World may have added the fund: an error can cost the donor meso, but can not give a guild fund for nothing. Known gap: when World applied the donation and only its answer was lost, the donor has paid and the fund has grown, but the donor gets the error and no 113, and the client shows the old count of the day until the next login; when World did not apply it, the meso is lost and only the log line tells. A second known gap, the other way: World saves the fund at once, but the channel saves the donor's meso with the character, later; a channel crash between the two leaves the fund on the guild and the meso with the donor. Not done: the client adds a share of the cost while a guild fund event runs, and the guild table names coins and experience for a donation; the client shows neither amount in the reply, so what the donor should get is not known from the client and the donor gets none.

Guild 92 (skill upgrade): the master raises a guild skill by one level. As in the client (`CGuildManager::FUN_1413b9ac0`), the next level must exist, its `requireLevel` must not be above the guild level, and its `upgradeCost` is taken from the guild fund; World saves the guild before it answers; the members get the new fund (51) and the skill (55). Errors sent: 22 (not the master), 42 (guild level), 43 (fund). The client's "highest level" text has no code in the error packet, so that case answers the unknown error. The client pays the skills 1001 to 10000 from an event fund, which the server does not keep: those upgrades are refused with 43.

Guild 90 (skill use): the master uses a guild skill. As in the client (`CGuildManager::FUN_1413b8fe0`), the row of the skill's level must exist, its `requireLevel` must not be above the guild level, its `cost` is taken from the guild fund, and the skill must be neither running nor cooling down (the time of use plus the row's `duration`, and plus its `cooltime`; `CGuildSkill::IsUsable`). World saves the guild before it answers; the members get the new fund (51) and the skill with its time of use (54), the master the answer (90), and each member who is online gets the skill's effect for the duration; a member who enters a field while the skill runs (login, channel or map change, after joining) gets it for the time left. A member who is between two maps at the moment of the use gets the skill (54) at once and the effect on entering the next field: the channel stores the time of use before it asks the field for the effect, and every field entry reads it. Errors sent: 22 (not the master), 42 (guild level), 43 (fund), 44 (still running). The third value of a guild skill is the time of use, as the client reads it (`CGuildSkill::GetRemainingDuration`). The `cooltime` is a new value of the server's guild table: a database ingested before it reads as 0 until the next ingest. A row with no `duration` is refused with the unknown error (it would never count as running, so it could be paid for twice in a row). Known gaps: the "recall" kind of skill does nothing more than the effect; the event-fund skills (1001 to 10000) are refused.

Guild 91 (personal skill): a member buys a personal guild skill (ids above 10000) with meso. As in the client (`CGuildManager::FUN_1413b96e0`), the row of the skill's guild level must exist, its `requireLevel` must not be above the guild level, the member's meso pays its `cost`, and the member must not have the effect already. The channel takes the meso, gives the effect for the row's `duration` and answers 91; the guild does not change. Errors sent: 42 (guild level), 20 (meso), 44 (already has it). Known gaps: the client's event discount on the cost is not applied (the server has no such event value), and the meso is saved with the character, not at once.

Guild 74 (PvP history): the client asks for the guild PvP history when the PvP tab opens (`CGuildManager::RequestPvpHistoryAndSeason`, guildmanager.cpp:1144). The server runs no guild PvP, so a member gets the empty list (74 with the count 0, read by `OnPacketPvpHistory`, guildmanager.cpp:2805). Guild 75 (PvP season) is sent once per login after it, with no body (guildmanager.cpp:1152); the answer is the empty season list (75 with the count 0, `OnPacketPvpSeasonHistory`, guildmanager.cpp:2826).

Guild 0 (the own guild): the byte after the creation time is the guild's championship state (`CGuildInfo::Read`, commonfiles/guilddef_1.cpp:1261, shown by the championship page of the guild window; state 2 is needed to ask for a match, guildmanager.cpp:1181). KMS2 sends 0 for every guild and the server runs no championship, so 0 is sent (it was 1). The npc shop block before the storage list is sent as KMS2 sends it, a set flag and an empty shop list (`CGuildInfo::ReadNpcShop`, :1443; it was a clear flag, which the client reads the same way). The rest of the packet has the layout KMS2 sends from build 2495 on; older builds send 4 bytes less in the header.

Guild 99 (open a guild event): a member whose rank has the mini game right opens one of the guild events of `table/guildevent.xml` (`CGuildManager::FUN_1413ba080`, guildmanager.cpp:857). The World server checks what the client checks (the event has a row, the guild is a day old, the right, the event is not open), keeps the time of opening in memory and tells every member (56, `FUN_1413c3780`, guildmanager.cpp:2533: the chat line with the opener's name); the sender gets 99 (`OnPacketOpenEvent`, guildmanager.cpp:1911). An event is open for 30 seconds (`CGuildCustomEvent::IsActive`, guilddef_1.cpp:957). The guild a member loads lists every row of the event table, since the client only asks for an event its guild lists; the table is read by the ingest of this build, and with older data the list is empty and the client never sends 99. The refusal for a guild younger than a day uses the unknown error text: the number of the client's own text for it is not known.

Guild 100 (enter a guild event): while an event is open a member asks to enter it (`CGuildManager::SendGuildMode100`, guildmanager.cpp:881). The client reads no answer. The channel checks what the client checks (the event has a row, it is open, the member is not in its map) and moves the member to the event's map. A map of the instance table's guild event type has one room per guild: the first member to enter makes the guild's room and the others join it, so two guilds that open the same event do not meet. What runs in that map is the map's own script; the server has no guild event logic of its own.

Guild 109 (storage gift): a member gives an item of the guild storage to a member (`CGuildManager::FUN_1413bb490`, guildmanager.cpp:1046; the body is the item as `ItemEntity::Write` writes it, commonstruct_wa.cpp:84, then the receiver's name). The client sends it only for an item its copy of the storage holds. The server keeps no guild storage and sends no storage item, so the request cannot come from an unchanged client; one that comes is checked (in a guild, the receiver is a member, the count is positive, the storage holds the count) and refused with the result byte of 109 (`OnPacketStorageGiftResult`, guildmanager.cpp:2963). The gift itself, the need-period refusal (result 54) and the log rows of 112 are not handled: they need the storage.

Guild 115 (vs-game ticket): a member with the mini game right puts the guild in the queue of the guild vs game (`CGuildManager::SendGuildMode115`, guildmanager.cpp:1425, no body; the client asks only without a ticket (f354) or an open game (f350)). It is taken while a GuildVsGameOpen game event is on (KR 550001: Saturdays 10:55:10 to 12:00, local time), else refused with error 47 (`s_guild_err_isNotVsGameTime`); without the right, the no-authority error. The World keeps the queue (GuildVsGameLookup) and tells the guild's online members with 118 (`OnPacketVsGameTicket`, guildmanager.cpp:2656: registered, then whether to show the line; a second ask of a guild that waits gets 118 without the line). When a second guild takes a ticket the two are matched: the World has the first channel make a field of the guild vs game map (the lowest map id of the GuildVsGame instance fields, 66200001) with both guilds set as its teams, and both guilds' members get 119 with the match's id (`FUN_1413c45e0`, :2679: f350 set, f354 cleared, a 30 second entry prompt). A field that cannot be made sends 117 (match cancelled: f354 cleared and a line) to both. When no GuildVsGameOpen event is on anymore the waiting guilds get 117. Chosen here: guilds are matched in the order of their tickets, the first blue; no minimum of members online (the client has `s_guild_err_requireOnlineUserCount`, error 48, but no data names the count); a match guild asking again gets error 47; a match lasts until its field ends the game (guild_vs_game_end_game) or goes (no one came, or all left, before the end), its channel goes down or 30 minutes pass, and then both guilds get 119 with 0 (the client's open game is cleared, no prompt); the match ids of a World start begin at the clock's seconds, so a field left from an earlier start cannot end a new match; a guild that disbands while it waits keeps its place until it is matched or the event ends.

Guild 116 (vs-game ticket withdrawn): the client sends it only while it holds a ticket (`CGuildManager::SendGuildMode116`, guildmanager.cpp:1444, no body). For a member with the mini game right the World drops the guild's ticket and tells its members with 118 (no ticket; the line when there was one to drop, so a guild already matched or without a ticket gets 118 without the line). Without the right: the no-authority error.

Guild 120 (vs-game entry): the answer to the entry prompt of 119 (`CGuildManager::SendGuildMode120`, guildmanager.cpp:3386, no body). A member of a matched guild moves into the match field (on the match's channel, as a rank duel join does); the arrival is checked as a match arrival, so a field that has ended its game sends the member to the return field. Chosen here, as the rank duel and Maple Survival joins: a member who is dead, in a dungeon, in an instance those joins refuse, or in a rank duel or arena field is not moved and the World is not asked. Then, without a match (none, not made yet, ended), or when the move fails, the answer is 119 with the game id 0, which clears the client's open game without a prompt, and the member stays where they are. A member already in a match field is ignored.

Guild 112 (storage log): the client asks for the guild storage log once when its tab opens, and asks again on every opening until an answer comes (`CGuildManager::SendGuildMode112`). A member gets the empty list (112 with the count 0): the server handles no storage gift (109), so there is nothing to list.

Guild 101 (house upgrade): the body is the theme chosen for the next house level (the level itself is not sent; it is the guild's own plus 1). As in the client (`CGuildManager::FUN_1413ba820`), the house row of that level and theme must exist, its `requireLevel` must not be above the guild level, and its `upgradeCost` is taken from the guild fund. World saves the guild before it answers; the members get the new fund (51) and the house (57: the master's name, the level, the theme). Errors sent: 22 (not the master), 42 (guild level), 43 (fund); a level or theme without a row answers the unknown error. Members who are inside the old house map stay there until they leave; the next entry goes to the new map. Each guild has its own room of a house map: two guilds with the same house level and theme do not share a house.

Guild 102 (house theme): the body is the new theme of the house at its level. As in the client (`CGuildManager::FUN_1413babf0`), the house row of the guild's level and that theme must exist, the theme must be another one than today's, and the row's `reThemeCost` is taken from the guild fund; saved and sent as 101 (51 and 57). Errors sent: 22 (not the master), 43 (fund); the client's "same theme" text has no code in the error packet and answers the unknown error, as does a theme without a row.

Guild 114 (NPC upgrade): the body is the kind of guild NPC, which the master raises by one level; a kind the guild does not have yet is at level 0. As in the client (`CGuildManager::FUN_1413bb000`), the row of the next level must exist, its `requireGuildLevel` and `requireHouseLevel` must not be above the guild's level and house level, and its `upgradeCost` is taken from the guild fund. World saves the guild before it answers; the members get the new fund (51) and the NPC (59: the master's name, the kind, the level). Errors sent: 22 (not the master), 42 (guild level, and the house level too, for which the client shows the same text), 43 (fund); "highest level" has no code and answers the unknown error. A guild starts at house level 1, which is what the level 1 rows of the NPC table ask for; the level 2 rows ask for house level 2.

Narrowed, with the reason:
- Guild 65 (emblem text): `CGuildManager::SendGuildMode65` (guildmanager.cpp:689; a text of at most 500 characters, sent only with rank right 64) is the only writer, and nothing in the client calls it. The guild emblem is set through the Ugc upload of kind GuildEmblem and its confirmation, which the server handles. A normal client never sends 65, so it has no case and stays counted.
- HomeAction 10 (the OX quiz event of a home): an event the owner creates with an entry fee, a player limit and a share rate, with its own map, a host who types questions and visitors who answer O or X. The client sends ten kinds of request under this number (create, confirm, enter, start, question, answer and others). The server has none of the event, and the client's only refusal text for it depends on a content switch, not on an answer, so every kind is read and logged. What the server would have to send is not in the data: the create window shows a fee list that comes only from the server (a count and that many fees), and the prize, the share, the cool times, the event map and its portal come in the server's answers too. `OxQuiz.xml` in the server tables is the question bank of the regular OX event, not of this one, whose host types the questions.
- The user OX quiz fields (61000011 and 61000021, their scripts' OxQuizUGC widget): parked, client-gated. No schedule entry or OpenMassive item opens them; the client's way in is the OX quiz dialog (`UIOxQuizDialog`, UI 0x4f7d, its promote and create buttons), whose manager (`FUN_140dff2b0`, request the promote, and `RequestNewQuiz`) is not decompiled, so the packets of the host's quiz and of the widget's steps (CreateWall, StartRound, EndRound, PeekQuiz, CleanUpQuiz, ShowHostUI, HostUIChange, MoveAllUser, EndGame) are not known. The widget is the generic one (actions do nothing, conditions false), so the scripts stop at their first widget step.
- HomeAction 14 (PvP control of a home): the start button of the PvP control window sends a constant 1 and the six values of the window. The server has no PvP mode for homes. The client reads only the six values back and has no refusal text, so it is read and logged. The data has the cubes: the function cubes of items 50400174 (PvP team 0, a console), 50400175 (team 1) and 50400176 (team 2) have control type PVP. The client opens the window only on the server's HomeAction 14 (sub 0, six flags); it reads the team of a cube from its file (commonfiles/objectdata.cpp:309), and nothing else in the client source uses it; there is no rule for a match (teams, start, score, end). The C++ source does not name the six flags; it only passes them to the window's Flash file. So neither the match nor when to open the window can be built from the client.
- FurnishingStorage 4 (take a stored furnishing item into the bag): the server keeps every furnishing item in the furnishing storage and has no bag tab for them, so the request is dropped and the item stays stored. The client reads no refusal under this packet. Placing the item from the storage works. The client never sends it: its only writer, `CNetworkService::SendRetrieveFurnishingItem` (@0x141538600, networkservice.cpp:491), has no caller or pointer in the exe, and it also needs the item's m_88 time (the third long of the item block, always 0 from the server) to be in the future.
- EventReward 2 (event box), 5 (buddy event page opened), 12 and 13 (snowman event), 15 (level-up event) and 16 (event character): each belongs to an event kind that the server neither stores nor sends to the client (its event table has no record for the event box, snowman, level-up package and burning level-up events, and the data import has no case for them). The client's dialogs take their steps and rewards from the event the server sent, so without it they have nothing to show and the server has nothing to pay out. The client reads answers only for 12 and 13, and has no refusal text for any of the six, so they are read and logged. Sub-type 5 is sent by any player whose client has feature 0x82 when the VIP entry of the buddy event page is clicked; it is not a GM mode.
- Ugc 12 (the path of an uploaded wedding profile picture): the upload itself (kind 10) is not accepted and a marriage stores one profile text where the client keeps one per partner, so the request never arrives in normal play and is only logged.
- Revival 3 (pet object id; the client's pet state 0x30 sends it every 3 seconds while the owner is dead and revival is not blocked): only logged. What puts the pet in that state and what the revive costs is not found in the client, so no rule is written. It is not counted in the table above.
- RequestPet 15 (pet attack button; one bool): the game menu shows the button for a pet whose row has type 1 and flips the client's own flag when it is pressed (`UIGameMenuDialog::OnPetAttack`, `CPetManager::SendRequestPet`). The client uses the flag only to draw the button and reads nothing back for it. A summoned pet here follows its owner and does not fight, so the flag has nothing to switch; it is read and dropped. The server's answer 15 (it sets the client's flag) is sent only at login.
- Quest 17 (epic quest restart; no body): the game menu sends it only while an epic quest restart game event runs, the character has not restarted yet and stands in a field the event allows. The server's event table has no such event, so the client shows its "event ended" text and sends nothing. Which quests a restart resets is not known from the client. Logged if it arrives.
- Quest 33 (complete an alliance quest with completion tickets; the body is the quest id) is handled. The client sends it from the alliance quest window for a quest in progress whose basic `property` has `AvailableCompletionTicket` while the feature `FameCompletionTicket` is on (`UIAllianceQuestDialog::CanUseCompletionTicket`, unit_140bf60f0_uialliancequestdialog.cpp:597). The quest is completed without its conditions and with its rewards, and the server takes as many items of tag `FameCompletionTicket` as the Lua `calcFameCompletionTicketCount` gives for the quest's rank (S 90, A 60, B 50, C 20, D 10, other 0; the alliance argument is not used). The tickets are taken only when the completion went through. A quest without the property, a cost of 0, too few tickets or a completion refused (a full bag) get Quest error 7, the client's text for a ticket that could not be used; the client reads no other answer. The property is a new part of the quest table, so it reads false until the data ingest of this build is run.
- Quest 34 (accept all alliance quests) and Quest 35 (complete all) are handled. Neither has a body: the alliance is the talk NPC's, whose kind is the alliance's `officerNpcKind` in `table/famedef.xml` (`CFameTable::GetAllianceByNpc`, commonfiles/famegradedata.cpp:830). 34 starts each mission of that alliance in the lists sent (Alliance missions, under Fixes) that can be started, then Quest 0x22 names the alliance (the client shows `s_quest_alliance_accept_all_<alliance>`); 35 completes each held alliance quest of that alliance, daily or weekly, whose conditions are done, then Quest 0x23. With none started or completed, or no alliance for the talk NPC, the answer is Quest error 3 or 2. The client's accept-all also takes a weekly quest with a `groupID` but no `unrequireGroup` (`CanAcceptAllQuests`, unit_140bf60f0_uialliancequestdialog.cpp:612); the server does not start those (8 quests in the data). Quest 19 (re-enter a quest's hidden field) is handled: a started quest whose data has `useReEntryHiddenMap` moves the player to its first progress map. The flag is a new part of the quest table, so it reads false until the data ingest of this build is run. Quest 28 (find a player for a quest) is handled: the found player is tested against the quest's `user_find` condition (job, level, gender, title, ride, shown equipment) and the condition goes up. The effect named in the condition's code (`effectId`, `effectLevel`) is not given: the client does not show who gets it.
- RequestItemEnchant 9 (refund of enchant experience): sent from a message box that the client opens only for an item whose enchant record has experience and a version byte other than 1. The server writes that byte and always writes 1, so the box never opens. Which materials the refund gives back is not shown by the client, so the request is read and logged.
- RequestItemEnchant 13 and 14 (enchant simulation): sent by a simulation window that nothing in the client opens, and the client reads no answer under either number. They need a GM account and are then only logged.
- RequestItemEnchant: The GM debug buttons 15 to 18 are handled for a GM account. 15 (force enchant): the enchant of the item in the window succeeds at once and nothing is consumed. 16 (fill ingredients): the materials of that enchant are put in the bag; the first button tops the bag up to what the enchant needs, the second gives 10 of each (the server's reading of the two button names). 17 and 18: the item's stored fail charges are filled to a full charge rate or emptied. 19 has a sender in the client that nothing calls, so it is not counted; it is logged for a GM account.
- Party 32 to 34 (dungeon match): see the matchmaker under Fixes; the match-found box has no confirm wait or decline, and the World side is tested only through the queue (it needs channel clients).
- Divorce is handled. It is driven by the npc scripts of the divorce lawyers (script conditions weddingState, marriageDate and coolingOff; script function attribute divorce) and three wedding sub-types. Filing alone (function individual): married for more than 30 days and holding the fee of 1,000,000 meso, which is taken; the marriage goes to state 3 or 4 (filed by the first or the second partner of the wedding packet) for a cooling off of 7 days from the filing time, which the wedding packet carries as its first time. The other partner can agree (individualAgree): the couple is divorced (state 5) and the filer gets the fee back with a mail. The filer can take it back (individualcancel) until the cooling off ends: married again, no fee back. When the cooling off ends the couple is divorced without a request.
