The groom and bride steps run: the groom is the hall's reserver, see Wedding 26 above. shadow_expedition_points stays false above 0: nothing in the scripts, the client or the tables says what raises the gauge points. create_field_game and field_game_* (17 files: hide and seek, Maple Survival, guild vs game, water gun battle) are whole game modes; hide and seek runs in part (the Run in part line below), Maple Survival runs (see Maple Survival (Mushking Royale)), guild vs game runs (see Guild vs game, under Guild; with its registration and match), the water gun battle is not built. set_pvp_zone and pvp_zone_ended run for zone types 1, 2, 3, 5 and 6 (the Red Arena, the free-for-all, Treasure Island, 61000035's one punch and item rounds; see Individual arena); type 4 (guild, 64000001) is logged at Debug and not built (its teams need the unreachable guild PvP register, see Individual arena), nor are the other pvp_zone_* actions. add_buff with its skill-set flag (arg5) is not applied (logged at Debug): the KR client takes a skill set only from an additional effect's own skill set id (AddSkillSet, additionalcli_2.cpp), and most ids the scripts pass with the flag are no skill set. 70000040 (12 uses) has none: applied, it would only put on its skillUseDisable mask 62 (skill main types 1-5, the active job skills are type 2) for 2 s, renewed every second. The round phases do not confine it to a countdown: once a player is seen the loop runs to the end of the map, on the Red Arena stage (box 102, where the face-off is fought; its stage drops are an Onyx Crystal and Valor Tokens, no combat items), the Crimson Tear Mine platform, the Glorious Arena PvP zone (guild zone type 4, not built) and the rank duel spawn strips (the KR rank duel season has ended), so applying it would leave the Red Arena duelists without job skills for the whole fight. The flagged wedding pinata effects (99940044, 99940042) do carry skill sets (99930044, 99930042); the server has no additional-effect skill sets, so they are skipped too. field_war_end (4 files) runs: a clear gives the war's reward (see Field war). arcade_spring_farm_*, arcade_boom_boom_ocean_* and arcade_three_two_one{,2,3}_* run (the Run in part line below); the event arcades 3-5 (feature Event, the Three Two One games) open with the ArcadeOpen game events (Event arcades), which in KR have all ended and run only through the replay.
  - Run in part: dark_stream_* (59 files, fields 02000350 and 02000498). start_game opens the score dialog (DarkStream 0xC5 command 0), a spawn of spawn_monster gives its score once all its monsters are dead (command 1), and clear_round of the round start_game named sends the result (command 2: score, best score before the run, exp, meso, habi, then the reward items); the scripts have no fail or dungeon clear, so the run ends there and the player leaves with the result dialog (RequestMoveField 1) or the portal the script opens. Missing: what damage_penalty takes from the score (the client shows a score below 0, nothing says when it is taken; it is kept, not applied); the result gives each player in the field the row of table/darkstream.xml (ingested as DarkStreamTable, rows by round, a locale's row replacing the plain one) for the rounds cleared, or the row below when there is none: the meso, the habi, and the items (id,rank,count, the order of ItemEntity; to the bag, else by mail), sent in the result as ItemEntity::Read reads them (int id, short rank, int count, four bools sent as false). Inferred, not given: the exp is the row's expRate times the Dark Stream room's rewardExp (dungeonroom.xml), the rule of the rooms' own rewardExpRate; the client reads rewardExp and rewardExpRate but uses neither, the exp table has no Dark Stream row, and the client's exp type list has no Dark Stream type, so no level-based rule is given. How several items are listed in one row is not shown (each shipped row has one item; the reader takes further triples after a comma or semicolon). Habi is HaviFruit (the client icon list puts it after lu/Rue, as Maple2 puts HaviFruit after Rue). How a run ends before the last round (death or leaving) is not in the scripts; leaving ends it with no result. The best score is kept per character (table dark-stream-record: the score and the time it was set; the migration AddDarkStreamRecord, removed with the character): the result sends the best before the run (0 before a first run), and the run's score replaces it only when it is higher (an equal score keeps the older record and its time). The web ranking (irrq.aspx) answers the Dark Stream boards from it, built like the trophy boards: 0x21 is the header, a count and rows of rank, character id, name, profile image, two ints, the score and the time it was set, as GameRanking::DarkStream reads them (the best 200, or with a search name the row of that character); 0x1f is the character's rank on that board (0 without a score). The request's ranking id is the board's job tab: the ranking dialog adds a tab per job code under the board, 0 for every job. Not settled by the client: the two ints of a row are read but used nowhere (sent as 0); how equal scores rank is chosen here (the earlier record first). No season is kept, so the boards of the season before (0x22, and 0x20 for the own rank) are empty and rank 0.
  - Run in part: arcade_spring_farm_* (the Spring Farm arcade, arcade id 1). The lobby asks with PlayArcade mode 10 (enter) and mode 9 (scores). Enter moves the player to the game's field (table/arcadegame.xml, ingested as ArcadeGameTable); an unknown game, an event arcade (isEventArcade) no running ArcadeOpen event names, a game without a field, a dead player, a player in battle (as the client's lobby refuses it) or one already in the field is refused. Each player gets a field of their own: the arcade fields 51000001-51000006 are solo instance fields (table/Server/instancefield.xml Type 1), and an instance field entered without an owner or a room is a new field each time (FieldEntryRules.NewFieldPerEntry). start_game opens the score dialog (0xD8 Open, the open type is the arcade id) and the life bar (LifeInit, chosen as 1); each interact object use and each spawn cleared adds its score once (Score); an object that keeps its state when used (Ui, Telescope) scores once per object in the game, while the shipped scored objects are all Mesh (interact type 19000022, reset after 3 s) and score on each use; clear_round of the open round adds the time score (the whole seconds left times the rate, the remain value; for the open round only); a player's death takes a life, and 0 lives ends the game at once; end_game ends it once. The end pays only the game's players, the characters in the field at start_game, and sends each the Result: score, best before the game, exp, meso. Chosen here: the exp is the arcade exp curve (CommonExpTable arcade) times the row's expRate; the reward row is the one of table/arcadereward.xml (ArcadeRewardTable, a locale's row replacing the plain one; a second node with the same arcadeId adds its rows to the first one's, though the shipped file has one node per arcade, ids 1-5) with the most rounds at or below the rounds cleared, none below the first row; no habi is given, and items only by an event arcade's event (Event arcades). The best is kept per character, arcade and Arcade season (table arcade-record, the migration AddArcadeRecord, removed with the character); a higher score replaces it, an equal or lower one keeps it; without an Arcade season no best is kept. The scores list (mode 9) sends the bests of the current Arcade season (region = the season id, 0 and no rows when there is none). After each Result the server sends HideAndSeek 0x12B mode 3 with the player's object id: for the player's own object the client closes the life bar (dialog 0x4efc, the one mode 4 opens as UIArcadeLifeBar) and shows the hud again. A game player (of any of the three arcades) who leaves the field before the end gets no Result, which is what closes the score dialog: on the leave the server sends PlayArcade mode 13 (no payload; the client's CloseDialog of the score dialog 0x4ee4) and the same HideAndSeek 3.
  - Run in part: arcade_boom_boom_ocean_* (arcade id 2, entered from the lobby like Spring Farm). start_game opens the score dialog and the life bar with life_count (20); set_skill_score keeps the score of each skill id; a hit by a non-player skill with a score, or a buff granted with one (each grant, a refresh too), adds it; the score does not go below 0. Chosen here: only a hit that lands scores (not evaded, not on an invincible player: damage type Normal, Critical or Block); a negative skill score also takes a life, and so does a death, but a hit that takes a life and kills takes only that one; the time score of a round is the whole seconds survived, at most the round's duration, times the round's rate, given once at clear_round of the open round, so a game that ends inside a round (0 lives, the fail box's end_game) pays nothing for it (the client has no say: the score is the server's). 0 lives ends the game and moves the game's players to portal 44 (the fail box's portal, as the scripts' fail end does).
  - Run in part: arcade_three_two_one_*, arcade_three_two_one2_* and arcade_three_two_one3_* (Three Two One, the event arcades 3, 4 and 5 of the fields 51000004-06, entered from the lobby while their ArcadeOpen event runs). start_game opens the score dialog with init_score and the life bar with life_count; start_round (round, ui_duration, the order of the script attributes) shows the three buttons for ui_duration seconds (PlayArcade 7, the client's couple dance window); each player picks with PlayArcade 11 (an int direction: the buttons send 2, 1, 3); result_round hides the buttons and sets the user value ThreeTwoOneResult the script reads, 1 when every game player in the field picked and none picked the round's direction, else 0; result_round2 of a win adds 1000 and the streak (PlayArcade 8 type 0), of a loss takes a life and resets the streak. Chosen here: the win score 1000 and no pick = a loss (the scripts give neither); the rounds cleared of the reward row are the rounds won, since every script's clear_round passes round 1 and runs after a loss too; the score starts at init_score (10000), so a game with no win still ends at 10000 and keeps that as the best. 0 lives ends the game and moves the players to portal 44, as above.
  - Run in part: create_field_game HideAndSeek, field_game_constant and field_game_message (the hide-and-seek fields 61000023-61000029, feature MassiveHideAndSeek). The game moves the main script through its steps by setting each step's user value on the trigger that made it, after the script's own times: WaitForEnterUser (EnterableSeconds), GameRuleNotice (GameRuleNoticeTick), DivideIntoTeams (DivideTeamsSeconds), MoveGameArea (MoveGameAreaTick), BeInHidingTeams (BeInHidingTeamsSeconds), LookingForATeams (LookingForATeamsSeconds), TeamMatchResult (TeamMatchResultTick), GameExitNotice (GameExitNoticeTick), End; fewer players than RequireUserCount at the end of WaitForEnterUser or DivideIntoTeams goes to ShortOfUser (ShortOfUserTick), then End. A constant is kept when its locale is absent or the server's (the ingest drops the ones of a disabled feature); a key no hide-and-seek field gives is ignored with one Debug line, and a missing time moves on at once. MoveGameArea splits the players in the field and sends each one's team to the field (HideAndSeek 0x12B mode 0: in the game, team 1 hiders or 2 seekers), then moves the hiders to the PortalGameAreaID portal; field_game_message SetEventUI shows its text banner to its team (custom 1 the hiders, 2 the seekers); LookingForATeams moves the seekers to the same portal and gives them the SeekTeamSkillSetID skill set (mode 5); TeamMatchResult takes it back (mode 6) and opens each team player's result dialog (mode 8); a seeker who leaves the field while the seekers look gets mode 6 on the way out, so the skill set's quick slots do not stay locked. A team left empty by players leaving ends the game: in MoveGameArea with ShortOfUser, while hiding or seeking with the result (the other team wins, but without the winner reward, see below). Hiders become objects: at MoveGameArea each hider rides one of the field's riding objects (table/Server/hideAndSeek.xml ridingObject, ingested as HideAndSeekTable; RideOnAction type 3, which the client reads with the base fields only) and gets its object's life bar (mode 2; the client hides the hud for every bar type but 2); the props of the map's xblock whose model is that ride's nif (ObjectSearchIndex) are listed per ride, and the one a hider takes is hidden for the field (mode 1, `WorldService::HideEntities`: invisible, no collision). The seekers get their lives bar with the seek skill set (mode 2 type 2, the hud's slot mode). The seek skill (90001159, the set's only skill) is a const 2500 arrow: its hit takes 2500 from the hider's object (the ride's health: 5000 for the KR rides, so 2 hits) and sends the hider's bar its hits left (mode 4); the bar counts hits, the object's health divided by the skill's const damage, rounded up. The player itself takes no damage, and other skills do nothing to a hider. At 0 the hider is caught: off the object (RideOff type 26), its bar closed (mode 3), the field gets the new info of both players (the catch and the seeker's kills), the hider goes to the PortalFailAreaID portal and gets the watch dialog (mode 7). A seek shot whose landing (Skill CubeMagicPath; the position is the client's, so it is pulled back onto the line to the seeker at the seek attack's range, rangeProperty distance 750) is farther than the landing splash's 200 (skill 90001160, a condition skill of 90001159 that does no damage: value 0, not const, so the hits come from 90001159) from every hider still hidden costs its seeker a life once SeekTeamInvincibleTick has passed from the start of LookingForATeams (MAIN__6 tells the seekers they lose no life for hitting non-hider objects during it, shown to them as a banner for InvincibleNoticeTick); a seeker with no lives left loses the skill set and its bar and watches. The last catch ends the seeking with the seekers' win, the last seeker's last life with the hiders'. TeamMatchResult takes back what the game gave (the skill set, the objects, the bars, the watch dialog) and gives each team player its team's reward (Winner or Loser RewardItemID, RewardItemRank, RewardItemCount of the server's region) to the bag, or the mail when the bag is full; the result dialog shows the reward only once it is given. No one gets a reward unless the game's players still in the field are at least RequireUserCount, and a game that ended because a player left (a team emptied, or the last hider or the last seeker with lives went) pays no winner reward: the team left behind gets the loser's. The result also raises each team player's trigger conditions with the field's codes: EventHideAndSeekStart and EventDailyQuestStart (hideandseek_start, dailyquest_start) for both teams and EventHideAndSeekWin (hideandseek_win) for the winners, which count achieves 23400056-7 and the CN quests 80010530 and 80010540-3; each once per player per game, and none for a player without a reward or in a game that ended because a player left. Hidden props cannot be shown again: the client has no inverse of `WorldService::HideEntities`, so after the result or a reset they stay hidden for the players still in the field until they enter it again. A player who arrives after the split gets the players' info and the hidden props, and the watch dialog while the game runs; one who arrives after End is sent back to its return field, as the script's End sends everyone. A player who leaves gets back what the game gave, and a new create_field_game in the field (the script's reset) first takes back what the old game gave. The hit path touches FieldPlayer.ApplyDamage, FieldManager.PlayerHostile and the player target pool (a seeker's skills reach the hiders while it seeks), and SkillHandler's CubeMagicPath for the misses. Chosen here: a third of the players seek (at least 1, and at least 1 hides): no field's constants and no client code give the seekers' count; the result's round is 1 (the client only hands it to the dialog); the seekers have 3 lives (no constant or client code gives a count); a hider's ride is a random one of the field's, first among those with a prop on the map no hider has taken yet (nothing says which props the server hides); the hiders' bar type is 1 and every bar starts full (the client treats every type but 2 alike); the watch dialog's int is WatchCameraID (the client keeps it and never reads it) and it opens unfolded; a miss is a landing with no hidden hider within the splash radius (the client sends no hit for the props it strikes); the invincible message shows as a text banner for InvincibleNoticeTick; the reward rules after a leave and the start minimum for any reward (so that two characters cannot farm the winner reward by one of them leaving); the landing clamp to the attack's range; the events raised at the result, once per player per game and not after a leave (no client code or data says when). The result exp is 0: HideTeamExp and SeekTeamExp are 0 in all seven fields. The watch dialog's camera buttons move the client's own camera and send nothing. Its two emotion buttons play the cheer emotions 90210001 (Applaud) and 90210002 (Glowstick), which the client gives only to a caught hider (the Info "death" flag) and on the performance map (field 2000064; `CSkillSystem::IsEmotionLearned`); Emote Use takes these two there only, learned or not, and refuses them everywhere else, also when learned. The client plays them through Emote Use only (the emotion state 0xb). Tested: CheerEmoteTests and the caught hider in FieldHit_SeekSkillCatchesInTwoHits.
  - give_guild_exp (13): type 2 would give the guild exp of a GuildGainExp table row, as the client's winMiniGameGuildExp suggests; neither pack has a GuildGainExp table, so the amount and the mapping are not known.
  - set_mini_game_area_for_hack and unset_mini_game_area_for_hack (13): anti-hack areas. The client's check for them is not known.
  - give_exp (1) and start_tutorial (1): one use each, and the script does not settle the rule.
  - announce: no shipped script uses it.
- Web ranking (irrq.aspx) boss boards: types 0x47 (71), 0x48 (72), 0x51 (81) and 0x5b (91) are answered from the boss clears the game keeps. The ranking dialog's boss boards (raid 7, chaos raid 8, reverse raid 9, lapenta 0xf and turka 0x11) add one tab per dungeon room of that group type with a BossRanking, the request's ranking id is that BossRanking, and every tab has three modes that ask for the same types on all five boards (CGameRankingCli::BuildKey): the clears per character (0x48, the own rank 0x47), the first clears (0x51) and the fastest clears (0x5b). An earlier note here said 71 and 72 are raid-only types the client never asks for; that was wrong. The first change of a room to Clear keeps one row (table boss-clear, the migration AddBossClear) when the room has a BossRanking and its group type is one of those five: the BossRanking, the room, the leader, the clear time in seconds (the time the play-time missions are scored with) and the date; its members (table boss-clear-member) are the players of the room's party in the field, led by the party leader when the leader is one of them, else by the first of them. When none of the room's party is in the field (the party broke up or was left during the run), or the room was entered without a party, the room's first user is kept alone when in the field; with nobody to keep, the game logs it at Debug. A second Clear of the same room (the state change repeating, a reload) keeps nothing. Colosseum rooms also have a BossRanking, but their board (0x10) asks for other types, so they are kept on their own (Colosseum line below); fame challenge rooms are kept for their own board (next line). A member goes with its character (a deleted character drops out of the rows and counts); the leader has no key to the character, and a row whose leader's character is gone shows its first member, as the client does; a clear with no member left is not shown. 0x51 and 0x5b rows are read as GameRankingItem::BossKillRecord reads them: rank, a long the client does not use (sent as the row id), the leader's character id, an int the client does not use (sent as the BossRanking), the clear time in seconds (shown as minutes and seconds), the date, and the members (character id, name, profile image, level and job code; the client keeps the first 10). 0x48 rows are rank, character id, name, profile image and three ints of which the client shows the second (the clears); the first goes out as the BossRanking and the third as 0, neither is used. Not settled by the client, chosen here: 0x51 is the earliest clears first, 0x5b the fastest first and then the earlier one, equal ones by the row id; 0x48 is the most clears first, equal counts by the earlier last clear, then the character id; the best 200 rows, and with a search name (0x48 only, as the client asks) the row of that character. No season is kept. Rooms ingested before this change have no BossRanking: re-ingest the dungeon rooms.
- Web ranking (irrq.aspx) fame challenge board: types 0x8d (141), 0x8e (142), 0x97 (151), 0xa1 (161) and 0xa2 (162) are answered from the same kept clears (the enum names FortressRumble* and their placeholder writers are replaced; their "S rank" and "early victory" reading was not the client's). The ranking dialog's board 0xc adds one tab per fameChallenge room with a BossRanking and asks with it, in three modes (CGameRankingCli::BuildKey, the dialog's tab labels): the clears per character (0x8e, the own rank 0x8d), the first clears (0x97, no own rank) and the fastest clears (0xa2, the own rank 0xa1). A clear of a fameChallenge room with a BossRanking is now kept as the boss clears are. 0x8e rows are GameRankingItem::BossRumbleCount, the same as 0x48 rows. 0x97 and 0xa2 rows are GameRankingItem::BossRumbleRecord, one character each (the shipped fame challenge rooms take one player, and the dialog's tooltip shows character info, not a party): rank, a long the client does not use (sent as the row id), the character id, name, profile image, level, job code, an int the client does not use (sent as the BossRanking), the clear time in seconds and the date. Not settled by the client, chosen here: a character has one row on 0x97 (its first clear, the earliest first) and on 0xa2 (its fastest clear, then the earlier one), so its own rank on 0xa2 is its place there; 0x8e is ordered as 0x48; the best 200 rows. The seasons of these rooms (seasonRankRewardID) are not kept. No re-ingest beyond the one the boss boards need.
- Web ranking (irrq.aspx) world boss board: types 0x6f (111), 0x70 (112), 0x79 (121), 0x83 (131) and 0x84 (132) are answered from the same kept clears; they were not in GameRankingType and went unanswered. The ranking dialog's board 0xb adds one tab per worldBossDungeon room with a BossRanking (the shipped one is 24090004, the Crimson Balrog field 02000410) and asks with it, in three modes (CGameRankingCli::BuildKey, the dialog's tab labels "Greatest Contribution", "Earliest Clear" and "Damage Dealt"): the clears per character (0x70, the own rank 0x6f), the first clears (0x79, no own rank) and the clears with the most damage (0x84, the own rank 0x83). A clear of a worldBossDungeon room with a BossRanking is now kept as the boss clears are (the room's first Clear: the field's 15-minute survival), and the row also keeps the party's damage (the migration AddBossClearDamage, a Damage column on boss-clear, 0 for the other rooms): the sum of the damage the room's players dealt to its bosses (npcs of class 3 and up, the server's IsBoss) before the room had its result. 0x70 rows are GameRankingItem::BossDamageCount, the same as 0x48 rows. 0x79 rows are GameRankingItem::BossDamageDate: rank, a long the client does not use (sent as the row id), the leader's character id, an int it does not use (sent as the BossRanking), the row's point as a long, the date and the members as on 0x51. 0x84 rows are GameRankingItem::BossDamageAmount: rank, a long not used (the row id), the leader's character id, a long not used (0), an int not used (the BossRanking), three longs of which the client shows the third as the point, a fourth long not used (0), an int shown as a rank point when not 0, the date and the members (the client shows the leader's name and image). Not settled by the client, chosen here: the point of 0x79 and 0x84 is the party's damage; the 0x84 rank point is sent as 0 (nothing on our side gives one); 0x79 is ordered as 0x51, 0x84 the most damage first, then the earlier clear, then the row id, and the own rank 0x83 is the place of the character's best clear there; 0x70 is ordered as 0x48, with a search name the row of that character (0x70 only, as the client asks); the best 200 rows. Not tried live.
- Web ranking (irrq.aspx) Colosseum board: types 0xcb (203), 0xcc (204), 0xc9 (201) and 0xca (202) are answered from the best Colosseum run per character and season (table colosseum-record, the migration AddColosseumRecord, removed with the character). The ranking dialog's board 0x10 has a tab for the current Colosseum season and up to three seasons before it (seasondata_bosscolosseum, weekly), each with a tab for every job and one per job code, shown only when a colosseum room has a BossRanking; the current season's tab asks for 0xcb (0xc9 for the own rank) with no season, the others for 0xcc (0xca) with the season id, and the request's ranking id is the job tab (0 for every job). The current season is the one whose start and end hold the time. The first end of a colosseum room with a BossRanking keeps the run of its first user, when in the field and it cleared a round: the rounds of dungeon_clear_round and the room's seconds (the time the play-time missions are scored with), in the season that holds the time (none outside every season); dungeon_disable_ranking (the continued game) keeps nothing. It replaces the character's best of that season with more rounds, or the same rounds in fewer seconds (an equal run keeps the older record). Rows are read as GameRanking::BossColosseum reads them: rank, character id, name, profile image, two ints and a long the client does not show (sent as 0), the rounds (shown as the points), the seconds (shown as minutes and seconds), an int not shown (0) and two time strings not shown (the time the run was set). Not settled by the client, chosen here: most rounds first, then fewer seconds, then the earlier record, then the character id; the best 200 rows, and with a search name the row of that character. A second run in the same room (the script goes back to its start after each end) is not kept: the room's seconds would count both runs.
- House commendation and web ranking (irrq.aspx) house board: the home info dialog's recommend button sends RequestCube 0x19 (CommendHome), which the server left unanswered. The server now keeps the commendation (table home-commend, the migration AddHomeCommend; the rows go with the home and with the commending account) when the player stands in a home that is not its own and has not commended today: the client's rule for the button (`IsNotRecentDayChange`: no commendation yet, one more than a day ago or one on an earlier calendar day of the server's time zone) is checked again. The refusals are ResponseCube 0x19 with a ShowBuildNotice code: 76 outside a home, 78 for the own home, 81 for a second one that day, 77 when the row is not saved. The voter gets 0x19 with its account and the commendation time (the client keeps it and turns the button off), the field gets the home's scores (ResponseCube 0x1c, the dialog's "current / total") and HomeCommand 1 for the owner (the client finds the owner's PC by object id and refreshes its MyInfo dialog), sent to the field the owner is in, on any channel: the new scores go to the other channels through the World (FieldPlotRequest architect_score), which set the loaded home's and the online owner's copies; the commend_home condition is counted. HomeCommand 0 at login now carries the account's last commendation time instead of 0, so the button is off after a relog the same day. A home's architect scores (Home, the field entry) are its commendations of the current month and of all time; at the monthly reset each channel reads them again for its loaded homes and online owners, so the month's score starts from 0. The ranking dialog's board 6 asks for 0x3f (0x3d for the own rank) for the current month without a date and 0x40 (0x3e) with the yyyymm month for the months before (`CGameRankingCli::BuildKey`, FUN_140b3df90); these four types were not answered. Rows are read as GameRanking::UgcMapCommendation reads them: rank, the owner's character id (portrait), name and profile image, the commendations (shown as the points), an int the client does not use (0), the owner's account id and the home's name (the outdoor plot's name, else the indoor one). The own rank is the asking account's home. Not settled by the client, chosen here: most commendations first, then the home that reached its count first, then the account id; the owner's character is its last played one, a home whose account has no character is left out; the best 200 rows, and with a search name the row of that character's home.
- Web ranking (irrq.aspx) requests of type 0: the ranking dialog sends a request whose key type is 0 when the board it shows has no tab. `UIGameRankingDialog::BuildRankingKey` and `BuildMyRankKey` return before `CGameRankingCli::BuildKey` without a tab, and the season boards (Dark Stream 3, Colosseum 0x10 and the others with a SeasonRankType) only get tabs from a season of the client's own Table/SeasonData_<type>.xml whose start and end hold the server time (`CSeasonDataTable::Refresh`, `GetUpcoming`). The client has no answer reader for type 0, so nothing the server sends can fill such a board: the fix is a season in the played client's table. The server used to answer these requests with a 400, which made the client send them twice more, 500 ms apart; it now sends the empty answer it gives a board with nothing to serve, and logs at Information that the played client's season table has no season at the server time. Reading the request body is now one place (GameRankingRequest), with tests built from the client's writer.
- Quest completion currency: the completed quest row is stored first (with the currency held before the reward), then the reward's mesos, merets and other currency are added in memory and stored by the next session save. A crash in between keeps the quest completed without that currency: a loss to the player, not a reward twice. Adding them before the store would go around the currency setters, which also send the wallet packets and count the quest conditions; left for a change of those setters.

## Two local changes a server operator must know

1. **All servers bind to loopback (127.0.0.1) by default.** To serve other machines, set `MS2_BIND_IP` to the address to listen on (`0.0.0.0` for all interfaces, as upstream); the server has no hardening for the open internet beyond upstream's.
2. **Test hooks.** Two environment variables turn on test hooks. Neither does anything unless it is set.
   - `TST_CMD_FILE`: once a second, the game server reads a file named after this prefix and the character id, and runs each line as a chat command **as the logged-in player**. Never set it on a server that others can reach.
   - `TST_PKT_DIR`: the server appends every packet that clients send, including chat, to a log file per character in this directory.

**LOCALE must be the played client's locale.** The server takes script branches and locale rows from LOCALE, the client from its own region; with a KR client and LOCALE=NA, for example, the client lets an outfit with no break row be dismantled (its KR script gives lucky bag coupons) while the server's NA branch previews and pays nothing. The ingest prints a warning when MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML is set and its pack's region (its one table/<region>/ folder) is not LOCALE.

## Errata in commit subjects

Published commits are not rewritten, so wrong numbers in their subjects are listed here.

- `6fea6fcf` and `fce39da6` say "Guild 0x0054". The opcode is `RecvOp.Guild = 0x004B`.
- `e319062d` says "RequestPet 0x00C2". The opcode is `RecvOp.RequestPet = 0x0078`.
